Census correction: `Sym.ir-name` **and** `trace-saved` must stay `ptr`, NOT `CStr` — `ir-name` is compared by *pointer identity* (`import-alias-one`; non-interned) and null-checked, so a `CStr` retype lowers `=`/`!=` to `strcmp` (`strcmp(x,null)` crashes); `trace-saved` mirrors `ir-name`. ~14 redundant casts deleted (raw/plain contexts only; the `(cast ptr:T (x f))`→`ft:ptr:T` asserting-into-`ref` let-binding idiom keeps its cast) + the `defer-scope` cast; 168/168 tests, bootstrap fixed point. **14.2 batch 2 (remaining 16 `compiler-types.nuc` structs) DONE (2026-07-12), byte-identical**: `StructDef`/`UnionDef`/`UnionTemplate`/`StructTemplate`/`Scope`/`Val`/`ProgDefn`/`BinOp`/`Method`/`TmplConformance`/`MonoJob`/`MacroDef`/`RMacro`/`LabelEntry`/`CastRule`/`NarrowUndo` all retyped per the idiom (full field list in stage14/type-safety.md §14.2). Two new non-identity findings beyond the batch-1 checklist: `Method.ir-name` stays `ptr` (same null-check-before-strcmp danger as `Sym.ir-name`, at `defn-ir-name`/`generic-method-bind-adapt`); `Val.val` stays `ptr` for a *dispatch* reason, not identity — several match/cond phi accumulators `conj` it into a `(Vector ptr)`, and `CStr` doesn't adapt to a bare `ptr` multimethod parameter, so the retype broke `conj` outright (caught by rebuilding, not by inspection). `RMacro.wrap-sym` turned out to be a plain string despite its name (fed to `intern-symbol` by content, never a Node/Sym pointer) — safe as `CStr`. Verification stronger than per-batch: diffed `build/nucleusc.ll` byte-identical (zero lines) against a from-scratch baseline of the pre-batch source built with the same boot compiler, plus 168/168 tests and bootstrap fixed point. All 16 structs' scalar cross-reference fields are now done; remaining `compiler-types.nuc` work is the parallel-array fields (14.4) and `Scope.syms`/`cleanup-slots` (14.5). **14.3 batch 1 (`scope.nuc`/`type-utils.nuc`/`type-mangle.nuc` param/return typing) DONE (2026-07-12), byte-identical**: `Scope*`/`Type*`/`Val*`/`Node*` params and returns across these three files retyped to `(raw T)`/`CStr` (full list in stage14/type-safety.md §14.3); ~20 redundant casts deleted (in-file plus `require-derefable` call sites in `nucleusc.nuc`/`union-emit.nuc`). One genuine non-inert finding, caught only by `make test` (not by inspection): `program-defn-record`'s `irn` param looked like a safe `CStr` retype (matching `ProgDefn.ir-name`, already `CStr`) but is null-checked with `=` right in the function body — the retype turned that guard into a crashing `strcmp(irn, null)`, segfaulting on *every* compile. Reverted `irn` to `ptr` in both `program-defn-lookup`/`program-defn-record` (same class as the `Sym.ir-name` precedent). Verification: 168/168 tests, bootstrap fixed point, `build/nucleusc.ll` byte-identical (zero lines) against a from-scratch pre-batch baseline. **14.3 batch 2 (`abi.nuc`/`union-registry.nuc`/`union-emit.nuc` param/return typing) DONE (2026-07-13), byte-identical**: `Type*`/`StructDef*`/`UnionDef*`/`UnionTemplate*`/`StructTemplate*`/`EnumDef*`/`Val*`/`AbiInfo*` params and returns across all three files retyped to `(raw T)`, `Sym.taint`-shaped params to `(raw Scope)`, and genuine C-string params (struct/union/type lookup keys, diagnostic names, IR-fragment-building helper returns) to `CStr`; ~30 redundant casts deleted (in-file rebinds plus direct call-site casts made unnecessary by the retyped callee). `abi-class-eightbyte`'s signature-only retype preserved its byte-exact SysV classification logic untouched (no reordering). One new trap of the same class as batch 1's `program-defn-record`: `abi-print-param`'s `name` param looked `CStr`-safe (matches other name-string retypes) but is null-checked (`= name null`, distinguishing a `declare` from a `define`) — left `ptr`. A second, distinct trap: memo-lookup keys (`boxedfn-memo-lookup`/`dyn-memo-lookup`'s `key`, `dyn-type`'s `proto`) are compared by pointer *identity* for dedup, not content — a `CStr` retype would be functionally harmless today (the values are interned) but silently changes the comparison semantics from identity to `strcmp`, so the whole `g-boxedfn-*`/`g-dyn-*`/`fatptr-*` type-erasure-memo family (already earmarked for 14.5's hand-rolled-growables pass) was left untouched this batch. `Node*`-shaped params (`call`/`form`/`use-node`/`binders`/`arms`/`arms-raw`) were deliberately deferred to the nucleusc.nuc/generics.nuc batches, where the bulk of Node-walking code lives, rather than partially retyping `Node*` in just these three files. Every ragged `(aref (cast ptr:ptr (udd arm-X)) i)` lockstep walk deleted in favor of `(invoke (udd arms) (cast usize i))` returning a `(ref Arm)` with direct field access — build site + layout-classify + payload-union build (union-registry.nuc), the unwrap/construct/match-binder/match-clause/exhaustiveness readers (union-emit.nuc), `union-drop-arm` (nucleusc.nuc), and `repl-declare-union-ctors` (repl.nuc). `arm-is-typed-ref`/`arm-is-err-field`/`union-layout-classify`/`union-arm-index-in` refactored from raw-array params to `(ref Arm)`/`(ref (Vector (ref Arm)))`. Two findings: (a) a `(Vector (ref Arm))` field type resolves inside `compiler-types.nuc` (imported at nucleusc.nuc:23, long before `(import-use vector)` at :463) because `prescan-imported-types`/`prescan-struct-names` register the `Vector` template + `Arm` name globally before any form is emitted — same precedent as the `g-uniondefs` defvar; (b) despite the struct-layout change this reconverged in **one pass** (`make bootstrap` PASSED before `update-bootstrap`), because the refactor changes only the compiler's internal data structures, not the IR it emits for any construct, and Vector-instance stamping is deterministic across old boot and new compiler. `arm-fnames` was dead storage (written, never read) on the old `UnionDef`; the `Arm.fnames` field is likewise written-only, retained to match the design's named shape. Trap avoided: the `x:ref:(Vector T)` colon-before-paren spelling is a known reader mis-parse — used list-form `(x (ref (Vector (ref Arm))))` for params and colon-paren `x:(ref ...)`/list-form for lets. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed all boot IRs. **14.4 batch 2 (`&where` quad → `Constraint`, `Conformance.args` → `(Vector CStr)`) DONE (2026-07-13), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** Introduced `Constraint{proto:CStr, var:CStr, args:ptr, nargs:i32}` (compiler-types.nuc, before `Method`) and replaced the four parallel `&where` arrays (`con-protos`/`con-vars`/`con-args`/`con-nargs`) on **both** `Method` and `TmplConformance` with one `(constraints (raw (Vector (ref Constraint))))` field (`num-constraints` kept as the cached count). `proto`/`var` retyped `ptr`→`CStr` (every comparison already had a `CStr`/StrView operand, so byte-identical in lowering + semantically honest); `args`/`nargs` stay a ragged `ptr[]`+count (assoc-type Arg nodes). Converted `Conformance.args` to `(raw (Vector CStr))` (dropped the `nargs` field; count is `(count args)`) and folded `conformance-args-eq` into a value-equality helper over two Vectors (element `CStr` ⇒ `!=` is a strcmp); `(Vector CStr)` already existed (`g-blanket`/`g-macro-decls`) so no new instance for it. Every ragged `(aref (cast ptr:ptr (mm con-X)) i)` walk gone → `(constraints-at (mm constraints) i)` → `(ref Constraint)` with direct field access; the two builder fixpoints (`register-generic-defn`, `emit-extend`) build the Vector via `(vector-new-in (addr-of g-arena-alloc))`. Three findings: (a) **`count-pattern-nodes` was NOT deleted** — despite the design's premise it sizes the `tyvars` array, not the constraint arrays (which were exactly `nc`-sized); it and all its call sites stay live. (b) Reconverged in **one pass** again (`make bootstrap` PASSED before `update-bootstrap`) — new `(Vector (ref Constraint))` instance stamps deterministically across old boot + new compiler; only internal data structures changed, not emitted IR. (c) The honestly-nullable `constraints` field (most Methods are non-generic) forces a `raw→ref` assertion at every `invoke` read — centralized in a `constraints-at` helper, sound because guarded by `num-constraints > 0`; the builder fixpoints invoke a local `ref` Vector so need no cast, and `conformance-args`' `die-at`-guarded return needed the same one-shot cast. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed all boot IRs; assoc-types/valid/blanket examples diff-exact. **14.4 batch 3a (StructDef `field-names`/`field-types` → `Field{name:ptr, type:(raw Type)}`) DONE (2026-07-13), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** The highest-surface 14.4 batch (~40 `field-names` + ~64 `field-types` sites across abi/cheader/compiler-types/union-registry/generics/repl/nucleusc). Replaced `StructDef`'s two parallel arrays with one `(fields (raw (Vector (ref Field))))` field (`num-fields` kept as the cached count — read on the hot `struct-field-index`/ABI paths). **`Field.name` stays `ptr` NOT `CStr`** (unlike batch-2 `Constraint.proto`): `struct-field-index` matches a selector against it by pointer identity on the hot field-access path (NS-5 identity substrate) — a `CStr` retype would lower that to `strcmp`; `Field.type` is `(raw Type)` (binds implicitly into the readers' bare-`ptr` locals; returns fine into `?ptr:Type`). Two shared accessors in abi.nuc (earliest reader): `field-at (fields i):ref:Field` (batch-2 `constraints-at` raw→ref cast, sound under the `num-fields` loop bound) and `struct-set-fields (sd fnames ftypes nf)` — the single array→Vector conversion point (loop-in-place builders redirected to *local* arrays + one call, byte-preserving their extract/emit logic; names not re-interned — arrays already hold canonical interned pointers). `fields` is nullable (`register-struct` pre-registers name-only structs). abi.nuc IR-lowering zone: field *access* retyped, eightbyte-classification algorithm untouched → **`make abi-test` (`abi-interop`) PASS**. cheader text emitters: access retyped, string assembly untouched; typedef-alias shares the source struct's `fields` Vector handle. **Reconverged in ONE pass** (`make bootstrap` PASSED before `update-bootstrap`, despite touching abi.nuc — emitted IR is a function of field *types*, which `field-at` reproduces exactly, so the parallel-array→Vector change is invisible in output IR; the OLD boot's parallel-array StructDef and NEW Vector StructDef emit identical IR for the new source). `make test` 168/168; `make abi-test` green; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed all boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical. **14.4 batch 3b (`Type.params`/`param-names`/`opt-defaults`) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass — closes 14.4 entirely.** Folded `Type`'s **parallel** param pair (`params` = `Type*[]`, `param-names` = name-string[], both length `num-params`) into a single `(params (raw (Vector (ref Field))))` field, **reusing batch 3a's `Field{name, type}` struct** (no new element type). `num-params` kept as the cached count. The `opt-defaults` tail asymmetry was resolved by **not folding it**: it is a *ragged tail* array of length `nopt` (not `num-params`) holding defaults for only the trailing `nopt` &optional params (slot k ↔ param index `required + k`), so — like `Constraint.args`/`Arm.fnames`/`StructDef.origin-args` — it stays a raw `ptr[]`+count, untouched, keeping the tail-offset arithmetic at its two live sites (`emit-call` default-fill read, `emit-defn` build write) byte-for-byte. `param-names` was **dead storage** (no builder ever stored non-null; the REPL renders positional `%aN`; `emit-defn` builds a *local* names array for the prologue but never writes it onto the Type) — so the field is dropped and `Field.name` left null (byte-faithful, same as batch 1's dead `arm-fnames`). Two shared build accessors in abi.nuc: `make-param-vec (ptypes np):(ref (Vector (ref Field)))` and `type-set-params (t ptypes np)` (the single array→Vector conversion point, mirroring `struct-set-fields`); reads **reuse `field-at`** (Type's `params` is the same `(raw (Vector (ref Field)))` element type as StructDef's `fields`). `boxedfn-sig-token` retyped to walk the Vector (both callers now pass a Vector; `boxedfn-type` builds the param Vector once via `make-param-vec` and reuses the handle for both the token and the Type). One wholesale-handle site (`repl.nuc` redefinition path) materializes the fn Type's param Vector back into a temp raw `Type*[]` before `generic-remove-matching-user-method` (which compares against the untouched raw `Method.param-types`). Reconverged in **ONE pass** (`make bootstrap` PASSED before `update-bootstrap` — internal representation only, invisible in emitted IR). `make test` 168/168; `make abi-test` (`abi-interop`) green; territory-specific example checks (boxedfn/closures/dyn/fnptr/optional/rest) diff-exact against `tests/expected/*.out`; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed all boot IRs; round-2 byte-identical. Confirmed no remaining lockstep parallel-array idiom on `compiler-types.nuc` (the leftover raw `ptr[]` fields — `Constraint.args`, `Arm.fnames`/`ftypes`, `Type.opt-defaults`, `StructDef.origin-args`, tyvar/binding name arrays — are all *single* ragged arrays with no partner, deliberately left raw). **14.5 batch 1 (`g-include-paths`/`g-link-args` fixed-64 `malloc` → `(Vector CStr)`) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass — the coldest, simplest 14.5 sub-step (per the design doc's cold-first ordering).** Retired the two fixed-64-slot `malloc` buffers (and their `g-num-include-paths`/`g-num-link-args` counters, deleted outright) for `(ref (Vector CStr))`, matching the `g-blanket`/`g-macro-decls` idiom exactly: lazy `(cast (ref (Vector CStr)) (vector-new-in (addr-of g-arena-alloc)))` on first use. One ordering wrinkle distinct from every other 14.x global-init site: `add-include-path`/`add-link-arg` fire from argv parsing in `main`, which runs *before* `compiler-init` (i.e. before `arena-allocator` flips `g-arena-alloc.kind` to `ALLOC-ARENA`) — harmless, since an `AllocHandle` defvar zero-fills to `ALLOC-LIBC` and `vector-init-alloc` copies the handle *by value* into the Vector at construction, so these two vectors simply stay libc-backed (malloc/realloc) for the process lifetime regardless of when `compiler-init` later runs. `conj`'s `elem:T` slot needed an explicit `(cast CStr path)`/`(cast CStr arg)` at the call site (a bare `ptr` argument does not auto-adapt into a generic `CStr` parameter, per the existing `macro-jit-ensure-decl`/`intern-str` precedent) — the two `add-*` functions' own `path`/`arg` params stayed `ptr` unchanged (no null-check/identity risk found on them). The old fixed-64 buffers had **no capacity check at all** (a 65th `-I`/`-l` silently wrote out of bounds), so this closes a real correctness bug, not just a refactor. Read sites (`try-import-path`'s `-I` search-path loop, the clang link-line assembly in the batch driver) gated on `(!= g-include-paths/g-link-args null)` before `count`/`invoke`, since either vector may never be constructed if no `-I`/`-l` was ever passed. Verified beyond `make test`: `-I<path>` (both the attached `-I/dir` and space-separated `-I /dir` forms) resolves a `.nuch` import from a nonstandard directory end-to-end (compile + link + run), and `-lm`/`-L` thread through to the final `clang` link command, including multiple `-I`/`-l` flags in a single invocation. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point reconverged in **one pass** (again invisible-in-IR internal storage change, no `update-bootstrap` needed first); `make update-bootstrap` refreshed all boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical. **14.5 batch 2 (`g-vtable-keys`/`-names` + `g-ns-prefix-keys`/`-vals` → element-struct `Vector`s) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** Added two new `compiler-types.nuc` structs — `VtableEntry{key:ptr, name:ptr}` (the Stage-13 type-erasure vtable dedup memo, keyed per `(concrete-type, protocol)` or per-signature bare-fn forwarder) and `NsPrefixEntry{ns:ptr, prefix:ptr}` (the Stage-12 N4 per-namespace IR-mangling-prefix table) — and retired both hand-rolled cap-doubling pairs (`g-vtable-keys`/`g-vtable-names`/`g-vtable-count`/`g-vtable-cap` and `g-ns-prefix-keys`/`g-ns-prefix-vals`/`g-num-ns-prefix`/`g-cap-ns-prefix`) for one Vector global apiece: `g-vtable-table:(ref (Vector (ref VtableEntry)))` and `g-ns-prefix-table:(raw (Vector (ref NsPrefixEntry)))`. Both element structs' fields stay `ptr`, not `CStr` — `vtable-memo-lookup`/`ns-ir-prefix-set`/`-get` compare keys by pointer identity, never `strcmp`, since every key/value is already `intern-str`'d (same class of finding as 14.3/14.4's identity-key exceptions). The `ref` vs `raw` split between the two new globals is deliberate, not arbitrary: `g-ns-prefix-table` is `raw` because `compiler-init` explicitly resets it to null once per compilation unit (a null literal cannot be assigned to a `ref`-typed variable under the non-null-pointer flow checker), while `g-vtable-table` is never explicitly reset, so it stays `ref`; both read/write sites do a one-time raw→ref cast guarded by a null check where needed (the `abi.nuc` `field-at`-helper idiom). `vtable-memo-lookup`/`vtable-memo-put`/`ns-ir-prefix-set`/`ns-ir-prefix-get` rewritten from manual arena-alloc+memcpy+`aset!`/`aref` cap-doubling to `count`/`invoke`/`conj` over the Vector; `compiler-init`'s four-line reset (`g-ns-prefix-keys`/`-vals`/`g-num-ns-prefix`/`g-cap-ns-prefix` → null/0/0) collapsed to one `(set! g-ns-prefix-table null)`. Bonus correction found in-flight: a stale inline comment above the vtable memo claiming "INERTNESS: nothing calls ensure-vtable-for yet ... byte-identical" no longer matched the tree (TE-3's closure-boxing path already calls it twice, at the `Fn`-env and struct-protocol box sites) — deleted rather than left to mislead the next reader. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point reconverged in **one pass** (internal storage change only, invisible in emitted IR); `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs; 6+ namespace/vtable/dispatch examples diff-exact against `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.5 batch 3 (`g-boxedfn-keys`/`-types`/`-count`/`-cap` + `g-dyn-keys`/`-types`/`-protos`/`-count`/`-cap` → element-struct `Vector`s) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** Added two new `compiler-types.nuc` structs — `BoxedFnEntry{key:ptr, ty:(raw Type)}` (the Stage-13 `BoxedFn`-instance dedup memo, keyed per signature) and `DynEntry{key:ptr, ty:(raw Type), proto:ptr}` (the `(dyn P)`-instance memo, additionally carrying the protocol name — confirming the design note's guess that this is a 3-key memo, not 2) — and retired both hand-rolled cap-doubling families (`g-boxedfn-keys`/`-types`/`-count`/`-cap` and `g-dyn-keys`/`-types`/`-protos`/`-count`/`-cap`, both union-registry.nuc) for one Vector global apiece: `g-boxedfn-table:(ref (Vector (ref BoxedFnEntry)))` and `g-dyn-table:(ref (Vector (ref DynEntry)))` — both `ref` (never explicitly reset in `compiler-init`, same as `g-vtable-table`; unlike `g-ns-prefix-table`). Identity-vs-content audit: all fields stay `ptr`/`(raw Type)`, never `CStr` — `key` is looked up by pointer identity (`boxedfn-memo-lookup`/`dyn-memo-lookup`, values already `intern-str`'d at the call site) and `ty` is looked up by `sdef` pointer identity (`boxedfn-canonical`/`dyn-canonical`/`dyn-proto-of`); this exact finding had already been flagged (not yet acted on) by 14.3 batch 2's memo-lookup audit, so this batch confirmed rather than discovered it. The union-registry import-ordering caveat the design doc raised turned out to be a **non-issue**, same shape as batch 1's arena-ordering finding: union-registry.nuc is imported from nucleusc.nuc *after* both `(import-use vector)` and `(import-use arena)` have already run (vector.nuc pulls in `allocator` itself), so `Vector`/`AllocHandle` are fully registered by the time these two `defvar`s are parsed — no different from where `g-vtable-table` sits in nucleusc.nuc. More fundamentally, `pending-union-deps-ready`/`drain-pending-union-irs` (union-registry.nuc:158-185) never engages for these two element structs at all: `BoxedFnEntry`/`DynEntry` have no by-value `TY-STRUCT`/`TY-UNION` fields (only `ptr`/`(raw Type)`, both pointer-kind, lowering to opaque `ptr` per that function's own doc comment), so the stamped `(Vector (ref BoxedFnEntry))`/`(Vector (ref DynEntry))` instances never have a dependency to defer — same as every other 14.5 batch's new Vector instances. `boxedfn-memo-lookup`/`-put`/`boxedfn-canonical`/`dyn-memo-lookup`/`-put`/`dyn-canonical`/`dyn-proto-of` rewritten from manual arena-alloc+memcpy+`aset!`/`aref` cap-doubling to `count`/`invoke`/`conj`/direct field access over the Vector. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point reconverged in **one pass** (no 2-stage manual workaround needed, contrary to the batch's own risk flag); `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical; `boxedfn`/`dyn-comb`/`dyn-protocol`/`comb-storage` examples diff-exact against `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.5 batch 4 (`Generic.methods`/`num-methods`/`cap` → `(ref (Vector (ref Method)))`) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** Retired the hand-rolled `methods:ptr`/`num-methods:i32`/`cap:i32` triple (compiler-types.nuc) and its manual cap-doubling grow thunk (`generic-add-method`, generics.nuc) for a single `(methods (ref (Vector (ref Method))))` field — `ref`, not `raw`, matching `g-vtable-table`/`g-boxedfn-table`/`g-dyn-table` (each `Generic` is individually arena-allocated once by `generic-new` and never explicitly reset to null, unlike `g-ns-prefix-table`). `generic-new` builds the Vector via `(cast (ref (Vector (ref Method))) (vector-new-in (addr-of g-arena-alloc)))`; `generic-add-method` collapses to one `(conj (g methods) (cast ref:Method m))`. `generic-remove-matching-user-method` (the REPL redefinition path, which used to hand-shift the tail of the array left by one slot) needed a genuinely new primitive — **`Vector` had no delete operation** (only `insert`, its mirror-image) — so added `remove-at ((self (ref (Vector T))) i:usize):void` to `lib/vector.nuc`, shifting `(i,len)` left by one and shrinking `len`, styled identically to `insert`; the REPL call site collapsed to `(remove-at (gg methods) (cast usize i))`. ~30 read sites across generics.nuc and nucleusc.nuc (`generic-find-method-exact`, `generic-binds-for`, `finalize-generics`, `generic-resolve`, `operator-user-resolve`, `check-generic-templates`, `abstract-call-via-generic`, `defn-ir-name`/`defn-form-mangled-name`, `generic-resolve-nullable`, `generic-has-receiver-method`, `derive-closure-conformance`, `builtin-op-result-type`, `node-type-call`, `emit-dispatch`) converted from `(dotimes (i (gg num-methods)) ... (aref (cast ptr:ptr (gg methods)) i))` to `(dotimes (i (cast i32 (count (gg methods)))) ... (invoke (gg methods) (cast usize i)))`, keeping every downstream local's declared type (`ptr:Method`/`(raw Method)`) unchanged — only the initializer expression changed, per the "retype the cast, keep the local" rule. **A real bug surfaced during verification, not code review**: a mechanical grep-and-replace across the two files caught every site written as `(gg methods)`/`(g methods)`/`(igg methods)`, but missed the one site written through the `->` threading macro — `gcheck`'s intrinsic-operator peephole at generics.nuc (`(-> g (cast ptr:Generic _) (_ methods))`, expanding to the equivalent of `(g methods)` but textually invisible to a literal-string search). Left un-migrated, this line still `aref`'d the new Vector-struct pointer as if it were a raw `Method*[]`, reading garbage and segfaulting deep inside `check-generic-templates` — but only for programs that exercise the A2 def-time checker on a **bounded generic with `&where` constraints** (e.g. `reduce` in `lib/iterator.nuc`), so `make` (the compiler's own self-compile) and simple Vector-only programs built fine while `(import-use vector)` alone (which transitively pulls in `iterator.nuc`) crashed every target compile. No `gdb`/`valgrind` in the container; diagnosed by rebuilding with `clang -fsanitize=address` (compiler-rt's static archives were missing for this clang install, so the working recipe was `clang -c -fsanitize=address build/nucleusc.ll` + `clang -c -fsanitize=address src/repl_shim.c` linked with **`gcc`**, which supplies `libasan` — ASan's stack trace pointed straight at the stray `aref` in `gcheck`). Lesson for future `.methods`/`.field`-style grep-based migrations: also grep for `(-> ` and `_ methods)`/`_ field)` head-position-substitution uses of the field name, not just the literal accessor form. **`make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point reconverged in one pass; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical**; `assoc-*`/`coll-iter-test`/`comb-*`/`dyn-comb`/`dyn-protocol`/`generic`/`hashmap-*`/`hashset-*`/`iterator-test`/`protocol`/`string-protocols-test`/`vector-*` examples (25 total) diff-exact against `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.5 batch 5 (`Scope.cleanup-slots`/`ncleanups`/`ccleanups` → `(ref (Vector (ref Cleanup)))`) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in one pass.** Retired the hand-rolled `cleanup-slots:ptr`/`ncleanups:i32`/`ccleanups:i32` triple (compiler-types.nuc) and its manual cap-doubling grow thunk (`scope-push-cleanup`, scope.nuc) for a single `(cleanup-slots (ref (Vector (ref Cleanup))))` field — `ref`, not `raw`, matching `Generic.methods`/`g-vtable-table`/`g-boxedfn-table`/`g-dyn-table` (each `Scope` is individually arena-allocated once by `scope-new` and never explicitly reset to null, unlike `g-ns-prefix-table`). `scope-new` builds the Vector via `(cast (ref (Vector (ref Cleanup))) (vector-new-in (addr-of g-arena-alloc)))`; `scope-push-cleanup` collapsed to one `(conj (sc cleanup-slots) (cast ref:Cleanup slot))`, deleting the whole cap-doubling/`memcpy` grow block. The sole read site, `emit-scope-cleanups` (nucleusc.nuc) — which walks armed cleanups in *reverse* registration order at scope exit/early-return to fire `defer`/Drop/legacy-free actions — converted from `(aref (cast ptr:ptr (sc cleanup-slots)) i)` over an `ncleanups`-1-down-to-0 count to `(invoke (sc cleanup-slots) (cast usize i))` over `(cast i32 (count (sc cleanup-slots)))`-1-down-to-0, keeping the reverse-order loop shape and the `cl:ref:Cleanup` local's declared type unchanged (only the initializer expression changed, per "retype the cast, keep the local"). Mindful of batch 4's `->`-threading miss, grepped the whole tree not just for the three field names but separately for `(-> ` thread-macro forms and bare `Cleanup` struct usage across `src/`/`lib/`/`.nuch` — confirmed all three fields are read/written in exactly three files (`compiler-types.nuc`, `scope.nuc`, `nucleusc.nuc`), no thread-macro or aliased-variable indirection hiding a fourth site this time. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` fixed point reconverged in **one pass**; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical; `drop-defer`/`with-lifecycle`/`with-autofree`/`ce3-owning-closure` examples (defer/with/Drop-exercising) diff-exact against `tests/expected/*.out`. Remaining Stage 14: 14.5 sub-step 6 (`Scope.syms`) **explicitly DEFERRED per OQ2 (2026-07-14) — no profiling evidence shows `scope-lookup` is a bottleneck, and 14.5's own "Done when" criterion allows deferral; phase 14.5 is now DONE**. **14.6 pilot (`Cleanup` struct → `defunion`) DONE (2026-07-14), controlled refresh reconverged in ONE pass — the first codegen-adjacent 14.6 change, and it did NOT need the drift-then-reconverge cycle the plan anticipated.** The `defstruct Cleanup` (six `ptr`/`ref` fields, doc-comment "exactly one shape is set") became `(defunion Cleanup (libc-free (slot ptr)) (drop (slot ptr) (dropfn ptr) (dropret (raw Type))) (defer (node (raw Node)) (scope (ref Scope))))`. Dead-field audit: the legacy `drop-ty` field was **write-only** (set at construction, never read) — dropped, same as `param-names` in 14.4 batch 3b. Sites: the three construction sites (`emit-defer` + the autofree and Drop `with` arms in nucleusc.nuc) rewritten to arm ctors via `(ptr-set! (new Cleanup) (Cleanup-<arm> …))` — `ptr-set!` stores the union value through the `(ref Cleanup)` arena slot with **no struct-by-value ABI boundary** (a by-value `conj` would be the compiler's first `byval`/`sret` use, deliberately avoided); the null-probing `cond` in `emit-scope-cleanups` → an exhaustive `match` (no `_`, so a fourth shape breaks it loudly); `emit-drop-cleanup` re-signatured to take the three payload fields directly instead of `cl:(raw Cleanup)`, deleting the receiver cast and the `(cast ptr:Type (c drop-ret))` reads (`dropret:(raw Type)` reads `(dropret kind)` directly). `Scope.cleanup-slots` stays `(ref (Vector (ref Cleanup)))` and `scope-push-cleanup` stays `(… slot:ptr)` — the Vector still holds heap `(ref Cleanup)` pointers, arena-stable across the many compiler frames a Scope outlives. **One-pass byte-identical because no codegen *lowering* changed** — only which constructs the compiler's own source *uses* (struct+`cond` → `defunion`+`match`); old boot and new compiler lower `defunion`/`match`/`ptr-set!` identically, so `old_boot(new_src) == new_compiler(new_src)`. The Cleanup↔Scope mutual reference resolves because `prescan-struct-names` registers every type name before the defunion's ctors emit. **JIT-reachability confirmed emit-path-only in the sense that matters**: `emit-scope-cleanups` *is* reached when the compiler JIT-compiles a `defmacro` body using `with`/`defer` (via the `emit-defn` epilogue), but it emits only `load ptr`/`call @free`/`br` — never a `%Cleanup` type. `Cleanup` is a compiler-runtime type that never appears in any emitted user/JIT module (lives only in the compiler's own batch module, where `%Cleanup` is always defined), so the `(Maybe <struct>)` JIT gotcha structurally cannot apply — verified with a `defmacro` mallocing+autofreeing inside `with` (JITs+runs clean) plus a standalone program mixing all three shapes on one scope (fires `defer` → `drop` → `free` in reverse registration order on both fall-through and early-return). `make test` 168/168; `make abi-test` green; `make bootstrap` fixed point; `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs; round-2 `make clean && make && make bootstrap` byte-identical; `drop-defer`/`with-autofree`/`with-lifecycle`/`ce3-owning-closure` examples diff-exact. **Pilot verdict: the defunion-migration pattern validates cleanly** — the remaining 14.6 `case`-over-enum sweeps (`MethodKind`, `UnionDef.layout`, `AbiInfo.kind`, `Type.kind`/`NodeKind`) change no representation (the enum stays), so they are pure `cond`→`case` exhaustiveness sugar and are expected byte-identical without any refresh; carry forward the `(ptr-set! (new T) (T-arm …))` idiom for storing a fresh union value into an arena slot without introducing struct-by-value ABI. **14.6 `MethodKind` case-over-enum sweep DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** Correction to the plan's premise: this codebase's `case` (`lib/macros.nuc`) is a **macro over `cond`**, not a compiler-checked exhaustive match — `(case form v1 r1 v2 r2 … default)` expands to `(cond (= form v1) r1 (= form v2) r2 … true default)` with a required trailing unpaired default, so the "exhaustiveness" this sweep buys is documentation-level, not a new compiler diagnostic. Audited all `MethodKind`-comparison sites in `src/generics.nuc` (39 raw grep hits on the three `METHOD-*` constants; 14 comments, 25 actual code sites) and found exactly **one** genuine multi-arm ladder — the method-kind tally in `finalize-generics` (a 3-branch `cond` keyed on `(mk kind)`) — converted to `(case (mk kind) METHOD-GENERIC (set! has-generic 1) METHOD-INTRINSIC (set! has-intrinsic 1) (inc! n-user))`. The other 24 code sites were either `.set!` constructions (4) or single-kind `when`/`and` guards with no sibling arm on another kind in the same construct (20, including `valid-resolve-type`'s three *sequential* single-guard tiers in separate loops, which read like a ladder in prose but are not one `cond`/`if`-chain) — correctly left as-is. Since `case` expands via quasiquote to the exact same `cond` AST the hand-written code had, the change is expansion-identical; verified anyway: `make clean && make` against the committed boot compiler, `make test` 168/168, and a stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (same boot compiler both sides) was **zero lines** — no `make update-bootstrap` needed. The assoc-*/comb-*/dyn-*/generic/protocol example family (16 programs) all matched `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.6 `UnionDef.layout` case-over-enum sweep DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** Audited all `LAYOUT-*`-comparison sites in `src/union-emit.nuc` + `src/union-registry.nuc` (53 raw grep hits on the field name `layout` plus the four `LAYOUT-*` constants; 20 comments, 33 actual code sites) and found exactly **two** genuine multi-arm ladders, both converted: `union-instance-type` (union-registry.nuc) — a `lay:i32 (udd layout)` read followed by two sequential `when`/`return` guards on `LAYOUT-NICHE-MAYBE`/`LAYOUT-NICHE-ERRPTR` plus an unconditional final return — became one `(case lay LAYOUT-NICHE-MAYBE (let … (return pt)) LAYOUT-NICHE-ERRPTR (let … (return pt)) (return (type-for-sdef (udd sdef))))`; and `emit-match` (union-emit.nuc) — the same two-guard shape keyed on `(niche-layout-of (sv type))` re-evaluated per comparison (matching `case`'s own doc-comment contract that `form` is re-evaluated and should be pure) — became `(case (niche-layout-of (sv type)) LAYOUT-NICHE-ERRPTR (return (emit-match-niche-errptr …)) LAYOUT-NICHE-MAYBE (return (emit-match-niche-maybe …)) 0)`, with the trailing `0` a discarded pass-through default (already the exact idiom `fprint-node`'s pre-existing `case` uses at nucleusc.nuc:569) so the subsequent unrelated `is-int-type`/structural-match code — which this ladder does not touch, since niche-layout-of never returns `LAYOUT-ENUM` and the ENUM/TAGGED split there is a different discriminant entirely — still runs unconditionally after. The other 31 code sites were correctly left alone: two whole-function **producer/classifier** bodies (`niche-layout-of`, `union-layout-classify`) that *construct* a `LAYOUT-*` value from unrelated predicates (null/kind/pkind checks, arm-shape rules) rather than dispatching on an already-known one — not expressible as one `case` over a repeated `form` at all; five **single-kind guards** (`emit-unwrap`/`emit-unwrap-or`'s ERRPTR checks, `emit-niche-ctor`/`emit-make`'s TAGGED checks, `union-target-rewrite`'s ERRPTR check) each naming only one of the four constants with an unrelated fallthrough, not a sibling arm; one **single-named-kind `if`/`else`** (`emit-niche-construct`, which names only `LAYOUT-NICHE-MAYBE` and treats the else branch as the implicit ERRPTR case) left as a plain `if` rather than forced into `case`; and two **combined set-membership tests** (`is-niche` in `defunion-register` and its negation guarding the backing-struct IR-type emission) that `or`/`and` two named constants together but map both to the *same* action, so `case` (whose whole point is different actions per arm) would add ceremony, not clarity — plus assignment/field-write sites (`layout:i32 (union-layout-classify …)`, `.set! ud layout layout`) that are not comparisons at all. `niche-elem` reads were untouched everywhere (a separate field access alongside the `layout`/`lay` comparisons, never folded into a payload). Verified: `make clean && make` against the committed boot compiler, `make test` 168/168, and a stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (same boot compiler both sides) confirmed the **entire** 1228-line diff sits inside the two edited functions' own bodies (`union-instance-type`'s label renumbering from the two-guard→one-`cond` merge, `emit-match`'s local-alloca suffix renumbering) with zero `@.str` pool drift and zero spillover into any other function in the 113k-line module — no `make update-bootstrap` needed. `unions.nuc` (LAYOUT-TAGGED via `Shape`/`(Result i64 i32)`), `errptr.nuc` (LAYOUT-NICHE-ERRPTR via `!ptr:Pt`), `maybe.nuc`/`optional.nuc`/`value-maybe.nuc` (LAYOUT-NICHE-MAYBE via `?ptr:Pt`), and a one-off all-payload-less `defunion` scratch program (LAYOUT-ENUM) all diff-exact/behave correctly, exercising every one of the four layout kinds through both edited functions. This closes the `UnionDef.layout` sweep item; `AbiInfo.kind`/`Type.kind`/`NodeKind` remain for future 14.6 batches. **14.6 `Type.kind` sweep, batch 1 (`src/type-utils.nuc` + `src/type-mangle.nuc`) DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** Unlike the MethodKind/UnionDef.layout files, these two files are almost entirely built from `(when (= (tt kind) TY-X) (return R))` chains, so nearly every candidate was a genuine ladder: **8 converted in `type-utils.nuc`** (`type-to-ir` 21 arms, `type-to-c` 21 arms, `type-size` 19 arms, `int-width` 13 arms, plus `is-int-type` 11 arms/`is-unsigned` 6 arms/`is-float-type` and `is-ptr-like` 2 arms each — the latter four all return the *same* result across every matched arm with a differing default, but unlike the UnionDef.layout sweep's excluded `is-niche` (one combined `or`/`and` of two constants in a single incidental guard expression), these are a genuine sequential multi-arm chain over the same form with a sibling arm per named kind in the same construct, matching the established "ladder" criterion rather than the narrower "combined same-action test" exclusion) — leaving alone exactly 3 genuine single-kind guards with no sibling arm (`type-is-strview`'s `TY-STRUCT` check, `ptr-pkind`'s `TY-PTR` check, `pkind-flow-check`'s `TY-PTR` check); `pkind-meet`/`type-as-pkind` dispatch on `PTR-*` pkind values, not `TypeKind`, so out of scope. **2 converted in `type-mangle.nuc`** (`type-mangle-token`, `type-spelling`), both full-width with no incidental guards found — several arms keep non-trivial bodies (`TY-PTR`'s nested `if`, `TY-STRUCT`/`TY-UNION`'s `->` accessor chain, `TY-FN`'s `fnty-intern` call) as direct transcriptions into the `case` arm position. No arity/awkwardness limit hit even at 21 arms; comments interleave between clauses exactly as in the `nuch.nuc`/`fprint-node` precedent. Verified: `make clean && make` against the committed boot compiler; `make test` 168/168; **`make bootstrap` PASSED directly** (`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`, no `update-bootstrap` needed); a pre-batch stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (same boot compiler both sides) was non-empty (294 hunks) but confirmed — by mapping every hunk's line number against the pre-batch IR's function boundaries — to sit **entirely inside the 10 edited functions** (43+43+39+27+5+5+27+13+46+46 = 294 hunks, zero spillover, zero `@.str` drift), the same branch-label-renumbering shape the UnionDef.layout sweep saw. Given this touches `type-to-ir`/`type-size`/`type-mangle-token`/`type-spelling` (used by every function signature, generic instantiation, and symbol name in the compiler), the broad example sweep (`hello`/`closures`/`generic`/`protocol`/`dyn-comb`/`struct`/`vector-test`/`hashmap-test`/`optional`/`boxedfn`) all diff-exact matched `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.6 `generics.nuc` `type-eq` ladder sweep DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** Read the live function (drifted slightly from the design doc's original line-number estimate, but not in shape): two identity/null-guard early exits, two Stage 13 L7 `TY-FN`↔`(ref TY-PTR)` unification special cases (compare *both* operands' kind+pkind together in an asymmetric way, not a single-value dispatch — confirmed not part of the ladder, left untouched), a `(!= (aa kind) (bb kind))` precondition (also a guard, not a ladder member — left untouched), then the genuine tail ladder: four sequential `(when (= (aa kind) TY-X) ...)` arms (`TY-STRUCT`/`TY-UNION`/`TY-PTR`/`TY-TYVAR`) each returning a computed 0/1, falling through to `(return 1)`. Converted the tail to `(case (aa kind) TY-STRUCT (if (= (aa sdef) (bb sdef)) (return 1) (return 0)) TY-UNION (if (= (aa sdef) (bb sdef)) (return 1) (return 0)) TY-PTR (return (type-eq (aa elem) (bb elem))) TY-TYVAR (if (and (= (aa sdef) (bb sdef)) (= (aa num-params) (bb num-params))) (return 1) (return 0)) (return 1))` — a direct transcription; the two special-case early returns and the kind-mismatch precondition are byte-for-byte unchanged. Verified: `make clean && make` against the committed boot compiler; `make test` 168/168; **`make bootstrap` PASSED directly** (`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`, no `update-bootstrap` needed); a pre-batch stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (same boot compiler both sides) was 105 lines, confirmed — by mapping against the pre-batch IR's function boundaries (`define i32 @type-eq` at line 27652, next `define` at 27892) — to sit **entirely inside `type-eq`'s own body**, zero spillover into `type-join` or any other function, zero `@.str` pool drift. Given `type-eq` is one of the hottest, most safety-critical functions in the compiler (called anywhere two types are compared), ran a wide example sweep beyond the standard set: `generic`/`protocol`/`dyn-comb`/`dyn-protocol`/`struct`/`unions`/`errptr`/`maybe`/`optional`/`value-maybe`/`vector-test`/`hashmap-test`/`boxedfn`/`closures`/`assoc-types`/`assoc-types-extend-cross` (16 programs) all diff-exact matched `tests/expected/*.out`. **14.6 `NodeKind` sweep, batch 1 (`src/union-registry.nuc` + `src/union-emit.nuc`) DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** `Node.kind:i32` compared against the seven `NODE-*` constants (`NODE-INT`/`NODE-STR`/`NODE-SYM`/`NODE-CELL`/`NODE-FLOAT`/`NODE-KEYWORD`/`NODE-CHAR` — note `NODE-STR`/`NODE-CELL`, not `NODE-STRING`/`NODE-LIST`); this NodeKind-tag mechanism is distinct from head-symbol-identity dispatch (`(= head 'ptr)` etc., pervasive in both files' type-position bodies), which is a different, explicitly out-of-scope mechanism. Audited all 50 raw hits (18 + 32) and found exactly **one** genuine ladder: `parse-type-from-node` (union-registry.nuc) — sequential `NODE-SYM`/`NODE-CELL` guards falling through to an unconditional `die-at` default — converted to `(case (n kind) NODE-SYM (do …) NODE-CELL (do …) (die-at line "unable to parse type expression"))`, moving the ~300-line `NODE-CELL` body (including its internal head-symbol dispatch, left untouched) verbatim into a `do`. Everything else was incidental (single-kind guards, single-named-kind `if`/`else`, combined single-kind+content guards) and correctly left alone. `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` PASSED on the first pass (no `update-bootstrap`); the union/match example family (16 programs) diff-exact. **14.6 `NodeKind` sweep batch 3 (`src/generics.nuc`) DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed — completes the file-by-file `NodeKind` sweep of `generics.nuc`.** Highest-risk file short of `nucleusc.nuc` itself, since it's the `node-type`/`gcheck`/`valid-walk` half of the `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep. Audited all 97 raw hits; found **16 genuine ladders**, two families: (a) the literal-kind ladder (`NODE-INT`/`NODE-FLOAT`/`NODE-STR`/`NODE-CHAR`/`NODE-SYM`[/`NODE-KEYWORD`]) present almost verbatim in `node-type`, `gcheck`, and `valid-walk`, each converted to `(case (nn kind) ... 0)` followed by the **untouched** `(when (!= (nn kind) NODE-CELL) (return ty-void))` fallback and the rest of the function exactly as before (the batch-2 `type-node-to-c` discarded-`0`-default idiom, chosen so the huge CELL-dispatch body needed zero internal reflow); (b) 13 small `NODE-SYM`/`NODE-CELL`(/default) ladders in the type-pattern/AST-shape helpers — `defn-name-only`, `node-mentions-tyvar`, `node-mentions-tyvar-named`, `pattern-determines-tyvar`, `gbind-decl-type`, `gbind-name`, `valid-decl-type`, `subst-self-node` (a near-twin of the already-converted `type-mangle.nuc` `subst-tyvars-node`), `extend-proto-name`, `unify-tpat` (both arms needed an explicit `(do ...)` wrap), plus `emit-extend`/`register-imported-conformance` (the batch-1/2 "positive-arm-plus-negated-guard" shape). Every conversion was verified paren-neutral **before** editing with a purpose-built global-stack pairing script (reports which physical open-paren each close matches) — caught, ahead of time, that `collect-pattern-tyvars`' and `unify-tpat`'s indentation was misleading about true nesting (a `(let (st ...) FORM1 FORM2)` whose second form is a sibling, not nested, inside the first). Left alone, matching every established precedent category: single-kind guards (majority), combined single-kind+content guards, single-named-kind `if`/`else`, two-different-variables guards (`node-template-of`/`node-is-ptr-wrapper`, matching the batch-2 `cheader-template-instance` exclusion), combined OR-same-action (`sig-provides-call`), construction sites (`conf-arg-to-type`), and all head-symbol-identity dispatch (mechanism b — `node-type`'s ~40-arm `hp`/`'let`/`'with`/... tail, `gcheck`'s special-form dispatch, `emit-extend`'s `'ref`/`'raw`/`'ptr` checks — untouched, out of scope). No converted function's return value changed for any input (pure `cond`-chain→`case`-arm relocation, arm bodies moved verbatim); `emit-node`/`emit-list` (nucleusc.nuc) untouched, so the lockstep is unaffected. `make clean && make` succeeded against the committed boot compiler; `make test` 168/168; **`make bootstrap` PASSED on the first pass** (no `update-bootstrap`); a pre-batch stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (same boot compiler both sides) was 4847 lines / 318 hunks, confirmed by mapping every hunk to its enclosing `define` to sit **entirely inside the 16 edited functions** (hunk counts: `node-type` 103, `gcheck` 40, `valid-walk` 32, `emit-extend` 26, `unify-tpat` 23, `collect-pattern-tyvars` 16, `pattern-determines-tyvar` 13, `register-imported-conformance` 13, `gbind-decl-type` 10, `extend-proto-name` 10, `defn-name-only` 6, `node-mentions-tyvar` 6, `node-mentions-tyvar-named` 6, `valid-decl-type` 6, `gbind-name` 4, `subst-self-node` 4 — summing to exactly 318), zero spillover, zero `@.str` pool drift. Given this touches `node-type` itself plus its `gcheck`/`valid-walk` mirrors, ran the widest example sweep of the 14.6 series: `hello`/`closures`/`generic`/`protocol`/`dyn-comb`/`dyn-protocol`/`struct`/`unions`/`errptr`/`maybe`/`optional`/`vector-test`/`hashmap-test`/`boxedfn`/`assoc-types`/`assoc-types-extend-cross`/`and-narrow` (17 programs) all diff-exact matched `tests/expected/*.out`. **Recommendation: close out the tree-wide `NodeKind` sweep here** rather than attempt `src/nucleusc.nuc` (222 raw hits, containing `emit-node`/`emit-list` — the codegen half of the lockstep, where an error changes emitted IR rather than a checker's return value) as a mechanical batch sweep; if ever wanted, treat `emit-node`'s own literal-kind ladder as a single-function, slow, manually-verified change (the `type-eq`-sweep template), not a full-file sweep.

**14.6 `NodeKind` sweep, batch 2 (`src/cheader.nuc` + `src/repl.nuc` + `src/nuch.nuc` + `src/type-mangle.nuc`) DONE (2026-07-14), byte-identical, no refresh needed.** Audited all 59 raw hits (26 + 16 + 11 + 6). Found **6 genuine ladders** (a higher hit rate than batch 1's 1/50, for a structural reason — these files are AST-shaped type/form interpreters that dispatch on `NodeKind` more than union-registry/union-emit's match-arm code did): `type-node-to-c`/`extract-type-node`/`cheader-mentions-closure`/`emit-cheader-defconst` (cheader.nuc), `emit-defunion-import` (nuch.nuc), `subst-tyvars-node` (type-mangle.nuc, resolving the "different concern" leftover the `Type.kind` batch-1 status flagged). `type-node-to-c` needed care to avoid a spurious new string-literal occurrence (which would have triggered a large `@.str` pool-renumbering diff per the LW-3 precedent) — fixed by keeping the shared trailing default **outside** the `case` (a discarded-`0` default arm plus the original single physical `(return "void*")` after it), rather than duplicating the literal inside the `NODE-CELL` arm. `cheader-mentions-closure` required judgment to distinguish it from the textually-similar, already-excluded `union-target-rewrite` "narrowing bail-out guard" precedent (the deciding factor: whether the preceding same-form kind-check is a *complete*, always-returning arm — genuine ladder — or a narrow content-filtered special case that most values fall through — incidental guard). **`src/repl.nuc` had zero genuine ladders** — all 16 raw hits are single-kind guards, content-narrowed special cases (e.g. the `'quote`-peeling checks), or `NODE-*` constant registrations, a valid, useful finding on its own. Verified: `make test` 168/168; `make bootstrap` PASSED on the first pass; a pre-batch stash/rebuild-baseline/restore/rebuild `build/nucleusc.ll` diff (759 lines) confirmed entirely inside the six edited functions plus one harmless same-count `@.str` reorder swap (zero net pool growth). Beyond the existing `l8-cheader-*`/`l13-cheader-*`/`sm3-*`/`n6-*`/`s1-*` cheader/nuch suite tests (all passed unchanged), hand-verified the two functions with no direct test coverage for their converted shape: a manual `--emit-nuch`/import round-trip exercising both of `emit-defunion-import`'s arms (a plain `defunion` and a parametric template) end-to-end through compile+link+run, and a manual `--emit-cheader` run exercising `emit-cheader-defconst`'s both arms plus `type-node-to-c`'s `ptr`/`union`-wrapper branches — both matched expected output exactly.

Remaining for follow-up 14.6 batches: `AbiInfo.kind` (explicitly SKIPPED, optional), `abi.nuc`'s `Type.kind` slice (leave-alone IR zone), and `NodeKind` compares in `src/generics.nuc` (98 raw hits) and `src/nucleusc.nuc` (222 raw hits — by far the largest and highest-risk, given heavy head-symbol-dispatch mixing) — both untouched, likely warranting a separate decision on whether to attempt them at all. Next: the remaining 14.6 `case`-over-enum sweeps → 14.7 (record-only). Type-safety complement to Stage 13 R2/R3 (loops/substrate); keeps the zero-cost hot-path invariant. | [stage14/type-safety.md](stage14/type-safety.md) |
| 14 (macro casts) | Macro conditionals without casts — shared `type-join` absorbing elem-less `ptr` into the typed side at pkind `raw` (MC-1), quasiquote/`gensym` results retyped `(raw Node)` (MC-2), cast deletion in `lib/macros.nuc` + castless test (MC-3), docs sweep (MC-4) | **Done — MC-1 + MC-2 + MC-3 + MC-4** — `type-join` in `src/generics.nuc` absorbs bare↔typed joins (MC-1, byte-identical); `ty-raw-node` accessor + retyped `emit-quote`/`emit-qq-list`/`emit-qq-form`/`gensym` + `node-type-call` lockstep (MC-2, required bootstrap reconverge); `lib/macros.nuc` MC-3 cleanup deletes all 38 vestigial `(cast ptr:Node …)` member-access casts and the 4 single-arg branch-unification `(cast ptr …)` casts across `+`, `*`, `-`, `/`, `and`, `or`, `case`, `->`, retypes `acc`/`cur`/`rest`/`result` locals to `(raw Node)`; design doc's claim that `tests/repl/redefinition.in` macros need cast migration verified **stale** (only `(cast i64 …)` there, no pointer casts); new castless test `examples/macro-cond-nocast.nuc`; 140 tests pass; `make bootstrap` byte-identical fixed-point. MC-4 (docs-only, no code changes) rewrote `docs/macros.md`'s sharp-edge section to the narrower element-type-only collapse rule and dropped its cast recipe, dropped the cast caveat pointer from `docs/toplevel.md`'s `defmacro` row, resolved the `design/progress.md` "Known constraints" bullet on the `(raw Node)` trap, and updated `context/conventions.md`'s Stage-10 N2 bullet 3 to the join-absorption rule. This workstream is **complete**. | [stage14/macro-conditional-casts.md](stage14/macro-conditional-casts.md) |
| 14 (AVR) | AVR microcontroller targets — cross-compile to ATtiny1634 (classic, `-mcpu=attiny1634`) and AVR32DD20 (modern Dx, via family `-mcpu=avrxmega3`); backend registration + `--mcpu`/`--mmcu` plumbing, 16-bit correctness (`ptr-int-ir` 2→`i16`, descriptor-parameterized qq helpers), avr-gcc link driver, volatile-MMIO device libs, `fn-attr`-based ISRs, Harvard hazards (addrspace(1) function values → v1 diagnostic; rodata placement; `defvar` const form; mapped-flash niche collision), all-`ABI-MEMORY` AVR aggregate ABI | **AVR-0 through AVR-8 done — the AVR workstream is complete** — design doc written; toolchain ground-verified in-container (LLVM 19 AVR backend present; `attiny1634` a known `-mcpu`, no Dx device entries; valid AVR ELF object emitted end to end; the function-value addrspace(1) verifier error reproduced; `__mulhi3`/`__divmodhi4`/`__muldi3`/`__addsf3` libcalls confirm the avr-gcc/libgcc link dependency); compiler blockers surveyed with file:line (binary 32/64 `ptr-int-ir`, 64-bit `emit-qq-helpers` in the *target* module, hardcoded clang+PIC link with empty CPU, no fn attrs/addrspace/const globals; in our favor: scalar code bypasses `abi-classify`, `volatile` landed, minimal programs reference zero runtime symbols). **AVR-0 done (2026-07-16)** — container now carries the AVR toolchain (Arch `pacman`: `avr-gcc` 14.2.0, `binutils-avr` 2.43.50, `avr-libc` **2.2.1 ≥ 2.2 so no Microchip DFP `-B` fallback**, `simavr` 1.6). Header-free spike driven `clang --target=avr -emit-llvm` → `llc -mcpu=<cpu> -filetype=obj` → `avr-gcc -mmcu=<device>` → `avr-objcopy -O ihex` for all three devices: **ATtiny1634** (`-mmcu=attiny1634`, links, ihex, both `__mulhi3`+`__udivmodhi4` linked, genuine `call`, `const`→`.data`/RAM), **AVR32DD20** (codegen `avrxmega3`, linked **`-mmcu=avr32dd20` — avr-gcc knows the exact device, no `-B`/family fallback**, `const`→`.rodata`/flash 0x8164 zero-RAM), **ATmega328P** (sim target). **`simavr` runs the ATmega328P ELF and exits cleanly** (attiny1634 not in `simavr --list-cores`; `qemu-system-avr` not installed — atmega328p is the pragmatic CI device as the doc predicted). No blockers hit; no `src/`/`lib/` changes (pure toolchain verification). **AVR-1 done (2026-07-16)** — the compiler now knows the `avr` target: `targets-init-all` registers the `LLVMInitializeAVR*` quartet (declared in `src/llvm.nuch`); new `--mcpu=<cpu>` flag → `g-mcpu-override`, threaded via a `cpu:ptr` field on `Target` into both `LLVMCreateTargetMachine` calls; reloc keyed on the triple (`reloc-for-triple`: Static for `avr`, PIC otherwise); `ptr-align` derived from the datalayout (`LLVMABIAlignmentOfType` on `ptr` — 8 on x86-64/aarch64, 1 on AVR) instead of `:= ptr-size`. One codegen fix was needed to pass the gate and it **revises ground-truth §2.8**: minimal programs do *not* reference zero runtime symbols — every non-`(exclude-prelude)` program force-emits `lib/node.nuc`'s arena/intern runtime, whose `unsafe/ptr+` hit a latent `emit-ptr-add` bug (hardcoded `i64` GEP index + sext-to-`i64` gated on `g-target-ptr-bytes`, inconsistent at non-8-byte targets). Fixed by routing the index coercion and annotation through `ptr-int-ir`/`ptr-int-type` (byte-identical on host; the i16 optimization stays AVR-2). Gate: `--target=avr --mcpu={attiny1634,avrxmega3} --emit-llvm examples/arith.nuc` emits the AVR datalayout/triple and `llc -mtriple=avr -filetype=obj` lowers it to a valid `Atmel AVR 8-bit` ELF. `make bootstrap` byte-identical (stage1.ll == stage2.ll, no `update-bootstrap`); old-boot-vs-new `--emit-llvm` diff empty on `ptr+`-heavy examples; `make test` **185/185** (180 + 5 new AVR gates, llc step conditional on `command -v llc`); `make abi-test` PASS. `compile-and-link` reloc/cpu wired but not gated (IR-emission only; avr-gcc link driver is AVR-3). Remaining phases AVR-2…AVR-8; the AVR-2 fixes are the long-standing 32-bit blockers too; byte-identical on hosted targets throughout. **AVR-2 done (2026-07-17)** — 16-bit correctness. `ptr-int-ir`/`ptr-int-type` (src/type-utils.nuc) changed from a binary `(if (= g-target-ptr-bytes 4) …)` to a proper 2→`i16`/4→`i32`/else→`i64` selection (`ty-i16` was already a registered singleton); audited every other direct `g-target-ptr-bytes` use and found the rest are plain derived arithmetic that already scales to any width, so no further changes there. `emit-qq-helpers` (src/nucleusc.nuc) — the hand-emitted `__cons`/`__append` IR for the Node-cons cell, hardcoded for 8-byte pointers (`malloc(i64 40)`, `align 8` throughout) — is now derived from the `Target` descriptor: size = `16 + 3*ptr-size` (fixed `i32,i32,i64` header + three pointer fields), and each store/load's `align N` = `gcd(struct-offset, ptr-align)` (`gcd(0, ptr-align) = ptr-align`) via a new small helper; GEP index arguments are untouched since LLVM resolves them from the struct type + datalayout, not from hand computation. Host (`ptr-size=8`, `ptr-align=8`) reproduces the exact prior literals (`40`; aligns `8,4,8,8,8,8`) — confirmed byte-identical; AVR (`ptr-size=2`, `ptr-align=1`) computes `malloc(i64 22)` and `align 1` throughout. One lockstep hole surfaced and fixed as a direct consequence: `node-type` for `sizeof` (src/generics.nuc) hardcoded `ty-i64` while `emit-sizeof` already returned `(ptr-int-type)` — invisible on host (both i64) but would have mistyped `(as usize (sizeof T))` on AVR once `ptr-int-type` started returning `ty-i16`; now mirrors `(ptr-int-type)`. New fixture `tests/fixtures/avr2-16bit.nuc` (usize + sizeof + tagged union/match + runtime quasiquote) and `run_avr2_16bit` gates in tests/run-tests.sh (both reference devices) check the emitted IR for `malloc(i64 22)`/`align 1`/`ptrtoint … to i16` and the *absence* of the old `malloc(i64 40)`, then round-trip through `llc`. `make test` **191/191** (187 + 4 new), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the **first pass** (no reconverge — host output unchanged by construction), `make abi-test` PASS, host `--emit-llvm` diff empty on `examples/quasiquote.nuc` and `examples/arith.nuc` (old boot vs new). **Two latent AVR bugs found but deliberately left unfixed** (out of AVR-2's scope, not reached by its scalar/plain-union gate): (1) `aref`/`aset` (src/nucleusc.nuc:~2767/2773) hardcode `i64` for the sext target and GEP index — same class as the `emit-ptr-add` bug AVR-1 fixed, not yet applied here; (2) `union-emit`'s niche error-code decode (src/union-emit.nuc:~219/1285) does `(strcmp (ptr-int-ir) "i32")` else-branch `trunc <ptr-int> to i32`, which would emit an illegal `trunc i16 to i32` on AVR now that `ptr-int-ir` returns `"i16"` there — only reachable via `!T`/error-niche programs on AVR, already blocked separately by the unaddressed mapped-flash niche collision (AVR-6). Recommend folding both into AVR-6/AVR-7. Independently re-verified (2026-07-17): clean build, `make test` 191/191, `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `make abi-test` PASS, host `--emit-llvm` diff empty on `examples/quasiquote.nuc`/`examples/arith.nuc` (old boot vs new), AVR fixes reconfirmed (`malloc(i64 22)`, `align 1`, `ptrtoint … to i16`, clean `llc` object emission for both `attiny1634` and `avrxmega3`). **AVR-3 done (2026-07-17)** — the avr-gcc link driver, the first *end-to-end* AVR gate (a real linked `.elf`, not just IR/an object). `src/nucleusc.nuc`: two new override globals `g-linker-override`/`g-mmcu-override` beside `g-mcpu-override`; three new argv flags — `--mmcu=<device>` (AVR link-line device name, distinct from the LLVM codegen `--mcpu`), `--linker=<cmd>` (override the link driver regardless of triple), `--link-arg=<arg>` (verbatim pass-through, routed through the existing `add-link-arg`/`g-link-args` mechanism that already backs `-l`/`-L`, no second list) — usage string updated to list all three. `compile-and-link`'s link-command construction now keys the driver on the triple (`avr-gcc` for an AVR triple via `strncmp … "avr" 3`, `clang` otherwise; `--linker=` overrides either) and, on an AVR triple, inserts `-mmcu=<device>` where device is `g-mmcu-override` if set else falling back to `g-mcpu-override` (so `attiny1634`, whose `--mcpu` already names the exact device, needs no separate `--mmcu`, while a family core like `avrxmega3` needs `--mmcu=avr32dd20` for the device-accurate spec file). The `driver:ptr` local reuses the same StrView-literal-then-statement-`set!` idiom `target-init` already uses for `--mcpu`, avoiding the StrView/ptr join-collapse gotcha (context/conventions.md) that a value-position `if` would hit. New freestanding fixture `tests/fixtures/avr3-link.nuc` (MMIO blink, `(exclude-prelude)` — the always-force-emitted intern/arena/node runtime references host-only `perror`/`malloc` and cannot link against avr-libc) and a new `run_avr3_link` harness gate (real link + `avr-size` footprint check, conditional on `avr-gcc`, SKIPs otherwise) exercise both reference devices end to end: **ATtiny1634** (`--mcpu=attiny1634`, mcpu doubles as device) → `avr-size` **278 text / 0 data / 0 bss**; **AVR32DD20** (`--mcpu=avrxmega3 --mmcu=avr32dd20`, family codegen + explicit device) → **310 text / 0 data / 0 bss** — freestanding-handful-of-bytes footprints, not the kilobytes a pulled-in host runtime would add. `make test` **193/193** (191 baseline + 2 new AVR-3 gates), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass (link-step-only change, no emitted-IR change), `make abi-test` PASS, hosted link path unaffected. One spec-vs-reality correction found during verification: the design doc's phrasing implied a bare `--mcpu=avrxmega3` with no `--mmcu` would fail to link — it doesn't; avr-gcc accepts it as a generic family layout (no device linker script). The meaningful device-accurate gate is the explicit `--mmcu=avr32dd20` case, and the real proof that `--mmcu` overrides device selection at link time is that a bogus `--mmcu=no-such-device-xyz` paired with a *valid* `--mcpu` is correctly rejected by avr-gcc (`cannot read spec file 'device-specs/specs-no-such-device-xyz'`). **AVR-4 done (2026-07-17)** — MMIO helper library, device register files, and three examples (`lib/`/`examples/`-only; no `src/` changes). `lib/avr.nuc`: device-agnostic `reg8-read`/`reg8-write`/`reg16-read`/`reg16-write` over `(unsafe/cast (ptr :volatile ui8) addr)` + volatile `deref`/`ptr-set!`, plus `bit-mask`/`reg8-test-bit`/`reg8-set-bit!`/`reg8-clear-bit!`/`reg8-toggle-bit!` — builtins-only so it works under `(exclude-prelude)`. Three `defconst` device-register files, addresses cross-referenced against the real avr-libc 2.2.1 headers rather than transcribed from memory: `lib/avr/attiny1634.nuc` (`iotn1634.h`; classic I/O-space SFRs need `_SFR_IO8`'s `+0x20` offset applied to get the memory address Nucleus's `deref`/`ptr-set!` actually reads/writes — confirmed at `sfr_defs.h:141`, e.g. `PORTB=0x2D` from `_SFR_IO8(0x0D)` at `iotn1634.h:153`), `lib/avr/avr32dd20.nuc` (`ioavr32dd20.h`; modern Dx memory-mapped `PORT_struct`, no offset — `PORTA` base `0x0400`, `DIRSET`/`OUT`/`OUTTGL` at struct-field byte offsets), `lib/avr/atmega328p.nuc` (`iom328p.h`; the third, CI-simulatable device — GPIO + USART0, the latter already `_SFR_MEM` so no offset). `examples/avr-blink.nuc` (ATtiny1634, classic read-modify-write PORTB toggle) and `examples/avr-blink-dx.nuc` (AVR32DD20, modern write-1-to-DIRSET/OUTTGL idiom — deliberately different code shape, not a bug) both link to a real `.elf` via the AVR-3 `avr-gcc` driver: **858 text / 0 data / 0 bss** and **890 / 0 / 0** respectively (independently re-verified by the orchestrator, exact match). `examples/avr-uart-hello.nuc` (ATmega328P polled USART0 TX, `UBRR0=51` for 9615 baud @ 8 MHz) is the milestone's real behavioral proof, not just an IR/link check — `simavr -m atmega328p -f 8000000` genuinely transmits `Hi!\n` (bytes 72,105,33,10) repeatedly; **independently re-verified by the orchestrator**, byte-for-byte match, **1070 text / 0 data / 0 bss**. Per the design doc, these examples are host-undiffable (no expected-stdout harness fits a freestanding target) and deliberately NOT wired into `tests/run-tests.sh`/`make test` — that's AVR-8's `make avr-test` target. `make test` **193/193 unchanged** (no new gates, confirmed both by the implementer and independently), `make bootstrap` byte-identical (no `src/` touched). Import-path note for future AVR example authors: `(import-use "…")` is a literal CWD-relative path (no `lib/`-search), so these files use `(import-use "lib/avr.nuc")`/`(import-use "lib/avr/attiny1634.nuc")` and must be compiled from the repo root — same convention `examples/comb-shapes.nuc` already uses for `"lib/list.nuc"`. **A pre-existing, general (non-AVR-specific) compiler bug was found and reported, not fixed** (out of AVR-4's scope): a binop with a bare-int-literal **left** operand and a typed non-literal **right** operand mistypes in `node-type` (`src/generics.nuc`'s binop typing reports the literal's default `i32` instead of adapting to the right operand's type, while `emit` correctly adapts) — when the result flows into a coercion (return/let-init/typed arg) this manufactures a malformed `trunc i32 <i8-value> to i8`. Reproduces on plain `(defn f (x:ui8):ui8 (return (_+ 1 x)))`; hidden in normal code because the literal is usually on the right. `lib/avr.nuc`'s `bit-mask` (`1 << bit`, where `shl` forces the literal left) hit this directly and works around it locally with `(unsafe/cast ui8 1)` (`(as ui8 1)` is rejected as a lossy narrow even for the constant 1). Root fix belongs in `node-type-call`'s binop typing: adapt to the non-literal operand rather than always operand 0, then re-converge the bootstrap — **independently reproduced and confirmed by a separate verification pass**: a build-test-runner agent compiled the same minimal repro, `(defn f (x:ui8):ui8 (return (_+ 1 x)))`, and inspected the emitted IR directly, confirming the exact malformed instruction (`%t3 = trunc i32 %t2 to i8` where `%t2` is already `i8`); flagging here as a confirmed, still-open bug for a future milestone (candidate: fold into AVR-6/AVR-7 alongside the two AVR-2-era latent bugs already tracked above, or fix standalone given it's general-purpose, not AVR-specific). **AVR-5 done (2026-07-17)** — the generic `(fn-attr <name> "<attr>" …)` top-level directive, letting any `defn` carry LLVM string function attributes; AVR interrupt handlers (`"signal"`/`"interrupt"`) are the first consumer. `src/compiler-types.nuc`: new `FnAttrEntry {name:ptr attr:ptr}` element-struct — another instance of the §14.5 batch-2 small-table idiom (`name` identity-compared via interned pointers, `attr` only ever printed, so both stay `ptr`). `src/nucleusc.nuc`: new `g-fn-attr-table (raw (Vector (ref FnAttrEntry)))` global (reset to null in compiler-init, mirroring `g-ns-prefix-table`); `fn-attr-add`/`emit-fn-attrs-for` helpers beside `ns-ir-prefix-set`/`-get`; a new `emit-fn-attr` directive (house-style precedent: `emit-set-ir-prefix`) parsing `(fn-attr name "attr" ...)`, dispatched via a new `'fn-attr` arm in the top-level `case hp` block, with `"fn-attr"` reserved in `g-special-form-set` so a `defn`/`defvar`/etc. cannot shadow it. There is deliberately no forward-reference prescan for the table, so `fn-attr` MUST precede the `defn` it targets in the unit — matched by the function's *source* name (equal to the emitted `@`-symbol in the default `user` namespace). `emit-defn`'s `define`-line suffix was refactored from a single `is-noreturn` branch into a composable sequence (base `)`, optional ` noreturn`, optional matching string attrs via `emit-fn-attrs-for`, then ` {`) — confirmed non-regressive: with no matching `fn-attr` entry the emitted bytes are byte-for-byte the historical `") noreturn {\n"` / `") {\n"`, verified directly on `die-at` (still emits `define void @die-at(...) noreturn {`) and on ordinary functions (`define i32 @main() {`, unchanged). **`defisr` scoping decision.** The design originally sketched a `(defisr <vector> …body)` macro wrapping both the `fn-attr` call and the `defn`. Not provided: this compiler's top-level dispatch matches literal head symbols *before* any macro expansion, so a top-level `(defmacro defisr …)` invocation — followed by a call to it at the top level — dies `unknown top-level form`, confirmed empirically (true even for a macro expanding to a single `defn`), and independently reproduced by a second verification pass. This is a genuine architectural constraint, not a shortcut: building a top-level macro-expansion pass is well beyond AVR-5's scope, and baking AVR-specific `__vector_N`/`"signal"` logic into a compiler builtin would violate the design's own layering (keep `fn-attr` generic, `defisr` a library convenience). The convention instead is two adjacent top-level forms — `(fn-attr __vector_N "signal")` then `(defn __vector_N ():void …)`, `fn-attr` first — documented in a `lib/avr.nuc` block comment and demonstrated in the new `examples/avr-isr.nuc` (a TIMER1_OVF ISR, `__vector_13` on the ATmega328P; new Timer/Counter1 registers — `TIMSK1`/`TCCR1B`/`TCNT1` + `TOIE1`/`CS10`/`CS11`/`CS12` — added to `lib/avr/atmega328p.nuc` for the example). **Verification.** `make test` **194/194** (193 baseline + new `run_avr5_isr` gate: link + `avr-objdump`), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `make abi-test` PASS, IR-level byte-identity confirmed for existing non-ISR functions. The ISR gate is independently confirmed via disassembly, not just link success: `avr-objdump` shows the `__vectors` table's slot 13 as `jmp ... <__vector_13>` (the strong symbol overrode avr-libc's weak `__bad_interrupt` default) and `<__vector_13>` ends in `reti` (not a plain `ret`) — genuine proof the `"signal"` attribute changed codegen, not just IR text. **AVR-6 done (2026-07-17)** — Harvard hazards, four sub-items. **(1) Function-value addrspace(1) diagnostic** — `Target` gains `prog-as:i32` (src/compiler-types.nuc), parsed by a new `datalayout-prog-as` helper (src/nucleusc.nuc) that scans the `-`-separated datalayout for a token-bounded `P<n>` segment (not a bare substring search — the character before `P` must be `-` or start-of-string), 0 when absent; set in `make-target-for-triple` from the freshly-obtained `dl-str`. AVR's datalayout carries `P1` (program memory = address space 1, where `@f = global ptr @f` is an LLVM verifier error); every existing target has `prog-as=0`. The diagnostic fires in `emit-symbol-ref`'s TY-FN branch (the single chokepoint materializing a bare function *symbol* as a first-class value, feeding both `funcall`-value sources and box construction) when `prog-as != 0` — `die-at` naming the function, instead of a confusing raw LLVM verifier error. **A second chokepoint was found and fixed**: a lambda-lifted closure boxed into BoxedFn/`dyn` reaches the vtable-emission machinery without going through `emit-symbol-ref`, and the vtable itself (`internal constant { ptr, ptr }`) is equally addrspace(1)-incompatible on AVR (confirmed: `llc` rejects it) — a new `avr-reject-fn-value` check was added to both `ensure-vtable-for` (BoxedFn/dyn) and `ensure-fnfwd-vtable` (bare-fn box). Both diagnostics independently reconfirmed by the orchestrator: `tests/fixtures/avr6-fnvalue.nuc` dies `cannot use function 'add' as a value on this target...call it directly instead` on `--target=avr` and compiles clean on host; `examples/boxedfn.nuc` dies `type-erasure (BoxedFn / dyn) construction materializes a function as a data pointer...boxed closures, dyn, and function values are not available on AVR in v1` on `--target=avr` and compiles clean on host. The full fix (emitting `ptr addrspace(1)` throughout vtable/funcall/BoxedFn machinery) is out of v1 scope, per the design doc. **(2) Rodata placement** — empirically verified, no code change: on classic AVR (ATtiny1634) a string literal lands in `.data` (VMA in RAM ≠ LMA in flash — crt copies flash→RAM at startup, accepted v1 SRAM cost); on `avrxmega3` (AVR32DD20) it lands in a dedicated flash-mapped `.rodata` with zero RAM cost. **(3) `:const` declaration attribute** — `(defvar :const name:type init)`, reusing AT-1's `parse-decl-attrs` registry (new `DECL-ATTR-CONST` bit, src/compiler-types.nuc) rather than a separate `defvar-const` top-level form — symmetric with the existing `:volatile` spelling and funnels through the same validation chokepoint. `emit-defvar` (src/nucleusc.nuc) now selects `constant` vs `global` (four combinations crossed with the existing private/public linkage choice); rejected with a clear error everywhere else the attribute registry applies (`let`/`with` binding, struct/union field, defn param, pointer-target type) since only a `defvar` global has an independent storage class to select. Independently reconfirmed: `(defvar :const answer:i32 42)` emits `@answer = constant i32 42`, an untouched `(defvar mutable-count:i32 0)` still emits `@mutable-count = global i32 0`, and `tests/fixtures/avr6-const-let.nuc` (`:const` on a `let` binding) dies `':const' applies only to a defvar global, not a field, parameter, or binding`. **Gap fix (2026-07-17):** the original landing emitted `constant` storage correctly but did not reject `set!` against a `:const` global at compile time — `(set! a-const-global v)` still type-checked and emitted an ordinary `store` into read-only storage (UB, segfaulted at runtime on the host). Fixed by adding a distinct `readonly-global:i32` flag on `Sym` (`src/compiler-types.nuc`) — kept separate from `is-const`/`const-val`, the unrelated `defconst` compile-time-substituted-value mechanism, whose reuse would have misrouted ordinary reads of a `:const` global through its inline-substitution branch. `emit-defvar` sets the flag post-`scope-define` when `DECL-ATTR-CONST` is set; `emit-set` now `die-at`s (`"set!: cannot assign to '%s' -- declared :const"`) before the RHS is emitted. New fixture `tests/fixtures/avr6-const-mutate-rejected.nuc` + `run_reject` gate; reads of a `:const` global reconfirmed to still go through the normal `load` path (not `defconst`'s inline substitution) via a direct IR check. `make test` **199/199** (198 + 1 new), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `make abi-test` PASS. **(4) Niche-collision link recipe** — on AVR32DD20, `err E`'s `0xFFFF-E` encoding aliases real flash-mapped addresses in the top of the address space; v1 mitigation reserves the top 256 bytes of the 32 KB flash via AVR-3's `--link-arg=` flag: `--link-arg=-Wl,--defsym=__TEXT_REGION_LENGTH__=0x7F00` (avrxmega3's linker script guards the text-region length with `DEFINED(__TEXT_REGION_LENGTH__)`). No compiler code — a documented build-script recipe; not independently re-verified by a separate pass (the implementer verified both that the recipe links cleanly and that an artificially small region length produces a genuine `region 'text' overflowed` linker error, proving the mechanism is really enforced, not a no-op flag). A niche-layout engine remains deferred (stage10 C4). **Verification**: `make test` **198/198** (194 baseline + 4 new: `run_avr6_fnvalue`, `run_avr6_const` variants), `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the **first pass** (independently reconfirmed — this change touches `emit-symbol-ref`, two vtable emitters, `emit-defvar`, and the AT-1 attribute registry, so byte-identity was checked directly rather than assumed), `make abi-test` PASS. Existing-target byte-identity independently reconfirmed at the IR level (not just via `make bootstrap`'s stage1==stage2): `--emit-llvm` diffs of the rebuilt compiler against the current `bin/nucleusc` boot artifact are empty for `examples/arith.nuc`, `examples/fnptr.nuc` (exercises the fn-value path), and `examples/boxedfn.nuc` (exercises the BoxedFn path) — all three IDENTICAL, confirming `prog-as=0` makes both new checks genuinely dead code on hosted targets. **A latent, unrelated bug was surfaced but not fixed** (out of AVR-6's scope): `lib/hash.nuc:82` has an `i64→usize` cast that is lossy on AVR's 16-bit `usize`, dying before an AVR `dyn` construction is even reached — a separate AVR-2/AVR-7-flavored lib-portability gap; the AVR-6 dyn-diagnostic gate used a minimal hash-free fixture instead of `examples/dyn-protocol.nuc` to route around it. **Independent re-verification (2026-07-17, full)**: a build-test-runner agent independently re-ran the entire AVR-6 landing from scratch and reconfirmed all four sub-items, including the two the prior checkpoint note had flagged as unverified — clean build, `make test` 198/198, `make bootstrap` byte-identical on the first pass, `make abi-test` PASS; both diagnostic chokepoints reconfirmed with the exact error text (fn-value on `tests/fixtures/avr6-fnvalue.nuc`, BoxedFn/dyn on `examples/boxedfn.nuc`, both clean on host); `:const` emission/rejection reconfirmed on all invalid sites (`let` binding, struct field, `defn` param); byte-identity reconfirmed via IR diff on 3 examples. **Sub-item 2 (rodata placement)** now has direct `avr-objdump` evidence: ATtiny1634's `.data` section shows VMA in RAM / LMA in flash (a real flash→RAM startup copy, the accepted v1 SRAM cost); AVR32DD20's `.rodata` section shows VMA==LMA at a flash address with a zero-size `.data` (genuinely free, no RAM cost). **Sub-item 4 (niche-collision recipe)** now has direct link-output evidence: `--link-arg=-Wl,--defsym=__TEXT_REGION_LENGTH__=0x7F00` links cleanly on AVR32DD20, while an artificially small `0x40` region genuinely fails with `section '.text' will not fit in region 'text'` — confirming the region-length cap is a real enforcement, not a no-op flag. No docs sweep remains deferred (this pass performed it). Remaining Stage 14 AVR work: AVR-7 (ABI + numerics policy), AVR-8 (tests/docs/progress close-out for the whole AVR workstream, incl. the `make avr-test` harness for the AVR-4/AVR-5 examples). **AVR-7 done (2026-07-17)** — three sub-items, closing out the AVR workstream's compiler-code phases (only AVR-8's tests/docs/progress close-out remains after this). (1) **Aggregate ABI**: `abi-is-avr` (strncmp-`avr` prefix) routes every struct/union (any size) to `ABI-MEMORY` before the `sz>16` eightbyte gate — the SysV eightbyte classifier assumes 8-byte register-sized chunks, a fiction on an 8-bit target with no such registers, so AVR bypasses eightbyte classification entirely rather than adapting it. The two MEMORY emission branches (`abi-print-param`, `abi-arg-frag`) fold AVR into the pre-existing aarch64 plain-pointer path (no `byval` — AVR has no such attribute); `abi-emit-param-prologue`/`emit-struct-ret`/callee-`sret` verified architecture-neutral (unchanged). A ≤16-byte `Point` that the host register-coerces (`@sum(i32 …)`) becomes a plain-pointer MEMORY param + `sret` return on AVR (`@sum(ptr …)`, zero `byval`) and links via avr-gcc; independently contrasted against a large (>16-byte) struct still using `byval` on x86_64, confirming the bypass is target-keyed and hosted-target ABI codegen is untouched. `avr-objdump` on the linked AVR struct fixture shows sane codegen (correct pointer-arg prologue, no register-window overflow).
