The ruling is the scope: a header mode refuses every program whose **declarations** the real pipeline refuses, not every program it refuses, since no body is read in this mode and checking one would mean compiling the unit twice. `--emit-nuch` had the same defect in its silent form and is the worse half — it exported `(declare foo ((x i32)))`, a declaration with no return type, for a program that does not compile. One walk before any output, with every message taken from the chokepoint that owns it (`defn-parse-sig`, `die-empty-decl`, `die-import-not-found`), so the header modes say exactly what `--emit-llvm` says by construction rather than by copied string. The row's count of crashing shapes was too low and the corpus is why: probing every head the two header emitters dispatch on, rather than the three files that had been reported, found **ten**, and a first version of the fix closed the reported three, passed every gate, and still left seven — so the arity half became nine `require-<head>-form` chokepoints, each moved verbatim out of the top of its own emitter, which now calls it. Measured: 136 → 133 llvm/header disagreements over 396 programs, all 133 residual ones body errors and all in the `llvm=1 / header=0` direction; 0 IR / 0 diagnostic / 0 status / 0 C-header diffs against the baseline compiler (the only `.nuch` diffs being the three fixtures whose malformed header is now refused), byte-identical bootstrap, 69/69 headers, 657 tests. It also filed **item 45**: `(defstruct ())`, `(defunion ())`, `(defenum ())` and `(defprotocol ())` segfault under plain `--emit-llvm`, which is the real pipeline being wrong rather than a header mode diverging from it, so it is recorded rather than folded in. `docs/compiler.md`'s claim that a source which does not compile "produces the compiler's ordinary error rather than a header" was corrected — it overclaimed both before and after the fix. **W9 item 45 fixed** — `()` in a position that requires a name. Its own row named four heads and one chokepoint; both were wrong. Probing every top-level head found the same segfault in **twenty-three** shapes (`(defn () …)`, `(ns ())`, `(import ())`, `(export ())`, an enum member, a protocol signature, a template head), three of the four named heads die in a PRESCAN rather than at the chokepoint the row proposed, and the fix is not a diagnostic at all: every one of those positions **already owned** the right message for a name of the wrong kind (`ns: namespace must be a symbol`, `defenum: value must be symbol`) and the kind test that would have raised it was what dereferenced the null. One null-safe accessor — `node-kind`, answering `NODE-NIL` for the null node `()` reads as, beside `lib/node.nuc`'s existing `node-line`/`node-is-list` — makes each site's own refusal fire; a prescan simply skips, per the deferral rule, and `validate-header-forms` gained the same questions so the header modes refuse rather than going silent (which item 38 rates as worse than the crash). Zero crashes across 48 probe shapes and 44 of them byte-identical in all three modes; the four that differ are stated and bounded (`deferror`'s recovering contract, `def-rmacro`'s reader directive, one body error). 396 programs: 0 IR / 0 diagnostic / 0 status diffs, and nine C-header/`.nuch` diffs that are the point — a colon-annotated `defstruct`/`defenum`/`defunion`/`defmacro` name was silently *emitted* as a header before and is refused now, in `--emit-llvm`'s words, and a colon-paren-fused `defconst` name used to reach the C header as the literal line `#define (null) 2`. Byte-identical bootstrap, 69/69 headers after regenerating the two `lib/node` ones, 685 tests. **W9 item 40 fixed** — a struct's LAYOUT and a `defunion`'s registration now resolve on reachability, like their names (W1a), their signatures and their value names (W8 G-0). Its row named three kinds and they are three different things: a struct's field table is a registration, so `emit-defstruct` splits at the line where it starts writing IR and a prescan calls the first half — every `%Name = type {…}` line stays exactly where the definer wrote it, which is why an old-vs-new sweep over 396 programs is **0 new errors, 0 changed error texts, and 17 files whose only diff is a type line moving within the type section with an identical multiset**; a `defunion` arm CONSTRUCTOR already worked below its import (its signature rode `prescan-union-ctors` since W1a) and what was missing was the `UnionDef` behind `make`/`match`/by-value, which needed `emit-defunion`'s no-op-on-existing-UnionDef guard re-keyed on `ctors-emitted`, the half only it performs; and a `defmacro` is **ruled** to stay with the emitter, because defining one runs codegen and materializes a JIT module — there is no half of it that writes no IR. The silent case was the important one: a by-value parameter of an unlaid-out struct classified to `i0` and its only symptom was an unlocated `failed to parse generated IR`, so every new test links and *runs*. Layouts want their own sweep and a **POST-order** one — a field's `(array T N)` extent is a constant expression, so a file's layouts need its own and its imports' `defconst`s registered first — and the permissive branch **abandons** rather than degrades, since a provisional length is a miscompile and not a missing message (`g-array-ok` mode 3 arms `g-layout-defer` from three frames inside a `ref`-returning parser). `StructDef.laid-out` replaces `emitted` as the answer to "has a layout", set at `struct-set-fields`, the single chokepoint. The same fix closed the defect `context/conventions.md` had carried as *"latent, still unfixed"* since W1d — the identical `i0` miscompile with no import at all, `(defn f (v:S) …)` above `(defstruct S …)` in one file — and inverted three W1d cycle-pinning tests, re-pointed to assert the answers (11, 7, 5) rather than re-baselined. Bootstrap moved and re-converged, its stage1/stage2 diff 52 lines and all of them type definitions; `make test` 691, abi/layout/check-headers green. Two residues measured and recorded rather than left implicit: an `(array T N)` extent only a macro can fold, which follows from the macro ruling, and the `.nuch` spelling of a `defunion`, filed as **item 46** — `emit-defunion-import` keys the registry on the bare name where every other definition-side probe uses `qualify-name`, which is a pre-existing defect that reproduces identically on the committed boot. **Item 42 ruled and closed 2026-08-15, and its ruling was free**: implicit conversions do not compose (one conversion, built-in or user, never both) turned out to be what the compiler already did in all five positions probed, so the recommendation cost zero lines — and the item's actual worth was the question that measurement forced, *where* the `defcast` registry is consulted, whose answer was "two of the nine typed slots". A rule was refused **on its own exact registered pair** at `let`/`with` init, both returns, `.set!`, `aset!`, struct-literal fields and union payloads; filed and fixed together as **item 47** by giving `coerce-int-val`'s final fallthrough — the chokepoint every typed slot already funnels through — the registry lookup that had been reachable only from `safe-coerce-val`. Additive by construction (the user rule runs only where the caller was about to die) and by measurement: 365 corpus programs, 0 IR diffs, 0 new errors, 0 changed error texts, bootstrap byte-identical for the compiler's own generated code. A near-miss note now names the rule that almost applied, which is what makes the ruling distinguishable from "your rule was never registered" — the failure mode that let `examples/implicit-cast.nuc` document a cast that never fired for its whole life. `make test` 697. **Item 46 fixed 2026-08-15 — the last open item, and W9 closes at forty-seven found, forty-seven fixed, none open.** The fix is two `qualify-name` calls, and it is only two because `emit-defunion` had already written the rule down in a comment one function away: the registry takes the canonical KEY and `union-ctor-form` takes the SOURCE spelling, "because what it builds is source … re-parsed in this same file, where the bare name resolves". `emit-defunion-import` used the source spelling for both, so a header carrying `(ns nsu)` filed its backing struct as `nsu/Opt` and its UnionDef as `Opt`, and `make`/`match` looked where nothing was. The arm constructors needed nothing — `emit-nuch-declare-import` already qualifies a synthesized `declare` under the header's namespace, emitting the `@nsu__Opt-Some` the library's object exports — so the row's hedge about the ctor path resolved to zero work. Item 40's `.nuch` layout residue then closed at exactly the size its row predicted: `prescan-union-layouts` joins `prescan-struct-layouts` in `prescan-nuch-signatures`, plus one guard change to `ctors-emitted`, the same discriminator item 40 introduced — pinned by a diamond import, since getting it wrong drops the declares or doubles them. The defect survived because the un-namespaced spelling makes both keys the same string, which every existing test and all 69 generated headers exercised in agreement; a bare-header control keeps the fix a re-keying rather than a rename. Additive across 242 corpus programs (0 IR diffs, 0 new errors, 0 changed error texts), verified to fail on the committed boot in both import orders. **Item 23's "in part" was retired the same day as stale bookkeeping**, its dispatch half having shipped as item 35 on 2026-08-14: re-probing confirms two namespaces may each define `describe (x:i32):i32`, that each emits its own symbol, and that a bare call is refused at the ambiguous use with both candidates and their definition sites named. `make test` 702. **With W9 closed, the stage's external gate was re-run (2026-08-15) for the first time since 2026-08-01** — the ~25,000-line Doom port, which is the only check in the stage that compiles a program this repo's authors did not write. **Both demo gates are bit-exact again, byte-for-byte the 2026-08-01 output, so nothing in W8/B/W9 moved the generated code**; what moved is what the front end accepts. The port as committed no longer builds: B4's R4 (a name is defined once per unit, equal values included) rejects 7 duplicate `defconst`s across five file pairs, W8 G-5's flip rejects the same 13 lazily-initialized singletons the 2026-08-01 run named, and `as` refuses 6 raw-`CStr`-to-non-null casts. The migration is 15 files and 47 lines, measured in a scratch copy — the port repository was not modified. It also exposed a hole in this repo's own tests: every redefinition pin used *different* values, so the equal-value case real code hits was unpinned; `b4-redefinition-same-value` and `b4-redefinition-same-value-fix` (the recommended one-owner migration, which runs) close it at 702 → **704 PASS / 0 FAIL**. The finding worth carrying forward is about measurement, not the rulings: R4's blast radius was counted on `src/`+`lib/`+`examples/`, where duplicate `defconst`s number **0**, and a naming rule is precisely the class of change whose cost lands entirely on code the rule's author does not have. Running totals and the per-item notes live in the stage document; this row is a pointer, not a second copy — stage-scoped detail in [stage15-stress-test/progress.md](stage15-stress-test/progress.md) | [global-init.md](global-init.md), [stage15-stress-test/diagnostics.md](stage15-stress-test/diagnostics.md), [stage15-stress-test/resolution.md](stage15-stress-test/resolution.md), [stage15-stress-test/ergonomics.md](stage15-stress-test/ergonomics.md), [stage15-stress-test/nullability.md](stage15-stress-test/nullability.md), [stage15-stress-test/selector-ambiguity.md](stage15-stress-test/selector-ambiguity.md), [stage15-stress-test/progress.md](stage15-stress-test/progress.md) |
| 16 (ergonomics) | Ergonomic and convenience fixes ([stage16-ergonomics/overview.md](stage16-ergonomics/overview.md)) — `macrolet`, keywords in place of the `&rest`/`&where` special symbols, variadic `set!` replacing `.set!` | **macrolet DONE (2026-08-15)** — `let` for macros ([stage16-ergonomics/macrolet.md](stage16-ergonomics/macrolet.md)): CL's shape, sequential bindings like Nucleus `let` (not CL's parallel ones), visible in its own body like `defmacro`, shadows a global macro for the body only, refuses to shadow a special form (the macro table is consulted *first*, so `let` would otherwise be taken over silently). Built from a two-way split of `emit-defmacro` into register + `compile-macro-body`, a `push-function-state`/`pop-function-state` pair beside `reset-function-state` (a macrolet compiles a macro **mid-function**, which the mono-worklist answer cannot serve — the macro must exist before the body it scopes over emits), and a `g-macrolet-stack` probed at the top of `find-macro`, the registry's only reader. Works in argument position mid-ABI-walk, in a loop, in a `cond` arm, inside a monomorphized template body, in the REPL, and **inside a `defmacro` body** — the case that ruled out the hoisting alternative. 721 tests (was 704), `make bootstrap` converges first try, all 146 examples byte-identical. Surfaced and fixed three pre-existing bugs (all reproduced on the stage-15 boot): a macro whose expansion is an **atom** emitted `@alloc-node` into a JIT module that never declared it (`g-qq-used` was answering two questions; split out `g-node-ctor-used`); a **non-symbol macro param** segfaulted the compiler (`strcmp(null, "&rest")` — `Node.s` is null on an INT node); and a user `defmacro` **typed at the REPL** aborted the session (missing `open-module-streams`, which the `compile-time` arm beside it has). **Container literal element types DONE (2026-08-16)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/container-literal-elements.md](stage16-ergonomics/container-literal-elements.md)) `[…]`/`#{…}`/`{…}` now infer `Keyword` from a keyword element alongside `i32`/`f64`/`CStr`. Reader-only: a `NODE-KEYWORD` row in `lit-elem-kind`, a `"Keyword"` row in `lit-kind-type`, four diagnostics. The constraint that decides the whole item is that `lit-kind-type` returns a spelling the readers stamp with `lit-sym`, so an element type must be **a single symbol** — which `Keyword` is and `(raw Node)` is not. Keyword literals are the first bracket literal whose element type the collection import does not reach, so `#{:a :b}` needs `(import-use keyword)`; the missing-import note names the file, and that is pinned rather than left to chance. Same pass fixed a pre-existing hole: **`#{1.0 2.0}` never worked** — floats were a documented element kind but `f64` had no `Hash` conformance, so a float set read fine and then failed to instantiate `insert`. `f64`/`f32` now fold their bit pattern; there is no bitcast operator, so the bits come back through the `(ref Self)` receiver the protocol already passes, and `-0.0` normalises to `+0.0` (it is `=` to `0.0` but differs in bits, so it would otherwise be silently unfindable). `NaN` deliberately gets no special case — it is `=` to nothing, itself included. Container-literal **refusals had no test coverage at all** before this; `run_s16_keyword_literal_refused` adds the four mixed-kind paths, the quoted-symbol refusal, and the missing-import note. 728 tests (was 721), `make bootstrap` converges, abi/layout green. **Symbol elements deferred** behind a *layering* question: a symbol value is `(raw Node)`, which is not a single-symbol spelling, is nullable where the collections want non-null, and has no `Hash`/`Eq`. An earlier draft called this a semantic fork — that `'foo` would have to mean a `Node` in macro position and a `Symbol` in literal position — and **checking Common Lisp, Scheme and Clojure retired that claim**: all three make a symbol an ordinary first-class value meaning the same thing everywhere, and even Scheme's syntax objects (the one genuine two-type system in the family, and it exists for hygiene) bridge by explicit `syntax->datum`/`datum->syntax` rather than by position. The question that is actually open is whether the macro layer should traffic in `Node`, the **compiler's** structure, at all — Clojure's macros see plain data and never its `Expr` tree; all three answer no. `lib/keyword.nuc` is already a symbol type in CL's terms (intern table, cached hash, id equality), so the work is largely re-parameterising it, and the cost that separates the options is the `Node`/arena dependency — decide it alongside `compile-time-imports.md`. **Symbol VALUES DONE (2026-08-16)** — the "make `Node` respectable as a value" path, chosen because the compiler itself deals in symbols and further string→symbol refactoring is planned (the compiler already links `Node`/arena, so the artifact-size cost that shaped the alternatives does not apply to its own use). Two parts: a `hash` overload on `(ref (ref Node))` in `lib/hash.nuc` folding the canonical pointer, and `quoted-datum-type` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) called by **both** `emit-quote` and `node-type`'s quote arm — a node-type↔emit-node lockstep pair — plus `ty-ref-node` beside `ty-raw-node`. `(HashSet (ref Node))` / `(HashMap (ref Node) V)` now accept bare `'foo` with no cast, and need no import beyond the collections since `Node` is in the prelude. Three of the four recorded blockers were softer than the measurement implied: `=` on `(ref Node)` already worked (only `hash` was missing, and it needs **no `extend`** — that takes a struct template, but a bare overload resolves); nullability was a *typing* artifact, since `'foo` lowers to `intern-symbol` whose signature already returns `ref:Node`, so typing it honestly is a fix rather than a laundering; and the spelling constraint is reader-only, irrelevant to the compiler-internal use. The typing rule is **conditional on the quoted datum** — only `NODE-SYM` becomes non-null, because `'(a b)` builds cells and `'()` IS null; a blanket flip would type null as non-null and silently disarm the flow checker, so `run_s16_symbol_values` pins both directions plus backward compatibility (non-null narrows into a `(raw Node)` slot). 733 tests (was 728), bootstrap converges, abi/layout green. **Symbol LITERALS DONE (2026-08-16)** — `#{'a 'b}` / `['a 'b]` / `{'k 1}` infer `(ref Node)`. Admission is a **shape** check (`lit-is-quoted-sym`: a 2-element `NODE-CELL` headed by `quote` with a `NODE-SYM` argument), not a node-kind check like every other element kind — which is why its neighbours stay refused, each pinned by a fixture: `'(a b)` (quoted list), `'1` (quoted int), bare `a` (variable reference), `(f x)` (call). Two forced signature changes, both from `(ref Node)` being compound so `lit-sym` cannot spell it: `infer-lit-type` returns a **kind** (`!i32`) rather than a spelling, and `lit-kind-type` became `lit-type-node (kind line)` returning a fresh type **node** — fresh per call is load-bearing, since the readers stamp the element type into both the `ref` binding and the `alloca`. 737 tests (was 733), bootstrap converges, abi/layout green. **The field/value ambiguity was deliberately NOT fixed** (field access is due a larger rethink, and solving it here would pre-empt that design): in head position a selector resolves as a field name and the quote is silently stripped, so `(m 'count)` on a symbol-keyed HashMap returns `HashMap`'s `count` field — a wrong answer rather than an error. Lookup must be spelled `(invoke m 'k)`; the example says so at the point of use and docs/collections.md states it as a rule. **The constraint to carry into the field-access redesign: an explicitly quoted symbol is a value, never a selector.** **Compile-time-only imports DONE (2026-08-16)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/compile-time-imports.md](stage16-ergonomics/compile-time-imports.md)) in the recommended order, (b) then (a). **(b) the prelude split**: `lib/prelude.nuc` dropped its unconditional `(import-use node)` and now holds only forms that emit no IR, so a trivial `main` emits **1 define / 169 IR lines / 1343 bytes of `.text`** where it emitted 17 / 1119 / 4569, and links no node or arena symbol at all. The node runtime is `(import-use node)` like any other library, and the demand is **diagnosed by name** (`quote needs the node runtime — add (import-use node)`) rather than auto-imported — which matches how `[…]`/`{…}`/`:kw` already behave, and is the same check `import-ct` then needs for its own promise, so it was built once. **There are two emission sites, not one**: `emit-quote-tree`, and the `&rest` call site in `emit-call-with-args`, which folds trailing arguments into `@make-cell` cells nowhere near a quote — a check on quote alone would have left that a link error with no location. Measured blast radius was 7 of 150 examples. The split also exposed a second, entirely undeclared leak: `lib/arena.nuc` imports `stdio.h`/`stdlib.h`/`string.h`, so the prelude's node import had been handing **every** program `printf` and `malloc`, and twelve examples, seven fixtures and several inline heredoc fixtures were using one without importing it. Worth remembering as a class: a transitive import is an undeclared dependency that only surfaces when the intermediate node is removed. `w9-multi-object-weak-prelude` asserted a weak `arena-init` in two objects as its proof that an imported file is duplicated per object; that premise died with the split, and it now asserts the same property on the fixture's own inlined import — which is what it was always really about. **(a) `(import-ct lib)`**: registers a library's compile-time surface (types, signatures, constants, macros) and emits none of its definitions, so `lib/error.nuc`'s `with-handler` can call `node-at` in its JIT'd body without every error-handling program carrying the node runtime. Implemented by redirecting the **definition stream** and letting the emitter run unchanged — deliberately, because conventions.md warns twice that a pre-pass mirroring an emitter must mirror its skips and inherits none of its diagnoses, and a register-only walk is exactly that pre-pass. **Only that one stream**: the type stream is free at run time and a dropped `%Name = type` dangles a later reference, and the decl stream is guarded by *already-declared latches* (`g-malloc-decl-done`, `g-trap-declared`, `g-nuch-registered`) — measured, redirecting it made `lib/arena.nuc`'s own `defmacro` JIT a `@malloc` call into a module whose declare had been discarded. The general shape: **any latch meaning “this is in the module” becomes false the moment emission is redirected.** The promise is kept by `Sym.ct-only`, set at the three sites whose definition went to the sink and *not* in `scope-define` (which would also have caught C-header and `.nuch` names, whose definitions live in libc or a `.o` and are not withheld), and read by `reject-ct-only` at the two chokepoints that turn a Sym into program IR — placed after the `is-const` arm, since a `defconst` is a compile-time substitution with no storage and survives fine. **The rule that took the most iterations: compile-time-only is a property of the UNIT, not of one import edge.** A library asking for a compile-time surface must never take the runtime away from a program that imports the same library for real — the first implementation let the CT import claim the path on `g-imported`, which was correct in one import order and silently wrong in the other. `prescan-imported-signatures` already walks the whole graph before any emission and follows every import form *except* `import-ct`, so what it visited **is** “reachable for real”; `g-real-reachable` snapshots it and `ct-sink-here` asks per file. Two consequences: the decision is per file rather than a nesting depth (an ordinary import reached *through* a compile-time-only one is still ordinary — measured, `lib/vector.nuc` imported for real by the program and also pulled in by a ct-imported library had every definition marked ct-only), and a template stamp belongs to no file, so `drain-mono-worklist` writes to `g-def-stream-program`. **The headline consequence:** `examples/avr-blink.nuc` with its `(exclude-prelude)` line removed links for the ATtiny1634 at **882 bytes of text against 858** with it — the old “no macros at all, or no AVR” choice is retired. What still costs bytes there is the string table, emitted whole into the program module including entries only a macro's JIT module used (126 bytes of `.data`); that is a separate leak of the same kind, and option (d) — `--gc-sections` — would drop it. 752 tests (was 737), `make bootstrap` converges, abi/layout/check-headers green. Also unchanged on purpose: the REPL still cannot quote (`use of undefined value '@intern-symbol'`), which it could not before this work either — `require-node-runtime` exempts `g-interactive` so REPL behaviour is byte-for-byte identical, and the bug belongs to the separate “`import` doesn't seem to work in the REPL” item. **Link-time reclamation DONE (2026-08-16)** — the same design's option (d), and neither half of the sentence describing it survived contact. It is not a `build.sh` change: `build.sh` runs `nucleusc`, and `nucleusc` writes the object file itself through `LLVMTargetMachineEmitToFile`, so `-ffunction-sections` handed to the *link* driver is a no-op. Nor is it reachable as a flag — `TargetOptions::FunctionSections` is exposed by neither `LLVMCreateTargetMachine` nor `LLVMTargetMachineOptions*` in LLVM 19, and the `-function-sections` `cl::opt` is registered only by `codegen::RegisterCodeGenFlags`, which `llc`-style tools construct and libLLVM does not. Measured, and worth keeping: **`LLVMParseCommandLineOptions` returns cleanly for an option that does not exist**, so the probe that matters is whether the emitted object changes (it did not). The section names are therefore spelled in the IR — `section ".text.<sym>"` / `.rodata` / `.data` / `.bss` on every `define` and global, which is exactly what `-ffunction-sections -fdata-sections` produce — with `-Wl,--gc-sections` added to the link line. Three rules, each measured rather than assumed: **the name must be the symbol's** (MC merges same-named sections and a merged section survives if any definition in it does); **`.bss` versus `.data` is decided by the NAME, not the initializer** — `getELFKindForNamedSection` maps a `.bss` prefix to SHT_NOBITS and gives the initializer no vote, so a zero global under `.data.` is PROGBITS and newly costs file bytes (a 64-element zero array cost 256) while a non-zero one under `.bss.` is a **hard error**, `SHT_NOBITS section cannot have non-zero initializers`, not a silent zero — which is what makes claiming `.bss` safe, and `global-section-prefix` claims it only for an initializer the compiler itself rendered as the type's zero; and **ELF only**, since a Mach-O specifier is `SEGMENT,section` and COFF collects with `/OPT:REF`, with JIT modules excluded separately through `emitting-program-ir` because a JIT module is built for the HOST while the spelling follows the TARGET. The string table is the one site that takes the decision as a parameter instead of asking `ir-sections-on`: it is written at module-assembly time, after the stream swaps that predicate reads have been undone, so a CT/macro module would otherwise look like the program module. **Measured:** `hello` 467 → 329 bytes of text+rodata+data, `list` 3784 → 2852, `callable` 3799 → 2773, `quasiquote` 3886 → 2946, `operators` 1920 → 1522; `avr-blink` on the ATtiny1634 882/126 → 626/0 text/data with the prelude and 858/0 → 604/0 under `(exclude-prelude)`, retiring §7's predicted 126-byte `.data` leak, and the four AVR examples now link at 346–862 bytes where they occupied 858–1070. **The compiler's own binary is the sole regression** (+0.46 % `.text`, 574693 → 577333): `-rdynamic` puts every symbol in the dynamic symbol table and a dynamically-exported symbol is a GC root, so adding `--gc-sections` to its own link changes nothing byte-for-byte (measured) and what remains is per-section alignment padding — the Makefile is deliberately unchanged, and `-rdynamic` is load-bearing for the macro JIT. No new flag: `--link-arg=` arguments are appended *after* the compiler's own, so `--link-arg=-Wl,--no-gc-sections` is the escape hatch for a program that resolves its own symbols with `dlsym`. Two golden-IR tests needed relaxing because they anchored on the end of a `define`/global line, which is where the section clause goes. 755 tests (was 752), `make bootstrap` converges after a two-stage boot refresh, abi/layout/check-headers/avr green. **Collection literal variables DONE (2026-08-17)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/collection-literal-variables.md](stage16-ergonomics/collection-literal-variables.md)): `[…]`/`#{…}`/`{…}` elements may be any typed expression — a local, a global, a `defconst`/`defenum` member, a call — not only a scalar literal. The restriction was a phase artifact: the three readers rewrote the literal into an already-typed `let` at read time, so the element type had to come from a token's node *kind*, and `read-program` finishes before any binding is registered (for a local the answer did not exist at any price). The readers now emit `(vector-lit <g> e …)` / `(hashset-lit …)` / `(hashmap-lit …)` — three heads reserved in `g-special-form-set` — and `emit-collection-lit` builds the same expansion once element types exist. Element type comes from the want channel when armed and from the elements otherwise: an int/float literal **adapts** to the value elements (`[1 n]` at `n:i64` is a `(Vector i64)`, exactly as `(conj v 1)` on one already is), everything else is fixed and must agree, and a value is never narrowed. Empty literals became legal with a declared type. **Target-first turned out to be a soundness fix**: `(with ((v (ref (Vector i64))) [1 2 3]) (invoke v 0))` printed `8589934593` — `0x2_00000001`, two `i32`s read as one `i64` — because the declared type was ignored and the reader's guess won; it prints `1` now and stamps no stray `Vector.i32`. (The *general* hole — a `(ref (Vector i32))` binds to a `(ref (Vector i64))` slot and passes as that parameter with no error — is unrelated to literals and deferred to its own document.) The design's hardest question inverted under implementation: it assumed the three heads needed real `node-type` arms with stamping kept out of them, but **`node-type` must return null**, because Rung 3 *overwrites* emit's type rather than asserting against it and E depends on a want already consumed by then — an arm that recomputed would silently retype the value. Two more the plan missed: the gensym stays minted in the **reader** or every `%__gs_N` in the IR renames, and a want must not excuse a mixed literal (the element scan runs in both paths). 773 tests (was 760), byte-identical IR across all 30 standalone literal-using examples and fixtures, `make bootstrap` converges, abi/layout/check-headers/avr green. **Keyword markers DONE (2026-08-16)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/keyword-markers.md](stage16-ergonomics/keyword-markers.md)) `&rest`/`&where`/`&optional`/`&repr` → `:rest`/`:where`/`:optional`/`:repr`. **Four markers, not the two the item named**: `&optional` (39 uses) and `&repr` (18, a `defunion` layout marker) were also live, and leaving either would have kept `&` reserved, which is the whole motive. Two halves of the framing needed correcting before starting. **There is nothing in the reader to simplify** — `&` is already an ordinary symbol character (`is-sym-char` is a deny-list; 38 is not on it), there is no `&` prefix dispatch and no reader-macro entry, so each marker was a plain interned `NODE-SYM` matched positionally by a content compare on `Node.s`. The change buys nothing there and everything in *conventions*: keywords already carry markers through `parse-decl-attrs` (`(defvar :const …)`, `(:volatile status:i32)`, `(ptr :volatile ui8)`), so the language had two spellings for one idea. And **`&` is not freed outright** — `.&` (field-address) is a live special form with ~200 uses, and `&` is illegal in any *definition* name regardless (`ir-name-illegal-char`/`check-ir-name-legal`); what is freed is `&` as a **prefix sigil**. The mechanical part was also smaller than "hundreds of sites" suggests: **fourteen recognition sites, seventeen comparisons**, collapsed into three helpers (`marker-named`/`marker-spelling`/`marker-any`) that take the marker's **bare** name (`"rest"`) — which is what stops the roster being re-spelled at each site, and removed every `"&rest"` string literal from the recognition path in one step. Nothing in the compiler *constructs* a marker (every `intern-symbol` call in `src/` was checked), so conventions.md's `intern-symbol` sweep trap does not apply. Three sites needed thought rather than substitution: `macro-parse-params`' name-collection test is **negated** — it does not read like a detection site — and sits below a `"param must be a symbol"` check that rejects a `NODE-KEYWORD` outright, so the marker test had to move ahead of it; `declare-param-type` sits directly above the arm that reads a keyword operand as a **type** (`:i64`), so without the marker check first `(declare f (a:i32 :rest xs:i32):i32)` reports `unknown type: rest` instead of the explicit refusal; and `defunion-strip-repr` was the one site comparing with `strcmp` rather than the overloaded `=`. **Two boot refreshes, not three**: `lib/macros.nuc`'s thirteen `&rest` headers are inside the compiler's own translation unit via the auto-prepended prelude, so a one-commit flip dies on the prelude before a single compiler form emits — dual-accept + refresh, then sweep-and-retire *together*, because the boot only has to **read** the new spelling, not still accept the old one. The sweep is one `sed` over 58 files in `src/ lib/ examples/ tests/ docs/ context/`; `design/` (~460 occurrences) is historical record and was left alone. The retirement is a located hard error naming the replacement, placed at **`desugar-params`** rather than `emit-defn`'s own scan because desugar runs straight off the reader: a `&where` defn no longer registers as a template, so `prescan-defn-signatures` would otherwise die `unknown type: T` before the marker was ever seen — and the other three marker-bearing shapes (`defmacro`/`macrolet`, `extend`, `defunion` arm chains) are not desugared at all, so each needs its own chokepoint. Measured rather than assumed: **`:rest` in a param list was already grammatically free** (it misses the `NODE-SYM`-guarded scan *and* `extract-name-and-type`'s keyword-headed-**cell** branch, which needs a `NODE-CELL`); **`fuse-colon-paren` does not fire on a marker** because it requires a *trailing* colon and a keyword's is leading and already stripped, so `:where(Ord T)` parses — though `:where((Ord T))` is **not** its no-space form, it is one constraint spelled `((Ord T))`; **`print-node` already emits `:name` for a `NODE-KEYWORD`**, which matters because `.nuch` is a **serialization format** (macros, templates and `extend` clauses export verbatim) — `check-headers.sh --fix` after the sweep reported *"regenerated 0"*, the sed-written headers already matching the compiler; and an **`:optional` default that is itself a keyword value** is safe because it lives one level down inside a 2-element cell while the marker scan walks the top level only. **Two discoveries.** `&repr` **had no test coverage at all** — documented in docs/structs-unions.md, used by no example, fixture or library; both modes are now covered. And **`run_stdlib_table` had been dying silently, hiding a real regression**: it did `out="$(python3 scripts/gen-stdlib-table.py --check 2>&1)"`, the exact `set -e` trap its neighbour `run_headers_generated` documents at length — a failing command substitution in a bare assignment kills the unit before its `FAIL` line, so the harness saw an empty result file, printed nothing, and only the script's **exit code** carried it. `make test` had been exiting 1 while showing zero FAILs. Behind it: the prelude split earlier this stage removed the `prelude → node → arena → stdio.h/stdlib.h` chain, so **165 libc functions** (`printf`, `malloc`, `free`, `exit`, `fopen`, …) stopped being available without an import while `docs/stdlib.md` went on claiming all 220. Both fixed — the unit now reports, and the doc is regenerated to the 55 `string.h` functions actually reachable (it is host- and libc-dependent by construction, which is why it is generated and gated rather than hand-maintained). **Generalisable: a harness that decides pass/fail by scanning output must *name* an empty result** — this one counted it but printed nothing, so "N tests, zero FAIL" was only as good as the guarantee that every unit spoke. Deliberately deferred: the **`has-rest`-by-count bug** (`src/nucleusc.nuc:15100`, `src/nuch.nuc:570` infer `has-rest` from a param-count *difference*, so an `:optional` defn registers `has-rest = 1`) — pre-existing, real, and not to be folded into a spelling sweep; and a never-instantiated **parametric** union's arms are never parsed at all, so a marker error there is silent — verified pre-existing against the equivalent `:repr mode` diagnostic, and a question about when a template body should be checked. 760 tests (was 755: +4 assertions, +1 unit that had never reported), `make test` **exits 0 for the first time**, `make bootstrap` converges after each of the two refreshes, abi/layout/check-headers/avr green. **Broad auto-cast to bool EVALUATED, not started (2026-08-18)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md](stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md)): recommends splitting the item and dropping half of it. The **dedicated `bool` type** is worth doing and is a *divorce from the integers*, not a new type — `bool` already maps to `ty-i1` in `builtin-type-name` and already dispatches as its own `TypeKind`, so the work is under 70 `:i1` sites, moves no IR (`type-to-ir` still emits `i1`), makes the `_Bool` C mapping honest rather than coincidental, and **deletes** types.md's `{0,1}` range rule rather than adding one. It is also a hard prerequisite for the second half: "all numbers are true" and "`bool` is an `i1`" cannot both hold, since `false` would be a number and therefore true. **The number rule is the half to drop.** In a static language every conditional it newly admits has an already-known answer — `(when n:i32 …)` is a constant, and so is `(when p:ptr:T …)` since Stage 10 made `ptr` non-null — so it shortens **zero** lines of working code, while silently inverting `(when (str-empty? s) …)`, `(when (char-is-digit c) …)` and every C `int` predicate, all of which return `i32` (confirmed by running them; ~40 such predicates in `lib/`+`src/`). It is precisely the blind spot conventions.md records from W9 item 31, where a wrong `i1` survived every bootstrap because its only consumers were truthiness tests — the proposal enlarges that set and removes the type error that today forces the author to write `(!= x 0)` and say which comparison they meant. And it is **the only option that cannot be revised**: it gives `(when n:i32 …)` the opposite of the meaning anyone would later want, where the alternatives leave it an error. What survives is **nil punning** (`raw`/`CStr`/`?T`/value-`Maybe`; 1,135 null-test sites in `src/`), and it belongs at the **six condition sites** — `cond` test, `while` condition, `not` operand, `_and`/`_or` × lhs/rhs, which are the whole surface since `if`/`when`/`case`/`if-some` are macros over `cond` — and **not** in the coercion set as the item words it: a lossy implicit coercion contradicts types.md's "exactly `as`'s safe set" invariant (the compiler already answers `(as bool n:i32)` with `lossy conversion from i32 to i1 -- use unsafe/cast`) and would make a `bool` parameter a universal overload candidate, against the already-decided rule that dispatch is stricter than assignment. The one cost the framing misses: `test-true-nonnull`/`test-false-nonnull` prove non-nullness by matching node **shapes**, so a bare symbol needs its own arm in the same change or `(when m (m kind))` typechecks the test and then fails on the body — the sugar would work everywhere except its motivating case. Left open: **zero-is-false**, the larger prize (a further 522 sites, and C semantics fit this codebase's `(!= flag 0)` idiom where Lisp's do not), which stays available indefinitely because nil-punning-only leaves `(when n:i32 …)` an error. **C interop, both directions, checked by diffing emitted IR against clang rather than reading the mapping tables:** the type divorce is source-level and moves **nothing** at the boundary — `type-to-ir` keeps emitting `i1`, `type-to-c` keeps emitting `_Bool`, the C header parser already accepts `bool` (stdbool expands first), `.nuch` already spells it `bool`, `sizeof`/align/struct layout already agree with C (8-byte `{flag:bool n:i32}` both sides, measured), `abi-classify` never looks at `TY-I1`, and `check-headers.sh` stays green. What part 1 *fixes*: `(+ true true)` is currently **`false`** (i1 addition wraps at one bit). What it *exposes* — all three pre-existing, none created by it, but `bool` goes from 3 sites in the tree to every predicate's return type: **(a)** no `zeroext` on any `_Bool` parameter/return (zero occurrences of `zeroext`/`signext` in the whole tree; clang declares `zeroext i1`), including the 14 already-exported `_Bool` comparison overloads; **(b)** the variadic-promotion miscompile recorded below; **(c)** `%BF = type { i1, i32 }` where clang emits `{ i8, i32 }` — layout agrees, but an `i1` load reads bit 0 only. **Export direction:** the divorce alone breaks no C consumer (headers and symbols byte-identical), but the **predicate-retyping sweep is a published API break** — `int32_t f()` → `_Bool f()`, and a stale consumer gets silently inverted booleans: measured, `is_pos(-2)` returns **-256** through a stale `int32_t` declaration, so `if (is_pos(-2))` fires when the predicate is false (`setg %al` leaves the input's `0xFFFFFF00` in `eax`'s upper bits). **`zeroext` does not fix that and must not be sold as a shim** — it lowers to `andb $1,%al`, masking `al` and saying nothing about bits 8–31; re-measured with the attribute, the stale consumer still reads -256. `_Bool` is defined in `al` only, so the remedy is recompilation, verified correct against both the plain and `zeroext` objects. **Scoping decision (2026-08-18): no consumers outside the compiler, so breaking changes are free and long-term interop *correctness* is the objective** — which retires the migration concern and **inverts one recommendation**. Earlier drafts said keep the mangle token `"i1"` (it lands in the ELF symbol, the `.text.*` section name, the `asm()` label and the C-side name — `define i32 @ov.i1` / `int32_t ov_i1(_Bool a) asm("ov.i1")`); that advice existed only to spare consumers a relink, so with none the token should be **renamed to `"bool"`** — it is a permanent user-visible name in every generated header and would otherwise be spelled after a type the language no longer has. Free today (no bool overload set exists), dearer with each one added. Same for `type-to-c` emitting `_Bool` where the preamble already includes `<stdbool.h>`. Re-ranked by demonstrated harm: **(1)** the variadic-promotion miscompile below is the only actual bug and is not about `bool` at all; **(2)** missing `zeroext` is a real spec divergence with **no demonstrated miscompile** — attempts to break it failed on x86-64 (`setg %al`) and RISC-V (`sgtz a0,a0`), both clean in both directions, so it is cheap insurance, not a fire; **(3)** the `{i1,i32}` vs `{i8,i32}` question is narrower than it looked and should be **left alone** — storing a `bool` field over a byte pre-dirtied to `0xAA` yields a clean `0x01`/`0x00` at both -O0 and -O2, so there is no defect to justify a codegen change plus bootstrap re-converge. **What the scoping decision does NOT relax:** `.nuch` records the *source spelling* (24 `i1` spellings across 7 committed files), and the compiler is its own consumer of that format during bootstrap — retiring the `i1` spelling still needs the dual-accept → refresh → retire shape the `&rest` flip used, because the boot compiler must be able to read the new source before the old one can be rejected. **Container type sugar EVALUATED (2026-08-22)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/container-type-sugar.md](stage16-ergonomics/container-type-sugar.md)): the filed spelling `ref:HashMap:(CStr i32)` **buys zero characters** and should be dropped. `ref:(HashMap CStr i32)` is the same 22 characters and already works in every type position (Stage 14's CP-1 chain fuse, just never applied to collections in the docs); making the filed form splice its paren group as an argument list would cost the reader a pointer-kind special case to keep `p:ptr:(fn i32)` wrapping. The paren-free `ref:HashMap:CStr:i32` (20) works too, but only while every type argument is a single token. **Two characters is not why nobody adopted it** — the sugar appears in no real source file, against 55 `(ref (Vector T))`, 43 `(ref (HashSet T))` and 36 `(ref (HashMap K V))` for the list form; the type expression is the cost, not its punctuation. Recommendation is therefore **type aliases** — `(deftype SymTab (ref (HashMap CStr i32)))` → `m:SymTab` — the one option that removes the expression from the use sites rather than compressing it, and the one that adds no reader rule: there is no way to name a type in Nucleus today (no `deftype`, nothing alias-like in `src/`, `docs/` or `design/`). Transparent, not nominal (same stamped type, same mangled name, one overload, `type-spelling` untaught), which is what keeps the bootstrap byte-identical until a file adopts one; two resolution sites beside the existing `struct-lookup-ref`/`struct-template-lookup-ref` probes, one `BK-TYPE-ALIAS` row for the one-symbol-one-kind guard, parametric aliases deferred to phase 2 where they meet `collect-pattern-tyvars`. **Aliases and the colon sugar compose, and that combination is the real answer** — a single-token type name already works through the plain colon sugar in every declaration position (`defvar` name, field, param, return, `let`, `with`, chain tail) with nothing new to implement, so `(defvar g-special-form-set:NameSet …)` needs only the alias; on that binding the sugar alone saves 5%, the alias alone 32%, the two together 37%. That also explains why the sugar was never adopted — it is at its best exactly when the type is one token, and the tree has almost no single-token collection types to use it on; aliases create them, and in doing so make arity-driven chains (which only compress multi-token expressions) largely pointless. Rejected: implicit `ref` for collections (`m:(HashMap CStr i32)`), which would make the spelling lie about representation against the Stage 10 pointer-kind discipline. **Phase 1 DONE (2026-08-22)** — `deftype`/`deftype-` with transparency, forward references, alias-of-alias, `.nuch` export, C-header expansion, REPL, and namespace-level privacy; **788 tests (was 778), `make bootstrap` byte-identical**, `examples/type-aliases.nuc`. Three corrections came out of building it. **`guard-name-kind` cannot carry collisions** the way the design assumed — it skips every row reporting the kind being defined, and struct/template/enum/alias all report `NK-TYPE`, so type-over-type is invisible to it *by construction*; because aliases are probed last a colliding alias is **dead, not merely ambiguous**, so `deftype` needed its own `type-name-collision` running ahead of the same-site early return, hence on both the prescan and emit passes — catching a clashing type declared above *or* below it. (`defenum` after an alias is still uncovered: `prescan-defenum-names` is per-form during emission, the same pre-existing gap as `defenum`-over-`defstruct`.) And **"the `.nuch` carries definer forms verbatim" was wrong** — both `.nuch` sides are explicit form lists, so an unlisted form is silently dropped; the header emitted `(declare twice ((n Count)) :Count)` naming a `Count` it did not carry. The lesson: *"the format carries X verbatim" is a claim about a dispatch table, not about the format*, and reading one output file cannot tell the two apart. That shape then bit twice more — `--emit-cheader`'s `type-node-to-c` resolves a `NODE-SYM` by spelling, so an alias leaked out as `struct Count twice(struct Count n);`, a header that does not compile; and the REPL's own top-level form chain gave `unknown: deftype`. **A new top-level form has six dispatch sites in this compiler** — prescan, emit, `.nuch` export, `.nuch` import, C header, REPL — plus the special-form set and `text-token-is-definer`; only the first two follow from the feature's description. Two fixture bugs also read as product bugs before they read as fixture bugs: a shared test header defining `(defenum E A B)` made `(deftype A …)` a real collision with an enum *member*, and a public `defn` with a private-alias signature put the private name into the header through its own `declare` — a pre-existing export leak unrelated to aliases. Probing also **turned up a silent mis-parse**, independent of the option chosen: `x:ref:Vector:ref:Node` puts `ref` and `Node` in template-argument position, where `collect-pattern-tyvars` collects any unresolvable symbol as a tyvar — bare `ptr` *is* resolvable, which is why `ref:Vector:ptr:i8` gets an honest arity error and this does not. The `defn` is classified as an unmonomorphized template, `emit-defn` skips its define, and nothing is reported; the call site then fails with `no matching method for overloaded 'f'` about a function that was dropped from the module. **CT-D DONE (2026-08-22)** — two guards in `collect-pattern-tyvars`: a pointer kind in type-argument position is refused by name (only `ref` reaches it, which is the whole asymmetry), and any type *pattern* whose argument count misses the template's arity now gets the arity error the concrete path already gave, with the count. Adding the arity check cost nothing — 792 tests, byte-identical bootstrap — which is itself the evidence no legitimate pattern relied on the gap. `docs/types.md` gained the container-chain spellings and the rule that a type argument may not itself be a chain. **Adoption DONE (2026-08-22)** — `NameSet`, `ConstraintVec`, `ImportVec` in the compiler's own sources, each verified byte-identical against a pre-adoption `build/nucleusc.ll`. Adopting a new form in `src/` needs `make update-bootstrap` **first** (`boot/nucleusc.ll` is the compiler that builds `src/`) — the two-commit dance the repo history already shows. The §3.10 rule decided all three placements by itself: `NameSet` is only ever a `ref` so the kind went inside, while `ConstraintVec`/`ImportVec` are `raw` on a field and `ref` once a site has proved non-null — a nullability difference the Stage 10 pointer-kind discipline exists to keep visible, so it stayed at the use site. `(ref (Vector ptr))` (24 sites) was deliberately **not** aliased: Stage 14's type-safety work retypes those individually, and one alias name over all of them would have to be unwound site by site. **Phase 2 DONE (2026-08-22)** — parametric aliases `(deftype (Vec T) (ref (Vector T)))`, working in every declaration position, in a generic method's **receiver**, across a `.nuch` and a real object-file link, in `--emit-cheader` and in the REPL; **799 tests (was 778)**, byte-identical bootstrap, all of `abi-test`/`layout-test`/`avr-test`/`check-headers` green. They substitute the argument **nodes**, not `StructTemplate`'s spellings, because in a receiver pattern an argument may still be a free tyvar with no `Type*` to spell. The design's receiver diagnosis was half right: an unexpanded `(Vec T)` matches no template and no pointer wrapper, so `collect-pattern-tyvars` walks past it and `T` is never collected — the opposite mechanism from the predicted "alias name collected as a tyvar", same consequence. Two traps worth keeping: `MAX-TYPE-ALIAS-DEPTH` in the parse path did **not** protect the pattern path, and `(deftype (A T) (A T))` hung the compiler until that descent got its own guard (a depth counter is a property of each recursion site, not of the concept — and the symptom is a hang, so only a `timeout` catches it); and inserting a form at the top of an existing `case` arm turns the *next* form into a case LABEL, surfacing as a type error on the label far from the edit. **CT-A dropped** — arity-driven flat chains compress only multi-token type expressions, which is exactly what aliases and CT-D remove, and they would couple a template's arity to the meaning of its existing use sites. **`as` sugar DONE (2026-08-23)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/as-sugar.md](stage16-ergonomics/as-sugar.md)): `baz:CStr` for `(as CStr baz)`; evaluated and implemented in one pass, atom form only, at emit time. **804 tests (was 799)**, `make bootstrap` byte-identical first try, abi/layout/check-headers/avr green, `examples/as-sugar.nuc` + four rejection fixtures. The reader was **not** touched: `baz:CStr` already lexed as one symbol, `split-typed` cut it downstream, and `emit-symbol-ref` **discarded** the type half — so the value-position slot was free, but occupied by a silent no-op that also let `baz:NoSuchType` compile. The change is ~30 lines in `emit-symbol-ref` (split into a wrapper + `emit-symbol-ref-bound`) and its `node-type-sym` lockstep partner, both calling two new shared rules: `value-annot-type` (generics.nuc — the destination, null when unresolvable, which is what lets node-type keep its never-aborts contract) and `as-convert` (nucleusc.nuc — steps 1–8 lifted out of `emit-as`, so the sugar *calls* the rule instead of mirroring it). The finding that decided the implementation site: **seven passes strip a value-position annotation and key on the bare name** (`set!` target, closure capture detect + rewrite, Stage 10 non-null flow facts, const folding, the two typing halves), so a *tree* rewrite in the reader or desugar would silently change what they all see — `(when (!= p:?ptr:N null) (p k))` narrows, `(when (!= (as ?ptr:N p) null) (p k))` does not. Emitting the cast at the value read leaves the node a `NODE-SYM`, so all seven keep working and `set!`/`addr-of` keep the lvalue reading for free. Exposure was measured first, by instrumenting the compiler over `src/` + 150 examples + `lib/`: **16 sites**, all `for`/`dotimes` loop counters whose annotation names their own type — identity casts emitting no IR, which is why the bootstrap converged byte-identical on the first try. Adoptable set for a future migration: **2411 of 3327** `(as …)` in `src/`+`lib/`; none adopted here, deliberately, since migrating in the same commit would have made that bootstrap result meaningless. Price accepted: a **third meaning** for one atom (declaration / type / cast, by position) on a tree that already has locals named `raw`, `ref`, `fn`. **Boot refreshed + first adoption (follow-up, same day).** Adopting the sugar in `src/` reported as "does not work when the type is `usize`" — the *boot* did not, and `make` builds `src/` with `bin/nucleusc`. The stale boot's reading is "discard the annotation, use the bare name", which is silent and harmless wherever an ordinary coercion re-derives the type (16 of 17 adopted sites: `:CStr`, `:i32`, `:i64`) and fatal at the one whose type **selects an overload** — `(invoke g-include-paths j:usize)` dispatched on `i32`. Fixed by the standing two-commit dance; `make update-bootstrap` could not run (its `$(BIN)` dependency was the failing build), so the seed was the relink escape — the already-built `build/nucleusc` emitted the new `boot/nucleusc.ll`, `make boot-binary` relinked `bin/nucleusc`, `make windows-boot` kept the committed Windows IRs in lockstep. **The adoption is IR-neutral, verified: 0 diff lines over 155,044** against a pre-adoption copy of the tree compiled by the same compiler. Generalized in context/conventions.md — before adopting a new spelling in `src/`, ask what the boot will *silently* make of it, not whether it rejects it; a reinterpreted spelling fails only where the difference is observable, which makes a stale boot look like a type-specific defect. **Selector position (follow-up, §11).** Continuing the adoption, `(mapping name:CStr)` in `src/cheader.nuc` died with `get: no field 'name:CStr'`: a **selector never reaches `emit-symbol-ref`** — `selector-literal-sym` classifies any bare `NODE-SYM` as a field name first (the callable-values §3.5 always-a-field rule), and the Stage 15 W7 demotion did not save it because the local is `name`, not `name:CStr`. Fixed in that one shared classifier, on a rule needing neither scope lookup nor a resolvability probe: **a field name can never carry a colon**, so an annotated symbol is unambiguously a value; quoted `'k:T` keeps the always-a-name reading. Sharing the classifier is what keeps the `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep by construction — emit (`emit-get-with-callee`) and node-type (`callable-get-type`) both call it. It also makes `(m k:CStr)` a third escape hatch from the field-wins limit, and the only one that works for a *global* key. 804 tests, `make bootstrap` fixed point in one pass; the boot needed the same relink refresh again, since `bin/nucleusc` predated the fix and so could not compile the `src/` site that motivated it. Excluded and diagnosed rather than supported: parenthesised types (the colon-paren fuse claims `q:(ref P)` as a call in every list context and cannot be made value-aware — `emit-callable-value` now names the mistake and points at `deftype`, as in [container-type-sugar.md](stage16-ergonomics/container-type-sugar.md)), the four self-evaluating names (`null:raw:T` is matched above the split), and computed operands (`(f x):CStr` lexes `:CStr` as a *keyword*, so it is not even a near-miss). **REPL libraries DESIGNED, not implemented (2026-08-24)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md)), off the filed item "`import` doesn't seem to work in the REPL". `import` works; what does not is everything a library needs *after* it: **16 of the 34 modules in `lib/` fail to import**, every collection and string module among them, and no generic, lambda or collection literal evaluates at the prompt at all. Seven independent defects, three of them the same mistake — **a global whose invariant holds for one module and one process, reused by a driver that assembles many modules and never exits**. `StructDef.emitted` means "written once this process", so a type emitted into a discarded REPL module is marked done forever (`context/repl.md`'s module-assembly invariant is the hand-maintained workaround, and `lookup-or-make-anon-struct` has already forgotten it). `drain-mono-worklist` has **one call site in the compiler**, at the tail of `emit-toplevel-forms`, which a prompt entry never reaches — so every stamped body, lambda lift and closure method is a call to a function never emitted, while the two sibling queues (`drain-init-worklist`, `dyn-annot-record`) *were* REPL-adapted. And `die-at` longjmps out of `do-import` past every save/restore, which is why the whole session afterwards reports `lib/iterator.nuc:1`. Two things the transcript hides: the REPL's recovery arm is **dead code** (`repl_try` is called twice per form, so a throw resumes at the second call — `tests/expected/repl-s16-macrolet.out` documents it by *lacking* a line), and a failed import leaves `g-importing` dirty so a retry is a **silent no-op**. Root cause is the prelude: the REPL never loads it, hand-mirroring `Node` and four of seven `NODE-*` ordinals, and that mirror writes `%Node` into the preamble without setting `emitted` — which is exactly what makes the real prelude unloadable, so the diagnostic saying `import the prelude` names an inapplicable fix. Staged R1–R5 with R1 (the unwind) first, because every other fix is unobservable while state corruption masks it; R4 (an `emitted` module epoch) carries the byte-identical-`build/nucleusc.ll` bar. Also found: **none of the eight existing REPL fixtures imports a Nucleus library** — `lib/` is tested only through batch compiles, which always get the auto-prelude — which is why a total failure of the REPL's library surface never showed up in `make test`. Evaluated and deferred (§3.6): replacing `repl_try` with Nucleus's own `!T` channel — the direction Stage 10 §7.2 already named, and which the reader half of the REPL loop already uses. It is gated on `!void`, which does not exist (`docs/strings.md:329`): `try` propagates only inside an `!T` function, so one converted `die-at` pulls its whole transitive call graph with it, and emit functions overwhelmingly return `void`. 634 `(die-at` sites across 235 of 1048 `defn`s — roughly double the ~347 at E4, which is what a stall at the reader boundary looks like from outside. R1 instead reshapes the shim to `repl_protect(body, ctx)`, keeping the `setjmp` frame live (today's has returned before the `longjmp`, which is UB) and making the double-arm bug unrepresentable. The prize that would justify the full conversion is **multi-error reporting** — `die-at` calls `exit(1)` and `src/` has no error-count machinery, so the compiler reports one error per run — not REPL tidiness. **R1 DONE (2026-08-24)** — the unwind ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.4, "R1 as built"): `repl_try` is replaced by `repl_protect(body, ctx)`, a protected call whose `setjmp` frame is still live when `repl_throw` fires — the shape that makes the double-arm bug unrepresentable — plus a small `jmp_buf` depth stack so a `die-at` from an *unprotected* path exits instead of jumping into a returned frame. Handing C a Nucleus `defn` needed no cast: type the shim parameter (`(declare repl_protect (body:(fn void)(ptr) ctx:ptr):i32)`) and pass the bare name; the obvious `(as ptr fname)` is refused. A `ReplState` struct spells the D4 roster **once** — reader state, the import/prescan lists, `g-toplevel-depth`, namespace and privacy flags, the three stream globals — and `repl-restore` also truncates 23 append-only registries to a per-form watermark, so a died import no longer answers name lookups with symbols that have no body: `(hashset-oom)` after an aborted `hashset` import now says `unknown: hashset-oom` with the W1c note instead of dying in the JIT on `@hashset-oom`. `g-generics` needed a *second* watermark (`GenericMark`): truncating the vector recovers only the generics the import **created**, while `generic-register-method` also appends to pre-existing ones — and it must restore `finalized`/`mangled`, which `generic-add-method` clears as a side effect. The D5 ordering fix could not be the literal "move the push": the pre-order `g-prescan-sigs` push is also the walk's **cycle breaker**, and a cycle not containing the unit root recurses forever without it — so the marker splits into `g-prescan-inflight` (pushed before the read, popped after) and `g-prescan-sigs` (pushed only on a completed prescan), with `path-in-unit-exact` consulting both so it keeps its old answer. Measured IR-neutral: all 152 `examples/*.nuc` emit byte-identically against the pre-change compiler and `make bootstrap` converged with **no** boot refresh. 805 tests (was 803): `tests/expected/repl-s16-macrolet.out` gains the `error (recovered)` line it never had — the visible proof the recovery arm runs — and a new `tests/repl/import-error.in` pins all four R1 behaviours in one file (attribution back to `<repl>`, the rolled-back symbol, a retry that reports rather than silently no-ops, an unrelated import still working). Not rolled back, each recorded with its reason in §3.4: the preamble memstream, the string pool, three `raw` (nullable) tables, the startup/argv tables, and the JIT session. **R2 DONE (2026-08-25)** — one unit-entry path for both drivers ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.1, "R2 as built"): `repl-register-node` is deleted and `repl-preload-macros` becomes `repl-preload-prelude`, feeding the reader `"(import-use prelude)"` instead of `"(import-use macros)"` so the REPL boots through the same import arm batch `main` splices — the prelude imports `lib/macros.nuc` itself. The hand mirror was **silently wrong, not merely partial**: it bound four of the seven `NODE-*` ordinals, so `NODE-FLOAT`/`NODE-KEYWORD`/`NODE-CHAR` had no binding at all at the prompt while a batch compile accepted them; all seven now evaluate to 0..6 and match a batch compile. Deleting it also closes D2 — nothing hand-writes `%Node` into the preamble any more, so the real prelude loads. **The 34-module probe goes 18 clean → 33 clean**, one fresh session each: every `(Maybe T)`, `StrView` and `(Result T E)` failure is gone, and the lone survivor is `node`, whose duplicate-symbol failure §6 already scopes out as a JIT symbol-resolution item. Startup cost **+22 ms (188 → 210 ms**, median of 15, measured against a comparison binary differing only in the boot literal) — the prelude's registrations, since `lib/macros.nuc` is common to both. 806 tests (was 805): a new `tests/repl/prelude.in` pins the seven ordinals, the standard macros, `"string.h"`/`"stdio.h"` re-import dedup (the prelude re-imports a header the REPL preloads), a `?i32` `defn` + `match`, and a `(ref StrView)` signature. Found but **not** fixed, and pre-existing (the pre-R1 boot fails identically): **D8** — `macro-jit-declare-raw`'s dedup list is scoped to the CT module's own buffers while the assembled REPL module is *preamble + ct-decl + ct-def*, so `(import-use error)` (which `import-ct`s node, putting `declare ptr @alloc-node()` in the preamble) followed by any import that JITs a node-building macro dies with LLVM's `invalid redefinition of function`. R2 does not widen it — the boot prelude adds no function declares — it only makes it reachable, by making both halves succeed on their own; it belongs with R3's declare backfill or R4's latch rule. **R3 DONE (2026-08-25)** — the REPL-owned monomorphization drain ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.2, "R3 as built"): `drain-mono-worklist`'s only compiler call site is the tail of `emit-toplevel-forms`, which a prompt entry never reaches, so every stamped body, lifted lambda and closure `invoke`/`drop` was a call whose callee was never emitted. `repl-flush-mono` drains into a module of its own — untracked on the main dylib, because a `defn`'s per-impl module carries a resource tracker the next redefinition removes, which would silently un-define every unrelated stamp drained alongside it — and `repl-backfill-progdefn-decls` publishes one ABI-lowered `declare` per `define` into the preamble *after* the module is assembled, since `g-mono-drained` is a persistent cursor and a body emitted for entry 2 is otherwise invisible at entry 6. **The reported transcript now works end to end**: `(import-use hashset)`, `#{1 2 3}`, `(contains? #{1 2 3} 2)` → `1`. Also fixed **D8**, a declare collision found during R2 (`(import-use error)` then `(import-use arena)` → `invalid redefinition of function 'alloc-node'`), whose root cause is §3.3's principle one level down: a dedup list scoped to the CT module's own buffers, describing a module that is assembled as *preamble + ct-decl + ct-def* — **a latch is a claim about a buffer, and is only correct if it names the buffer it is about.** The same work found the `extern` declare dedup process-wide, so `(extern stderr:ptr)` reached by two collection libraries landed in the first import's module only. 808 tests (was 806), `make bootstrap` at its fixed point. One asymmetry recorded rather than fixed: `emit-compile-time` still copies the preamble unfiltered; five probes found no case where that matters, and the D8 latch stops the duplicate being written rather than filtering it out. **The implementation session was interrupted after the code landed and before its design note existed** — the note was written afterwards by reading the diff, so it describes the code rather than the author's intent. **R4 DONE (2026-08-25)** — the `emitted` epoch ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.3, "R4 as built"): `StructDef.emitted` was a boolean latch meaning "written once in this process", which is right for batch's single module and wrong for a REPL that opens a fresh stream trio per entry and frees the buffers. A `StructDef` now records *which buffer* holds its `%Name = type {…}` line — `emit-epoch` (the module, bumped by `open-module-streams`; starts at 1 so the header modes, which open none, still differ from the default 0), `in-type-buf`, `in-preamble` — and two functions in `src/type-utils.nuc` are the whole interface: `sdef-in-module` replaces every read that meant "already present in the module being assembled", `sdef-note-emitted` every write. `emitted` keeps only the question it also answered — "does this type have a definition at all" — which is what the `defstruct`/`defunion` redefinition diagnostics ask; that split is W9 item 40's `laid-out` lesson applied again. **Collapsing the copy sites needed the module's shape changed, not its call sites tidied**: a REPL module was assembled as *preamble + its own type buffer*, which is exactly why the preamble could only be appended to after the JIT and why each arm carried its own `strdup`/`fputs`. R4 inverts it — `repl-absorb-type-buf` appends the type buffer to the preamble *before* assembly and marks the StructDefs whose lines are in it, and the assembler stops emitting the type buffer separately, so **the preamble is the type section of every module**. Six ad-hoc copies (§3.3 counted four; R3 had added two) became one rule, and `context/repl.md`'s hand-maintained invariant became a description of a mechanism. A module assembled *inside* another — `repl-flush-mono`'s drain — calls `repl-cycle-type-buf` first, absorbing the entry's buffer early and starting a fresh one, so neither module defines those types twice. **D6 closed**: a `?i32`-returning `defn` then `(match …)` at the next entry used to die `IR parse error: Cannot allocate unsized type %Maybe.i32`. One extra defect fell out and is the same one — a capturing `vfn`/`mfn` at the prompt died `base element of getelementptr must be sized` because its `%__vfn_env_N` line went to the *def* buffer while the `invoke` body it types is drained into a later module; under the REPL it now goes to the type stream, batch keeps `g-out` and its IR is unmoved. §3.3's claim that `lookup-or-make-anon-struct`'s bypass folds into the normal path holds only for the *description*: deferring it to the queue would move every `%__anon_struct_h*` line in the compiler's own IR, so the eager write stays and the struct is **queued as well**, making the line replayable at zero IR cost. Verification: **emitted IR byte-identical for 186 fixed inputs** (all 152 `examples/*.nuc` + all 34 `lib/*.nuc`, `--emit-llvm`, pre-R4 binary vs. rebuilt, stdout and stderr) plus 96 header-mode outputs; `make bootstrap` at its fixed point with no boot refresh; 808 PASS / 0 FAIL (the two new cases were appended to `tests/repl/prelude.in` and `tests/repl/generics.in` rather than added as files); `make abi-test` and `make layout-test` PASS; all 12 REPL fixtures pass; 33 of 34 `lib/` modules still import in a fresh session; REPL startup 213 → 218 ms. Still open (**D9**), and now expressible: a type is recoverable across modules only if it is queued or absorbed — a `defstruct` inside a REPL `(compile-time …)` is neither, and a `?T` stamped by a CT *body* is lost to a plainer bug (`emit-compile-time` flushes `g-type-stream` at its top and reads `g-type-bufp` at assembly time without flushing again; a one-line fix that moves batch CT-module IR, so it wants its own gate). **R5 DONE (2026-08-25)** — import coverage and diagnostics, and the last stage of this design ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.5, "R5 as built"): `import`, `import-prefixed`, `import-ct` and `unsafe/import-private` fell to the *expression* arm and were compiled as a call whose head was `import` (D7); all six spellings are now one top-level arm dispatching to the five batch emitters. **The recorded objection was stale twice over** — R3's declare backfill is keyed on definitions, and Stage 15 B2b had already deleted `inject-import-aliases`, so no spelling injects alias `Sym`s at all; the comment naming that function is deleted. **Two defects were hiding behind "compiled as a call"**, both REPL-only and both fixed: a private definition is emitted `internal`, which is right for batch's single module and invisible to every later REPL module, so `unsafe/import-private` imported `pd/priv-secret` and then died `Symbols not found` at the call (`weak_odr` under `g-interactive`); and `do-import` asks `ct-sink-here` **three times**, which in the REPL disagreed with itself — an imported file at the prompt runs at `g-toplevel-depth` 1, the depth that means "unit root" everywhere else, so `emit-toplevel-forms` re-sampled `g-real-reachable` from *inside* the file being sunk, and the recording branch then filed a compile-time-only import on `g-imported` (the EMITTED list), deduplicating away a later real import of the same library. The re-sample is now guarded on `g-ct-emitting == 0`; batch reaches depth 1 only at the real root, where it is 0. Diagnostics: the seven `(import the prelude)` messages route through W1c's `unknown-type-message` via a two-`%s` wrapper, so `?T` now answers `unknown type: Maybe — not defined anywhere in this compilation unit` plus a `note:` naming `lib/prelude.nuc` (no ptr-hook was needed — `union-registry.nuc` already called that function and `union-emit.nuc` resolves nucleusc.nuc helpers through the whole-unit prescan); every import at the prompt prints `  imported <lib>` or `  <lib> already imported`, answered by **measuring** the four registries an import can grow rather than by re-deriving `do-import`'s dedup gates — which is what makes it right for the C-header spelling, whose only tell is that it registers externs once; and the per-method conformance detail became `\n  note:` lines below its headline instead of bare `fprintf`s above it. Verification: batch output byte-identical for the same 186 fixed inputs (`.ll` + stdout + stderr + exit code) plus 96 header-mode outputs — **no stderr differed**, the rewritten messages being unreachable in a well-formed unit; `make bootstrap` at its fixed point; **809 PASS / 0 FAIL** with the new `tests/repl/import-forms.in` fixture covering all six spellings; `make abi-test` and `make layout-test` PASS; five REPL fixtures re-recorded for the import confirmation lines and nothing else. **This completes the design**; two items are left open deliberately — D9 above, and the `(import-use node)` duplicate-symbol item (§6), which is a JIT symbol-resolution question rather than an import one. **D9 DONE (2026-08-25)** — the type-recoverability residue R4 filed ([stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.3, "D9 as built"): **a type is recoverable across modules only if it is queued or absorbed.** Two instances, two lines each. `emit-compile-time` drained and flushed `g-type-stream` at its *top* and then copied `g-type-bufp` into the CT module at assembly with no second flush, so any type the CT body itself stamped was written after the buffer was last materialized — it now re-drains and re-flushes immediately before assembly, exactly as `compile-macro-body` always has. And `emit-defstruct` now `conj`s its `StructDef` onto `g-pending-unions` after its eager write, the shape R4 gave `lookup-or-make-anon-struct`, so a `defstruct` inside a REPL `(compile-time …)` is replayable into a later module. **The design was wrong about the batch/REPL split for BOTH instances, in opposite directions.** The flush is a plain *batch* defect, not a REPL one: `(compile-time (defn ctm (n:i32):?i32 (some n)) (match (ctm 7) …))` dies `IR parse error: Cannot allocate unsized type %Maybe.i32` under `--emit-llvm`. And the `defstruct` case, framed as prompt-only, has a batch shape the queue entry cannot close — in batch `g-module-epoch` never advances, so `sdef-in-module` stays true and the drain skips; a *body-position* use of a CT-defined struct compiles with **zero** `%P = type` lines and dies at link with `base element of getelementptr must be sized`. **Closed by a ruling, rather than recorded**: a `defstruct` inside `(compile-time …)` defines a *program* type, so that arm now sets `g-out` to `g-type-stream` — explicitly, not by inheriting the previous arm's — and the type line lands where both modules read it. Silent invalid IR at exit 0 is the worst failure shape available, and the REPL half of the same change already asserts that a CT struct is a program type, so batch disagreeing was exactly the divergence class the document exists to remove. The ruling is corpus-IR-neutral by construction: a form-aware scan of 441 files including `run-tests.sh`'s heredocs finds exactly one `defstruct` inside a `compile-time` block in the whole tree, D9's own new fixture. It also makes the queue entry redundant for this shape — measured with a scratch compiler built with the `conj` removed, which reproduces the REPL fixture byte-identically — and it is kept anyway as the standing "replayable across epochs" invariant, proven inert. The remaining batch shape — naming a CT-defined type in a **signature**, which fails earlier with `unknown type: P` — stays open and is confirmed to be prescan ordering: `prescan-struct-names` is a flat walk over the top-level form list and never descends into a `compile-time` body, proven by discriminator (a `printf` first in the CT block does not run before the diagnostic). **The queue entry's IR-neutrality proof, which R4 named and did not discharge**: `emit-pending-struct-ir-type` and `emit-defstruct` agree on all four counts — same `(sd ir-name)` header, `", "` separator and `" }\\n\\n"` terminator; same field count (`defstruct-fill-layout` computes `(- (node-len cc) 2)` from the same node and `struct-set-fields` stores it as `num-fields`); same per-field `Type*` (`struct-set-fields` stores the very `ftp` array `emit-defstruct` prints from); and no inserted padding or repr member (that rule lives in `emit-union-ir-type`, which dispatches to the plain renderer whenever `is-union` is 0). Verification: emitted IR **byte-identical for the same 186 fixed inputs**, swept again in full after the ruling, (152 `examples/` + 34 `lib/`, `--emit-llvm`, `.ll` + stdout + stderr + exit code, pre-D9 binary vs. rebuilt) plus 96 header-mode outputs — and the sweep is explicitly *not* the gate for the flush, which moves CT-module IR the sweep cannot see, hence the new batch unit; `make bootstrap` at its fixed point with no boot refresh; **813 PASS / 0 FAIL** (809 + four new assertions in `run_s16_d9_ct_types`, of which three FAIL on the pre-D9 binary); new `tests/repl/prelude.in` §7 asserting the *value* `7` from a CT-defined struct at a later entry; all 13 REPL fixtures pass; `make abi-test` and `make layout-test` PASS. Only §6's `(import-use node)` duplicate-symbol item is left open in that design. Remaining Stage 16 item — variadic `set!` — not started. **C header layout fidelity DONE (2026-08-26)** — ([stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md)): a C struct member whose type the parser cannot represent is stored as `ptr`, silently, so `(import-use "setjmp.h")` gives `%__jmp_buf_tag = type { ptr, i32, ptr }` — 24 bytes where C says 200, no error, no warning, and the resulting `alloca` handed to `setjmp` smashes the stack. The rule is already written at `src/cheader.nuc:583-585` and `c-parse-type` already raises `cheader-mark-unrep` on all five shapes; it has two consumers (the function-declaration boundary and the typedef recorder) and **neither is a struct member**.
