  **B3 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/file.nuc`: `File`, an owning descriptor closed by `Drop`, with `file-open-read`/`file-create`/`file-open-append`/`file-open-flags`, `file-read-to-string` (bytes, not validated UTF-8 — the compiler reads source whose encoding is the program's problem to diagnose, not the reader's to refuse), `file-write-bytes`, and a `Writer` conformance; plus `BufWriter`, a `File` and a 64 KiB staging buffer, which is the load-bearing half — IR emission is millions of small writes and a `FILE`'s buffer was the only thing `fprintf` had that a bare descriptor does not. A `write-str` of at least a bufferful skips the buffer: staging it would be a memcpy that buys no syscall. `file-close`/`buf-writer-close` exist beside the `Drop` path because `close` can fail late and `Drop` returns `void`; both set the descriptor to -1 so a later `Drop` cannot close a number some other `open` has since been handed. New errors `io-open-failed`, `io-read-failed`. Two findings. **#28, not fixable in the library**: `open(2)`'s flags are C preprocessor macros, which Nucleus deliberately does not import, so `O_RDONLY`/`O_CREAT`/`O_TRUNC`/`O_APPEND` are spelled out as Linux/glibc `defconst`s and the module is Linux-only as written — the real fixes (a target-keyed constants module, or object-like `#define` evaluation in the header import) are out of B3's scope. **#29, fixed in the compiler**: `(try (write-str f sv))` died with *"match: arm 'ok' binder count does not match its field count"* whenever `f` was a struct **value** binding rather than an already-`(ref T)` parameter. `emit-try` sizes its ok arm from `(node-type operand)`, and `node-type-call`'s tier 0 was still `generic-find-method-exact` — `params-type-eq` only — so the lvalue implicit address-of that `generic-resolve` gained was invisible to the type side; the call typed unknown, `try-ok-nfields` fell back to its 1-field default, and the generated `match` was one binder wide against `!void`'s payload-less arm. Fixed with `generic-find-method-accepting` (`src/generics.nuc`), the call-side companion run as node-type-call's tier-0 second pass, kept separate from `generic-find-method-exact` because that one also answers the *definition*-side question where `(name, param-types)` must stay an exact key. This is the `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep charging for a change made on one side only; bootstrap stayed byte-identical because nothing in `src/` yet calls a method with a struct-value receiver. `examples/file-test.nuc`; 944 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. `docs/io.md` §4–§5.

  **B4 done (2026-09-01)** — `lib/intern.nuc`: `Symbol`, the interned-identity type whose absence was the sole justification for Stage 14's "the interned substrate stays raw `ptr`" ruling. The layout is the design's: `[hash:usize][len:usize][bytes…][NUL]` with the `Symbol` holding the address of the first **byte**, not the header — one word, and all four properties at once. `=` emits a single `icmp eq ptr` (verified in the emitted IR, not assumed); `symbol-len`/`symbol-cached-hash` are loads behind the pointer; `symbol-as-view` allocates nothing; `symbol-as-cstr` is free, because the bytes are still NUL-terminated, so every surviving FFI seam takes a Symbol with no copy. Table: open-addressed, linear probing, grows at 3/4 load — not a `HashMap`, whose keys want to be interned, which would be circular. Conformances `Eq`/`Hash`/`ToStr`/`ByteStr`/`Str`, the last two through the view. No collision with the compiler's `Sym` (a binding record), so the `Name` fallback spelling was not needed. **`Keyword` rebased** (#4): it is now one `Symbol` and nothing else — one word instead of three, no `id` counter, and `lib/keyword.nuc`'s 256-entry fixed array with a linear scan and a `strcmp` per probe is deleted, cap and all. **The benchmark the plan asked for earned its place immediately.** `tests/fixtures/s17-intern-bench.nuc` timed `symbol-intern` at **8x slower** than the compiler's own `intern-symbol` over the same corpus — a stage-level failure by the plan's own standard. The table was not the cause: `strview-hash` folded through `reduce` over a `ByteIter`, so every byte cost an indirect call through a closure plus a `(Maybe ui8)` construction and match (#30) — a cost paid by every interned name, every `HashMap` probe and every `StrView` key compare in the library. Replaced with a direct byte loop (same FNV-1a, same results): 8x → 1.4x at -O0, and at -O3, which is how the compiler is actually built, `symbol-intern` is ~1.5x **faster** than `intern-symbol`. The generalizable finding: an `Iterator` fold is a fine default and the wrong tool inside a primitive everything else calls — `strview-eq` and the `ByteStr` defaults are worth the same look before the C-track conversion puts them under compiler-scale load. `examples/intern-test.nuc` (identity, the header, the two bridges, HashMap keying, and identity surviving a rehash past 3000 names); 945 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Docs: `stdlib.md` `Symbol` + rewritten `Keyword`, `types.md` (the 256-keyword limit is gone).

  **C1 done (2026-09-01)** — diagnostics converted and `src/format.nuc`'s helper zoo deleted. All **636** `fmt-*` call sites became `fstr`, and the **39** non-REPL `fprintf stderr` sites became `eprint`; `format.nuc` went 258 → 198 lines and is now only the IR/C identifier layer. What this removes is not typing but a hazard: each `fmt-*` took exactly as many conversions as its name said, and one too many made `snprintf` read a garbage vararg and segfault the compiler (the trap `context/conventions.md` documents). Arity is now a macro-expansion fact. **`fstr` (`src/strfmt.nuc`) rather than `str-alloc` directly**: it is `str-alloc` on the compiler's arena — the process lifetime `arena-strndup` gave, so no call site changes how it stores the result — handed back as a **bare `ptr`** rather than the honest `CStr`, because a branch join of a `CStr` arm and a `ptr` arm collapses to void and `(if c base (fstr …))` is a shape that occurs. Two compiler-local conformances ride along and are scheduled for deletion in C6–C7: `ToStr` on bare `ptr` (the compiler's strings still are one, and the specifier census over all 636 sites found no `%p`, so a `%s` argument was a C string by construction — this deliberately does NOT go in `lib/`, where "every pointer prints as text" would turn a type error into silent garbage), and a `Hex` value type for the `%016lX`/`%04lX`/`%016lx` float bit patterns. **The rewriter** is `scripts/stage17/rewrite-fmt.py`, paren-aware per migration-tooling.md §2, with a `--stderr` mode for the `fprintf` half; it **refused zero sites** — the census came back `%s`=781, `%d`=141, `%ld`=18, `%%`=17, `%016lX`=9, `%04lX`=4, `%016lx`=2, `%c`=1, all modelled — and is run to a fixed point, since a `fmt-*` nested in another's argument list is consumed as source text in one pass. Three findings. **(1) The boot compiler gates the source**: `fstr` needs the lvalue implicit address-of, so `src/` could not use it until `make update-bootstrap` refreshed `boot/nucleusc.ll` and `bin/nucleusc` — one shim generation, as `try-boot` needed. **(2) One name collision, and only one**: importing the string stack early brings `char-at` into scope, which `src/reader.nuc` had defined for `(ptr, i64)`; renamed to `cstr-byte-at` (284 uses), the suffixed-name convention `src/` already follows. **(3) Two `%s` arguments were typed `ptr:Node`** — `Node.car` holding a path string, which `%s` read as bytes without complaint and `to-str` will not; both now carry an explicit `(as ptr …)`. The type system found a place the format string was lying. `sanitize-for-ir`/`ir-name-token` and friends stay as they are: they never used `snprintf`, so none of C1's hazard applies, and `ir-name-token`'s pointer-unchanged fast path is an identity contract that belongs with the substrate conversion (C6–C7). Self-compile time unchanged (1.29s before and after); 945 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical. Deferred as planned: the 69 `printf`-to-stdout sites (C2 — converting them before the IR stream would reorder against a block-buffered `stdout`) and the REPL's 44 stderr sites (C5).

  **Lvalue-only implicit address-of done (2026-09-01)** — the resolution of #18/#24, argued in [stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md](stage17-native-strings/borrow-conventions.md). A struct **value** held in a binding now reaches a parameter that wants a pointer to exactly that struct, with no `&`: `(strview-byte-len sv)` where `sv:StrView`. Restricted to a **binding**, because `(ref T)` does not distinguish a read borrow from a write one — an rvalue would let a mutating callee append into a temporary and drop it, which is #25's class of defect — so `(show (strview-from-cstr "x"))` stays the ordinary argument-type error. Three pieces: `Val.lvalue-sym` (`src/compiler-types.nuc`), the binding a value was loaded from, set at the single place a Val becomes an lvalue (`emit-symbol-ref-bound`'s load arm) so a call result or literal has it null by arena zero-init; `binding-address-val` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`), factored out of `emit-addr-of` so the explicit `&x` and the implicit form share one owner for the escape-taint rule; and `params-accept-args` (`src/generics.nuc`), the dispatch half. **The dispatch half is not optional and its tier is load-bearing.** `generic-resolve` picks a method from argument types before any coercion runs, so without it every protocol method — `byte-len`, `sub-bytes`, the whole `ByteStr`/`Str` surface, which is most of #24's real friction — failed with "no matching method". Writing it into the tier-2 widen pass (which already receives argument nodes, so it looked like the natural home) broke `parse-test`: `from-str` selects its overload by a phantom first argument, and tier 2 lets every argument widen, so `i32` also matched the `i64` and `f64` overloads. The adjustment is **exact**, not a widening, so it runs at tier 0 as a second pass after the strict one finds nothing — an exact overload still wins and no resolving call can become ambiguous. Generalized into `context/conventions.md`. Dogfooded immediately: the 45 two-line `X__v`/`X` bindings A2's migration script created across five examples collapsed to one line each, and A3's four `(print-sv (addr-of seg))` sites are `(print-sv seg)` again — all byte-identical output. The implicit and explicit forms emit the identical call (the slot address is already an SSA name); the pre-coercion load is left dead and removed by the optimizer. Only a *binding* is an lvalue — a field, an array element and a deref are not, though C++ counts all three. 940 tests (`examples/lvalue-addr-of.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/s17-rvalue-addr-of.nuc`), `make bootstrap` byte-identical. **The `(ref T)` read/mutable split is deferred**: §3.4 removes the friction that raised it, so it no longer blocks Stage 17 and wants its own stage.

  **D1 done (2026-09-01)** — `scripts/stage17/ir-snapshot.sh`, the emitted-output identity gate ([migration-tooling.md](stage17-native-strings/migration-tooling.md) §1). `snapshot` / `verify`, three emissions per input (`--emit-llvm`, `--emit-cheader`, `--emit-nuch`) plus captured stderr, plus both Windows cross-emissions of every `lib/*.nuc`; 420 inputs, 2,596 artifacts, ~2 min. Re-baselining needs `--force`, so it can never be silent. Two corrections to the spec, both found by running it. **`src/nucleusc.nuc` is not an input.** The spec listed it as the biggest, most feature-dense source in the tree, which it is — and it is also the source being converted, so its emitted IR moves with every batch by construction and could only ever be re-baselined. `make bootstrap` covers that ground properly, as a fixed point (stage1.ll == stage2.ll) rather than against a baseline. The Windows cross-emission moved onto `lib/*.nuc` for the same reason. **`set -e` plus `pipefail` made the reporter silent**: `cmp` and `diff` exit non-zero on a difference, which is the *expected* case in the reporting branch, so the first real diff killed the run before it printed anything — it looked like a pass with lost output. Both guarded with `|| true`. The path-dependence caveat the spec said to check first was checked and is clean: no absolute path appears in any artifact.

  **C2 batch 1 done (2026-09-01)** — `emit`, and the first 284 of the 945 emission sites (`src/scope.nuc` 1, `src/union-registry.nuc` 4, `src/abi.nuc` 77, `src/union-emit.nuc` 202). `(emit g-body-stream "  %" n " = load " ty "\n")` formats into one process-wide `String` and issues one `fwrite`. **It is not the shape migration-tooling.md §2 sketched.** That was `(write S (str …))`, which allocates and drops a `String` at every site on the compiler's hottest output path; `emit` allocates nothing. The buffer is shared, so `emit-flush` takes a **mark** — `byte-len` before the pieces are appended — and writes only the bytes above it before rewinding. That makes one buffer safe when an argument's own side effect emits: the inner text goes out first and the buffer is left as found, which is exactly the order `fprintf` gave. (A census found no such site today; the mark costs one `usize` and removes the class.) The sink is still a `FILE*` and `emit-flush` still `fwrite`s: converting the call sites and flipping the stream globals to `(dyn Writer)` are separate steps, and this way the flip is a change to `emit-flush` alone rather than to every site a second time. `scripts/stage17/rewrite-fmt.py --writes` did the rewrite (`fprintf`/`printf`/`fputs`/`fputc`, the last two with their operands swapped, `fputc`'s byte spelled as a literal piece); one new specifier, `%02X`. **Zero refusals.** Throughput A/B on identical input — old binary vs new, both compiling the same `src/nucleusc.nuc` — is flat within noise (1788–1911 ms vs 1822–1845 ms).

  **Snapshot re-baselined once (2026-09-01), reason: `string-truncate-unchecked`.** The emit buffer needs an O(1) rewind to a mark; the checked `string-truncate` validates a char boundary and returns `!void`, neither of which a buffer that rewinds on every use can act on ([library-gaps.md](stage17-native-strings/library-gaps.md) #31). Adding a `lib/string.nuc` function moves the emitted IR of every artifact that imports it, which is a real output change and so a real re-baseline. It was verified additive before being taken: across all 2,528 artifacts of the old snapshot, **902 added lines and 0 deleted**, every added line part of `@string-truncate-unchecked`. That is also the batch's own gate result — the 284 converted sites, all of which `union-emit`/`abi`/`union-registry` run for every program in the corpus, changed no other byte. 945 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  **C2 done (2026-09-01)** — all 848 emission sites, in five gated batches: `scope`/`union-registry`/`abi`/`union-emit` (284), `cheader`/`nuch` (97), `src/nucleusc.nuc` in three regions (467). Zero refusals from `rewrite-fmt.py --writes`; the four hand conversions are the shapes a format string can express and the script cannot reach — cheader's skip-reason `macrolet`, which takes the format string as a *macro argument* so the literal to split is not at the call site; two `fwrite`s of a byte range, now `strview` pieces; and the REPL's `#<ptr %p>`, which is `"0x"` plus `hex … 0` because glibc's `%p` is minimal-width lowercase hex and a padded spelling would not have been the same bytes. Region 3 of nucleusc.nuc is where `%%` outnumbers everything else (145 of 217 conversions), which is the doubling rule the tooling doc calls the single most important thing for the script to get right, carrying a batch on its own. Self-compile A/B on identical input is flat throughout.

  **C3 done (2026-09-01)** — the emission sinks are `String`s and `open_memstream` is gone from `src/`, along with every `bufp`/`sizep` out-parameter pair, 35 `free`s, 35 `fclose`s and 14 `fflush`es. That is a portability fix as much as a string one: `open_memstream` is glibc/musl-only. Owning buffers are values (`g-type-stream`, `g-decl-stream`, `g-def-buf`, `g-entry-stream`, `g-body-stream`, `g-repl-preamble`); the aliases stay pointers because that is what they are — `g-out`, `g-decl-out`, `g-def-stream` and `g-def-stream-program` are re-pointed at whichever buffer the form being emitted belongs to, and `import-ct` stands a discard sink in front of the definition buffer. They are `(raw String)`: null before a module opens, exactly as the `FILE*`s were. `emit-flush` gained the matching overload — an overload, not a `(dyn Writer)` sink, since the sink type is known at every call site, so it dispatches statically and boxes nothing — and `emit-all` is new, for appending a whole buffer without going through `emit`'s staging (the function bodies are appended wholesale once per function; staging them would copy every byte of the module twice).

  Four things the types found that the `FILE*`s had hidden. **A `String` cannot be a `FnState` field**: `src/compiler-types.nuc` is imported before `lib/string.nuc`, so a `%String`-typed field names a type the compile-time module's own type section does not carry, and the JIT died with "use of undefined type named 'String'" on the first `compile-time` form. The field is a pointer into the arena (`string-box`), and a pointer field is `ptr` in IR and carries nothing. **`push-function-state` was always a move** and only now says so — it installs fresh buffers because the nested emission calls `reset-function-state`, which would otherwise clear the outer function's. **The REPL's module-stream snapshot was aliasing a buffer** that `open-module-streams` clears in place; a copy left behind carries a stale length over a buffer the nested module then reallocates, so it moves the three buffers out and back. **"Is a module open" was the null-ness of `g-type-stream`**, which a value cannot express; it is now `g-module-open`, because the header-emit modes prescan with no module and must still record nothing as written.

  **C4 + C5 done (2026-09-01)** — `read-file`, `file-defines-name` and `file-exists` were each a `fopen`/`fseek`/`ftell`/`rewind`/`malloc`/`fread`/`fclose` ladder re-deriving a length the kernel already knew; all three are `file-open-read` + `file-read-to-string` with the descriptor closed by `File`, and `src/` no longer calls `fopen`. **`lib/file.nuc` being Linux-only does not reach here**: `O_RDONLY` is 0 on every platform that has it, and only the create/append flags differ — source loading needs none of them. `repl-read-input` reads with `read-line` into a `String`, deleting the capacity-doubling loop and the per-line 4 KB scratch allocation; `read-line` strips the terminator, so the newline is pushed back, because the accumulated text is source and the newline both ends a `;` comment and is what every reported line number counts.

  **D3 done (2026-09-01)** — `--strict-cstr`, the residual-C-string enumerator ([migration-tooling.md](stage17-native-strings/migration-tooling.md) §3): `src/strict-cstr.nuc`, the seam list at `scripts/stage17/cstr-seams.txt`, and `make strict-cstr`. **Baseline: 4,568 sites.** That is the number C6/C7 drive to zero, and it is a fact about the emitted program rather than about the text — the hook is `emit-call-with-args`, the funnel every direct and multimethod call reaches after macro expansion and after monomorphization. The visible proof is a `CStr` reported inside `invoke.pVector.StrView.usize`, an instantiation that appears in no source file. By callee, the top of the census is `string-as-cstr` (648) and the `CStr` arm of `to-str` (582) — which together are `fstr`'s C1 bridge, retired by C6/C7, not new debt — then `new-tmp` (234) and `type-to-ir` (148), the IR-name substrate; then C6's own work list, `strcmp` 620, `strlen` 83, `strncmp` 41, `strchr` 37, `strstr` 13, `strndup` 5, `strtol` 2 — every one of which equals the count of that call in the emitted `build/nucleusc.ll`, which is the check that the enumerator is not itself undercounting; then `intern-symbol`/`intern-str`/`arena-strndup` (212), the interned substrate C7 moves to `Symbol`. By file: `nucleusc.nuc` ~2100, `generics.nuc` 487, `union-emit.nuc` 272, `cheader.nuc` 242, `union-registry.nuc` 219, `abi.nuc` 147.

  Five departures from the spec. **The seam test is an allowlist of callee names, not "is it a declared C function"** — the obvious criterion fails on the first case that matters, since `strcmp` *is* a declared C function and is exactly what C6 deletes. What separates a seam from residue is whether the other side of the call is C, which is a judgement and so is written down; one `seam-prefix LLVM` line covers the whole LLVM C API, which is what would otherwise have made an allowlist unmaintainable. **Declarations are deliberately not checked** — `grep -n CStr src/*.nuc` already answers that, and the pass exists for the question grep cannot answer. **`=` on a C string does not reach the call funnel** — the compiler writes `call i32 @strcmp(…)` into the IR as *text* (`emit-binop-vals`), so the funnel hook never saw it, and that is three quarters of the compiler's strcmps: 618 emitted against 143 spelled `(strcmp `. This is the predicted class arriving on schedule — the same shape as Stage 16's compiler-synthesized member access — and worth stating plainly: the first version of the enumerator was wrong in precisely the way the enumerator exists to catch. It now hooks that emission point too, and a synthesized call counts under the same `cfn` entry as a written one, because it is the same site to C6. **Indirect calls are checked and cannot be exempted** (`emit-funcall-value`, `emit-box-invoke`): the seam list is name-keyed and those have no name, which is the intent — by C8 there must be no `CStr`-typed call through a function pointer. There are none today, so the check closes the hole before it opens. **A `CStr` in the signature is not enough, because the C-header importer does not produce one** — `(import-use "string.h")` lowers `char *` to bare `ptr`, so `declare i32 @strcmp(ptr, ptr)` carries nothing for a signature walk to find, and the whole of C6's work list would have been silently absent from the count. They are named instead, under a third keyword (`cfn`), for the same reason `strptr` exists: where the types cannot see a string, the list says so out loud. Retyping the importer's `char *` as `CStr` is the real fix and is not this stage's — it moves every C-header signature in the tree. What the pass still cannot see is a `ptr` that is a string but neither produced by a listed `strptr` function nor passed to a `CStr` parameter; `strptr` names the three producers every such pointer traces back to, so the substrate is counted even where its aliases are not. 945 tests, snapshot byte-identical (the pass emits nothing), `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  **C6 step 1 done (2026-09-01)** — `fstr` returns `StrView`; `fcstr` is the bridge. `fstr` was documented from the day it was written as returning a bare `ptr` "deliberately and temporarily… when the consumers take `StrView` the cast and the `string-as-cstr` both come off". This is that split, taken ahead of the consumers so the consumers can move in groups: all 637 existing sites renamed to `fcstr` (a pure rename — snapshot byte-identical), and each migrates back to `fstr` as its consumer's signature moves. `make strict-cstr` counts the `string-as-cstr` that `fcstr` expands to, so the remaining bridge *is* the progress number and `fcstr` is deleted when it reaches zero. `src/strict-cstr.nuc` was converted first, as a live end-to-end check.

  **C6 step 2 done (2026-09-01)** — the diagnostic path. `die-at` and `report-at` take a `StrView`; of their 688 call sites, 368 were already string literals and needed nothing (a literal *is* a `StrView`), 293 moved from `fcstr` to `fstr`, and the rest fed message *builders*, so the builders moved too: nine that always produce a message return `StrView`, and nine that return null for "no note applies" — `retired-form-message`, `unsafe-bare-message`, `qualifier-scope-message`, `qualifier-scope-note`, `cheader-skip-note`, `cycle-definer-message`, `generic-in-other-namespace-message`, `type-in-other-namespace-message`, `case-clause-hint` — return `(Maybe StrView)`. Census 4,568 → 4,186; `string-as-cstr` 649 → 350. 946 tests, snapshot byte-identical over 2,596 artifacts, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, all four diagnostic tiers re-checked by hand.

  Three findings, each of which cost time and none of which is about the string library. **`(Maybe StrView)` works, and the Stage 11 note saying it would not is stale** — re-measured in a `compile-time` body and in a `defmacro` body, both construct/`match`/print correctly. That is the second Stage 11 string limitation §1.4 has found stale; the list should stop being cited as current. **`if-some` is the nullable-*pointer* form** and refuses a `(Maybe T)` over a struct ("value must be (Maybe (ref ...)) — launder a raw pointer with (as-ref ...)"); the general eliminator is `match`. Documented in `docs/builtins.md`, because the name reads as if it covered both. **`(return "literal")` from a `StrView` function emitted `store %StrView <bare ptr>`**, which LLVM rejects *with no source location at all*: `emit-return` runs the coercion that materializes a chameleon literal into `{ptr,len}` only on its ABI-DIRECT branch, and a struct return took the other one. Fixed there, with `tests/fixtures/s17-strview-literal-return.nuc`. The general shape, since Stage 14's LW-2 hit the neighbouring half of it: `return` has **two** paths through the coercer, and a fix applied to one of them looks complete and is half a fix. The committed `bin/nucleusc` predates the fix, so `import-cycle-advice` carries a one-binding bootstrap shim until the next boot binary.

  **C6 step 3 done (2026-09-01)** — `type-to-ir` and `ptr-int-ir` return `StrView`. 148 census sites, 23 bindings retyped, and the last fixed-buffer IR type-string builder (`emit-funcall-value`'s variadic `callee-sig`, five `snprintf`s into an `alloca i8 512`) rewritten as a `str-alloc`. Census 4,186 → 3,933. 947 tests, snapshot byte-identical over 2,596 artifacts, `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  This is where library-gap #27 stopped being a diagnostic and became a miscompile. `(if c "i16" "i32")` in a `StrView`-returning function compiled clean and produced a view whose length was read off the end of the stack; the first thing the new compiler did on any input was ask a `Vector` to reserve that many bytes and abort. The cause was two independent defects that only bite together: `collapse-strlit-cstr` retyped **every** string-literal branch to `CStr` so the phi would stay a plain pointer, and `emit-struct-ret` then sized its spill slot from the *value's* type instead of the declared return type — which under opaque pointers is not a verifier error. Fix in [library-gaps.md §27](stage17-native-strings/library-gaps.md); the shape to carry forward is that a branch join cannot decide this, because a join sees only the arms and "is a view wanted" is a property of the destination. `ptr-int-ir` keeps explicit `return`s rather than the nested `if` its sibling uses: it has to bootstrap through a compiler that predates the fix.

  **C6 step 4 done (2026-09-01)** — the ABI argument-list builders, and then the borrow the conversion had quietly opened. `abi-arg-frag` returns `StrView`, so the three `arglist` accumulators become `String`s instead of `snprintf` into an `alloca i8 2048` — which was also a silent truncation waiting for a call whose struct-by-value fragments exceeded it. Census 4,186 → 3,891; `snprintf` in `src/` 22 → 12.

  Then the finding that mattered, [library-gaps.md §33](stage17-native-strings/library-gaps.md). Making `type-to-ir` return `StrView` had left **32 sites handing a non-NUL-terminated pointer to something that would `strlen` it** — `emit-load`/`emit-store`, `abi-ret-ir`, `abi-eightbyte-ir`, `rv-flat-add`, `emit-match-clauses`, two REPL locals, three `(strcmp (ptr-int-ir) "i32")`. Every one compiled clean, because a `StrView`→`ptr` borrow is what the language *accepts*; the snapshot stayed byte-identical because the arena's pages are zeroed and `strlen` stopped in the right place by luck. `--strict-cstr` now counts the borrow itself, which is the only way this class is visible at all — it is not a type error and grep has nothing to match. All 32 converted (`AbiInfo.reg0`/`reg1` and `RvFlat.ir0`/`ir1` are `StrView` fields now), borrow count 0, and holding it there is the precondition for the substrate conversion in C7.

  A second aggregate-return hole came out of the same session: `emit-struct-ret`'s by-pointer branch read **any** pointer as the address of the return struct, so `(return (info 'reg0))` — an IR type-name string — became a wild 16-byte load. Both of its branches now refuse a value that is not the declared return type. Same shape as C6 step 2's finding, one level down: `return` has two paths, and the aggregate one runs no coercion at all.

  **C6 step 5 done (2026-09-02)** — `Val.val`, the IR-text substrate. Every value the emitter produces carries its LLVM operand text in `Val.val`; that field is now `StrView`, and with it `new-tmp`, the label/GEP/load/store operand family (`emit-load`, `emit-store`, `emit-load-at`, `emit-store-at`, `emit-zero-store`, `emit-bitfield-load`, `emit-bitfield-store`, `gep-index-ir`, `strview-data-ir`), the `defvar` constant folder (`defvar-init-ir`, `defvar-addr-of-ir`, `defvar-emit-array-backing`, `defvar-const-init-ir`, `global-section-prefix`, `write-ir-section`), and the call/box/match operand parameters (`abi-emit-struct-call`, `build-fatptr-val`, `fn-emit-env-value`, `emit-errptr-is-err`, `emit-handler-call`, `emit-match-clauses`). Census 3,891 → 3,532; borrow count back to 0. 947 tests, snapshot byte-identical over 2,596 artifacts, `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  **The line C6 stops at is IR *text* vs. IR *names*.** An operand — `%t42`, `match.end7`, `zeroinitializer`, a rendered constant — is text: it is written once into a stream and never compared, so it becomes a view. A *name* — what lands in `Sym.ir-name` or `Method.ir-name` — is not: those are interned, compared by **pointer identity**, and null-checked for "no name", none of which a `StrView` supports. Converting them is C7's job (they move to `Symbol`, which has all three), and until then every crossing is an explicit `strview-from-cstr` bridge rather than a silent `(as CStr …)`. Two attempts to sweep past that line failed loudly and are worth recording: retyping the 16 IR-*name* locals produced a stray `^D` byte in the CT module (a non-terminated view reaching `scope-define`), and `compile-macro-body`'s `(set! last-val (bv 'val))` into a `ptr` local segfaulted the compiler before it printed anything. `Val.val`'s **empty view now means void**, which is the only thing its old `null` meant — arena zero-init supplies it for free; `defvar-init-ir` uses the same convention for "no constant rendering exists" (an initializer is never the empty string).

  **C6 step 6 done (2026-09-02)** — the diagnostic renderers, and the last two fixed-buffer builders. `type-display` and `fn-type-spelling` return `StrView` (the accumulator is a `String` now, not repeated `fcstr` concatenation); `arg-type-spellings` drops its `alloca i8 512` and two `snprintf`s; `nucleus_gensym` builds `__gs_N` with `fcstr` instead of a `malloc 32` + `snprintf`; `text-token-is` takes the token it compares as a view. **`src/nucleusc.nuc` and `src/generics.nuc` now contain no `snprintf` at all**, and the eight that remain in `src/` are the REPL's (five diagnostic buffers and the two float renderers §2.6 keeps) plus `cheader.nuc`'s two `popen` command lines. Census 3,532 → 3,420.

  `type-spelling` deliberately did NOT move, and the reason generalizes: it is the **conformance-registry key** and the generic-substitution replacement text, so it is compared with `strcmp` *and* by pointer identity (`tyvar-index-of`). That makes it substrate, not text — C7's job. `type-display` wraps it for messages and bridges with `strview-from-cstr`.

  **The snapshot was re-baselined (2,596 → 2,608 artifacts), for `lib/vector.nuc`'s `vector-bounds`.** Its `what:CStr` panic label became `StrView`, which is the honest type for it and 55 of the census's sites; the emitted format string moved from `%s` to `%.*s` because a view prints by length. 59 artifacts differed and every difference was verified to be that one signature: the `.str` constant, the `@vector-bounds` definition, its 11 call sites inside instantiated `Vector` methods, and `lib/vector.h` / `lib/vector.nuch` (regenerated — `make lib-headers lib-cheaders`, which the `headers-generated` gate catches). `Vector` cannot import `strview` (both `io` and `fmt` import `vector`, so it would cycle), but `StrView` is a **prelude** type, so the field reads need no import at all.

  **C6 step 7 done (2026-09-02)** — the C-header emitter's text renderers. `type-node-to-c`, `type-node-to-c-decl`, `type-name-to-c`, `cheader-array-extent`, `c-fn-sig-render` and both `#include` spellings return `StrView`; `niche-sym-to-c` returns `(Maybe StrView)` (its "not a niche sugar symbol" was a null string); `CDeclSite.sig` is a `StrView` field, compared by value rather than by `strcmp`. The three declarator accumulators became `String`s. Census 3,420 → 3,375. `type-name-to-c`'s HashMap is still keyed by `CStr` on purpose — the KEY is a type name (C7's substrate) and only the ANSWER is C text.

  One defect, caught by the snapshot rather than by any type: `cheader-c-ident` is documented null-safe because a marker parameter (`&optional`, `&rest`) has no name, so bridging it eagerly with `strview-from-cstr` put `strlen(null)` on the path and segfaulted `--emit-cheader` on five inputs. The fix is `cheader-c-ident-view`, one function that states the null contract once. The general shape: **`strview-from-cstr` is strict where `(as CStr …)` was lazy** — a bridge added at a site whose value may be null is a new call, not a cast, and every null-tolerant producer needs its own view-returning wrapper.

  **C6 step 8 done (2026-09-02)** — comparison and scanning, the phase's named work. **No `strncmp` remains in `src/`** (44 sites): the reader's eight named char literals and three float special-tokens became one length-checked `=` per name (the `strncmp` form needed a paired length test beside each one, which is the bug the shape invites); the `avr`/`riscv64` triple tests, the `ptr:`/`ref:`/`raw:`/`__bf.`/`__anon.`/`__vfn_env_` spelling tests and the ten CLI flag prefixes became `strview-has-prefix`; the two n-byte compares of two runtime strings (`same-ns-qualifier`, `cheader-include-spelling`) became `=` on two length-`n` views. `str-ends-with` is now three lines over `strview-ends-with` instead of a hand-rolled byte loop with two `strlen`s. Census 3,375 → 3,332.

  **Two by-value predicates went into `lib/strview.nuc`: `strview-has-prefix` / `strview-has-suffix`.** The existing `strview-starts-with`/`-ends-with` take `(ref StrView)`, and since the address-of is lvalue-only a *literal* prefix — which is what essentially every one of these 44 sites wanted — cannot be passed to them at all. That is the gap, not a style preference. **The snapshot was re-baselined for it**: adding two functions to `lib/strview.nuc` changes 72 artifacts, and every one was verified **purely additive** (`diff | grep -c '^<'` is 0 for all of them), which is the signature of a library addition rather than of the compiler emitting different text.

  What C6 does NOT touch, and why the remaining census is not C6's to spend: the 617 `strcmp`s, the 87 `type-spelling` returns, the 88 `intern-str`, the `scope-lookup`/`scope-define`/`generic-lookup`/`conformance-lookup`/`tyvar-index-of` arguments and the 29 remaining `strchr`s are all reads of **interned names** — §2.1's first row sends a value compared for sameness to `Symbol`, not to `StrView`, so they move in C7 with the substrate. Bridging them now would add a `strlen` per site and be reverted there.

  **The reader's integer path, and a library bug it exposed (2026-09-02).** `(parse i64 "99999999999999999999")` returned `(ok 9223372036854775807)`: the conformance delegated to `strtoll` and checked only that every byte was consumed, which it was — `strtoll` clamps and signals the overflow through `errno` alone. The source comment claimed the consumed-bytes check *was* the overflow detection, so the defect was documented as a feature. Full write-up in [library-gaps.md §35](stage17-native-strings/library-gaps.md); the fix is `strview-parse-magnitude`, a digit walk that tests the limit before each multiply, placed in `lib/strview.nuc` because it is a view operation and `FromStr` is the layer above it. Adopting it in the reader deleted the LW-4 errno dance, two `alloca i8` token buffers with their "integer literal too long" limits, and `__errno_location` — **the one glibc/musl-specific `declare` in `src/`**. `(parse ui64 …)` now exists, and the radix parameter (which `FromStr`, keyed only on the target type, cannot express) is what let the `0x` path adopt it too.

  **C6 step 9 done (2026-09-02)** — **no `strstr` in `src/` either.** All 13 sites were content searches: eight target-triple probes and the two space-delimited C/C++ keyword lists, whose `strstr` membership test with a space-padded needle is exactly `strview-contains` with a padded view. `lib/strview.nuc` gains `strview-contains` beside the two prefix predicates, for the same reason (§34: the `(ref StrView)` form cannot take a literal). Census 3,328 → 3,320; snapshot re-baselined for the one new definition, every artifact verified additive modulo `@.str.N` renumbering.

  **C6 is closed.** What remains of the C-string census in `src/` is one class: reads of **interned names** — 617 `strcmp`, 87 `type-spelling`, 88 `intern-str`, 29 `strchr` for a `:` or `/` in a spelling, and the `scope-lookup`/`scope-define`/`generic-lookup`/`conformance-lookup`/`tyvar-index-of`/`assoc`/`conj` arguments that carry them. §2.1's first row sends every one of them to `Symbol`, not to `StrView`, so they are C7's by construction; bridging them with `strview-from-cstr` would add a `strlen` per site and be undone there. The four `strndup`s are path slicing and go with the same substrate.

  **C7-1 done (2026-09-02)** — `lib/intern.nuc` split and `Symbol` promoted to `lib/prelude.nuc`, the two preparations [c7-interned-substrate.md](stage17-native-strings/c7-interned-substrate.md) §2.2/§2.3 settled. The text conformances (`ToStr`, `ByteStr`, `Str`) moved to `lib/intern-str.nuc`, exactly as `lib/strview-str.nuc` is split from `lib/strview.nuc`; `(defstruct Symbol p:(ptr ui8))` sits beside `StrView` in the prelude because `Node.s` becomes one in C7-3 and the reader, the macro JIT module and the bootstrap all build `Node`s. Being **one word**, it leaves `Node`'s layout untouched — the property that makes the rest of C7 fail as type errors rather than as wrong-layout miscompiles. Snapshot re-baselined (2,596 → 2,616 artifacts) for the added prelude struct and the new module's own artifacts; every diff additive.

  **C7-2 done (2026-09-02)** — **one intern table.** `intern-symbol` still returns a canonical `ref:Node` and still takes a `CStr`, but the bytes it stores in `Node.s` come from `symbol-intern`, so they now carry the `[hash][len]` header, the per-probe `strcmp` is a pointer compare, and `intern-hash` is deleted. `InternEntry` survives the sketch's death sentence: `lib/node.nuc` still owns a canonical-`Node`-per-spelling map, which is what `(= hd0 'as)` compares — what it no longer owns is a second table of *bytes*. Its helpers were renamed `sym-node-place`/`sym-node-hash`/`sym-node-grow`, because `intern-grow` collided with `lib/intern.nuc`'s and was silently resolving by overload mangling (`@intern_grow.usize`) in every module that imported both — a pre-existing C7-1 defect the snapshot diff exposed. §4's first risk is **resolved**: the macro JIT resolves `g-sym-table` against the compiler process like any other program global, so a quoted symbol and a reader-produced one of the same spelling are still `=`; no shim was needed.

  **The AVR gate is what forced the real layering, and §2.3's split was not deep enough.** `tests/fixtures/avr2-16bit.nuc` failed the moment `lib/node.nuc` imported `intern`, with `lib/hash.nuc:109: f64 is not supported on AVR`. `avr-reject-f64` (src/abi.nuc) refuses an `f64` **annotation** anywhere in the translation unit — not just a live one — and both `lib/numeric.nuc:39` (`(extend f64 Ord)`) and `lib/hash.nuc`'s `f64` conformance are reached from `strview`. So "making every macro-using program pull the entire string stack is not acceptable" turned out to be literal: it does not bloat those programs, it makes them **un-compilable for an 8-bit target**. Two fixes, both root-cause. **(1) `lib/fnv.nuc`** — `fnv1a-byte`, `fnv1a-int` and a new `fnv1a-bytes` lifted out of `lib/hash.nuc`, so the fold is available without the conformances; `strview-hash` and `symbol-intern-bytes` now share it instead of keeping two copies of the loop. **(2) `lib/intern.nuc` imports libc and `fnv`, nothing else** — `symbol-intern-bytes (src, n)` is the primitive (no `StrView` constructor needed), `symbol-as-view` builds its two words with `alloca`, and the `Eq`/`Hash` conformance **records** (not the `=`/`!=` overloads, which resolve on their own) moved to `lib/intern-str.nuc`. The header offsets moved too: `SYM-LEN-OFF -8` / `SYM-HASH-OFF -16` / `SYM-HEADER 16` were 64-bit literals and `usize` is **two bytes** on AVR, so they are now a `SymHeader` struct and `sizeof`.

  **Snapshot re-baselined (2026-09-02), reason: C7-2 + the `fnv` extraction.** 126 artifacts differ and every one was attributed before the re-take, by multiset-subset diff over `@.str.N`/`%tN`-normalized text: 17 lost lines wherever `strview` is imported (the old inline `strview-hash` loop, now in `fnv1a-bytes`); 69 in every `node` importer (`intern-hash`/`intern-grow`/`intern-raw-insert`); the `weak_odr` re-marking of `fnv1a-byte`/`fnv1a-int` in `lib/hash.nuc` now that they arrive by import; `hash.pSymbol` relocating to `lib/intern-str.nuc`; the disappearance of `@intern_grow.usize`; and, in `lib_intern.nuc.ll`, the entire `strview`/`char`/`error`/`iterator` stack — which is the point of the step. 2,616 → 2,624 artifacts (`lib/fnv.nuc`'s eight). 947 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, census unchanged at 3,320 with the borrow count at 0.

  **C7-3a done (2026-09-02)** — the spelling-test predicates C7-3 needs, landed alone as a library addition: `symbol-is (self, other:StrView)`, the `=`/`!=` overloads over `(Symbol, StrView)` that make it the idiom, and `symbol-contains-byte` (all 17 `(strchr (n 's) 58)` sites are presence tests, none uses the position). §2.1's decision (b), plus the operator spelling — which is not a second mechanism but the reason 326 comparison sites will need **no edit** when `Node.s` flips: `(= (n 's) "defstruct")` keeps working, and `(= (n 's) some-ptr)` stops compiling, which is the whole point. `=` on two `Symbol`s is identity and against a view is a `memcmp`; interning is what makes those one predicate. Snapshot re-baselined, all 28 differing artifacts purely additive (the four new definitions, nothing else).

  **C7-3b done (2026-09-02)** — **`Node.s` is a `Symbol`.** The field the whole phase exists for: `(defstruct Node kind:i32 line:i32 i:i64 s:Symbol …)`, with `Symbol` moved above `Node` in `lib/prelude.nuc` so the declaration order holds. C7-3a's bet paid — the 326 `(= (n 's) "literal")` comparison sites needed **no edit**, because `=` over `(Symbol, StrView)` was already the idiom. What did need editing is every site that passed the field to something still taking a `ptr`, and those are bridged rather than converted: 178 `(sym-ptr …)` and 18 `(sym-of …)` calls, both `src/strfmt.nuc` one-liners, both greppable, both deleted by C7-4/C7-5 when the registries stop being `ptr`-keyed. A Python driver (`typecheck → bridge the first error → repeat`) applied them, acting only on error shapes it could name and stopping for anything else.

  Four things fell out that were not bridges. **`src/reader.nuc`'s `fuse-colon-paren` is native** — a `memcpy` into a stack buffer became a scan over the token's own bytes (`strview-has-suffix` / `strview-find-byte` / `strview-take-bytes` / `strview-drop-bytes`), which is what surfaced library gap #36. **`intern-symbol` split**: `intern-node (sym:Symbol)` is the real entry point and `intern-symbol (s:CStr)` is the thin wrapper the `'foo` lowering still calls. **`cons` uses `calloc`** — in `lib/list.nuc` and in the three examples that hand-roll a cell — because a `Symbol` has no null to assign, and "this node has no name" is now `symbol-none?` over a zeroed field rather than `(set! (c 's) null)`. And **`emit-quote-tree` interns**: it used to store a bare `@.str.N` into `Node.s`, which after the flip is a pointer with no `[hash][len]` behind it, so `symbol-len` read 16 bytes before an `.rodata` object and the self-compile took a **SIGBUS** — bisected through the macro-defining files to a `gensym` macro. It now emits a `@symbol-intern-bytes` call, with the matching `declare` added at both macro-JIT declare sites.

  **A string literal can now carry an embedded NUL.** `lex-string` always counted the decoded bytes; the token dropped the count and downstream recovered it with `strlen`. A `Symbol` carries its length, so the count survives: `"a\x00b"` is three bytes, not one. `examples/hex-escape-test.nuc` had pinned the truncation *precisely so a fix would be visible* — its expected output, its comments, and `docs/types.md`'s "a string literal cannot carry an embedded NUL" are all updated.

  **Snapshot re-baselined (2026-09-02), reason: the `Node.s` flip.** 336 artifacts differ; attribution was mechanical this time — split every `.ll` into `define` blocks and diff the sets. **Zero functions removed**, three added (`strview-take-bytes` in 70 modules, `symbol-none?` in 26, `intern-node` in 13), five changed (`intern-symbol`, and `cons`/`int-node`/`main`/`print-list` in the edited examples). 239 of the 336 differ **only** in the preamble, and the only normalized preamble line that moved is `%Node`'s field type — plus two `.str` constants in `hex-escape-test`, which are the embedded-NUL fix. The nine non-`.ll` artifacts are the three new declarations, the prelude reorder, and `lib/macros.nuc`'s changed source text. 947 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, census 3,320 → 3,330 with the borrow count at 0.

  **Bootstrap refreshed (2026-09-02), and the one C7-3b shim is gone.** `lib/macros.nuc`'s `case` had to compare a keyword head to `"or"` by bytes, because the *boot* compiler's reader did not intern the way this one does, so `'or`'s and `:or`'s pointers differed under it (verified with a probe macro: arena `0x7f43…` vs heap `0x5598…`). With one byte table on both sides it is `(= (as ptr ((head 's) 'p)) (as ptr (('or 's) 'p)))` — still reaching through `Symbol.p`, because the prelude cannot import `lib/intern.nuc` where `=` on two `Symbol`s lives, but an identity compare rather than a compiler-synthesized `strcmp` against a literal (census 3,330 → 3,329). The two `_` sites in `->` were already identity (`(= child '_)`). Snapshot re-baselined; the single differing artifact is `lib_macros.nuc.nuch`, which carries the macro's source text.

  **C7-4a done (2026-09-02) — every registry key is a `Symbol`.** 45 struct fields and roughly 200 signatures: scope keys (`Sym.name`, `Scope`'s frame), struct-field names, `type-spelling`, protocol/conformance/generic/template/enum/type-alias keys, `Constraint`, `DynEntry`, `MonoJob.ns`/`InitJob.ns`, `NsPrefixEntry`, `NameRef`, `CDeclSite`, the `fn-attr` table, the narrowing fact vectors, the macro/lambda parameter-name arrays, and the two name TABLES a registry scans rather than keys (`EnumDef.members`, a template's `tyvars`) — the last of which turns `enum-member-index`'s per-member `strcmp` into an `icmp`. `src/generics.nuc` alone drops from 99 `CStr` mentions to 18, `src/scope.nuc` from 6 to 0. The census falls **3,329 → 1,561** and the borrow count stays at 0. The ir-name substrate (`Sym.ir-name`, `Method.ir-name`, `ProgDefn.ir-name`, `StructDef.ir-name`/`ir-prefix`, `trace-saved`) is deliberately *not* in this step — it is an emitted spelling, not a key, and retyping it would drag `src/format.nuc`'s return types into the same commit; that is C7-4b.

  The same typecheck → one-fix → repeat driver did the sweep, extended about a dozen times as new error shapes appeared (`Symbol` out-parameters, aggregate-return mismatches, null tests that had to become `symbol-none?`, bridge stacking that needed a per-iteration collapse pass). Its log was reviewed per burst, because the mis-propagations it produces are all of one kind — a *name-shaped* thing that is not a name — and the typechecker cannot tell them apart from real ones. Four were caught by reading (`nuc-source-name` is a path predicate, `cheader-escape-leading-digit` is the C-identifier layer, a keyword probe's `tok`, `check-ir-name-legal`) and **three by the gates**:

  - **`binding-probe` returns a payload record, not a spelling.** Two of its fourteen rows have no record and return the spelling itself as the payload-free "yes", which is what made the whole return look like a name; the driver retyped it to `Symbol` and wrapped every `MacroDef*`/`Protocol*`/`Sym*` in `sym-of`, i.e. interned a record pointer as a C string. `(defn main ():i32 (return 0))` segfaulted. A payload pointer must never be bridged.
  - **`op-name-token` lost its inner `ir-name-token`.** It composes `ir-name-token` then `sanitize-for-ir`; without the first, `?`→`_QMARK` silently downgrades to the blanket `?`→`_`. `make test` is blind to it — the bootstrap caught it as `@contains_.pHashSet` against `@contains_QMARK.pHashSet` in 54 lines. This is the SM-3 ordering `src/format.nuc`'s header comment pins, and the reason it is pinned there.
  - **`NameRef.qual` is a `StrView` slice, not a `Symbol`.** `(as CStr view)` compiles — it takes the `data` word — and then prints to the NUL, so the scope note said `'dp/Describe' is not in scope` for a qualifier that is `dp`. Caught by `b2a-scope-diagnostic`, which exists precisely because that note is the diagnostic's whole value.

  Two library additions came with it, gaps #37 and #38: **`symbol-intern` by value** (the primitive is `(ref StrView)` and `addr-of` is lvalue-only, so `(symbol-intern "Any")` did not compile), and **`symbol-none`** (C7-3b could *recognise* the zero `Symbol` but only `calloc` could write one — useless for an out-parameter a parser leaves unset). The literal case wants a **string-literal → `Symbol` coercion** interning at compile time, which would delete every explicit `(symbol-intern "…")` this step introduced; that is a code-generation change, deferred and recorded in library-gaps.md.

  **Snapshot re-baselined (2026-09-02), reason: gaps #37 and #38.** 28 artifacts differ — every module that reaches `lib/intern.nuc` — and each diff is exactly the new `@symbol-none` definition plus `@symbol-intern` becoming the overload pair `@symbol_intern.pStrView` / `@symbol_intern.StrView`. Zero functions removed, zero changed bodies. 947 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  **C7-4b done (2026-09-02) — the ir-name substrate, and the split inside it.** The substrate is not one thing. IR *operand* text — `Sym.ir-name`, `Sym.cslot`, `Sym.trace-saved`, `CastRule.ir-name`, `Cleanup`'s `slot`, and the `type-mangle-token` / `sanitize-for-ir` / `sanitize-for-c` / `ir-name-token` return types — becomes `StrView`: `%x.addr.7` carries a per-binding counter, the local population dominates by orders of magnitude, and interning it would pay a hash per binding to buy an identity nothing asks about. Link *names* and prefixes — `Method.ir-name`, `StructDef.ir-name`/`ir-prefix`, `ProgDefn.ir-name`, `Generic.ir-prefix`, `NsPrefixEntry.prefix`, `PrivFile.prefix`, `Cleanup`'s `dropfn` — become `Symbol`, because every one of them is identity-compared. The two consumers that want identity over a *local's* ir-name (`program-defn-lookup`, the macro-JIT declare set) are global-only and intern at their own boundary; `program-defn-lookup`'s membership test was a `strcmp` and is now one `icmp`. `ir-name-token`'s fast path now returns its argument with no copy, which the `ptr` return could not do. Census **1,561 → 1,351**, borrow count 0, 948 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, and the snapshot needed **no re-baseline** — all 2,624 artifacts unchanged, which is the evidence that retyping the substrate changed no emitted spelling.

  All three defects were the silent borrow rather than the wrong type. Five `(strview-from-cstr (as CStr null))` sites the driver produced from declarations it had just retyped — `strview-from-cstr` is *strict* where `(as CStr …)` was lazy, so `strlen(null)` crashed on `prescan-defenum-names` over the prelude's `(defenum NodeKind …)`. Then `%unbound.addr.49%` in `stage2.ll`: `(strview-from-cstr (as CStr slot))` where `slot` had become an `fstr` result, and an `fstr` buffer is not NUL-terminated, so `strlen` ran into the next arena allocation. The third changed the language.

  **`(= sv null)` is a compile error now (gap #39).** A `StrView` operand against the `null` literal fell into the `CStr` `strcmp` lowering and compared its `.data` word against `NULL`. W5c had closed exactly this trap for `CStr` and deliberately left the view case alone, reasoning that a 16-byte struct can never be null — right about the type, wrong about the consequence: being unable to *be* null did not stop `(!= (sym 'ir-name) null)` from being *written*, and retyping the field kept it compiling. `--strict-cstr` cannot see it, because the borrow is inside the operator's own lowering rather than at a `CStr` parameter. Rejected outright, with `str-empty?` named in the diagnostic and a regression test. Every REPL test failed on it and nothing else did — the case for `make test` sitting in the gate set beside the bootstrap and the snapshot, which both passed while the REPL was dead.

  **C7-5 done (2026-09-02) — `sym-of`, `sym-ptr` and `intern-str` are deleted, and the producers went with them.** Rather than convert 88 call sites one at a time, the two functions that *make* the compiler's minted names now mint `Symbol`s: `c-read-ident` (38 sites, the whole C header lexer) and `expand-ref-sigil` (the reader's `&T` expansion). The anonymous struct/union synth names, the vtable and `BoxedFn` memo keys, `RMacro.wrap-sym`, `FnAttrEntry.attr`, `path-import-default-prefix` and `legacy-marker-tail` followed. Picking `Symbol` over `StrView` for `c-read-ident` is what made it mechanical: a `StrView` **borrows silently** to the `ptr`/`CStr` those sites were feeding, so `strlen(view)` would have compiled and mis-run; a `Symbol` is a struct with no such borrow, so every misuse was a named type error. Census **1,351 → 678**, borrow count 0, 948 tests, `make bootstrap` byte-identical.

  **A string literal inside a collection literal is a `StrView` now (gaps #40, #41).** `lit-type-node` gave kind 3 the element type `CStr` — the one place in the language where `"…"` was not a view — and its own comment recorded the deferral. C7-5 forced the issue: `(contains? #{"const" …} (symbol-as-view tok))` is a view argument to a `HashSet CStr`, which is a silent borrow *inside the container's monomorphized `hash`/`=`*, where the census cannot see it. Flipping kind 3 to `StrView` then exposed #41: `StrView`'s `Hash` conformance lived in `lib/strview.nuc`, so a string-keyed container needed an import a `CStr`-keyed one did not, and a REPL session with only `(import-use hashset)` failed with *no matching method for overloaded 'hash'*. It sits beside `CStr`'s in `lib/hash.nuc` now, on `fnv1a-bytes` directly. `Eq` deliberately stayed in `lib/strview.nuc`: `(extend CStr Eq)` is code-free because the builtin `=` satisfies it, but a struct type conforms only through real methods and `StrView`'s need `strview-eq`, which `numeric.nuc` cannot reach without a cycle. **No bootstrap shim was needed for a language-level element-type change:** the five `#{…}`/`{…}` tables in the compiler bind through an explicitly typed local, which is the shape a `want` arms, so the same source compiles under both the old boot compiler and the new one.

  **Snapshot re-baselined (2026-09-02), reason: gaps #37–#41 and the C7-5 library moves.** 111 artifacts differ, and every changed line is attributable: the `@symbol-byte-at` and `@symbol-none` definitions added to each module that reaches `lib/intern.nuc`; `hash.pStrView` moving from a `strview-hash` call to an inlined `fnv1a-bytes` and appearing in the units `lib/hash.nuc` reaches; `%HashSet.cstr`/`%HashMap.cstr.i32` instantiations becoming `StrView` ones in the three literal fixtures; and the `hash` declaration moving between `lib/strview.nuch`/`.h` and `lib/hash.nuch`/`.h`. **No `.err` artifact changed** — not one diagnostic moved.

  **`COMPILER_DEPS` is now `$(wildcard src/*.nuc) $(wildcard lib/*.nuc)`.** The hand-maintained closure had been wrong three times — `src/reader.nuc`, then the eight collection modules at stage14 LW-5, now the string stack — and this time the symptom was `make bootstrap` diffing a **stale stage1 against a fresh stage2** and reporting a divergence that was really the staleness. Over-approximating costs a rebuild measured in seconds.

  **What is left of stdio in `src/`, after C2–C5:** two `fwrite`s (the `FILE*` half of `emit-flush`/`emit-all`, still needed while `stdout` and `stderr` are sinks), one `fread` (the `popen("clang -E …")` pipe, a declared §2.7 seam), two `fflush (as ptr stdout)` and one `setvbuf` (ordering the *JIT'd program's* stdio, not the compiler's), two `perror`s, and 9 `snprintf`s — the two the REPL's float printing keeps permanently (§2.6), five REPL diagnostic buffers (C5 territory), and `cheader.nuc`'s two `popen` command lines. None remain in `nucleusc.nuc` or `generics.nuc`. Both variadic `callee-sig` builders went in C6 step 3. No `printf`, `fprintf`, `sprintf`, `fputs`, `fputc`, `fgets`, `fopen`, `fclose` or `open_memstream` remains.

  **C8 done (2026-09-02) — `--strict-cstr`'s census 678 → 63 emitted sites, then the tool was deleted and the tripwire that replaces it is hard.** `scripts/check-cstr.py` counts `CStr` plus 27 libc string/stdio entry points per file in `src/` and fails on any difference from `scripts/cstr-allowlist.txt` **in either direction**; it runs as the `cstr-residue` unit of `make test` (949 tests). Both directions is the half the spec did not state and the half that matters over time — a one-directional check lets the list keep asserting a seam that no longer exists, and the next reader trusts it. The allowlist is **3 entries / 22 sites**, each audited against overview.md §2.7: `opendir`'s path and `readdir`'s `d_name`, `getenv`, `argv`, five LLVM-C message strings, two `symbol-from-cstr-unchecked` casts, and one `(= hp 'strdup)` that matches a *name* rather than calling the function.

  Deleted with the phase: `--strict-cstr` and `src/strict-cstr.nuc` (the allowlist supersedes the scaffold — its job was counting a *synthesized* `CStr` no source file shows, and that job is over), `scripts/stage17/cstr-seams.txt`, the `make strict-cstr` target, `fcstr`, and the `ptr` `ToStr` conformance. **Deleting the `ptr` conformance is what found the tail of the census**, and it is the transferable move: with `(to-str p out)` gone, every remaining `ptr`-as-string local had to declare what it was, and the compiler named all twelve one build at a time — `lex-char-literal`'s diagnostic, `read-lit-elems`'s `what`, `g-fn-sret-name`, `g-cur-fname`, `proto-sigs-resolve`'s notes, `CDeclSite.file`, the REPL's float buffer, `check-ir-name-legal`'s hand-built one-byte C string. Grep could not have produced that list.

  **Snapshot re-baselined twice (2026-09-02).** *(1)* Reason: `symbol-from-cstr-unchecked` (a new library function — `unsafe/cast` cannot make a struct from a pointer, so the inverse of `symbol-as-cstr` had to exist). 28 artifacts, verified **purely additive** by a diff script before re-taking: 0 removals, 0 changed lines, only the new definition appearing in each module that reaches `lib/intern.nuc`. *(2)* Reason: gap #42, the literal-join fix in `join-strlit-branch`. 4 artifacts, every changed line attributed — an `insertvalue %StrView` pair per arm, `phi ptr` → `phi %StrView`, one `extractvalue` at each ptr-consuming use, and register renumbering downstream.

  **Gap #42 — `(if c "a" "b")` was a `CStr`.** The join materialized a literal arm only when the target was an *armed* `StrView`; everywhere else both arms collapsed to bare pointers. It was invisible because a literal's rodata *is* NUL-terminated, so every consumer worked — the damage was to the idiom, since a `ptr`-typed local became the natural spelling for a two-literal `cond`, and each one is a site a later `fstr` arm would break silently. Second time in the stage (with #32's `emit-return`) that a coercion had two paths through it and only one was armed.

  **Found, not fixed: `doc`, `apropos` and `kind-of` are documented and do not exist.** `docs/toplevel.md:5`, `docs/compiler.md:320`, `docs/builtins.md:60` and `docs/emacs.md:86` describe them; nothing in the tree implements them, and the `docstring` fields on `Sym` and `MacroDef` are dead storage. Retyped to `Symbol` with honest comments rather than deleted — building the feature or cutting the docs is a decision outside this stage. **Resolved by Stage 18** (2026-09-03), which found the cause and restored the whole layer.

## Stage 21 opened — pointer-kind spellings designed (2026-09-16)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) takes up the deferred item "make the sugar general, or ban the ambiguous half" on the general side. Ground truth is a 437-row spelling × position matrix run against `build/nucleusc`: every atom form and every `&`/`ptr:`/`raw:`-led paren form works in all 23 positions; `?(Vector i32)`, `!(…)`, `?!(…)` and `&?(…)` fail in 21 of them because the fuse keys on a trailing `:` and the atom `x:?` has none. Two further defects came out of the probe pass and are in scope: `--emit-nuch` prints `addr-of` from every emitter (H4), and `tyname-resolvable` never strips `?`/`!`, so a sigil over a concrete type in a generic pattern is collected as a type variable and `(Vector i32)` is accepted where `(Vector ?Pt)` was declared (H5). Nothing built; the candidate rough edges the pass found are listed in [stage21-cleanup/overview.md](stage21-cleanup/overview.md).

---

## Stage 21 item 2 — one reader, designed (2026-09-16), built (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md](stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md) answers "there are two readers": it is not necessary. `src/reader.nuc` (1,260 lines) was kept apart from `lib/read.nuc` (800) by a structural habit — reader state in five compiler globals, saved and restored by hand at every nested read — and by two grounds the Stage 18 TF-3 ruling gave that are stale: collection-literal type inference left the reader in Stage 16 (only a post-order gensym remains), and `def-rmacro`, the other exclusion, never affected the file that wrote it — because the emitter registers it after the whole file is read; a first draft retired the form, the design now registers it in the reader as it is read (file-scoped, forward-only; session-scoped at the REPL), which makes it work for the first time. The apparent limitation — `Err` carries no payload, so a located reader message cannot ride `!T` — dissolved on probing: a custom `E` in `(Result T E)` target-types with bare `err`/`ok`, `match` binds it, and `try` propagates it into a `!T` caller through a `defcast E Err`, today; so the reader returns a typed `ReadError` value (`code line msg note`, arena-owned) and the side-field draft is recorded in one-reader.md §2.1 as the rejected option beside a fatter `Err`. Plan: R-1 grows the library reader to the compiler's feature set (collection literals with no gensym, W4c tracking, the error record, no string-literal cap; `def-rmacro` registered at read time), R-2 adopts it through one shim that renders the record via `report-at` and mints the gensyms in the reader's own order — every emitted `.ll` byte-identical, no boot refresh — and deletes `src/reader.nuc` (diagnostics move to `src/diagnostics.nuc`), R-3 folds the printer, R-4 replaces the four parity units with a read → print → read round-trip. Sequenced between item 1's PK-1 and PK-2 so PK-2's fuse change is written once. Brief premises corrected while verifying: the IR snapshot never held `build/nucleusc.ll` and three importers of `lib/read.nuc` will move with it; `docs/errors.md`'s "use `make` for a custom `E`" is stale and the two-tier rule (`Err` codes, `(Result T E)` payloads, `defcast` the bridge) was undocumented. **Built 2026-09-18, R-1…R-4** — one correction from the plan (the error value shipped as a hand-written `ReadResult` union, not a `(Result raw:Node ReadError)` instance, because template stamping loses pointer kind) and two pre-existing compiler bugs fixed along the way; gates, snapshot numbers and the fixes are in the "Known constraints / gotchas" entry below.

---

## Stage 21 item 1 — PK-1, `&` is `ref` in both worlds, built (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §3, adapted to the one-reader tree item 2 left: the `&` row of `read-macro-table-new` (`lib/read.nuc`) now wraps in `ref`, and there is no twin table to mirror (`build-rmacros`, `rd-macro-name` and the `reader-parity-*` units the design names are gone). So `(sizeof &Pt)`, `(Vector &Pt)`, `(deftype P &Pt)`, a `defprotocol`'s `&Self` and a list-form `(link &Pt)` all reach the parser as the canonical `(ref Pt)` — the node the type path already meant — and value-position `&x` / `(ref x)` and `(ref p 'f)` are the address-of forms, typed `(ref (type-of x))`. `addr-of` stays accepted in the value world until PK-5; it is no longer accepted anywhere in a type slot.

  **Reading sites given a `ref` arm** (`addr-of` kept beside each): `emit-list` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`, → `emit-addr-of`) and its lockstep twin `node-type` (`src/generics.nuc`, → `node-type-addr-of`); `fn-rewrite-captures` (a captured `&local` in a closure body now reads as `(ref local)` — the one arm without which `make bootstrap` diverges); `defvar-init-ir` (`(defvar q:&Pt &g)` → `defvar-addr-of-ir`); `defvar-check-init-order` (an address is not a read); `gcheck-special-form` (for `valid-walk`; inert on the tree's two `Valid` bodies in `examples/valid.nuc`, and a strict improvement for a `(ref x)` in one — the old path died as an unresolvable call); `macrolet-bind` (refuses a binding named by `pointer-kind-named`, since the macro table is consulted before the special-form chain — `macrolet: 'ref' is a pointer kind and may not be shadowed`); `retired-form-message` (`.&` now names `(ref p 'field)`). **One site §3 did not list:** `emit-callable-value`'s Stage 16 A3 guard, which reads a lone `(ref …)` argument to a callable *value* as the fused `q:(ref T)` annotation shape and refuses it — `(cb &n)` on a function-pointer, closure or `Vector` local now reads as `(cb (ref n))` and reached that refusal. `ref-cell-is-address` (beside it) discriminates: a 2-argument form, or a symbol operand bound in scope, or one that names no type, is a value address and passes through; a type operand keeps the A3 diagnostic (`as-sugar-paren` still pins it). The four cell-**building** sites (`fn-rewrite-captures`' rewrite, `fn-make-drop-method`, `fn-emit-env-value` ×2) keep building `addr-of`, which is what keeps the bootstrap byte-identical. Type-path `addr-of` arms removed: `parse-type-from-node` (`src/union-registry.nuc`), `node-is-ptr-wrapper` (`src/generics.nuc`), and `type-node-to-c` / `cheader-niche-no-c` / the vfn-env probe / `cheader-defvar-type-ok` (`src/cheader.nuc`) — a literal `(addr-of T)` in a type slot now falls to the ordinary `unknown type: addr-of` (grep confirmed none in `src/`, `lib/*.nuch` or `examples/` before removing them).

  **Gates.** `make bootstrap` **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll` with no boot refresh** — the §9 step-1 fixed-point argument held (`&x` emits identically under either head; every reading site matched). `scripts/check-headers.sh --fix` regenerated **3** headers, **11 lines, every one exactly `addr-of` → `ref`** (`lib/combinators.nuch` 2: `flat-map`, `join`; `lib/hashmap.nuch` 5: `hashmap-resize`, `assoc`, `dissoc`, `get`, `conj`; `lib/hashset.nuch` 4: `hashset-resize`, `insert`, `contains?`, `set-remove`) — the `&k` / `&out` / `&inner` spellings in verbatim-exported template bodies; `check-headers` clean after. `grep -o addr-of lib/*.nuch | wc -l` = **25**, all written-out: 23 two-argument `(addr-of s 'alloc)`-style (`hashset` 11, `hashmap` 5, `iterator` 4, `vector` 3) and 2 `(addr-of (unquote it-sym))` in `lib/macros.nuch`. `make test` **1049/0/0** (was 1048): `s21-pk1-ref-head` in `tests/suite-s21.nuc` over the new fixture `tests/fixtures/s21-pk1-ref-head.nuc` (every §3 position, both value arities, `(cb &n)` on a fn-pointer local — the pre-PK-1 compiler refuses the fixture at its `(ref a)` with `unknown: ref`), a lib-shaped `--emit-nuch` whose macro body `&k`, protocol `&Self` and `(Vector &Pt)` all print as `ref` with no `addr-of`, and the `macrolet` refusal; `s16-dot-forms-retired`'s pinned `.&` message updated. No REPL transcript pinned the reader's `addr-of` output, so none moved.

  **`ir-snapshot.sh verify`: 14 of 2,720 artifacts differ, none by an instruction.** 9 `.ll` artifacts — `lib/read.nuc` (host + both Windows variants), `lib/test.nuc` (host + both), `examples/read-sexp.nuc`, `examples/self-test.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/s21-read-error-value.nuc` — each differ by exactly three lines: the `@.str.N` constant `c"addr-of\00"` → `c"ref\00"`, its `[8 x i8]` → `[4 x i8]` GEP and the `insertvalue %StrView … i64 7` → `i64 3` length. That is `read-macro-table-new`'s own literal, compiled into every program that imports the reader; **§9/§10's "no `.ll` artifact differs" did not account for the reader being a library**. 5 `.nuch` artifacts (`examples/assoc-iter-return` 1 line, `examples/ref-sigil` 3, `lib/combinators` 2, `lib/hashmap` 5, `lib/hashset` 4) differ only by `addr-of` → `ref` (residual after normalising: 0). Re-taken **440 inputs, 2,726 artifacts** (the fixture adds 6). **`dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: 80 of 457 inputs differ, `.out` only** — no `.err` or `.rc` moved — and every one of the 80 contains a token-boundary `&`; 75 are exactly `addr-of` → `ref` under normalisation, the 5 with a residual are precisely the 5 source files this milestone edited (`lib/read.nuc`, `src/cheader.nuc`, `src/generics.nuc`, `src/nucleusc.nuc`, `src/union-registry.nuc`), whose text changed. Spot checks: `examples/ref-sigil.nuc` 9 `addr-of` → 0; `lib/hashset.nuc` 15 → 11 (the written-out ones); `src/generics.nuc` 61 → 8. Re-taken **458 inputs, 6 rejected, 1,374 artifacts**. Both `verify` PASS after the re-take.

  **Docs and context.** `docs/reading.md`, `docs/toplevel.md`, `docs/builtins.md` (reader-macro lists and the `addr-of` row, now `ref` / `addr-of`), `docs/special-forms.md` (same row, `.&` row), `docs/types.md` (the "`(addr-of T)` is a legal, if strange, way to spell `(ref T)`" paragraph replaced by the one-node rule); `context/conventions.md`'s "A standalone `&T` in a type slot" section rewritten to its `(ref T)` successor, the value-sugar paragraph now names the reach list, and "Prefer the `&` spellings" spells `(ref x)`; `examples/ref-sigil.nuc`'s header comment and `s16-ref-sigil-both-meanings`' comment corrected. Not touched: a pre-existing limitation the fixture met — a 2-element symbol-headed cell in a struct-literal slot is always a designated initializer, so `(Holder &a 7)` was refused as `no field 'addr-of'` before and is refused as `no field 'ref'` now (bind the address first); already on the candidate rough-edge list in [stage21-cleanup/overview.md](stage21-cleanup/overview.md), which predicted exactly this.

## Stage 21 item 1 — PK-2, the fuse gates on an open segment and fires per atom, built (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §4, made **once** in `lib/read.nuc` — the only reader since item 2 — so every `src/reader.nuc` twin the design names is moot. Three changes, all in the reader: (1) **gate** — `rd-open-segment?` runs `rd-seg-next` (the `&`-sigil state machine) over the atom's spelling from its initial open state and the fuse fires when the final segment is still open (`:` opens, `?`/`!` keep the state, anything else closes), replacing the "ends in `:`" suffix test, so `x:`, `?`, `?!`, `x:?`, `ptr:`, `x:ref:?`, the lone `:` and a keyword body `?` fuse before an adjacent `(` and `foo?`, `push!`, `!=`, `x:foo?` do not; the CP-2a lone-colon case now tests the spelling `":"` rather than length 1, since `?` and `!` are also open one-byte symbols; (2) **split** — one trailing `:` is stripped if present and the body split on `:`, so a trailing sigil run is a segment of its own (`x:?` → `x`, `?`); (3) **placement** — the one call moved from `rd-list` into `rd-form` (`rd-atom-form`, after every atom `rd-form` produces, the legacy-marker path included), passing the token's own line. The §4 table holds row for row: `?(V)` → `(? (V))`, `x:?(V)` → `(x (? (V)))`, `x:!(V)` → `(x (! (V)))`, `?!(V)` → `(?! (V))`, `):?(V)` → `(? (V))`, `x:&?(V)` → `(x (ref (? (V))))`, `x:?&(V)` → `(x (?ref (V)))` unchanged, `x:(V)` / `x:ref:(V)` / `:(V)` and both function-pointer groups unchanged; and the three consequences of the move: a reader-macro operand fuses (`&?(V)` → `(ref (? (V)))`, `&ptr:(V)` → `(ref (ptr (V)))`, `'x:(T)` → `(quote (x (T)))`, `~x:(T)` → `(unquote (x (T)))`), a literal element fuses (`[x:(T)]` → `(vector-lit (x (T)))` — `s21-def-rmacro`'s `[a:(T) b]` row, which pinned the old no-fuse reading, now expects `((vector-lit (a (T)) b))`), and a top-level `foo:(bar)` is one form (the pre-PK-2 reader gave a bare symbol then a list). The reader stays type-ignorant: the type parser still reports a bare-sigil head as `unknown type:` with an empty name until PK-3 gives `(? X)` its meaning — `docs/types.md` says so.

  **Gates.** `make bootstrap` **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll` with no boot refresh** (`src/` uses none of the new readings). `make test` **1050/0/0** (was 1049): `s21-pk2-fuse-open-segment` in `tests/suite-s21.nuc` — the fixture `tests/fixtures/s21-pk2-fuse-open-segment.nuc` reads every §4 row, the three consequences, the four closed-segment non-fusers and `&rest(x)` through `read-all` + `node-str` (golden), and a written file through the compiler's own `--dump-ast` (a sigil-paren parameter, a `):?(…)` return, `(as &?(…) x)`, `~x:(T)` in a quasiquote, `[x:(T)]`, top-level `foo:(bar)`, `foo?(x) push!(x)`). `make check-headers` clean after `check-headers.sh --fix` regenerated `lib/read.nuch` / `lib/read.h` for the two new declares (`rd-open-segment?`, `rd-atom-form`) — the only header lines that moved. **`dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: 1 of 1,374 artifacts differed, `lib/read.nuc.out` — the edited source's own AST**; no other input's `.out`, `.err` or `.rc` moved, which is §4's "zero in-tree spellings change meaning" proved over 458 inputs (the §1.5 census held: the only `?(`/`!(` before a paren in the tree is a comment in `examples/comb-storage.nuc`, and every `'x:(`/`~x:(`/`&x:(` hit is inside a comment). Re-taken **459 inputs, 6 rejected, 1,377 artifacts**. **`ir-snapshot.sh verify`: 11 of 2,726 artifacts differed, every one an artifact that compiles `lib/read.nuc` into itself or its header**: 9 `.ll` — `lib/read.nuc` (host + both Windows), `lib/test.nuc` (host + both), `examples/read-sexp.nuc`, `examples/self-test.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/s21-read-error-value.nuc` — each differing, function by function under SSA/`@.str` normalisation, in exactly `rd-form`, `rd-fuse-colon-paren`, `rd-list` and the two new functions, nothing else; plus `lib_read.nuc.nuch` and `lib_read.nuc.h`, the two new declares. No other `.nuch`. Re-taken **441 inputs, 2,732 artifacts** (the fixture adds 6). Both `verify` PASS after the re-take.

  **Docs and context.** `docs/types.md` §Type Syntax: the open-segment rule replaces "the colon is the last character of the atom", a "fires per atom" paragraph (reader-macro operands, literal elements, top-level; the node carries the atom's own line), a "sigil-paren forms" paragraph, the lone-colon paragraph gains `:?(…)`, the quoted-data caveat gains `'foo:(bar)`, and the function-pointer section's "trailing-colon name" is now "open-segment name"; `docs/reading.md`'s type-sugar row states the rule; `docs/macros.md`'s `(fst ~a)` note says what `~name:(T)` now reads as and why the list form stays the template idiom; `context/macros-jit.md`'s `~sym:type` note gains the `~sym:(Type)` reading; `context/conventions.md`'s function-pointer and lexer-sugar sections name the new gate. Nothing in the design turned out wrong for this milestone beyond what the brief already superseded (the `src/reader.nuc` twins and `g-peek-valid` no longer exist).
