## Stage 21 item 1 — PK-3 and PK-4a, the bare-sigil head is a type and a sigil over a concrete type is concrete, built (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §5 and §6a. **PK-3**, in `parse-type-from-node` (`src/union-registry.nuc`): the sigil-head arm now asks whether the head is *only* sigils (`type-sigil-run`, the new leading-`?`/`!` counter). If so it is the list form PK-2's reader gives `?(Vector i32)` — `(? X)` — and takes exactly one operand, parsed as the sole element (`'?' takes one type -- (? T)` otherwise, naming the head as written: `(?! A B)` says `'?!'`); a glued head (`?ref`, `!ptr`) rebuilds `(rest-head . cdr)` as before. `(?! X)` reaches `X` by recursion, `(?! X)` → `(! X)` → `X`, which composes the same value-Maybe-over-Result as `resolve-type-name`'s `?!` arm. **The near-miss** — `? (Vector i32)` with a space is a bare `?` atom and a dangling list — is `reject-bare-sigil`, called at the top of `resolve-type-name` (`a type sigil must be attached to its type -- write ?T or ?(T ...) with no space`, the sigil as written) and from the PK-4a refusal below so `(Vector ? (V))` in a generic pattern says the right thing. **The line-0 discipline reached more than `emit-alloca-form`**: `emit-as`, `emit-cast`, `emit-sizeof` and `emit-array-lit` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) and the `node-type-as` / `node-type-cast` / `node-type-alloca` mirrors (`src/generics.nuc`) all passed the type operand's own `line`, which for an interned symbol is 0 — `(as Nope 1)` reported `:0:` on the pre-change binary too, so §5's "at `alloca`" was one face of a class; every one is now `(node-line tn (cc 'line))`. The cheader source-node walkers needed nothing (confirmed: `cheader-mentions-closure` and `cheader-niche-no-c` return 0 for any head that is not `ptr`/`ref`, so a `(? X)` cell is a `(?ptr X)` cell to them). **PK-4a**, in `src/generics.nuc`: `tyname-resolvable` peels the leading sigil run and re-enters itself first (`?ref:Pt` → `ref:Pt` → `Pt`), so `?Pt`, `!i32`, `?&Pt` are concrete iff their operand is; `unify-tpat`'s NODE-SYM arm then parses them concretely with no change of its own; and `collect-pattern-tyvars` refuses a sigil over an *unresolvable* name rather than collecting it under a spelling the unifier never binds (`Vector: '?T' -- a type sigil over a type variable is not supported in a generic pattern yet; write the concrete type`, at the argument's line). `value-annot-type` closes with the same predicate: `q:?&Pt` in value position now casts as `(as ?&Pt q)` does — the two `define`s are body-identical. The tree needed no adaptation: no generic pattern in `src/`, `lib/`, `examples/` or `tests/` relied on `?T`/`!T` being a tyvar (grep, and `make test` after).

  **H5 on the pre-change binary.** `build/nucleusc` at `4547612` (kept as `nucleusc-pre`) compiles `tests/fixtures/s21-pk4a-h5.nuc` — `(defn f-qpt ((v (ref (Vector ?Pt)))):i32 …)` called with a `(Vector i32)` — cleanly, emits `define weak_odr i32 @f_qpt.pVector.i32(ptr %v.arg)` and the program prints `1`; the new binary refuses line 15 with `f-qpt: argument 1 has type ptr:Vector.i32, which does not match parameter type ptr:Vector.Maybe.Pt`. All four new units fail on the pre-change binary (run against it by swapping the binary under `build/nuctests`): the PK-3 fixture at its line 19 `unknown type:  —` (empty name), the diagnostics unit with the old empty-name message, the value-annotation unit with `unknown type '?ref:Pt' in the annotation 'q:?ref:Pt'`.

  **Gates.** `make bootstrap` **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll` with no boot refresh**. `make test` **1054/0/0** (was 1050): in `tests/suite-s21.nuc`, `s21-pk3-sigil-paren-types` (the fixture `tests/fixtures/s21-pk3-sigil-paren-types.nuc` — `?(V)`/`!(V)`/`?!(V)`, `x:?(V)`, `(x (? (V)))`, `):?(V)`, `&?(V)`, `?&Pt`, `(?ref Pt)`, in param, return, let, field, deftype, defvar, sizeof, alloca, as, a protocol signature, a lambda and a template argument, golden; then a lib-shaped `--emit-nuch` whose `defstruct`, `deftype`, `defprotocol` and `declare`s print `(? (Vector i32))`, `(! (Vector i32))`, `(?! (Vector i32))`, `(?ref Pt)`, re-imported from a `test-scratch-sub` header directory by a separately compiled consumer that links and runs — the §5 round-trip property), `s21-pk3-diagnostics` (the near-miss at `defn`, `defstruct`, `defvar`, `alloca`, `sizeof`, `as`, for `?`, `!` and `?!`, every one at line 3; the four arity refusals), `s21-pk4a-h5` (the fixture above refused; `tests/fixtures/s21-pk4a-sigil-concrete.nuc` — `(Vector ?Pt)`, `(Vector ?&Pt)`, `(Vector !i32)` declared and passed, golden; `(Vector ?T)` and `(Vector !T)` refused by the PK-4a message), `s21-value-annotation` (body-identical IR and a golden run). `make check-headers` clean (nothing in `lib/` moved). **`dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: 3 of 1,377 artifacts differed, all `.out` of the three edited sources** (`src/generics.nuc`, `src/nucleusc.nuc`, `src/union-registry.nuc` — their text changed), plus 9 NEW for the three fixtures; no other input moved. Re-taken **462 inputs, 6 rejected, 1,386 artifacts**. **`ir-snapshot.sh verify`: `PASS` over 2,732 artifacts — nothing differs** (PK-3/PK-4a change what is *accepted*; no in-tree program's IR, header or `.nuch` moved). Re-taken **444 inputs, 2,750 artifacts** (the three fixtures add 18). Both `verify` PASS after the re-take.

  **Docs.** `docs/types.md` §Type Syntax: the sigil-paren paragraph no longer says the parser rejects `(? X)` — it is the canonical list form, one operand, every position, printed by `--emit-nuch` — and a "sigil near-miss" paragraph; §Pointer kinds' uniform-`?` paragraph points at the paren spelling. `docs/generics.md` §Bounded generic `defn`: "a sigil over a concrete type is concrete" (H5 closed) and the PK-4b refusal. `docs/errors.md` §`!T`: the `!(T …)` row, and the "no sugar; write longhand" sentence replaced.

  **Two things the design did not say.** (1) The line-0 defect is a class, not `emit-alloca-form` (above). (2) `tyname-resolvable` mirrors `parse-type-name`'s acceptance set **without** the `deftype` alias and C-typedef arms, so `(deftype PtRef &Pt)` followed by `(defn f ((v (ref (Vector PtRef)))) …)` is H5 in another costume — probed on both binaries: `f` is silently a template, `(Vector i32)` is accepted, `f_alias.pVector.i32` stamps. Same shape for `q:PtRef` in value position (`value-annot-type`). Out of PK-4a's scope (the design's §1.4 lists what is treated as concrete and aliases are not on it); recorded in [stage21-cleanup/overview.md](stage21-cleanup/overview.md)'s rough-edge list.

## Boot refresh for Stage 21 — the gensym walk deleted, then `make update-bootstrap` (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §9 step 2, and the last step of [stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md](stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md) §4f. Two things, in order. **(1)** `mint-collection-gensyms` — R-2's post-order walk over every freshly read buffer, inserting a `nucleus_gensym` node as the second element of each `vector-lit`/`hashmap-lit`/`hashset-lit` cell in the order the retired `src/reader.nuc` had minted them, so that R could land with no refresh — is deleted from `src/nucleusc.nuc` along with its call in `read-source-or-report`; `emit-collection-lit` now takes the element list straight off the cell's `cdr` and mints the hygiene symbol itself, in the `let` that builds the expansion body, so a literal's `__gs_N` is numbered in **emission** order. Nothing else read the gensym element: `node-type-call`'s three `*-lit` arms are the unmodelled `return null`, `--emit-nuch` prints macro bodies verbatim but no committed `.nuch` contains a literal, and `lib/read.nuc` never minted one. `--dump-ast` now prints the reader's tree with no `__gs_N`, which is the intended baseline; the one pinned spelling, `s21-pk2-fuse-open-segment`'s `(vector-lit __gs_0 (x (T)))` in `tests/suite-s21.nuc`, became `(vector-lit (x (T)))`. **(2)** the boot refresh — the first since Stage 20 S2 (`b57e286`), so it also absorbs the six fixed-point-preserving commits between (S4, the one-reader move, PK-1, PK-2, PK-3/PK-4a) and is what lets PK-5 rewrite `src/` to `(ref x)` / `(ref p 'f)` / `?(…)`, spellings the refreshed boot now reads.

  **The pre-refresh gate is Stage 20 S1's, because the fixed point cannot hold across the deletion** — the committed boot mints at read time, the new compiler at emit time. `make bootstrap` failed as expected, and the failure was proved to be numbering alone: `diff build/nucleusc.ll build/stage2.ll` is **41,172 lines** (20,586 changed pairs, every one a `%__gs_N…` operand); after `sed -E 's/__gs_[0-9]+/__gs_N/g'` over both, **0 lines**; 3,479 distinct gensyms on each side. The direct self-consistency check from `context/build.md`: `src/nucleusc.nuc` compiled by `build/nucleusc` into `stage2.ll`, linked, recompiled by that binary into a stage 3 — **byte-identical, 13,560,457 bytes both**. `make test` **1054/0/0** (unchanged; the one pin moved with the change). **`ir-snapshot.sh verify`: PASS, 0 of 2,750 artifacts differ** — stronger than the design expected, and for a checkable reason: read-time post-order and emit-time order coincide unless a literal is nested in another literal, a gensym-minting macro expands before one in the same compilation, or a template body containing one is stamped twice, and no program in `tests/fixtures`, `examples` or `lib` has any of the three (the seven `*-lit` examples use only literals, in source order; no prelude macro calls `gensym`). That the new binary *does* mint at emit time is shown by a probe — a `gensym`-using macro called before a `[1 2 3]` — which the old binary numbers literal-first (`__gs_0` the literal, `__gs_1` the macro's) and the new one macro-first, byte-identical after normalisation. `build/nucleusc.ll` moved for the same reason the compiler's own numbering did: `src/` has three files with literals (`cheader`, `generics`, `nucleusc`) and macros minting between them. **`dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: 10 of 1,386 artifacts differ, all `.out`**, exactly the ten inputs whose snapshot carried a `__gs_N` element — `examples/{as-sugar,hashmap-lit-test,hashset-lit-test,keyword-lit-test,selector-value,symbol-keys-test,vector-lit-test}.nuc`, `src/cheader.nuc`, `src/generics.nuc`, `src/nucleusc.nuc` — 33 elements removed in all (2/2/4/4/2/4/3/6/2/4); with ` __gs_N` deleted from the snapshot text, nine diff by **0 lines** and `src/nucleusc.nuc` by exactly its three edited forms (`emit-collection-lit`, the deleted `mint-collection-gensyms`, `read-source-or-report`). One-reader.md §4f's "ten collection-literal files" was the right count.

  **The refresh.** `make update-bootstrap` (rewrites `boot/nucleusc.ll`, installs `bin/nucleusc`, runs `make windows-boot` for both `boot/nucleusc-x86_64-windows-{gnu,msvc}.ll` — the six host-header warnings per Windows target are the documented `--sysroot` fallback), then `make clean && make && make bootstrap` → **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll` / `PASS: bootstrap complete`** first time; `make test` **1054/0/0**; `scripts/check-headers.sh` **87 headers, all match** (nothing in `lib/` moved, no `--fix`). Both snapshots re-taken on the refreshed compiler: `ir-snapshot.sh snapshot --force` → **444 inputs, 2,750 artifacts**; `dump-ast-corpus.sh snapshot --force` → **462 inputs (6 rejected), 1,386 artifacts**; both `verify` PASS. The refreshed `build/nucleusc.ll` (13,560,457 bytes, `== build/stage2.ll`) is kept as the step-2 baseline PK-5a's "byte-identical against the step-2 build" gate compares against. `git diff --stat boot/`: each of the three boot IRs **108,240 lines changed** (163,866 insertions, 160,854 deletions over the three) — the accumulated source drift of the six unrefreshed commits plus the renumbering. Accounting for this step alone: HEAD's source through the old boot against the new `boot/nucleusc.ll`, gensym-normalised, leaves 3,166 lines, of which 2,010 name a `@.str.N` (the string-pool shift from the three head-name literals the deleted walk compared against) and 1,156 are the edited functions' own bodies and their `lit-*`/`make-cell` calls.

  **Docs.** `docs/compiler.md`'s `--dump-ast` row no longer says a literal's gensym is already present; `docs/reading.md`'s collection-literal row was already written for this state. `context/conventions.md`'s one-reader section says where the gensym is minted now.

## Stage 21 item 1 — PK-5b, the retirement: the compiler stops reading `addr-of` (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §7, second half. The four cell-**building** sites (`fn-rewrite-captures`' capture rewrite, `fn-make-drop-method`, `fn-emit-env-value` ×2 — the `symbol-intern "addr-of"` sites conventions.md's synthesis-grep rule names) mint `ref`; the six reading arms §7 lists are gone (`emit-list`, `node-type`, `fn-rewrite-captures`, `defvar-init-ir`, `defvar-check-init-order`, `gcheck-special-form`), and `grep "ref" next to "addr-of"` found no seventh — `macrolet-bind` and `emit-callable-value`'s `q:(ref T)` guard only ever matched `ref`. `emit-addr-of` / `node-type-addr-of` / `defvar-addr-of-ir` are **renamed** `emit-ref` / `node-type-ref` / `defvar-ref-ir` (mechanical, 12 `src/` sites; the names appeared in no `docs/` file, in two `context/conventions.md` lines — updated — and in design history, left as written). Every diagnostic noun says `ref`: `emit-ref`'s four, `emit-field-addr`'s eight (`require-derefable` / `die-nonliteral-selector` / `union-field-guard` / `reject-anon-ambiguous` / `emit-lvalue-receiver` all take the noun), `defvar-ref-ir`'s six. `retired-form-message` gains `'addr-of' was retired: write &x, or (ref x) / (ref p 'field)` beside the `.&` row; `parse-type-from-node`'s fall-through consults it before `unknown-type-message`, so `(addr-of T)` in a type slot answers the same (and so, now, does `(.& T)` — it used to say `unknown type: .&`); `addr-of` stays in `build-special-form-set`, so `(defn addr-of …)` is still `already names a special form`. **One site §7 did not list:** a generic body reaches `gcheck` before `emit-list`, and `gcheck`'s unknown-function arm said `in generic body: unknown function 'addr-of'` — the same hole `.&` and `.` had there — so that arm consults `retired-form-message` first. Probed at every position: value, field, `defvar` initializer, `let` type slot, parameter type slot (all three emit modes), generic body, macro template, REPL.

  **`tests/`.** `scripts/stage21/sugar-sweep.py` gained `--rules R1,R2` (each rule and its rule-specific refusals gate on the set; the default is all eight; `--dry-run` over `src/ lib/ examples/` still reports 0 with every rule on). Over `tests/**/*.nuc` and `tests/repl/*.in`: **3,634 rewrites — R1 3,203, R2 431 — and 8 refusals, every one `(addr-of rest)`** (`&rest` reads as the legacy marker), hand-written `(ref rest)`, the shape R3 keeps for the same reason; a second pass reports 0. The script is string-aware by design, so the programs a test **embeds** kept the old spelling — a one-off string-literal pass (`&`/`(ref p 'f)` inside literals, innermost first, escaped-string-aware) rewrote **122** more across 10 suites and refused one, the arity probe `(addr-of p 'x 1)`, which is now `(ref p 'x 1)` → `ref expects 1 or 2 args`. Hand review: `s16-ref-sigil-addr-of-ir-identical` → **`s16-ref-sigil-ref-ir-identical`**, comparing `&a` against `(ref a)` (the sweep had made both sides `&a`, a test of nothing); `s16-addr-of-2arg` → **`s16-ref-2arg`**, `s16-addr-of-2arg-node-type` → **`s16-ref-2arg-node-type`** (content kept, spelled `(ref p 'f)`); five pinned messages re-spelled (`ref: no field`, `ref expects 1 or 2 args`, `ref: field name must be a quoted selector`, `defvar: ref: 'ga' has type`, `ref: the receiver is a temporary struct value`); `tests/manifest/diagnostics.sexp`'s `g1-addr-of-const` row → `defvar: ref: 'G1K' is a compile-time constant`. New unit **`s21-addr-of-retired`**: value, field, `defvar`, `let` type slot, generic body, zero-arg and a parameter type slot under `check-probe-refused` (all three modes — a body is beyond what the header modes resolve, as suite-refusals.nuc documents), plus the reservation. Test arithmetic: 1054 + 1 = **1055**, three units renamed in place. Residue under `tests/`: the word survives only as unit/fixture **names** (`g1-addr-of-const`, `g4-addr-of-forward`, `s17-rvalue-addr-of`, `g1-addr-of-cross-file` — 7 lines), in `s21-pk1-ref-head`'s `check-not-contains nv "addr-of"` (the header-printer pin) and one fixture comment about the past; the three fixture files and `examples/lvalue-addr-of.nuc` keep their names, since both snapshot gates key on the path.

  **Gates.** `make`; `make test` **`nuctests: 1055 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped`**; `make bootstrap` **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`**; `make check-headers` **87 headers, all match** (nothing in `lib/` moved — its edits are comments). The compiler's own `.ll` against PK-5a's, function by function with SSA names and `@.str.N` normalised: **3 functions renamed, 12 changed — exactly the edited set** (`parse-type-from-node`, `gcheck-special-form`, `gcheck`, `node-type`, `retired-form-message`, `fn-rewrite-captures`, `fn-make-drop-method`, `fn-emit-env-value`, `emit-field-addr`, `emit-list`, `defvar-init-ir`, `defvar-check-init-order`; the last three of the building/noun group are length-identical, string swaps only), **14 string constants removed, 15 added** (`addr-of` and the thirteen nouns out; `ref`, the retirement message and the thirteen re-spelled nouns in). `ir-snapshot.sh verify`: **1 of 2,750 artifacts differs — `tests_fixtures_g1-addr-of-const.nuc.ll.err`, `defvar: addr-of:` → `defvar: ref:`**; no `.ll`, `.nuch` or `.h` moved (a swept fixture's `&x` and `(addr-of x)` were one node, and `lib/read.nuc`'s string table did not change this time). `dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: **18 of 1,386 differ — the 3 edited `src/` files and 15 of the 16 edited fixtures** (`g4-laundered-call.nuc`'s edit is a comment); each fixture's diff is exactly `(addr-of` → `(ref ` (proved by normalising the old output and `cmp`). Both re-taken: `ir-snapshot.sh snapshot --force` **444 inputs, 2,750 artifacts**; `dump-ast-corpus.sh snapshot --force` **462 inputs (6 rejected), 1,386 artifacts**. `grep -rn "addr-of" lib/*.nuch` **0**. `grep -rn "(addr-of " docs context` → the retirement documentation only (`docs/special-forms.md`'s new `addr-of` row carries the message; `docs/types.md` and `docs/builtins.md` point at it).

  **Docs with the code (PK-6's noun sweep, brought forward because the compiler now refuses the noun).** 97 code-form rewrites across 18 `docs/*.md` (the same string pass), then the prose by hand: `docs/special-forms.md` (the `ref` row loses `/ addr-of`, gains the retirement row; the lifecycle section's taint source is `ref` (`&x`); the selector rule names `ref`), `docs/builtins.md` (same table), `docs/types.md` §Pointer kinds (the PK-1 parenthetical is now the retirement statement), `collections.md`/`macros.md`/`stdlib.md`/`structs-unions.md` prose. `context/conventions.md`: every current-rule mention (the lvalue reading sites, the arity-overload lockstep — now `node-type-ref` — SV-1, the member-form selector rule, the synthesis sites, "Prefer the `&` spellings", the sink/alias example, the `(ref T)` section) rewritten; history keeps the word. `context/build.md`, `context/macros-jit.md`, `context/repl.md` likewise. The 21 `lib/`/`examples/` files whose **comments** still described the idiom as `(addr-of it)` now say `&it` (33 lines; comments, so no header or IR moved).

## Stage 21 item 1 — PK-5a, the sweep: `addr-of` out of the tree, the sugar in (2026-09-18)

[stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md](stage21-cleanup/pointer-kind-spellings.md) §7, first half. `scripts/stage21/sugar-sweep.py` — one paren-aware, string/comment/char-literal-aware rewriter over the reader's grammar (reader macros, the PK-2 open-segment fuse including the two-group function-pointer type, legacy `&rest`-style markers, `c"…"`) applying R1–R8 in the table's order, refusing what it cannot classify, `--dry-run`, per-rule histogram, refusal list, idempotent. The table held as written; the slot classifier is a **role walk** (value / type / binder list / parameter list / union arm / protocol signature / pattern / return / quote / quasiquote / match arm / definer name / type list), because a `:where` clause, an attribute keyword at a binder slot (`(let (:volatile d:i32 …))`, `(defvar :const …)`), a `defunion`'s `(Maybe T)` *head* and the parameter group of `body:(fn void)(ptr)` all put a type — or a non-type — where "the second element of a binding pair" does not look; the first three drafts each refused or mis-rewrote one of those and were found by the build (`src/generics.nuc:5308 unknown type: ref` was the fn-pointer parameter group read as a binder list). R8 also attaches a return type after a parameter list (`(params) T` → `(params):T`), which the table did not say and the `.nuch` printer already does. Under `quasiquote` only R1/R2/R3/R5 fire (a template is data until spliced; its `(Maybe X)` is not a type slot — and a `defmacro` body is JIT-only, so nothing there reaches the program `.ll`); under `quote`, nothing. The two `ref:StrView` locals in `src/nucleusc.nuc` (`emit-quote`, `emit-qq-form`) were renamed `tree:StrView` by hand first, as §3 prescribed. Swept: `src/*.nuc`, `lib/**/*.nuc`, `examples/*.nuc` less the four canonical-spelling examples — 223 files read, **134 modified** (src 15, lib 34, examples 85) — to a fixed point: the second pass and every later `--dry-run` report **0 sites rewritten**.

  **Histogram (first pass, 4,985 rewrites).** R1 `(addr-of x)` → `&x` **119**; R2 `(addr-of p 'f)` → `(ref p 'f)` **154**; R3 `(ref X)` → `&X` **1,036**; R4 `(ptr X)` → `&X` **52**; R5 `ref:`/`ptr:`-led token → `&` **2,698**; R6 `(Maybe X)` → `?X` **59**; R7 `(Result X Err)` → `!X` **0** (the one candidate is refused below); R8 `(name T)` → `name:T` **817**, `(params) T` → `(params):T` **50**. Skipped by rule, not refused: 4 `(Result X E)` with `E` ≠ `Err`; 2 R5 tokens under `quote` / in a pattern / as a definer name. **Refused, 18**, in four classes the design's "a `(Maybe X)` the script cannot classify" did not name, each proved on the compiler before it became a rule: **(a) a sigil over a type variable, 14** — `lib/coll.nuc:96` (`V`), `lib/combinators.nuc:140` (`S`), `lib/hashmap.nuc:287,414` (`V`), `:372` (`K`), `lib/hashset.nuc:386` (`T`), `lib/iterator.nuc:31,91` (`E`), `:92,111,114,135` (`S`), `lib/vector.nuc:293` (`T`), `examples/get-dispatch-test.nuc:28` (`V`) — `?E` is one bare symbol and `subst-tyvars-sym` substitutes by colon *segment*, so `(Maybe E)`, `?&E` and `?(Vector E)` stamp and `?E` never would (the first draft rewrote them and `lib/iterator.nuc` stopped stamping); the script's type roster is per file (definers, `defprotocol`/template parameters, `:where` symbols), after a global roster leaked an `E` from a comment; **(b) a paren operand in an exported slot, 2** — `lib/test.nuc:410` `(Result (Vector Diagnostic) Err)`, `lib/hashmap.nuc:472` `(Maybe (Entry K V))` — the `--emit-cheader` classifiers `cheader-template-instance` / `cheader-niche-no-c` (`src/cheader.nuc:3690`, `:3758`) read a `(Maybe …)`/`(Result …)` head or a sigil-led *symbol* and not a `(? X)`/`(! X)` cell, so the `!(Vector Diagnostic)` spelling exported `void* read_diagnostics(...)` — a by-value tagged struct declared as a pointer, the ABI-wrong declaration both classifiers exist to refuse; PK-3's "the cheader walkers need no change" held for the pointer walkers only (rough edge recorded in [stage21-cleanup/overview.md](stage21-cleanup/overview.md)); local slots (`let`, `as`, `sizeof`, `alloca`) take the paren form freely; **(c) an `extend` subject, 2** — `examples/operators.nuc:34,35` `(extend ptr:Cents Ord)` — `extend` reads a cell subject as a template application, so `&Cents` = `(ref Cents)` is refused there (recorded); **(d) `(Maybe (raw T))`, 0 sites in the tree** — a value-Maybe, where `?raw:T` is the niche pointer. Also refused by construction: R1/R2/R4/R6/R7 under `quote`, and any form with a comment inside it (a reprint would move or lose it).

  **Gate, compiler half.** `make` → `build/nucleusc.ll` **13,560,463 bytes against the step-2 baseline's 13,560,457: 12 diff lines, 6 pairs, every one a `%ref.addr.15`/`%ref.addr.17` → `%tree.addr.15`/`%tree.addr.17` operand** — the two prescribed renames, since a local's name is its `alloca`'s name; `sed 's/%tree\.addr/%ref.addr/g'` over the swept `.ll` → `cmp` **byte-identical**. §7's "byte-identical against the step-2 build" is therefore true modulo its own rename instruction, and no rule changed a node. `make bootstrap` **`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`**. **Gate, tree half.** `ir-snapshot.sh verify`: **65 of 2,750 artifacts differ — 0 `.ll`, 0 `.err`, 47 `.nuch`, 18 `.h`.** The 47 `.nuch`: parsed old and new with the sweep's own reader and canonicalised (`ref`/`ptr`/`&`/`ref:`/`ptr:`/`addr-of` → one node, `Maybe`/`?`/`(? X)` → one, `Result X Err`/`!`/`(! X)` → one, `(name T)`/`name:T` → one, `(ref ref X)` nested) → **47 tree-identical, 0 residual**; the diffs are not confined to signatures as §7 expected — a template or macro body prints verbatim, so `&k`, `?T` inside one are header text too. The 18 `.h` differ only in the *reason* of a "not exported" comment, **20 lines, all `uses a defunion-template instance type` → `uses an error-union or option type`**: a `(Maybe i32)` in a public signature was refused by `cheader-template-instance` (cell, template head) and its `?i32` successor by `cheader-niche-no-c` (sigil-led symbol) — same verdict, different classifier; no declaration moved. `scripts/check-headers.sh --fix` regenerated **38** (`lib/*.nuch` 29, `lib/*.h` 9; 124 lines each way); `make check-headers` clean; `grep -o addr-of lib/*.nuch | wc -l` **0** (was 25 after PK-1). `make test` **1054/0/0**. `dump-ast-corpus.sh verify`: **112 of 1,386 artifacts differ, all `.out`, no `.err` or `.rc` moved, and every one of the 112 is a swept file** (22 swept files' ASTs did not move — their rewrites were node-identical spellings: `(addr-of x)` prints as `(ref x)` either way after PK-1's reader). Both snapshots re-taken: `ir-snapshot.sh snapshot --force` **444 inputs, 2,750 artifacts**; `dump-ast-corpus.sh snapshot --force` **462 inputs (6 rejected), 1,386 artifacts**; both `verify` PASS. `grep -rn "(addr-of " src lib examples`: **51 hits, every one in a comment or a string** (one on a code line, `src/compiler-types.nuc:591`, is its trailing comment) — §10's "empty save the retirement message" is met for code; the prose is PK-6's. The `intern-symbol "addr-of"` synthesis sites and the `addr-of`-named functions in `src/` (36 lines: 11 `"addr-of"` strings, 5 `'addr-of` compares, `node-type-addr-of`/`emit-addr-of`/`defvar-addr-of-ir`) are PK-5b's, untouched; the compiler still accepts `addr-of`.

  **Docs and context.** `context/conventions.md` "Prefer the `&` spellings": the stale "`(ref T)` list form is still required where a parameter's type is parenthesised" (pre-PK-2) replaced by the sigil-paren spellings, and a paragraph naming the script and its four refusal classes. Stale claims the sweep exposed, each verified against `build/nucleusc` before correcting: `docs/allocators.md` and `docs/builtins.md` showed the `Allocator` protocol in the legacy `((alloc (raw ui8)) (params))` head form — which the compiler now *refuses* (`legacy 'name:ret' syntax is no longer supported`) — with a note that a parenthesised type "does not tokenise" in a return/parameter position; both now show `lib/allocator.nuc`'s spelling. `docs/structs-unions.md` and `docs/builtins.md`'s "known limitation: a `declare` with a parametric return type requires the list-form name node" — the name node is the refused legacy form and the example given was already the current shape; rewritten as the two accepted spellings. `lib/iterator.nuc` and `lib/allocator.nuc` carried "TOKENIZER NOTES" from Stage 11 (`name:(Maybe T)` does not tokenize; `(alloc:(raw ui8) …)` not accepted) — deleted; the one note that is still true (a `(Maybe E)` over a tyvar stays the list form, and why) replaces them. Design: §7 gains an "as built" table of the refusal classes and the two gate claims that were one step off; the status paragraph; three rough edges in overview.md (the classifiers, `extend`'s subject, `?E` vs the substituter — which PK-4b's walker change should take with it).

## `&`/`@` adoption in `lib/` and `examples/` (2026-09-06)

The sweep [ref-sigil.md](stage16-ergonomics/ref-sigil.md) §5 deferred, following the `src/` adoption in `a15f38e`: `(addr-of x)` → `&x`, `(deref p)` → `@p`, and `ref:`/`ptr:` → `&` at an **interior** colon-chain segment (`out-end:ptr:i64` → `out-end:&i64`, `?ptr:Val` → `?&Val`, `):ptr:Box` → `):&Box`). 105 files, output-identical, bootstrap converged. `examples/ptr.nuc`, `ref-sigil.nuc`, `colon-paren-types.nuc` and `errptr.nuc` are left in their canonical spellings — each exists to exercise one.

  **Two spellings the sweep does not take — and `a15f38e` had taken both, so `src/` was swept back the next day (2,469 sites: 2,361 cast operands to `ref:T`, 101 type expressions to `(ref T)`, 7 binding pairs to the attached `name:&T`; `build/nucleusc.ll` byte-identical, so the two spellings only ever differed as text).** A `&` at a TOKEN boundary is the address-of reader macro, so a standalone `&T` in a type slot is an `(addr-of T)` cell — the cost §6 accepted knowingly. It compiles (`parse-type-from-node` and `node-is-ptr-wrapper` both read that head as `ref`), but `--emit-nuch` prints `defprotocol` and generic-template forms **verbatim**, so `(as ptr:T x)` → `(as &T x)` and `(p (ref T))` → `(p &T)` put the spelling into the committed `.nuch`. Measured across `lib/`: **50** `(addr-of T)` in type slots, on top of the 42 genuine value-position ones already exported. The interior-segment forms have no such node — the lexer rewrites `&` to `ref:` before the parser sees anything — which is what makes the line between the two halves of §6 the line this sweep draws.

  **`--emit-cheader` rendered `(ref T)` as `void*`, and two goldens had it written into them.** `type-node-to-c` keys on the head symbol and named only `ptr`, so `ref` — its documented synonym since the Phase F flip — and `addr-of` fell through to the `void*` default. Adopting `ptr:T` → `&T` in `lib/` would therefore have degraded 11 public C headers (`char_encode_utf8(uint8_t*)` → `(void*)`), so the arm now names all three. That is a fix, not a side effect: 19 header files gain real element types (`hash_pi32(int32_t*)`, `strview_split(struct StrView*, struct StrView*)`, `dir_count(struct DirEntries*)`), three gain the `#include` the element type needs, and `w9-cheader-overload-distinct-symbols` / `w9-cheader-reserved-escaped` were asserting the imprecise spelling — exactly the "it will happily lock in a wrong spelling" failure the conventions file names two paragraphs above the renderer. `raw` still widens to `void*`, deliberately.

---

## Stage 18 — the REPL introspection layer, restored (2026-09-03)

Spec: [stage18-tooling/overview.md](stage18-tooling/overview.md) §1–§5. Triggered by a user typing `(type-of "foo")` at the prompt and getting *unknown: type-of — not defined anywhere in this compilation unit*.

  **The finding, and it is the reason the stage exists.** All sixteen meta forms in `docs/compiler.md` — `defined?`, `kind-of`, `type-of`, `dir`, `doc`, `apropos`, `complete`, `imports`, `casts`, `expansion-of`, `last-error`, `time`, `locate`, `forget`/`reset!`, `trace`/`untrace` — were documented in four files and implemented in none. They were **built** on 2026-05-08 (`f97bb47`, +542 lines to `src/repl.nuc`), are present at `c5d973d` (2026-05-28), and absent at `f909024` (2026-06-07). **No commit removes them**: `git log -S kind-of -- src/repl.nuc` reports only the two additions, and the history across that window carries replayed duplicate commits (`f97bb47`/`45746cf`, `854eb68`/`7bfb63d`, `4ac7488`/`c5d973d`) of which the line reaching HEAD never had the code. A rebase dropped it. The docs, written against a working implementation, outlived it by fifteen months.

  **Why no gate saw it.** The IR snapshot compares emitted output; `make bootstrap` only asks that the compiler compile itself; and no test named a meta form. **A feature can vanish from a self-hosted compiler without moving a byte of its output.** That is the transferable lesson, and the stage's most important deliverable is the gate rather than the code: `tests/repl/meta-introspection.in` pins thirteen forms by golden diff, and `run_repl_meta_loose` asserts by substring on the three a golden cannot hold (`dir` lists every library name, `imports` names the prelude's import set, `time` prints a measured duration). 949 → **951 tests**.

  **What the loss left behind.** `Sym.docstring` and `Sym.trace-saved` are exactly the zero-reader dead storage Stage 17 C8 flagged without knowing why — they are the readers' half of code that no longer existed. `Type.param-names` is the sharper case: it became `(params (raw (Vector (ref Field))))` with `Field.name` left null, and the comment justifying that cites "the REPL renders positional `pN`" — a field emptied *because* its only reader was already gone, with the absence used as the argument. Restoring the reader made it load-bearing again.

  **Phases R0–R7, every one snapshot-gated and byte-identical across 2,624 artifacts.** R0 dispatcher + the forms needing no new data; R1 the name walk (`dir`/`complete`, deduped through `scope-frame-find` since prescan and `emit-defn` both register a `defn`); R2 parameter-name capture (`Field.name` from `emit-defn`, `MacroDef.param-names` from `defmacro` — inert to the type system because `fn-sig-eq` compares only each Field's `type`); R3 `Sym.src-file`/`src-line` at the five Nucleus definers and `locate`; R4 docstrings, `doc`, `apropos`; R5 `last-error` + `time`; R6 `forget`/`reset!` + `trace`/`untrace`; R7 tests and docs.

  **Three defects in the recovered code, fixed rather than ported.** (1) The 2026-05 `repl-meta-sym-arg` accepted only `NODE-SYM` while carrying a comment saying "use the literal prefix string with `kind-of`" — so the documented `rmacro` answer was unreachable in the very implementation that documented it, since `'` cannot be written bare. Strings are accepted now. (2) `emit-fn-trace-shim` printed one `type-to-ir` per parameter, which is precisely the defect the surviving `emit-fn-thunk`'s comment records having fixed for the thunk: an aggregate parameter gets the wrong lowering and names a `%Struct` the module never defined. The port builds its parameter list through `abi-print-param-to`, and `(trace f)` on a struct-taking function works. (3) `Sym.trace-saved` went back to `ptr` — Stage 14 had retyped it to `StrView` with a comment claiming it saved `ir-name`, which was a description of a field nothing read.

  **The docstring literal is recorded, not stripped.** It is already an inert first statement, so removing it would move emitted IR for every `defn` in the tree that has one — a snapshot diff bought for nothing when the docstring only has to be recorded. `docs/toplevel.md` turned out to state the capture rule, single-string-body guard included, exactly right already.

  **Docs corrected beyond the restoration:** `doc` had no table row in `docs/compiler.md` or `docs/builtins.md` despite being referenced from `defn`'s docstring rule; `docs/emacs.md`'s `M-.` row said `(locate 'sym)` while its own prose said a quoted argument is rejected (the prose was right).

---

## Stage 19 — process control, staged ahead of the test-framework decision (2026-09-04)

Spec: [stage19-process/overview.md](stage19-process/overview.md) §1–§8. **Built 2026-09-04, all six phases.** 959 tests (955 + three phase gates + the example), IR snapshot byte-identical across 2,624 artifacts, bootstrap converged, `check-headers` and `check-cstr` clean.

  **Why it is its own stage.** The test framework needs process control; process control does not need the test framework. Every option in stage 18 §T5 except the in-process one is downstream of `lib/process.nuc`, and that decision is still open — scheduling this behind it would block a capability the language owes its users on a question about how its own tests are spelled. A self-hosted systems language that cannot run another program is incomplete on its own terms, and today the whole of the story is two raw `popen`/`pclose` declares the compiler keeps for itself (`src/cheader.nuc:15-16`), `read-pipe-output` (`:1481`, a `malloc`/`realloc`/`fread` loop returning a raw `ptr` with two out-parameters, written before `!T` and `String` existed), and two `system` call sites.

  **It is library work, not compiler work — verified by probe, 2026-09-04.** Compiled and ran against the current `bin/nucleusc` with no compiler changes: `fork`, `_exit`, `waitpid` and `WNOHANG` resolve from `(import-use "unistd.h")` and `(import-use "sys/wait.h")`; `pipe`, `dup2`, `close`, `read` and `execvp` all work, including a NUL-terminated `ptr:ptr` passed as `char *const argv[]`; an end-to-end fork → pipe → dup2 → execvp → read → waitpid probe printed the child's output and exit code correctly. `struct pollfd` is **not** opaque (`sizeof` 8, fields assignable, `POLLIN` 1) and `posix_spawn_file_actions_t` is 80 bytes, so neither spawn route is blocked. Nothing in this stage waits on anything.

  **The one real gap, and a correction to stage 18 §T3.** `cheader-macro-line` (`src/cheader.nuc:2787`) admits object-like `#define`s only — "a `(` immediately after NAME makes it function-like, which stays out". So `WNOHANG` imports and `WIFEXITED`/`WEXITSTATUS`/`WIFSIGNALED`/`WTERMSIG` do not. §T3's claim that Stage 17's constant import left `lib/process.nuc` needing **no** hardcoded platform knowledge is wrong: it needs exactly one piece, the wait-status layout (the probe's `_exit(7)` yields raw status 1792 = `7 << 8`), which is why §2.4 decodes it once behind a typed `ExitStatus` instead of at every call site.

  **Decisions.** `fork`+`execvp`, not `posix_spawn` and never `system`/`popen`: both alternatives are available, but a shell command line makes quoting the caller's correctness problem where an `argv` vector has none, and `posix_spawn`'s file-actions API is a second vocabulary to wrap for the same result. Everything the child needs is built **before** the fork — the window between `fork` and `execvp` is async-signal-safe only, so allocating an `argv` there is a deadlock waiting for the day the allocator holds a lock at fork time; that is why the surface is a `Command` record rather than a spawn taking a callback, which would invite exactly that. Capture polls both pipes, because draining them sequentially **deadlocks**: a pipe holds 64 KiB on Linux, so a child writing more than that to stderr while the parent reads stdout to EOF blocks forever — a hang, not a slow path. `Process` owns the child and `Drop` reaps it (a runner spawning 955 children otherwise leaks 955 process-table slots), with the hazard named rather than designed away: dropping a live child blocks, so `Drop` is the error path's safety net and an explicit wait is the intended one. And the module exposes `spawn`/`wait`/`try-wait`/`wait-any` alongside one-shot `run`, because a job pool needs `waitpid(-1)` — a fact about the *set* of children that no one-shot API can express at any width.

  **Phases, as built.** P1 spawn/wait/`ExitStatus` → P2 streams → P3 job-pool primitives → P4 retire the compiler's private process code → P5 `docs/process.md` → P6 `examples/subprocess.nuc`. `lib/process.nuc` is 415 lines. The P2 gate is a child that writes 1,288,895 bytes to *each* stream — a sequential drain hangs on it, and the test's `timeout` is the assertion. The P3 gate spawns three children with sleeps of 0.6/0.2/0.4 and requires them reaped 2, 3, 1. Out of scope as planned: filesystem operations, threads, Windows hosts, parent-side signal handling.

  **P4 removed `read-pipe-output`, the `popen`/`pclose` declares and both `system` call sites.** Three consequences beyond the line count: the link step now passes an **argv**, so a path with a space in it was the caller's quoting problem and is now nobody's; `clang -E` no longer needs `2>/dev/null` inside a command string, because stderr is a separate captured stream that gets dropped; and `--sysroot=<dir>` was being interpolated into a shell command line, so a sysroot path with a space in it was a latent bug that is now one argument. The cheader cache keeps its `ptr`+length interface — it stores a bare pointer in a `Node` and nothing ever frees it — so `cheader-capture` parks the captured `String` in a process-lifetime `g-cheader-bufs` and hands out `string-as-cstr`'s pointer: no copy, and the owner outlives every reader.

  **Five deltas from the plan (§8), three of them language findings.** (1) `process-try-wait` returns `!bool` plus a `process-status` accessor, not `!(Maybe ExitStatus)`: **`(Result (Maybe T) Err)` does not compile for a struct `T`** — the sugar does not parse, and the list form rejects `(ok m)` with "type mismatch for a field of arm 'ok'" while an unannotated `(ok (some st))` resolves `some` to the pointer-niche variant because the `ok` wrapper carries no want channel inward. Exactly the kind of gap §1 said this exercise exists to find; fixing it is a compiler change with its own gates. (2) `command-env` merges rather than appends, settling §7 open decision 4 — appending would have been three lines and wrong, since `getenv` returns the first match and a duplicate is never seen; `environ` needed an `(extern environ:ptr:ptr)` because the header reader takes function declarations and object-like macros, not `extern` variables, and the child sets it with a pointer store, safe in a window where `setenv` would not be. (3) `Command` holds one NUL-separated buffer plus a `(Vector usize)` of offsets rather than a `(Vector String)`, because **`Vector`'s `drop` frees its array without dropping elements** and N owning strings would have leaked N buffers. (4) P4 kept the cache interface, above. (5) The example is `examples/subprocess.nuc`: **a file named `process.nuc` cannot `(import-use process)`** — the root file is on no import list, so the import silently resolves to the file itself and every name from the module goes missing behind an accurate but misleading "no import in this unit reaches" note. All three language findings are now in `context/conventions.md`.

## Stage 18 — a native test framework: options (2026-09-03), decided (2026-09-04)

Spec: [stage18-tooling/overview.md](stage18-tooling/overview.md) §T1–§T9. **The recommendation was accepted on 2026-09-04 and the seven open questions ruled on, so §T6 is a plan now** — see the decision entry at the end of this section. The stage directory was renamed `stage18-repl-introspection` → `stage18-tooling` to hold both halves; all references were updated, including three source comments (`src/repl.nuc:122`, `src/compiler-types.nuc:598`, `tests/run-tests.sh`).

  **Why, before what.** The point is not to delete shell. Two reasons make it a stage: it is the **dogfooding that finds what the language is missing** — the same exercise as the stage-17 string sweep, aimed at process control, the filesystem and text matching, where the deliverable is the library that had to become good enough to survive the port; and **a language shipping in 2026 is expected to have a testing story, and "write a shell script" is not one.** The compiler's 955 tests are the proving ground, not the product; the product is a documented `lib/test.nuc` a user of the language can call. Line count is a proxy, not the objective — which is why §T5 option B (extract the declarative tests to manifests) is rejected twice: it removes 1.4% of the file *and* exercises no Nucleus at all.

  **The measurement is the deliverable.** Testing Nucleus is 15,895 lines of shell and Python across ten scripts, but 14,337 of them are `tests/run-tests.sh` and the other nine are generators, source-tree audits, or cross-toolchain scripts that run outside `make test` — so the addressable target is one file, not ten. Inside it: 183 functions holding 10,632 lines (74%), 347 `spawn` calls over 176 distinct targets, and **172 of those spawns are one-line calls to four declarative drivers** (`run_reject_at` 118, `run_reject` 43, `run_accepts` 7, `check_long` 4). With the `examples/` (161) and `tests/repl/` (16) loops, over a third of the 955 tests already cost under 200 lines. The expensive ~10,400 lines are ~171 bespoke units that write source files, emit IR, grep it, link with `clang`, run the binary and read `nm`. **This kills the obvious plan:** extracting the declarative tests to manifests removes ~1.4% of the file (§T5 option B).

  **The assertion vocabulary is six predicates, and the regex requirement is a mirage.** 552 `qgrep` sites (305 `-F`, 76 `-E`, 34 `-xF`, rest bare BRE), 171 string equalities, 70 emptiness tests, 21 file comparisons. Of the 76 `-E` patterns only 14 use alternation; the rest are literal IR text with a wildcard standing in for a compiler-generated SSA name (`'sext i32 %[A-Za-z0-9_.]+ to i64'`). A FileCheck-style glob covers all 76 — no regex engine needed — and `lib/strview.nuc` already covers the other 475 outright.

  **Typed values versus text: better on 57% of assertions, irrelevant on the rest (§T4).** Census of all 843 assertion sites by *what they are about*: 250 compiler diagnostics (29.7%), 209 emitted IR text (24.8%), 138 program stdout/exit status (16.4%), 111 `$ok`/`$bad` harness accumulators (13.2%), 90 generated C header (10.7%), 28 generated `.nuch` (3.3%), 17 external tool output (2.0%). **562 of them are substring probes against 72 whole-value comparisons** — and a substring probe cannot say *and nothing else* or *in this function*: 63 IR probes are module-scoped (an instruction emitted into the wrong function still passes; nothing in the file scopes an assertion, and the one unit that needed it hand-rolled an `awk` range), and 105 negative `! qgrep` assertions pass vacuously the moment a mangling or spacing change stops the pattern matching. The split that decides the rework is **who consumes the artifact**: `clang` consumes the IR text and the shell consumes program stdout, so those 364 sites are text *by contract* and want a scoped matcher, not a type. The other 368 are structure the compiler had and destroyed — `die-at` (662 call sites) renders `path:line: error: msg` and exits with no diagnostic record anywhere in `src/`, and `run_reject_at` (the file's most-used driver, 118 uses) recovers the fields with **two independent greps over one blob, so nothing checks that the location and the message belong to the same diagnostic** — a hole that is live, because `note:` lines carry locations too. Generated `.nuch` and `.h` (118 sites) need no new machinery at all: both have a parser in this tree already (`src/reader.nuc`, `src/cheader.nuc`'s `c-parse-*-decl`), so the honest assertion is a round trip — emit, read back with the reader that consumes it, assert on the declarations that registered. The 111 accumulators do not get rewritten; they **disappear** into `!T` propagation. **New option G** (structured diagnostics across the process boundary) captures the 368 while keeping every §T2 property, and is scheduled *before* the first migration category, since that category is the one it improves.

  **The missing runtime is the other half of the deliverable, not an obstacle.** No `lib/` module exposes a process API; `popen`/`pclose` exist only as raw declares in `src/cheader.nuc:15-16`, and there is no `fork`, `execv`, `posix_spawn` or `waitpid` in the tree. `lib/file.nuc`'s 15 entry points include no `mkdir`, `mkdtemp`, `unlink`, `rmdir` or `stat`; directory enumeration exists once, as raw `opendir`/`readdir` declares at `src/nucleusc.nuc:2815-2817` with a hand-validated `DIRENT-D-NAME-OFFSET`. Two more gaps surfaced in §T4: there is **no text→`Node` reader outside the compiler** — `Node`/`NodeKind` live in `lib/prelude.nuc` and are auto-imported into every program, `lib/node.nuc` operates on them, and `src/reader.nuc` is the only parser, so the language hands every program the AST type and no way to build one from text — and **no structured diagnostic**, wanted by the REPL and any future LSP independently of testing. Threads are absent and not wanted — bash's parallelism is process-level too. **A self-hosted systems language with no way to run a program is incomplete on its own terms**, so `lib/process.nuc` and `lib/fs.nuc` are owed to users whether or not a single test moves; the suite is what puts them under real load (955 units, 16-way concurrency, 168 scratch directories). **One thing got cheaper this stage:** the platform-constant import above means they need no hardcoded per-OS constant table.

  **Recommendation: F→C, with E as a separate track and a permanent shell trust anchor.** Replace the *harness* first (dispatch, job pool, buffering, ordered replay) with bodies still running as `sh -c`, gated on byte-identical output against the current script; then `lib/fs.nuc`; then a six-predicate `lib/test.nuc` — finished only when it is documented and an `examples/` program declares and runs its own tests, since a module only the compiler suite can use is a private harness with a public name; then migrate bodies by category, fixing every weakness the port hits in the library rather than working around it in the body. **If the migration stopped after `lib/fs.nuc` the language would still have gained two capabilities it lacks, both hardened by 955 tests** — a better outcome than option B reaches at completion. The in-process option — call `emit-*` directly and roll back with `repl-snapshot`/`repl-restore` — is fast and gives better evidence than grepping IR, but `repl-restore` truncates registries to watermarks rather than rebuilding the world, so it is not proof of independence across 955 tests; adopt it only alongside an implementation that can disagree with it. It also scores worst on the stage's own terms, being the one option that reaches its speed by routing around the capabilities the language is missing. **The property that caps everything:** a native runner is compiled by the compiler under test, so when that compiler is broken the runner has no verdict to give. A short shell script that compiles one program and diffs its output stays, permanently, and is the reason the rest is allowed to move.

  **Decided 2026-09-04: F→C with G folded in, seven rulings (§T8), plan in §T6.** Step 0 shipped separately as Stage 19, so the plan starts at a runner rather than a syscall. Phases: **TF-1** give `tests/run-tests.sh` a `--list`/`--unit` mode — pure shell work, and its byte-identical gate doubles as the **order-independence audit of the existing suite**, since a unit that only passes after some other unit has run fails when run alone; **TF-2** `build/nuctest`, a bounded job pool over Stage 19's `spawn`/`wait-any` with per-unit scratch directories, ordered replay and `--shuffle`, gated on byte-identical output *and* three shuffled seeds *and* wall-clock within 10% of the shell harness's 59s; **TF-3** `lib/read.nuc`, the text→`Node` reader the language owes its users regardless (§T4.6); **TF-4** `lib/test.nuc`, `deftest`, the six predicates plus §T4.3's scoped IR matcher, `!T` failure propagation (which *deletes* the 111 accumulators rather than translating them), and the s-expression suite protocol — done only when documented and when an `examples/` program runs its own tests; **TF-5** structured `nucleusc` diagnostics, scheduled **before** the migration because its first category *is* the diagnostic assertions; **TF-6** migrate 172 declarative + 177 loop + ~171 bespoke units, retiring each category's shell as it lands; **TF-7** `make test` = trust anchor + runner. **TF-E** (in-process) stays a separate track and the trust anchor is permanent.

  **The architectural choice that makes the migration tractable:** the runner is generic — a unit is a name, a command and a scratch directory — so one runner drives `sh -c` shell bodies and `<suite> --run <name>` native tests during the same migration and does not change when the bodies do. Each unit is one process, which makes per-unit isolation and order independence properties of the architecture rather than promises.

  **The seven rulings, with what each costs.** (1) The runner is **its own binary** (`build/nuctest`), which keeps the compiler's surface clean and makes the §T2.1 dependency visible — at the price of one more artifact a broken compiler must build before it can report anything, which is what the trust anchor covers. (2) **`deftest`**, buying the registration table (`--list`/`--filter`/`--shuffle`) and the test's own file and line, at the price of language surface. (3) **Fixtures embedded or on disk, by a written rule** — on disk when it contains a `"`, is shared, or exceeds ~20 lines; the quote clause is the live one, because 93 of the 292 heredocs carry a quote and escaped Nucleus inside Nucleus is unreadable, which is a language gap and not a fixture policy (§T9.1). (4) **Glob, not regex**, which §T1.2 measured as covering all 76 `-E` sites. (5) **S-expressions** for both the suite protocol and `--diagnostics=sexp`, read by `lib/read.nuc` — and the `Diagnostic` record **replaces** `die-at`'s rendering rather than sitting beside it, because two renderers for one diagnostic is how the text and the structure drift apart. (6) **Order independence is a requirement**, enforced by `--shuffle` at TF-2 and at every retirement; TF-1 is expected to find existing units that violate it. (7) **`make test` runs both** until each category's native version has returned identical verdicts on three consecutive green full runs, one shuffled — "stable" is per category, not global, and the dual-run cost is only real during TF-6.

  **Six things deferred on purpose (§T9):** a raw string literal, a richer matcher, other structured formats (JSON/EDN), *explicit* test dependencies (§T8.6 rules out incidental ordering, not declared ordering), a worker-pool protocol (trades isolation for speed, which the order-independence ruling just made a requirement), and a `nucleusc --test` convenience wrapper.

  **One root cause fixed in passing.** `run_example` shells out to `build.sh`, which runs `make`. Harmless when the tree is built — but with any `src/*.nuc` newer than `build/nucleusc`, 161 parallel jobs relink the compiler while sixteen others are executing it, and all 955 tests fail with `Text file busy`. `make test` hid it by building `$(BIN)` first. `tests/run-tests.sh` now runs `make -s` once before dispatch, so it is safe to invoke directly.

  **TF-7 — the end state 2026-09-10** (§T6.7). `make test` is `tests/check-compiler-works.sh` and then a recursive `make run-nuctests`; `tests/run-tests.sh` and `tests/nuctest.nuc` are **deleted**, and with them the `nuctest`/`$(NUCTEST)` targets. **The anchor's name says what it checks**, not what role it plays in §T6.9's argument — it compiles `examples/hello.nuc`, requires a binary to exist, runs it, and diffs against `tests/expected/hello.out`, in 25 lines of bash with four distinct failure messages and no `set -e` (errexit would kill it before its diagnostic reached stderr). **`$(NUCTESTS)` is deliberately NOT a prerequisite of `test`:** a prerequisite is built before the recipe runs, so the old `test: test-tools $(NUCTESTS)` would have had make build the suite binary — with the compiler under test — before the anchor could say a word, and a compiler broken badly enough to take that build down would stop `make` with a build error and no verdict. That is §T2.1 exactly, and it would have left the anchor decorative; the recipe now depends on `$(BIN)` alone and recurses. Verified by putting an error in a suite module: `PASS  compiler-works` prints, *then* the suite build fails. **Two stale artifacts, not one:** `run-tests.sh` had no units after the last TF-6 batch, and `nuctest.nuc` drove those units through `--unit`, so §T8.1's "the runner is its own binary" is satisfied by `build/nuctests` itself — a binary the compiler under test must build, which was always the ruling's point. `--shuffle` went with the TF-2 runner and is raised in `design/deferred/overview.md` rather than absorbed into a deletion; order-independence stays an external `shuf` over `--list` for now. **41 comments repointed** across `examples/`, `tests/fixtures/`, `tests/`, `scripts/`, `lib/test.nuc`, `docs/` and `context/`, so no reference to either deleted file survives outside the historical record; three `context/conventions.md` sections that documented the shell harness's mechanics were rewritten as the general lessons they actually are (the `pipefail`/`grep -q` trap, which three scripts in `tests/` can still hit; the tree-wide-grep rule, now about the suite's embedded string literals; and the empty-result-file rule, which the native suite closed by construction — a dead test is a missing record, and a missing record is a count mismatch against `--list`). Verified: the anchor broken four ways (missing compiler, one that refuses, one that exits 0 writing nothing, a wrong expected file), `make test` green at 966, `make bootstrap` byte-identical, `ir-snapshot.sh verify` 2,684 artifacts.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what holds across the whole tree 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what holds across the whole tree"). **The last batch: TF-6 is complete.** 8 shell functions → 11 tests, 360 further lines of the file retired: `run_abi_subtest`, `run_layout_subtest`, `run_no_line_zero`, `run_stdlib_table`, `run_headers_generated`, `run_cstr_residue`, `run_reader_parity` (one body over four corpora, four verdicts) and `run_diagnostics_sexp`, into a new twentieth module `tests/suite-audits.nuc` (253 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 520 → 163 lines and 8 → 0 unit functions, and **`make test` is 0 shell + 966 native = 966** — the 966-verdict invariant held 1:1 through all twenty-one batches. The 360 lines include the three dead `w1_*` helpers and four orphaned comment blocks earlier batches left behind when their callers went. **Two kinds of unit, one module:** the corpus walks (`w4a-no-line-zero`, the four `reader-parity-*`) and the spawns (`abi-interop`, `layout`, `stdlib-table-generated`, `headers-generated`, `cstr-residue`) share a shape the rest of the suite does not — the subject is a corpus or the source tree, not a program. §T7 rules the five scripts stay outside the framework and that outside means *not rewritten, not unrun*, so each unit runs the script and requires exit 0, with the script's own report as the failure text. **Three vacuity holes closed:** a sweep that swept nothing passed every assertion it never made, so both corpus loops now require at least one file walked (and, for parity, agreed on); `run_diagnostics_sexp`'s claim 5 compared `--diagnostics=text` against the default and was satisfiable by two empty streams, so the text form is now pinned — which also makes claim 1's "the same location the text mode printed" a comparison rather than a remark; and its claim 2 grepped a note prefix out of the blob, where `check-note-at` pairs the note with the record it belongs to, at its line. **Library gaps filled** in `tests/nuctests.nuc`: `Ran`/`ran` (a command's two streams kept apart and deliberately *unvalidated* — `examples/hex-escape-test.nuc` dumps an AST that is not UTF-8), `nucleusc-raw` (the format units cannot use a builder that passes `--diagnostics=sexp`, the thing under test), `check-script-ok`, and `check-golden-in` (a corpus loop needs the file named: "line 7 differs" says nothing about which of 226 files line 7 was in). **Verified by breaking it**, twelve ways — one per unit plus one per remaining sexp claim — each reverted by editing the source back, never with git. The five spawns were broken at their *subject*, never their invocation (an expected ABI sum, a `sizeof` in the layout oracle, a false availability row in `docs/stdlib.md`, a return type in a committed `.nuch`, an allowlist count), so a unit that spawned the right script and ignored its verdict would still have gone red; reader parity by making `readdump` print a trailing space, which turned all four red and named the file each time; and the line-0 sweep by flipping `check-no-line-zero`'s sentinel, which proves it reads the `line` field of real diagnostics rather than passing over an empty list.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what the runtime library does at the edges 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what the runtime library does at the edges"). 8 shell functions → 8 tests, 189 further lines of the file retired: `run_s17_read_line`, `run_s17_strview_literal_return`, `run_s17_strview_literal_join`, `run_s17_strview_null_compare_rejected`, `run_s19_process_status`, `run_s19_process_capture`, `run_s19_process_pool` and `run_repl_meta_loose`, into a new nineteenth module `tests/suite-runtime.nuc` (142 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 709 → 520 lines and 16 → 8 functions; `make test` is 11 shell + 955 native = 966. **A new module because none of these can be an `examples/*.nuc` golden test**, which is what they have in common: the read-line fixture needs a stdin the golden loop cannot give it (a child inheriting the harness's stdin blocks on a terminal), the process fixtures need a timeout because the failure under test is a deadlock, and the REPL session prints an elapsed duration that differs every run — which is also why `repl-meta-loose` stays a substring check rather than becoming a `tests/repl/*.in` golden beside the thirteen forms that are pinned exactly. **A needle that dropped the suggestion:** `s17-strview-null-compare-rejected` matched `!=: a StrView is never null` and stopped, where the message continues `— use (str-empty? &v) to test for the empty view` — the whole reason the diagnostic exists, since a `StrView` is a 16-byte struct that is never null and `(= sv null)` used to fall into the CStr `strcmp` lowering and compare `.data` against NULL, a silent SIGSEGV. Naming the predicate the author meant IS the fix, and the shell did not check it; pinned whole, at its line. **One library gap filled** rather than worked around (§T6.6's rule): `build-run-stdin`, in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input. **Verified by breaking it**, eight ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: the blank line's contents changed, the two StrView fixtures pointed at each other's expected output, the suggested predicate renamed, a decoded signal status changed, an overflow byte count moved by one, a kill signal changed, and the elapsed-time marker reworded. `make test` 11 shell + 955 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the Stage 14/15 remainder 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "the Stage 14/15 remainder"). 5 shell functions → 8 tests, 243 lines of the file retired (including two orphaned banners whose units had moved and five `spawn` lines): `run_s1_sugar_rets` (1 unit) into `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, `run_s1_block` (4) into `tests/suite-exports.nuc`, `run_w4a_sibling_forward` (1) into `tests/suite-imports.nuc`, and `run_w2a_order_identical` and `run_w2d_dsp_bitexact` (1 each) into `tests/suite-conversions.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 952 → 709 lines and 21 → 16 functions; `make test` is 19 shell + 947 native = 966. **A unit that asserts nothing, by its own design.** `run_w4a_sibling_forward` passes if the compile emits an error naming the referencing line OR emits no error at all — written that way deliberately, its comment says, "so this keeps passing once W1 removes the error entirely". W1 removed it: the compile is silent today, so the empty-`err` branch is the one that fires and the unit has been vacuous ever since, passing equally if the compiler stopped resolving the sibling reference, stopped linking, or stopped emitting a program at all. What is true now is stronger and is what is pinned — the compile is silent, no diagnostic reports line 0, and the program builds, runs and exits 7. **A `.nuch` that promised a symbol nobody linked:** the three `s1_block` surfaces were four, four and three `qgrep`s under one verdict each, all matched as substrings; `check-line` pins whole lines now, because a `(declare twice …)` whose parameter spelling drifted is exactly the failure this surface has and a substring cannot see it. The template stamps are COUNTED rather than found, so a re-drain emitting `@gmax.i32.i32` twice fails here instead of at someone else's link, and the cheader unit refuses `int32_t scale(` outright — `scale` is overloaded, so a bare prototype names a symbol the object never defines. **Two library gaps filled** rather than worked around (§T6.6's rule): `normalize-commutative` and `clang-run-file`, both in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input; the first replaces a 25-line inline `awk` program canonicalising commutative operand order, the second exists because a bit-exact float comparison needs the reference built with the fixture's own `-ffp-contract=off` and `cc-run-file` passes neither that nor `-O2`. **Verified by breaking it**, eight ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: the `noreturn` attribute dropped, the sibling program's exit status moved, a `.nuch` parameter type changed, an asm label truncated, a printed field changed, the `define` anchor dropped from a stamp count, a normalized module compared against a raw one, and the C reference built with `-ffast-math`. Two needed a second attempt — comparing the W2a fixture against itself agrees trivially, and `-ffp-contract=fast` does not change this kernel's output — neither of which said anything about the unit. `make test` 19 shell + 947 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — a type C cannot spell 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "a type C cannot spell"). 3 shell functions → 9 tests, 140 lines of the file retired (including three banners and three `spawn` lines): `run_closure_cheader` (3 units), `run_box_cheader` (4) and `run_cheader_c_include` (2), into `tests/suite-cheader.nuc`, which grows 788 → 921 lines. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 1,092 → 952 lines and 24 → 21 functions; `make test` is 27 shell + 939 native = 966. One subject: what `--emit-cheader` does with a signature C has no spelling for (a closure env, a `(BoxedFn …)`, a `(dyn P)`) and with one C *can* spell but only given the right `#include`. **A fixture that does not compile, and a needle written to accept that.** `run_box_cheader`'s last claim is that the definition site warns, and its own comment says "(at least one box-typed defn fires)" — only one does. `--emit-llvm` on `tests/fixtures/box-cheader.nuc` exits 1: `make-boxed` warns at line 19, then line 20 is an ERROR, because `(return 0)` cannot produce the `(BoxedFn (i32) i32)` it declares, so `use-dyn` at line 23 is never reached and never warns. The shell's `2>&1 >/dev/null || true` swallowed the exit status and its grep found the one warning that did fire; the comment recorded the symptom without the cause. `l13-cheader-warns` pins both halves now — the warning at 19 and the error at 20 — and the fixture defect is written up in `design/deferred/overview.md` rather than absorbed: fixing it edits an IR-snapshot input whose recorded stderr (`emit_all` snapshots stderr as well as stdout) changes with it, so it needs a re-take with a stated reason rather than a quiet edit inside a test-migration batch. **Absence with no anchor:** `cheader-c-include-only-when-named` asserted only that `<unistd.h>` is absent, which an empty or failed header satisfies — the `plain` prototype is required first; the omission units get the same treatment from the other side, since the closure header must still carry the `__vfn_env_0` typedef, the very type whose exposure caused the omission. **A claim guarded out of existence:** the generated header compiling on its own is the entire point of the `#include`, and the shell ran that check under `if command -v clang` — clang is a hard dependency of this project and never skips, so it is unconditional now. **Verified by breaking it**, nine ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: the env typedef renamed, two asm labels and two prototypes altered, an omission comment's name changed, an omission inverted to a presence, the two diagnostic lines moved, and the expected `#include` swapped for another header. `make test` 27 shell + 939 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what the compiler records when it reads a declaration 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what the compiler records when it reads a declaration"). 3 shell functions → 5 tests, 237 lines of the file retired (including three banners and three `spawn` lines): `run_builtin_tyname_resolvable` (1 unit) and `run_s16_optional_has_rest` (1) into `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, which grows 2,561 → 2,685 lines, and `run_s16_c1_bare_unsigned` (3) into `tests/suite-cimport.nuc`, 707 → 778. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 1,329 → 1,092 lines and 27 → 24 functions; `make test` is 36 shell + 930 native = 966. Three registration sites, one subject: a stamped method's type pattern, a `defn`'s parameter list, and a C header's declarator — each recorded the wrong thing and broke a use site far away from it. **The one type the comment singled out was the one never exercised.** `run_builtin_tyname_resolvable` loops over eleven builtin type names and its own comment says of `raw`: "the point of listing it is that tyname-resolvable must still answer for it" — and the next line is `[ "$T" = "raw" ] && continue`, before any file is written. `(Vector raw)` in fact stamps and its IR parses, so the claim was true, untested and testable; all eleven are exercised now. **A hand-rolled `comm` where the parser was the assertion:** the same function built the set of called `@names` and the set of defined-or-declared ones and diffed them to find a call with no define, which is exactly what `llvm-as` reports — as the function's own comment says ("LLVM's own parser is the only thing that catches it"). `check-ir-parses` replaces the pipeline. **Four FAIL names that no green run prints:** `run_s16_optional_has_rest` reports `s16-optional-forward-call`, `s16-optional-overload-widen`, `s16-optional-nuch-defmethod` and `s16-rest-still-folds` on failure and a single `s16-optional-has-rest` on success, so a reader diffing a red run against a green one sees four names that do not exist in the baseline. **Skips reported as passes:** `run_s16_c1_bare_unsigned` prints `PASS  s16-c1-bare-unsigned (SKIP: no cc …)` when `cc` is missing and again when the oracle fails to build — a green verdict for a claim nothing checked; `require-cc` already existed and raises a real `skip!`. **Halves of a stated claim, unasserted:** the comment says "the two bare-specifier FUNCTIONS register at all" and the shell asked about one, and the `.nuch` round trip is of an overload SET while it asked about the `:optional` member only — both members are pinned in each; C2's reason was matched with `[0-9]+` where the header line belongs, so a reason recorded against the wrong declaration read the same, and the line is pinned. **Verified by breaking it**, five ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: a bad type name substituted for `raw`, the widened overload's answer changed, a `declare` arity narrowed, the recorded header line moved by one, and the C oracle asked for the wrong struct's size — that last one twice, because the first attempt swapped two structs that are both 16 bytes and so asserted nothing. `make test` 36 shell + 930 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a typed slot and a receiver accept 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a typed slot and a receiver accept"). 2 shell functions → 6 tests, 172 lines of the file retired (including both banners and two `spawn` lines): `run_s16_se_template_ref` (4 units) and `run_s16_sv1_struct_value_receiver` (2), into `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, which grows 2,383 → 2,561 lines. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 1,501 → 1,329 lines and 29 → 27 functions; `make test` is 41 shell + 925 native = 966. Not a new module: SE-1 is which values may occupy a typed slot and SV-1 which may occupy a receiver position, and `suite-s16.nuc` already owns what a place is. **An assertion the call site satisfied on its own.** SE-2's claim is that a constructor whose type variable appears only in its RETURN type is *stamped* once per instance, and the shell asserted it with `qgrep -F` on the mangled name — a substring the CALL line carries as well as the `define`. A module that named both instances and defined neither would have passed, which is exactly the unmonomorphized-template failure `run_builtin_tyname_resolvable` exists to catch and which is still live in the same file (`emit-defn` skips the define while the call site still names its ir-name, an undefined symbol only LLVM's parser catches). Pinned on `define` and by COUNT now, so a re-drain that doubles a stamp fails too; the perturbation is the shell's own assertion — drop the `define` anchor and the count goes to 2. **One `got` for four greps:** both retired verdicts reuse a single shell variable across their greps, so the FAIL branch printed the LAST diagnostic whichever claim had failed, a report naming the wrong refusal. Every claim is a separate `check-place-refused` now, each with its own line, where the shell pinned no location at all. **Needles that stopped before the actionable half:** the two temporary-receiver refusals were matched as `.set!: the receiver is a temporary struct value, so it has no address` and, shorter still, `addr-of: the receiver is a temporary struct value` — both dropping `-- bind it to a name first`, the only part telling the reader what to do; the argument-mismatch needle likewise dropped the `takes:` prefix that says which call is blamed. All are pinned whole. **Verified by breaking it**, six ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: two refusal lines moved, the `define` anchor dropped from a stamp count, the element-0 read asked for 2 instead of 1, a printed field changed, and a struct name misspelled in a missing-field message. `make test` 41 shell + 925 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a program pays for what it never uses 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a program pays for what it never uses"). 2 shell functions → 10 tests, 174 lines of the file retired (including both banners and two `spawn` lines): `run_s16_prelude_split` (7 units) into `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, which grows 2,276 → 2,383 lines, and `run_s16_gc_sections` (3) into `tests/suite-linking.nuc`, 452 → 557. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 1,675 → 1,501 lines and 31 → 29 functions; `make test` is 47 shell + 919 native = 966. Split across two modules rather than kept together, because the two halves are answered in different places: what the prelude emits is a property of one module, which is `suite-s16.nuc`'s question, and what survives `--gc-sections` is a property of the link, which is `suite-linking.nuc`'s. **A count where a set was meant.** `s16-defmacro-costs-no-runtime` compared the NUMBER of `define` lines in a plain program against a macro-defining one, and two different sets of the same size compare equal — so the claim, that a `defmacro` adds no definition because a macro body is JIT'd against the compiler's own copies, was not the one being checked. The FAIL branch already ran a proper `diff`, so the tooling for the right assertion was sitting in the error path. Both sets are compared with `check-line-set` now, and each is separately required to be exactly `main`. **Absence with nothing requiring a module:** the measured case grepped for the absence of five runtime definitions, which a module emitting nothing satisfies; it is an exact define-set now, so a program that lost its own `main` fails. `s16-sections-elf-only` had the same shape from the other end — `grep -c 'section "'` equal to zero under two foreign targets, which a compiler emitting nothing under `--target=` also satisfies — so both targets must now emit `define i32 @main() {` before the absence is asked for. **A string constant pinned by index:** the `.rodata` row matched `@.str.0`, which is `"_+"`, a macro-table entry and not this program's; the program's own `"%d\n"` is `@.str.91`, matched by CONTENT now, so the row survives any change to how many macro strings precede it. **What the shell never looked for:** `--gc-sections` also reclaims the unused `:const` global — `gc-ro` is absent from the linked binary and `R gc-ro` returns under `--no-gc-sections` — so a per-definition section reclaims data and not only code, a half that had no coverage at all. **Two library gaps filled** rather than worked around (§T6.6's rule): `define-names` and `build-checked-with`, both in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input; the second exists because a link-time claim needs the binary's PATH for `nm` and the only helper that took an extra driver flag returned the program's output instead. **Verified by breaking it**, ten ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: a runtime name added to an expected define set, the macro comparison pointed at a set that is not the plain program's, the printed answer changed, two refusal lines moved, the intern call misspelled, the no-call assertion inverted, a `.bss` prefix rewritten to `.data`, the foreign-target absence inverted, and the dropped symbol asserted to survive. `make test` 47 shell + 919 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a literal element and a selector may be 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a literal element and a selector may be"). 3 shell functions → 16 tests, 204 lines of the file retired (including the per-function banners and three `spawn` lines): `run_s16_keyword_literal_refused` (10 units), `run_s16_symbol_values` (4) and `run_s16_selector_rule` (2), into `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, which grows 2,049 → 2,276 lines. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 1,879 → 1,675 lines and 34 → 31 functions; `make test` is 57 shell + 909 native = 966.
