No new module: `suite-s16.nuc` already owns what a place is, and a selector is a place's field name; the literal half belongs beside it because the refusals are the same shape — a form that looks like data and is resolved as a type or a variable. **Nine claims under two verdicts.** `run_s16_selector_rule` is the hand-rolled `ok=1 … || ok=0` accumulator again: two `echo PASS` lines covered a runtime output comparison, a bare-symbol diagnostic, three fixed-position forms and a non-symbol selector, so any one of the nine failing lost the same single verdict and the report could not say which. Six units now, one claim each. **The same needle for three different diagnostics:** the three fixed-position forms were all checked with the identical string `field name must be a quoted selector -- write 'x`, yet the compiler prefixes each with its own form — `.set!:`, `addr-of:`, `_get:` — and the entire reason those forms share a rule is that the reader is told which one they hit; three diagnostics all saying `.set!` would have passed. Each is pinned with its prefix now. **A needle that stopped before the discriminating half:** the mixed-literal refusals grepped for `set literal: mixed element types`, which cannot tell one mixture from another. Three of the five messages go on to name the two conflicting types (`-- 'Keyword' and 'StrView'`) and two do not; the three are pinned in full and the two are recorded in `design/deferred/overview.md`, so the inconsistency has somewhere to land instead of being frozen by a needle that never looked at it. **A blob grep over a ten-line preamble:** every refusal here compiled a source with a ten- or eight-line import header and grepped the whole stderr, so a diagnostic raised anywhere in the preamble satisfied it — all sixteen units pin the line now; and `s16-kwlit-missing-import-names-file` was two independent greps (the head, and the path `lib/keyword.nuc`) which a note belonging to some other diagnostic satisfied, pinned as one record with its note. **Verified by breaking it**, sixteen ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: five mixed-literal messages reworded, four element-refusal heads changed, the intern call misspelled, the quoted-list nullability refusal inverted, the printed field values changed, and the four selector prefixes swapped between forms. The nullable-slot unit needed its SLOT retyped rather than its datum — `(quote 1)` is also `(raw Node)`, so changing the datum asserted nothing; `(raw Node)` → `(raw i32)` is what contradicts the claim. `make test` 57 shell + 909 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a prefix must not change 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a prefix must not change"). 3 shell functions → 12 tests, 192 body lines retired (234 lines of the file, including the per-function banners and three `spawn` lines): `run_w9_bare_ref_prefix` (5 units), `run_w9_template_env` (3) and `run_w9_dyn_solitary` (4), into `tests/suite-namespaces.nuc`, which grows 776 → 1,067 lines. No new module: these are W9 items 41 and 43, the same question the namespace suite already asks — item 43 is the BARE half of the rule B4 implemented for the qualified half (a generic is the one registry R2 keys bare, so a file defining `w43-helper (x:i64)` and calling `(w43-helper 3)` emitted a call to a LIBRARY's `w43-helper (x:i32)` reached through a prefix the call never spells: nothing unresolved, the wrong candidate simply scoring better on the literal), and item 41 is a protocol method's vtable slot reached through a qualifier. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 2,113 → 1,879 lines and 37 → 34 functions; `make test` is 73 shell + 893 native = 966. **A purely negative assertion, in a function that knew better.** `w9-dyn-solitary-dispatches-indirectly` asserted only that no direct call to `add-k` or `name-of` appears, which an empty module satisfies — and twenty lines below it the sibling unit `w9-dyn-solitary-qualified-reaches-slot` carries the comment "An empty module would pass a purely negative assertion, so require the indirect call to be there as well as the direct one to be gone", and does exactly that. The hazard was known and applied to one of the two units. The native form requires the three boxed dispatches by COUNT before refusing the direct ones, so an empty or shrunken module fails on the count. **Three substrings, one diagnostic never established:** `w9-template-error-blames-library` grepped stderr for a location, a message fragment and the words "while instantiating", with nothing tying them to a single record, and never checked the `requested at <caller>:2` half at all — which is the only part naming the instantiation site and the entire reason the note exists, since the blamed line is the LIBRARY's and without it the reader has no way back to the call that asked. Pinned as one record now, note included. **A `mv` replaced by the mechanism that exists for it:** the `.nuch` separate-compilation unit moved the library source aside mid-test so `resolve-import` could not prefer it over the header; `test-scratch-sub` exists for exactly this (its own comment in `lib/test.nuc` says so), so the source sits in `l/`, the header in `h/`, and only `h/` is a search directory for the consumer. **Verified by breaking it**, twelve ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: six expected values moved — one of them to 103, which is the defect itself, a bare call reaching the prefixed library — the emitted call's operand width changed to the library's `i32`, the template error's blame moved to the caller, the boxed-dispatch count asked for as four, the direct-call refusal inverted, and the printed answer changed by one. `make test` 73 shell + 893 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the shape of the import graph 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "the shape of the import graph"). 6 shell functions and one shared helper → 17 tests, 214 body lines retired (307 lines of the file, including the per-function banners, two `spawn` section banners and six `spawn` lines): `run_w9_root_hoist` (2 units), `run_w9_root_cycle_skip` (2), `run_w9_source_outranks_header` (2), `run_w9_string_path_prescan` (3), `run_w5e_private_isolated` (6), `run_w5e_still_rejects` (2) and `w9_run_single_emission`, into `tests/suite-imports.nuc`, which grows 773 → 1,178 lines. No new module: §T6.6's "when a name may be used before its file is" is this question — W9 item 1 is the root file's membership in the graph, item 6 a string-path import joining the same prescan, W5e the key a private name gets, and all three are the same walk. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 2,420 → 2,113 lines and 43 → 37 functions; `make test` is 85 shell + 881 native = 966. **The two root-graph units asserted nothing that told them apart.** The hoist and the ordinary cycle skip emit the SAME four definitions, and the shell checked an exit status plus "no symbol defined twice" — claims both units satisfy. Their exit codes differ only because the two fixtures compute `2+3` and `2+4`, which is arithmetic and not mechanism. What separates the paths is WHEN the root's own forms are written: a hoisted root is emitted at the re-entry, so `main` precedes the library's `lib-fn`; a shut window takes the ordinary skip, so `lib-fn` precedes `main`. `check-order` pins it, and the perturbation for each unit swaps its two arguments — both then fail, which is the discrimination the old pair could not offer at all. **The duplicate check was a pipeline that had to be guarded against its own success:** `w9_run_single_emission` greps `define` lines, sorts and takes `uniq -d`, with `|| true` on each pipeline, and the shell's own comment records why — under `set -euo pipefail` a grep that matches nothing exits 1, "which would kill the unit mid-way and lose its second PASS line silently (the harness only flags a result file that is entirely empty)". The native form is `duplicate-define-name`, which already existed and is used by `w1-diamond` and `w1-two-routes` a few hundred lines above in the same file, plus a presence anchor so an empty module cannot satisfy a duplicate check. **Two refusals could not tell which file was blamed:** `w5e-public-collision-rejected` and `w5e-ns-private-collision-rejected` grepped the whole stderr blob for the two file paths independently, so a diagnostic blaming either file passed. The compiler blames the SECOND file at its own line and names the first inside the message; both are pinned as one record now, together with the note that states the rule — the only thing distinguishing the public refusal from the namespace-private one, since the two heads are identical in shape. **Library gap filled** rather than worked around (§T6.6's rule): `check-order`, in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input. **Verified by breaking it**, seventeen ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: nine expected exit values moved by one, the two emission orders swapped, an emitted symbol misspelled, a suggested spelling changed, an `import: cannot find` head reworded, and the two collision messages pointed at the blamed file instead of the earlier one. `make test` 85 shell + 881 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a namespace does to a name 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a namespace does to a name"). 16 shell functions → 33 tests, 563 body lines retired (827 lines of the file, including the per-function banners and twenty-one `spawn` lines): `run_b0_import_use_flatten`, `run_b0_import_prefixed_fn` (2 units), `run_b1_prefix_file_scope` (2), `run_b2a_scope_diagnostic`, `run_b2a_import_use_binds_namespace`, `run_b2b_prefixed_values` (2), `run_b3_two_vectors` (3), `run_b3a_ns_type_generic`, `run_b3_ns_type_diagnostic`, `run_b4_qualified_generic` (3), `run_b4_qualified_template`, `run_b5_protocol_kind` (3), `run_b5_did_you_mean` (2), `run_b5_export_kinds` (3), `run_b6_dyn_cross_ns` and `run_b7_qualified_macro` (6), into a new eighteenth suite module `tests/suite-namespaces.nuc` (776 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 3,247 → 2,420 lines and 59 → 43 functions; `make test` is 102 shell + 864 native = 966. **A new module rather than an addition to the two that already exist**, because they ask different questions: `suite-modules.nuc` is about what a unit BOUNDARY does to a name (the redefinition rule), `suite-imports.nuc` about WHEN a name may be used (order and reachability), and this one about which spellings reach which definition given a file's imports — R1 (type identity is namespaced), R2 (a generic's methods stay merged across namespaces, so `import-use` of two libraries that each declare a `describe` keeps working) and R3 (a prefixed import binds its library under the PREFIX and not under the library's own namespace), one cell per kind of binding: function, global, constant, enum member, type, protocol, generic, template, macro, `(dyn P)` box. **The shell checked the note and never the diagnostic it hangs off.** `run_b2a_scope_diagnostic` grepped stderr for `note: 'dp' is not in scope in this file` and `In scope here: dpx.` — both of them notes. The error itself is `extend: unknown protocol 'dp/Describe'` at line 25 of the fixture, and neither its text nor its location was asserted at all, so any diagnostic anywhere in the run could have supplied the notes; `check-error-at` plus `check-note-at` match one record. **A needle that dropped the discriminating half:** `b3-two-vectors-distinct` exists to show `va/Vector` and `vb/Vector` are two type identities and not one name with two spellings, and the shell asserted `let: init type mismatch for 'q'` — the prefix of the message that stops one character before the type names. The whole message, `value is ptr:va/Vector, slot is vb/Vector`, is pinned now; the perturbation that rewrites `slot is vb/Vector` to `slot is va/Vector` is precisely the identity collapse the unit is about, and the shell's needle would not have noticed it. **The wrong file was being blamed and nobody could see it:** `b7-export-overload-refused` compiles a consumer, and the error is correctly raised against the FACADE's `export` line (`b5-eovfac.nuc:3`) — the consumer did nothing wrong, which is the useful half of the diagnostic — and a grep over the whole stderr blob cannot tell that from an error on the consumer. **Three claims the comments made and the assertions did not**, each of which is what separates a pair of units that otherwise agree: `b0-prefixed-fn-namespaced` and `b0-prefixed-fn-plain` both return 42, and the difference between them (`@b0ns__b0-triple` against a bare `@b0-double`) lives only in the module; `b6-dyn-cross-ns` now says the box identity is the canonical `%__dyn.b6dp_Describe` and that the consumer's prefix spelling produced no second box type; and `b4-qualified-template` counts exactly ONE `@b4g__b4_twice.i32` for two i32 call sites, which is the memo claim, and finds it owned by the template's namespace rather than the call site's. **One library gap filled** rather than worked around (§T6.6's rule): `check-no-diag-containing`, in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input. Five of these refusals carry an anti-degradation guard — the message must not fall back to "not defined anywhere in this compilation unit", which for a name that IS defined and IS reachable is false and sends the reader looking for a missing definition instead of a wrong spelling — and a `qgrep` for its absence over stderr was the only way to write it before. **Verified by breaking it**, thirty-three ways, one per unit, each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: twelve expected exit values moved by one, two emitted symbols misspelled, one stamp count asked for as two, the box identity asked for under the prefix spelling, four `In scope here:` lists changed, three blamed lines moved, two suggested spellings altered, the type mismatch's slot type collapsed onto the value's, the field owner swapped, the overload refusal's blame moved from the facade to the consumer, and the `binds 'zx' to` note's prefix renamed. **Carried forward, not resolved:** the mechanical sweep of dangling `run_*` names in `design/` now also covers `examples/w9-dyn-ns.nuc:56`, which names `run_b6_dyn_cross_ns` in a comment; a comment-only edit to an example emits byte-identical IR, so the sweep forces no snapshot re-take. `make test` 102 shell + 864 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — a machine that is not the host 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "a machine that is not the host"). 12 shell functions → 36 tests, 300 body lines retired (479 lines of the file, including the per-function banners, the nine-triple `for` loop and seventeen `spawn` lines): `run_target_triple` (9 units), `run_avr_emit` (4), `run_avr2_16bit` (4), `run_w9_gep_index_width` (3), `run_avr3_link` (2), `run_avr5_isr`, `run_avr6_fnvalue`, `run_avr6_const`, `run_avr7_f64` (3), `run_avr7_struct` (2), `run_riscv_emit` (2) and `check_long` (4), into `tests/suite-target.nuc`, which grows 362 → 1,078 lines. No new module: §T6.6's "what the target decides, not the compiler" is this question, and `riscv-emit` and RV-6's struct ABI are plainly the same subject; `check_long` lands here as category (a) said it would, being a target-triple ABI probe with IR greps rather than a diagnostic row. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 3,726 → 3,247 lines and 70 → 59 functions; `make test` is 135 shell + 831 native = 966. **The shell asked the module header, and the header is the part a broken compiler gets right.** `run_target_triple` grepped for `target triple = "<t>"` — a string the compiler was handed. Every defect in this lane emitted that line correctly and went on emitting for the host: a 64-bit GEP index on an 8-bit MCU, a Node cell sized 40 for a 16-bit pointer, a SysV eightbyte classification on a part with no register to hold one, soft-float libcalls against a hard-float ABI. None is an error; all are silent. So each triple unit now compares its datalayout against **clang's own answer for that triple** — from the same LLVM the compiler links against, so it is an oracle rather than a string quoted from a previous run — and requires the target's pointer width on **all six** GEP indices of W9 item 15's fixture, which is freestanding, so silence is a real assertion too. **The RV-1 gate was testing llc rather than the compiler:** `run_riscv_emit` passed `llc -mattr=+m,+a,+f,+d,+c` on the command line and then asked whether a hardware `mul` came out, which asks whether llc can lower the IR when told the features, not whether the features ever reached the compiler's own TargetMachine. `riscv-llc-features` disassembles the object `nucleusc -c` produced and keeps the bare-RV64I llc run as the control that shows the two answers differ at all. **A case the shell claimed and never ran:** `run_avr7_f64`'s step 2 is commented "`double` spelling — AVR rejects" and re-runs step 1's command on step 1's fixture. `f64` and `double` are separate branches of one test in `src/union-registry.nuc:368`, and the `double` branch had never been compiled for AVR at all. It is a real case now, against source written into the test's scratch directory rather than a new `tests/fixtures/*.nuc`, which would force an IR-snapshot re-take. **Three assertions got stronger because the native form could carry them.** `avr-objdump -f` reports `architecture: avr:35` for attiny1634 and `avr:103` for the avrxmega3-family avr32dd20, so pinning it is what says `--mmcu` — not `--mcpu` — supplied the device name at link; the shell asked `file` whether the ELF was "Atmel AVR 8-bit", which is the same answer for both devices. `avr3-link-*` pins `data = 0` exactly rather than "under 256", which is the prelude-free floor the fixture exists to hold. And `w9-gep-index-width-avr-parses` builds its own negative: the module it just emitted, with `, i16 %` rewritten to `, i64 %`, is the historical defect verbatim, and llvm-as must refuse it with `but expected 'i16'` — an acceptance check says nothing until the same checker is shown refusing the thing the unit is about. **Two conditional arms became real skips:** `run_avr_emit`'s llc arm printed nothing when llc was absent and `run_avr3_link` printed `SKIP`, so the shell's PASS total varied by host with no record of why; every toolchain-gated unit is now a `skip!` with a reason that `--no-skip` can refuse. **Library gaps filled rather than contorted around** (§T6.6's rule): `count-matching-lines` (`check-match` answers "at least one", which cannot tell six pointer-sized indices from one and five left behind), `check-ir-rejects` (the complement of `check-ir-parses`), and `replace-all` (so a unit can build the module that is wrong in exactly one respect) — all three in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which is not an IR-snapshot input, so no re-take. **Raised, not fixed:** `--target=avr` still emits `align 8` on 16-bit pointer slots, recorded in `design/deferred/overview.md`; it is a codegen change and the units that would gate it are the ones being ported here. **Verified by breaking it**, thirty-five ways covering all thirty-six units — the two `avr2-16bit-*` share one helper and fail together — each reverted by editing the source back, never with git: nine pointer widths moved off the target's, four `long` models swapped, two `llc` triples made unresolvable, the qq-helper's `malloc(i64 22)` restored to the host's 40, the `i64`-absence inverted, six GEP indices asked for as five, the two link units given each other's device family, `__vector_13` moved to 12, the fn-value diagnostic's line moved by one, `constant` changed back to `global`, the `double` case's parameter changed to `f32` so AVR accepts it, `float` to `double`, `mul nsw i64` to i32, the AVR struct parameter changed to the host's `i32`, `lp64d` to `lp64`, and the riscv multiply's operands changed by one register. `make test` 135 shell + 831 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — which C declaration wins 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "which C declaration wins"). 9 shell functions → 9 tests, 389 body lines retired (508 lines of the file, including the per-function banners, two `spawn` section banners and nine `spawn` lines): `run_w3a_opaque_provenance`, `run_w3a_sdl_mixer`, `run_w3b_quals`, `run_w3b_skip`, `run_w3b_sdl`, `run_w3c_typedef`, `run_w3c_precedence`, `run_w3c_declare_params` and `run_w3c_declare_header`, into a new seventeenth suite module `tests/suite-cdecl.nuc` (297 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 4,234 → 3,726 lines and 79 → 70 functions; `make test` is 171 shell + 795 native = 966. **One question in three parts** (Stage 15 W3a, W3b, W3c): which C declaration the importer believes, and which one it refuses to guess at. W3a settles that a tag no header defines is opaque and that saying so names the C declaration's own header and line; W3b that a qualifier is legal anywhere a declaration-specifier sequence allows it and after every `*`, and that a declaration recognized as a function but not faithfully describable is SKIPPED with a located warning rather than emitted for the LLVM parser to choke on much later; W3c that a typedef resolves to what it names rather than degrading to `ptr`, and that an explicit `(declare …)` beats a header-derived declaration of the same function in either order. **The mapping is exactly one-to-one** — nine functions, nine units — because each shell function was already a single subject; what changed is inside them. Seven used hand-rolled `ok=1 … || ok=0` accumulators, three of them over 19, 19 and 18 `grep` rows, so a failure named the function and never the row, and every row after the first went unreported. As `try`-shaped `check-line` assertions each row names itself, and each pins a WHOLE `declare` line rather than a substring, so a wrong parameter type fails in the row that carries it rather than passing on a matching prefix. **Two skips were being reported as passes:** `run_w3a_sdl_mixer` and `run_w3b_sdl` both printed `PASS … (SKIP: SDL2 not installed)`. `run_w3b_sdl` is the §1.5 accept criterion — `(import-use "SDL2/SDL.h")` reaches the x86 intrinsics headers transitively, where `void _mm_clflush(void const *)` imported as `declare void @_mm_clflush(void, ptr)` and killed the whole compilation at `failed to parse generated IR` — so on a machine without SDL2 the unit that exists to prove that criterion counted as green. Both are `skip!` with a reason now, and `--no-skip` can refuse them. **Pinning the location caught an error the shell could not have.** The precedence conflict warning is at line 6 of `w3c-declare-header-conflict.nuc`; I first wrote 5, copied from the sibling `w3c-prec-first` fixture, and the unit failed immediately. The shell's `grep` carried no location at all, so it would have accepted the same warning raised anywhere in the file. The conflicting *header's* path and line are genuinely host-dependent (`/usr/include/unistd.h:339` here), so that message is pinned as its two stable halves — the explicit signature and the "which declares it as … ; the explicit declaration wins" tail — rather than as the middle. **A pre-existing defect fixed by the deletion itself:** lines 1474-1479 were a Stage 13 L8 banner orphaned from `run_closure_cheader`, which had no banner of its own — the W3a block had been inserted between them. Removing the block reunites them. **Verified by breaking it**, nine ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back, never with git. Six restore the actual pre-fix shapes: `%Mix_Chunk = type { i32, ptr, ptr, ptr }` (the typedef'd `Uint32`/`Uint8` fields degraded to `ptr` — a wrong layout, silently), `@w3b_int_const(i32, ptr)` (the east qualifier eaten as the parameter NAME, leaving `*p` to start a phantom parameter — wrong arity and wrong ABI, accepted at every stage), `@_mm_clflush(void, ptr)` (the only spelling LLVM itself rejects), `declare ptr @w3c_f_u8()` (every scalar typedef resolving to `ptr`), `@lseek(i32, i64, i32)` (the header winning over the explicit declaration) and `@w3d_bare_5(i32, i32)` (an unnamed parameter list emitted as all-`i32`). The other three move the opaque-provenance line, the by-value-of-unknown-layout skip warning's line, and the matching-header `declare`. `make test` 171 shell + 795 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a function pointer is worth 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a function pointer is worth"). 6 shell functions → 17 tests, 308 body lines retired (366 lines of the file, including six section banners, three `spawn` banners and six `spawn` lines): `run_w9_fnptr_compare` (2), `run_w9_fnptr_align` (3), `run_w9_fnptr_null_init` (2), `run_s16_fp2_indirect_call` (4), `run_s16_fp4_cheader_fnptr` (4) and `run_s16_fp5_cheader_fnptr` (2), into a new sixteenth suite module `tests/suite-fnptr.nuc` (295 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 4,600 → 4,234 lines and 85 → 79 functions; `make test` is 180 shell + 786 native = 966. **Six claims about one value, from two stages.** W9 items 18, 19 and 20 settle what a function pointer IS to the compiler — it compares as a pointer, it occupies one TARGET pointer's width, and `null` reaches it in every position; Stage 16 FP-2, FP-4 and FP-5 settle what it is at the C boundary — an indirect call goes through the same argument path as a direct one, the C importer recovers a real TY-FN from all four declarator positions, and the export side renders a real declarator rather than `void*`, which is not merely unchecked but not standard C, since ISO C defines no conversion between a function pointer and `void *`. **A skip was being reported as a pass:** `run_s16_fp5_cheader_fnptr` ends with `echo "PASS s16-fp5-c-consumer (SKIP: no cc)"`, so on a machine with no C compiler the one unit that exists to prove the generated header satisfies a real conforming consumer counted as green. The native suite has a third verdict, so it is a `skip!` with a reason now, and `--no-skip` can refuse it — a release check that cannot accept an unproven header can say so. **Two units bundled several claims behind one `ok=` accumulator**, the hand-rolled pattern `lib/test.nuc` exists to replace: whichever grep failed first was the only one the FAIL line named and the rest went unreported. `s16-fp4-recovered-type-is-checked` is three separate refusals — a wrong callback signature named in full, an arity mismatch through a struct member, and a typedef'd `s16_cmp` slot — and `s16-fp2-indirect-call-abi` is three ABI shapes (`Pt` as two INTEGER eightbytes in registers, `Big` returned through `sret`, and an i32 literal widened to an i64 parameter exactly as on the direct path). As `try`-shaped assertions each names itself, which the perturbation moving only the MIDDLE refusal is what demonstrates. **The align unit's first four assertions are an invariant, not a count:** NO ptr-VALUED slot may claim `align 1`, matched on the value type so that `store i1 %x, ptr %y, align 1` — correct at one byte — stays out of it; `type-size` had no TY-FN case, which is free on x86-64 but makes a strict-alignment backend split every fn-pointer access byte-wise. **Verified by breaking it**, seventeen ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: five expected exit statuses moved; a function symbol, an `sret` struct name, a `declare` return type and a C declarator changed to shapes the compiler does not emit; the `align 1` invariant inverted from `check-not-match` to `check-match`; the 32-bit target's slot width changed back to the host's 8; `ret ptr null` changed to `ret ptr undef`; the vararg promotion changed from `double` to `float`; two diagnostic needles retargeted; the C consumer's expected answer changed by one; and `qsort` given a callback of the wrong signature, which the silent-compile unit reports as `qsort: argument 4 has type ():void, which does not match parameter type`. `make test` 180 shell + 786 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a conversion costs at a width boundary 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a conversion costs at a width boundary"). 5 shell functions → 16 tests, 117 body lines retired (193 lines of the file, including five section banners, the two `spawn` banners and five `spawn` lines): `run_w9_as_literal_narrowing` (2), `run_w9_as_float_literal_narrowing` (2), `run_w9_bool_unsigned` (2), `run_w9_unsigned_index` (4) and `run_w9_arg_coerce` (6), into a new fifteenth suite module `tests/suite-conversions.nuc` (217 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 4,793 → 4,600 lines and 90 → 85 functions; `make test` is 197 shell + 769 native = 966. **One question at five boundaries** (Stage 15 W9 items 8, 30, 31, 32, 33): when a value changes width or signedness, what may the compiler do silently, and what must it emit? Items 8 and 30 settle that `as` may never be STRICTER than the implicit coercion at the same slot — the explicit spelling was refusing `(as i8 5)` as lossy while `(let (a:i8 5) …)` accepted it and emitted the very same `trunc i32 5 to i8`; 31 makes bool unsigned so every consumer of `is-unsigned` agrees; 32 widens a GEP index by its own signedness; 33 stops a call argument no conversion reaches from being passed untouched. **The rule is a COMPARISON and the shell asserted one side of it.** `run_w9_as_literal_narrowing` checked only that the `as` spelling emits `trunc i32 5 to i8` — true of any lowering at all, and silent on the spelling it exists to be compared against. Both units now emit the implicit form beside it and require the same instruction: `(let (a:i8 5) …)` gives the identical `trunc i32 5 to i8`, and `(let (a:f32 1.5) …)` the identical `store float 0x3FF8000000000000` with no `fptrunc`, so the sentence the item settled is the sentence the test reads. **The bool gate was the g3-zero-cost shape again:** the shell asked only that no SIGNED instruction reached a bool — no `sext i1`, no `icmp s*`, no `sitofp i1` — which a module that emitted nothing satisfies equally well, while what item 31 fixed is which instruction gets PICKED. The unsigned ones are pinned by presence now: `zext i1` at both widths and both values, all four `icmp u*` orderings, `uitofp i1`, and the computed-bool `zext i1 %t… to i32`. That is the half a run on this host cannot witness — `sext i1` and `zext i1` differ only above bit 0 and every consumer tests `(!= x 0)`, which -1 and 1 both satisfy, which is exactly why the defect survived every bootstrap until it was measured on the instruction. `run_w9_unsigned_index` had the same absence-only shape, so the `zext i8`/`zext i16` that replaced the `sext` are pinned beside its absence. **The refusing complement was already native and stays where it is:** `w9-as-literal-too-big`, `w9-as-literal-signed-into-unsigned`, `w9-as-float-inexact`, `-runtime` and `-global-inexact` are rows of `tests/manifest/diagnostics.sexp`, so this module owns the accepting side and the artifact only. The two retired `spawn` banners carried the reasoning for those five rows — the integer pair holding the boundary at magnitude and at sign, the float trio holding the VALUE edge, the KNOWLEDGE edge and the second asker that re-derives — and no manifest row carried it, so it moved into the manifest above those rows rather than being deleted with the banners. **Verified by breaking it**, sixteen ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: six expected exit statuses moved; the two implicit-half instructions changed to ones the compiler does not emit; the `fptrunc` absence inverted; `icmp ult i1` changed to `icmp slt i1` and `zext i8` to `zext i24` (the two presence assertions the shell did not make); `sext i32` swapped with `zext i32` in both index units; three refusal needles changed to a message the compiler does not print; the pinned diagnostic line moved; and one refused call made LEGAL, which the unit reports as "expected a rejection …, but it compiled". Two line counts recorded for the globals batch were corrected here after re-measuring: `tests/run-tests.sh` went 5,348 → **4,793** (not 4,796), so that batch retired **555** lines of the file, not 552. `make test` 197 shell + 769 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **The native suite runs sharded across cores 2026-09-10.** `make test` had grown to 282s and was getting worse with every migrated batch, for a reason that had nothing to do with the migration: `test-run-all` was a plain `while` loop over the registration list, while the `tests/run-tests.sh` it replaces has always dispatched through `spawn` at `NUCLEUS_TEST_JOBS=$(nproc)`. Measured on 16 cores before the fix: the shell half ran 213 tests in **43.1s** at **409% CPU**; the native half ran 753 tests in **239.3s** at **88% CPU** — one core, and not even a full one, because each test blocks on the `nucleusc`/`clang`/linker children it forks. **The port was never the regression.** Total CPU per test *fell* by a factor of three, 0.83s → 0.28s, because a shell unit forks `grep`/`sed`/`mktemp` per assertion where a native one matches in-process — `sys` time is 90.9s of the shell half's 176.2s. The suite was doing strictly less work and taking five times as long to do it. **The fix is `--shard <i>/<n>`** in `test-main`: shards **stride** the registration list rather than take contiguous blocks, since neighbouring tests share fixtures and cost and a block split hands one shard an entire expensive family. `run-nuctests` fans out `NUCTESTS_JOBS` (default `nproc`) processes and concatenates their record files. **It needs no locking, and that is a property the suite already had rather than one added here**: every test's scratch directory is `build/out/nt/<test-name>` (`test-scratch-set`), `make-dir` already falls back to `dir-exists?` so the concurrent mkdir of the two shared parents is safe, and `test-duplicate-name` already refuses a suite in which two tests could claim one path — the comment there anticipating "a shard silently cover one test twice" predates the flag by several batches. A sharded `--run` is gated by `(= (% k nshards) shard)`, so `NUCTESTS_ARGS=--run <name>` yields one record rather than one per shard, and the unsharded default `n=1` makes that arithmetic a no-op. **Result: `make test` 282s → 82.8s**, the native half 239.3s → 36.3s. **Oversubscription was measured and rejected** — 32 shards take 37.1s and 48 take 39.1s, both *worse* than 16 — so the ~900%-of-1600% ceiling is contention (`fork`/`exec` and scratch-directory I/O inside each test), not scheduling slack, and the next real gain is removing the per-test subprocess rather than adding workers. Dynamic per-test dispatch (`xargs -P16`) does reach 31.5s, 15% better than the static stride, but pays 753 process startups for it; a thread pool would get that balance without the startup tax, and is deferred with its three concrete blockers — no thread primitives anywhere (`lib/process.nuc` is fork/exec only), the runner's process-wide `g-test-scratch`/`g-test-no-skip`/failure buffer, and record interleaving — in `design/deferred/overview.md`. **Verified rather than assumed:** all 16 shards' records concatenated give 753 records whose name set is exactly `--list`'s 753, no test twice and none missed; six malformed `--shard` arguments are refused with exit 2. **IR snapshot re-taken** (`snapshot --force`), because `lib/*.nuc` are snapshot inputs and this changes one: 6 of 2,684 artifacts moved — `lib/test.nuc`'s own five, plus `examples/self-test.nuc`'s `.ll`, which `import-use`s it. Confirmed benign before re-baselining by normalising `@.str.<N>` serials and re-diffing: the entire remainder is four new string literals, `test-run-all` loading `shard`/`nshards` where it had the constants 0 and 1, and the new `test-parse-shard` body. `lib/test.h` and `lib/test.nuch` regenerated (`check-headers.sh --fix`) for the changed signature. 753 native tests pass; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; `make bootstrap` converges.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a global is worth before `main` runs 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what a global is worth before `main` runs"). 10 shell functions → 32 tests, 409 body lines retired (555 lines of the file, including six section banners and ten `spawn` lines): `run_g0_value_order` (6), `run_g0_value_scoping` (5), `run_g0_cycle_values` (3), `run_g0_still_rejects` (5), `run_g1_fold_cross_file` (4), `run_g2_cheader` (1), `run_g2_nuch` (1), `run_g3_zero_cost` (1), `run_g3_library` (1) and `run_g4_order` (5), into a new fourteenth suite module `tests/suite-globals.nuc` (797 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 5,348 → 4,793 lines and 100 → 90 functions; `make test` is 213 shell + 753 native = 966. **One question in five steps** (global-init.md): G-0 registers `defvar`/`defconst`/`defenum` names on the same whole-graph walk that already carried signatures, so a value resolves on reachability rather than emission order; G-1 folds a constant initializer; G-2 adds `(array T N)` and constant aggregates; G-3 runs what cannot be folded from `@__nucleus_init`, and only when there is something to run; G-4 diagnoses the one thing none of that fixes — an initializer reading a global whose own initializer has not run. **The oracle is a VALUE almost everywhere**, which is the family's own argument: a global resolved to the wrong constant links and runs, and an initializer that never ran leaves a zero indistinguishable from success unless something reads it — the shell already knew this and ran every positive unit, so what the port adds is the artifact wherever the claim is about the artifact. **The zero-cost gate was half a test:** §4.8 requires a unit with no runtime initializer to emit *nothing*, and the shell asserted exactly that absence, which is equally true of a module that emitted nothing at all; the unit now pins all nine constant-initializer shapes as emitted globals, `.bss` versus `.data` included (a runtime-initialized global also lands in `.bss`, so the section is what separates "folded to zeros" from "left for the initializer"), and the complement adds one runtime initializer and requires those same nine to be UNCHANGED beside the new `@g3-rt = global i32 0` — which is what says the classifier moved one initializer rather than the file. **The library test could see only the effect:** `g3-library-nuch` links a `.nuch`-exported library object into a consumer whose `main` never calls anything and asserted the exit status, where the mechanism is in the object — `nm` must show `__nucleus_init` as a LOCAL symbol (each object runs its own), `g3-lib-n` as a `.bss` definition, and the two functions as the only external definitions, four rows compared as a set. The section the linker actually emits here is `.ctors`, not the `.init_array` the shell's comment names, so the unit pins `llvm.global_ctors` and the symbol table rather than a section name. **A suggestion nobody was reading:** `g0-ns-value-not-leaked` grepped for `undefined: G0-NSK` where the message is `undefined: G0-NSK (did you mean 'g0-nsa/G0-NSK'?)`, and the parenthetical — the spelling that would actually work — is the whole point of the diagnostic; likewise the duplicate-global note carries the two ways out (rename, or namespace it and import prefixed) and the G-4 note carries the ordering rule plus the cycle caveat, all three pinned now. **The collision pair distinguishes the two orders for the first time:** Stage 15 B5 made the noun depend on which definer is emitted first and the BLAMED FILE moves with it — order1 blames the `defvar` while naming the function, order2 the reverse — and `w1_reject_multi` matches a message with no location, so the shell saw only half of the property its own comment describes. **Verified by breaking it**, thirty-two ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: seventeen expected exit statuses moved; the two collision nouns swapped with each other; four pinned diagnostic locations moved; one negative assertion inverted; the did-you-mean spelling changed to the namespace it is not; `weak_odr` dropped from a cross-file global; the C header's named extent replaced by its value; the exported array `extern` narrowed to a pointer; `.bss` changed to `.data` on the folded zeros; and `__nucleus_init`'s symbol type raised from local to global. `make test` 213 shell + 753 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — when a name may be used before its file is 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "when a name may be used before its file is"). 13 shell functions → 33 tests, 318 body lines retired (462 lines of the file, including five rationale headers, two section banners and thirteen `spawn` lines): `run_w1_mutual` (2), `run_w1_ns` (2), `run_w1_graph_shapes` (3), `run_w1_still_rejects` (2), `run_w1_declare_cycle_breaker` (2), `run_w1d_cycle_accepts` (5), `run_w1d_cycle_diagnoses` (7), `run_w1d_path_prefix` (4), `run_w1_deferred_union_payload` (1), `run_w1_late_overload_symbol` (1), `run_w1c_unreachable_file` (2), `run_w1c_defined_nowhere` (1) and `run_w1c_unreachable_type` (1), into a new thirteenth suite module `tests/suite-imports.nuc` (773 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 5,811 → 5,348 lines and 113 → 100 functions; `make test` is 245 shell + 721 native = 966. **The three sub-families are one walk:** W1a registers every REACHABLE file's signatures, protocols and type names before emitting anything, so a cross-file reference stopped depending on import order; W1d then made a mutual import pair legal, because once that prescan exists a cycle has nothing left to break; W1c is what survives, since with order gone an unresolved name is a typo, a genuinely absent symbol, or §2.7's reachability constraint. `w1_run` itself stays in the shell — forty other functions call it — so only the W1 units and their banner went, and the banner was rewritten to describe the helper rather than the retired block. **A vacuous test, found by running its fixture:** `run_w1_deferred_union_payload` writes a program whose first line is `(compile-time (printf "ct ran\n"))`, and `printf` left the prelude in the split, so the compile dies at line 1 with `unknown: printf` and never reaches the `(import-use string)` whose deferred union payload is the entire subject — the shell asks only whether stderr contains `undefined type`, so a *different* error every time reads as PASS. The native unit uses `(compile-time (+ 1 1))` (same structure, no prelude dependency) and asserts the real property: the compile is silent and the module parses, since `use of undefined type named '__anon_union_…'` is the LLVM parser's own message and `check-ir-parses` is what asks it; the perturbation restores the shell's line verbatim and the unit fails on the `unknown: printf` the shell passed over. **A dangling `Either` in a diagnostic, fixed rather than pinned:** `cycle-definer-message` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`) built its note as `"… back-imports. Either " + import-cycle-advice()`, producing "Either break the cycle -- …" with no `or` — the "Either …, or …" scaffolding belongs to `cycle-layout-message`, which has a second alternative to offer (use a pointer) where a macro has none; it now reads "The fix is to break the cycle -- …", keeping the advice text in one place. Nothing pinned it because the shell grepped the message and never read the note. **Three claims the shell's own comments made and its bodies never tested:** the graph-shape comment says the walk dedups on resolved path so a file reached twice is prescanned once, asserted only through an exit status that a doubled emission does not change (`duplicate-define-name` now states it over the whole module); the W1b comment says prescanning under the importer's namespace would "mangle it under the wrong prefix" and then checked only that the program returned 42 (both `@w1alpha__a-thing` and `@w1beta__b-thing` define lines are pinned now, in both import orders); and the W1e comment names `emit-nuch-declare-import`'s "already in g-globals" early return as the thing under test, so the module must carry the `define` and no `declare` beside it. **The by-value cycle case pins the signature, not just the answer:** its old failure was a silent miscompile — with no layout the ABI classifier saw a zero-sized aggregate and emitted `define … @w1-bcb(i0 %v.arg)` — so pinning `(i64 %v.arg.0, i64 %v.arg.1)`, two SysV INTEGER eightbytes, says the layout prescan ran where the exit status only says the call happened to work. **Nine stderr greps became nine located records:** both duplicate-definition refusals now name the blamed definer AND the file:line of the other one, which the message carries and `qgrep -F "duplicate definition of 'w1-dupe'"` discarded; the cycle macro and `deferror` refusals pin the note that names both cycle members, which is what proves the compiler identified the cycle rather than guessing; the path-prefix collision pins the binding already in force; both W1c notes pin the unreachable definer's path as a field; and `w1c-defined-nowhere`'s "and no note" is a search of the rendered diagnostic list rather than of stderr, so a note attached to any diagnostic fails it. **Verified by breaking it**, thirty-three ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: eleven expected exit statuses moved by one; the namespace prefix changed to a name nothing mangles to; four pinned diagnostic lines moved; two negative assertions inverted; the `%W1SC` type widened; the by-value signature narrowed to `i32`; one variadic-shaped call operand retyped; the `w1d` stdout changed to `w1e`; and the `compile-time` line restored to the shell's own. `src/nucleusc.nuc` is not an IR-snapshot input (the script says so at line 51), so the diagnostic fix forces no re-take. `make test` 245 shell + 721 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what the target decides, not the compiler 2026-09-10** (§T6.6 "what the target decides, not the compiler"). 3 shell functions → 12 tests, 289 body lines retired (304 lines of the file, including three rationale headers and three `spawn` lines): `run_c4_target_headers` (4), `run_platform_constants` (4) and `run_rv6_fp_abi` (4), into a new twelfth suite module `tests/suite-target.nuc` (363 lines). `tests/run-tests.sh` is 6,115 → 5,811 lines and 116 → 113 functions; `make test` is 278 shell + 688 native = 966. **Three stages, one subject:** what `size_t` is in a declaration (Stage 16 C4), what `O_CREAT` equals (Stage 17 platform constants) and how a struct of two floats is passed (Stage 14 RV-6) are all the target's to answer, and each was once answered by something written into the compiler instead — the host's headers under every `--target=`, a hardcoded constant table in `lib/file.nuc`, and the SysV classifier for every machine. **So no oracle is a number quoted in the test:** the C4 lane reads the widths back out of the emitted module (avr's `size_t` is `i16` where the host's is `i64`, with glibc's `__memcmpeq` as the independent witness that the host's text was *not* read after all); the constants lane compares against a C program clang builds from the same headers under `-Wall -Werror`, so the reference is the C compiler rather than this container; and the RV-6 lane names both triples explicitly and pins shapes derived from `clang --target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu` on structurally identical C. **A skip is a verdict about the environment, not a hole:** two units decide which lane they are by asking `clang -E --target=<t> -include string.h` whether the target's headers are reachable, so `c4-avr-target-headers` skips without them and `c4-missing-sysroot-falls-back` skips *with* them — a fallback cannot be observed on a triple that needs no fallback; here avr's are installed and i386's are not, so both run, where the shell hardcoded the same assumption and would simply have failed on a container where it did not hold. **Two `2>/dev/null`s hid two diagnostics:** the riscv64 lane emits C4's fallback warning (no riscv sysroot here) and the shell discarded stderr wholesale, which also discarded any error, so `rv6-ir` uses `check-no-errors` rather than `check-silent` — *which* triple lacks a sysroot is a property of the container, but an error would be a property of the compiler either way. **Nineteen `grep -E`s over `define` lines became nineteen whole lines:** the shell matched `^define .*@f_pair` and the like, so an argument list that changed shape while keeping its name passed; the 8 flattening shapes, 10 register-counting shapes and 8 x86 anti-leak shapes are now exact `check-line` assertions including the `section` attribute — the shell checked five of the x86 lines and a leak in any of the other three would have gone unseen. The variadic tail asked that no `float`/`double` appear anywhere in the `printf` call, which a call that lost its arguments also satisfies; `check-match` on the whole call now pins the five operand types in order, leaving only the SSA serials to `*`. **Six refusals that "the compile failed" became six located `undefined:`s:** the reserved `_MP_BASE`, the function-like `MP_FN`, the string `MP_STR`, the float `MP_FLOAT`, the logical `MP_LOGIC` and the decimal-overflow `MP_OVER` each now name the line that used them, so an unrelated error cannot stand in for the refusal; the clang-predefine case reads the first letter-initial `#define` out of `clang -E -dM` rather than naming one, since which it is is the platform's business (`linux` here) — and a leading-underscore predefine would be refused by the underscore rule instead, testing nothing. **Verified by breaking it**, twelve ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: `strlen` → `strlenx`; avr's `i16` → the host's `i64`; the fallback warning's `HOST` → `host`; a pinned line 1 → 2; one digit in the admitted-macro output; `MP_FN` → the admitted `MP_PUB`, which produces no diagnostic at all; `8` → `9` for the private macro; `O_CREAT` → `O_EXCL` in the C reference alone, where the printed 64 vs 128 shows the oracle really is read from clang; `f_pair`'s `i64` → `i32`; `fpr7_dd`'s integer-convention pair → the x86 double pair; one variadic `i64` → `double`; and `f_farr2`'s x86 `<2 x float>` → the riscv flattened pair. `make test` 278 shell + 688 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a place is 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what a place is"). 3 shell functions → 9 tests, 263 body lines retired (281 lines of the file, including three rationale headers and three `spawn` lines): `run_s16_dot_forms_retired` (3), `run_s16_set_places` (3) and `run_s16_set_generic` (3), into the existing `tests/suite-s16.nuc` rather than a new module — the Stage 16 language surface is where they belong. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 6,396 → 6,115 lines and 119 → 116 functions; `make test` is 290 shell + 676 native = 966. **The three are one ruling read three ways** (dot-forms.md §5 steps 4-6): `.` and `.&` are gone, `set!` takes a place, and a computed key dispatches through the `set` generic — with every retired spelling RESERVED, so the answer is the replacement rather than "undefined function" and no user definition can shadow it into silence. **The claim the shell's own header made and its body never tested:** "every place delegates to the writer it replaced, so the spellings must emit identical IR", and then the unit only checked that a program printed the right numbers, which four different code paths could equally do; `s16-set-places` now compiles one function four ways (`(set! (p 'y) 2)`, `(set! (get p 'y) 2)`, `(set! (_get p 'y) 2)`, `(set! (deref (addr-of p 'y)) 2)`) and compares the modules whole — `compile-source` writes every fixture to the same `t.nuc`, so `; ModuleID` and `source_filename` already agree and `check-golden` rules on the artifacts rather than on filtered ones, and all four are byte-identical. **Twelve `qgrep -F`s over stderr became twelve located diagnostics:** each retirement message, the two reserved-name refusals, the two not-an-assignable-place refusals, the two missing-field refusals and the missing-`set`-method refusal is now one `check-error-at` against one record, so the message and its line must come from the same diagnostic; `()` in a place position keeps its own row, since it reads as a null node and the place judgement has to precede any dereference of it. **One accumulator per function became one assertion per claim:** each shell unit threaded an `ok=1` variable through three to seven probes and printed a single FAIL naming none of them, dumping up to ten lines of concatenated stderr; the nine units now fail at the assertion that failed, with wanted and got. **Verified by breaking it**, nine ways — one per unit — each reverted by editing the source back: a retirement message with `2-argument` changed to `3-argument`; a pinned line 2 → 3; one digit changed in each of the five programs' expected output; a pinned line 5 → 4 on the missing-method refusal; and one of the four place spellings given a different constant, where `check-golden` names the differing IR line and both store instructions. `make test` 290 shell + 676 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what an identifier may be in generated IR 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what an identifier may be in generated IR"). 1 shell function → 3 tests, 114 body lines retired (130 lines of the file, including its rationale header and one distant `spawn` line): `run_w9_ir_name_positions`, into an eleventh suite module `tests/suite-ir-names.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 6,526 → 6,396 lines and 120 → 119 functions; `make test` is 299 shell + 667 native = 966. **W9 item 39:** LLVM's unquoted identifier has two rules — a body character class and a first-position rule — and the compiler applied only the first, only to global symbols, so the failure was at IR-parse time on a line of generated IR the user never wrote. **The shell asserted only that the programs printed the right numbers:** it never read the IR, so it could say the escape happened but not what it was; each unit now pins the escaped name in every position it occupies — type, union arm, global, function, parameter, `let` binding, match binder and `label`/`goto` target — as whole IR lines, with the `.addr.N` scope serial left a glob and everything around it exact. **`check-ir-parses` is the assertion the item is actually about:** `--emit-llvm` never reads back what it writes, so exiting 0 says nothing about whether the module is valid, and being invalid was the bug — handing it to `llvm-as` states that directly, and editing `_QMARK` back to `?` in an emitted module reproduces the item's own quoted error, `define i32 @add_QMARK(i32 %ok?.arg, i32 %n!.arg)`, at that column. **A regex sweep became the header line by line:** the C-callable unit asserted `! grep -v '^/\*' … | qgrep -E '\b[0-9]+[A-Za-z_]'`, which passes on a header that declares nothing at all, and now pins each declaration (`#define _2LIM 9` included), the six symbols `nm` finds in the object as a set, and the absence of any `asm` label — that last being the actual ruling, since C's first-position rule is identical, `sanitize-for-c` escapes with the same `_`, the C spelling and the link symbol agree, and no label is needed; the consumer also builds under `-Wall -Werror` where the shell ran plain `clang`. **One helper moved rather than being copied:** `CLib` and its `c-lib`/`c-lib-run`/`c-consumer-run` machine (write a library, emit its header beside it, compile its object, link a C consumer against it) lived in `tests/suite-cheader.nuc` and now has a second caller, so its 82 lines moved to the shared layer in `tests/nuctests.nuc` and are documented in `docs/testing.md` — a pure move, no behaviour changed. **Verified by breaking it**, six ways in the source, each reverted by editing it back: the `@_2count` global asserted in its unescaped spelling; `%ok_QMARK.arg`/`%n_BANG.arg` asserted as `%ok.arg`/`%n.arg`; the `%_2acc.addr.*` glob with its escape removed; `check-not-contains "asm("` inverted; one symbol dropped from the `nm` set, which fails on the count; and one digit changed in a program's expected output. `make test` 299 shell + 667 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the declarations a header mode must refuse 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "the declarations a header mode must refuse"). 2 shell functions and their shared helper → 48 tests, 212 body lines retired (250 lines of the file, including the two rationale headers and two distant `spawn` lines): `run_w9_header_validation` (20) and `run_w9_empty_name_position` (28), into a tenth suite module `tests/suite-refusals.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 6,776 → 6,526 lines and 122 → 120 functions; `make test` is 302 shell + 664 native = 966. **The two go together** because they are one defect counted twice: both header modes run the compiler's PRESCAN layer and never its EMISSION layer, and every prescan defers its diagnosis to emission, so with no emitter downstream nothing ever asked and a truncated definer or a `()` in a name position reached a raw `(node-at form N)` dereference — W9 item 38 found it through the header modes, item 45 through `--emit-llvm`, and the fix is the same walk. They also shared one shell helper, `w9_header_agrees`, so retiring either alone was not possible. **The three-way agreement became a comparison of records rather than of stderr blobs:** the shell captured each mode's stderr and required the three strings equal, and under `--diagnostics=sexp` the same claim is `diag-list-text` over three `(Vector Diagnostic)`s (severity, file, line and message per record) — plus, per unit, the located message itself through `check-error-at`, which the string comparison could not do, since three modes agreeing on a *wrong* message passed. That is 44 compiler messages now stated in the suite rather than deferred to whatever the compiler happens to say. **`Compiled` gained `exit`:** `ok?` is `success?`, which cannot tell 1 from 139, and the whole of item 38 is that a segfault also produces no output, so "refused" and "crashed" were indistinguishable to a helper reading `ok?`; the field is `exit-code` (128+signal for a signalled child) and both header modes are required to exit exactly 1. **Two boundaries, asserted rather than assumed:** a BODY error is out of scope by construction (no body is read), so `--emit-llvm` refuses `(defn w38-body (x:i64):i32 (return (as i32 x)))` and both header modes still succeed, and `deferror`/`def-rmacro` are refused by `--emit-llvm` and rightly silent in the header modes — the shell asserted only the `--emit-llvm` half of each and left the rest to a comment, where `check-probe-llvm-only` asserts both, so a crash in either mode is still a failure. **The two negative controls became whole artifacts:** `w38ok.nuc` was checked with four `qgrep -F` substrings and `w45ok.nuc` with two, and both headers of both are now pinned line by line — which is what makes the template visible as the one declaration the two surfaces disagree about, each rightly (a `.nuch` consumer can stamp `w38-tw` from the body it carries, and C has nothing to stamp it into, so the C header says `/* w38-tw: generic template; not exported */`); reading the C spelling of the inline union is likewise the only way to see the walk descended into it rather than skipping a field it could not name. **Verified by breaking it**, seven ways, each reverted by editing the source back: a message with one letter changed (`defn: bad form` → `bad forms`); a fixture's pinned line 7 → 8; one character off a generated C header line (`} W38Box;`); routing `deferror` through the refusal helper rather than the boundary one, which fails on `--emit-cheader exited 0, not 1`; the required exit 1 → 2; the stdout-must-be-empty check inverted to `check-non-empty`; and the three-way comparison itself inverted, which fails every unit with both lists printed. An eighth attempt was invalid and is worth recording: a file carrying a signature error *and* a body error does not make the modes disagree, because the signature error aborts before the body is reached. `make test` 302 shell + 664 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what two objects owe each other 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what two objects owe each other"). 3 shell functions → 12 tests, 214 body lines retired (251 lines of the file, including the three rationale headers and three distant `spawn` lines): `run_w9_lib_standalone` (4), `run_w9_multi_object` (3) and `run_w9_shared_init_warning` (5), into a ninth suite module `tests/suite-linking.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 7,027 → 6,776 lines and 125 → 122 functions; `make test` is 350 shell + 616 native = 966. **The three go together** because a `.nuc` import is INLINED, and every consequence of that is a question about two objects rather than one: which of the duplicated copies the linker keeps, which definitions a unit owns and exports, and the one thing the compiler cannot decide alone — a run-time initializer on a global the unit does not own, which every importing object runs again on the one shared global. `lib/` is the corpus all three are about, since `make lib-so` links all 43 objects. **A regex over `nm` became the whole symbol table:** the shell asked two questions of the two objects (is `w9-bump` weak in both, is `w9-side-bump` undefined in main.o) with `grep -cE ' [WV] w9-bump$'` and `qgrep -E '^ +U w9-side-bump$'`; the objects have five symbols and four, and every one is now pinned, so the unit also states that `w9-count` is a single weak *object* rather than two private ones, that `w9-get` is a copy too, and that `main` and `w9-side-bump` are the only strong definitions either object carries — `nm-typed` normalises `nm` to `<type> <name>` lines and `check-line-set` compares them as a set, since `nm` sorts by value and every symbol in a relocatable object has value 0. **The shell's own argument, tested, turned out to be sharper than the shell's test:** its comment says a linker that kept two private copies would also link, so 1 + 2 = 3 is "reachable only if the two objects share one `w9-count`" — making the global `defvar-` disproves that, because the program still prints 3, the one weak `w9-bump` that survived carrying its own file's copy with it; the symbol table catches it exactly (`V w9-count` becomes `b w9share_p1__w9-count`), so the value and the symbol table are complementary rather than redundant and the ported comment now says which answers which. **Two search directories became one:** the shell used `-I inc -I share` with the generated `.nuch` in a third directory, but what is load-bearing is only that `w9side.nuc` is NOT on the search path (`resolve-import` tries `.nuc` in every search directory before any `.nuch`, so the source would win and the call would stop crossing the object boundary), and `-I` takes one directory at a time (`nucleusc.nuc:18859`). **Diagnostics instead of stderr greps:** all five arms of the initializer family are about what the compiler said — `qgrep -F "dshare.nuc:3: warning: defvar: …"` for the one that warns, `[ -z "$out" ]` for the three that must not — and under `--diagnostics=sexp` these are `check-warning-at` against one record (severity, file and line matched together) and a new `check-silent`, which reports the diagnostics it found; `check-no-errors` is not that claim, since a *warning* is what three of these units assert the absence of. **One assertion the shell skipped and one it could not make:** its `-c` loop over `lib/` did `continue` when a file would not compile at all, and the emit loops gate that, so this one now requires the object too; and `w9-linkage-ownership` read `define internal …` out of the IR, which is a word rather than a property, so the unit now also compiles the object and pins its five linkable names — the private definer's absence from the symbol table is what says `internal` meant something. The `_pN` counter stays a glob, since N is the 1-based creation order of files that own private names (`nucleusc.nuc:5323`) and moves when an unrelated file gains a `defn-`. **Shared infra, deduplicated:** four functions in `tests/nuctests.nuc` repeated the same fifteen lines of "run a prepared command and read the result" and three more repeated "run the binary just built", now `compiled-run`, `run-stdout` and `run-exit` — which is what made `emit-checked`, `object-checked` and `build-checked` eight lines each rather than another three copies; `driver-link` split from `driver-link-run` for the same reason, since a program whose answer is an exit status links identically to one whose answer is stdout. **Verified by breaking it**, seven ways, each reverted by editing the source back: feeding the duplicate-`define` scanner a module concatenated with itself (`lib/allocator.nuc defines @arena-init twice`); the `defvar-` above; writing `w9side.nuc` onto the search path, which turns `U w9-side-bump` into a weak inlined copy; `defn-` → `defn` on the private definer; a compile-time initializer for `d-runs`, which fails all three of the units that measure it; pointing the owner-silence unit at the importing file; and a temporary `lib/zz-share.nuc` + `lib/zz-user.nuc` pair carrying a real shared initializer, plus a `lib/zz-broken.nuc` whose exported signature names an unresolvable type — that last one correcting a wrong first attempt, since a file whose *body* calls an undefined function passes `--emit-nuch` and `--emit-cheader` (neither mode compiles bodies), so the perturbation has to be in the signature. `make test` 350 shell + 616 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — a mangled name on every export surface 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "a mangled name on every export surface"). 3 shell functions → 17 tests, 270 body lines retired (303 lines of the file, including three section banners and two distant `spawn` lines): `run_ns6` (6), `run_sm3` (6) and `run_w9_ns_symbol_ownership` (5), into an eighth suite module `tests/suite-exports.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 7,330 → 7,027 lines and 128 → 125 functions; `make test` is 362 shell + 604 native = 966. **One claim in three spellings:** a public name the compiler mangles — by namespace (`geom/area` → `geom__area`), by `?`/`!` (`full?` → `full_QMARK`), or by which namespace OWNS it — must be the same symbol on all three export surfaces, and a separately compiled consumer must link against the object and run. They also share one machine (write a library, emit `.ll`/`.nuch`/`.h`, write a consumer, emit its IR, link the two objects, compare stdout), so the port replaces three hand-rolled copies — which had drifted apart in their `import` spellings and in what they bothered to check — with `Exports` + `Consumer` + `link-two`. **Every emit in all three units was `2>/dev/null || true`:** a failed emit left an empty file, the greps then failed, and the unit reported a missing symbol rather than the compiler error that caused it; `emit-for-file` fails with the diagnostic, and where `clang … 2>/dev/null && [ "$(bin)" = "…" ]` reported one FAIL for a link error and a wrong answer alike, `link-run` prints clang's output and `check-eq` prints want against got. **Ten assertions got stronger, none weaker:** the header checks were unanchored substrings (`qgrep 'geom__area'` matches `geom__areaX`) and are now exact declaration lines; `n6-cheader-c-legal` gained the object side, since a header name is only right if something defines it; `n6-nuch-carries-ns` asserted `(ns geom)` alone and now also both `(declare …)` lines, which carry the BARE spelling the importer re-keys; `n6-import-resolves-mangled` checked `area` and not `perimeter`, and now pins both numbered call lines; `sm3-lib-symbols` and `sm3-nuch-roundtrip` moved from `-F` substrings to whole lines; and `w9-ns-symbol-ownership` gained the `weak_odr` linkage of the imported definition, which is what lets two objects that each inline it link at all. **Three assertions the surfaces demanded and nobody made:** `.` is no more a C identifier character than `?` is, so an overloaded `?` method needs two right answers in the header — the sanitized spelling and the real symbol on an `asm` label — and `sm3-cheader-fn-legal` checked neither for the pair, where it now pins `int32_t even_QMARK_i32(int32_t x) asm("even_QMARK.i32");` and its `i64` twin; the same gap existed for the namespaced type, so `b3-ns-type-export-surfaces` now pins `int32_t gt__pt_sum(struct gt__Pt* p) asm("gt__pt-sum");`; and `sm3-cheader-typenames` checked four spellings of the mapping but not the union tag enum, so `enum Shape_QMARK_tag` and both arm constants are rows now. **One capability:** `ir-symbols` + `check-same-symbols` replace `grep -o … | sort -u` into two files plus `cmp`, comparing by mutual containment rather than a sort so the answer does not depend on where in each module a symbol first appears, and printing both sets when they differ — the shell's argument for comparing sets rather than asserting literals is preserved, because it is right. **A discovery, from a diagnostic that was already good:** an unprefixed `(import "path")` binds the library under the FILE STEM, not under the `(ns …)` the file declares — the shell's fixtures worked only because their names and namespaces happened to agree, and naming them `w23o-w23a.nuc` broke `w23a/describe` with `'w23a' is not in scope in this file`, the note listing the stem; the same holds for a generated `.nuch` imported bare, which is why `export-surfaces` names its artifacts after the library. Recorded in `context/build.md`. **Verified by breaking it**, five ways, each reverted by editing the source back: a `geom__area` → `geom_area` header spelling; dropping the `asm` label from an overloaded declaration; one wrong digit in the linked program's expected output; asking for a symbol base neither module defines, which fires the non-empty guard; and comparing the two modules on the base `@w23`, whose sets genuinely differ (`alone: @w23a__describe / together: @w23a__describe @w23b__describe`) — the set comparison itself working rather than its guard. Two `.nuc` fixture comments were retargeted off `tests/run-tests.sh`; they are IR-snapshot inputs, and the snapshot verifies byte-identical, so no re-take. `make test` 362 shell + 604 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the C declarator shapes a header parser drops 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "the C declarator shapes a header parser drops"). 2 shell functions → 8 tests, 289 body lines retired (307 lines of the file, including both section banners): `run_cd_declarators` (6) and `run_cd4_declarator_list` (2), into a seventh suite module `tests/suite-declarators.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 7,637 → 7,330 lines and 130 → 128 functions; `make test` is 379 shell + 587 native = 966. **The pair goes together** because it is one claim in two halves: a C declaration is shared specifiers plus a *list* of declarators, each carrying its own pointer depth, extents and bit-field width — CD-1/2/3 is the list inside a declaration, CD-4 the list after an aggregate body — and the retired L family's own comment already named this pair as sharing its methodology. **Five assertions got stronger, none weaker:** the three CD-2 signatures were prefix regexes (`^define i32 @f_ta\(i32 ` says nothing about the rest of the line) and are now the whole `define` line including the section; CD-4's array storage was the substring `global [3 x %__carr.cd4_G] zeroinitializer` and is now the whole `@gv = …, align 8` line; `cd1-mixed-pointer-refused` matched its located error with the path wildcard `[^ ]*cd-declarators\.h:47` and now goes through `check-opaque-at`, pinning the resolved path, the line, the message and the absence of any `%cd_mixed_ptr = type`; and both "the import was silent" checks were `[ -s "$d/m.err" ]` on a file the shell also appended link errors to later in the same unit, where they are now `check-empty` over the compile's own stderr. **Four assertions the shell's comments claimed but never made:** CD-4's table covered 8 of the 11 types, so `cd4_E`, `cd4_F` and the minted `%__carr.cd4_G` element are now rows — which is what says an array declarator in a later position anchors on the *body* rather than a placeholder; the comment on `cd4_Ep` said a pointer declarator must be a typedef-table entry "NOT a second StructDef, or it shadows the record" and asserted neither half, so `%cd4_Ep = type` must now be absent and `@f_ep` must take a `ptr`; and CD-3 gained `%cd_tag` and `%__carr.cd_anonarr`. **The `tcp_info` witness asks three questions instead of one:** size and the struct's own alignment are both unchanged by a bit-field run that consumed the wrong storage, so it now also prints the offsets on either side of the run (`tcpi_options` at 5, `tcpi_rto` at 8) from both compilers — member offsets on an imported C struct work on the Nucleus side, which is what makes the stronger oracle available. **Two skips replace two green ticks:** both `sizeof`-vs-`cc` units ran their oracle inside `if command -v cc` and echoed `PASS … (SKIP: …)` when it was absent, and now open with `(try (require-cc))`; the `tcp_info` unit probed for `netinet/tcp.h` by trying to build its C oracle and echoing PASS on failure, and now syntax-checks the probe with clang (a hard dependency, so the probe never skips for its own reasons). **Verified by breaking it**, four ways, each reverted by editing the source back: widening `%cd_bits` to `[2 x i8]` failed the type table; moving the opaque row's declared line from 48 to 47 printed the real diagnostic beside the expectation; inverting the `%cd4_Ep` absence to a presence check failed, which is how a negative assertion is shown to be about something; and adding 1 to the `tcpi_rto` offset on the Nucleus side reported ``line 3 differs / want "tcpi_rto 8" / got "tcpi_rto 9"``, also the proof that both compilers really ran. **Raised, not absorbed: 59 dangling `run_*` names in `design/`** — four batches of this migration have retired shell functions that the Stage 15 and Stage 16 documents still name as the live gate in "where the gate lives" tables. This batch retargeted its own two rows and the three fixture headers whose opening comment named a retired function (`cd-declarators.h`, and `l1-members.h` / `l2-arrays.h` from the previous batch); the remaining sweep is mechanical but spans documents this work has no other reason to touch, so it belongs to review as one cleanup at the end of TF-6. `make test` 379 shell + 587 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a C header import loses 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what a C header import loses"). 6 shell functions → 21 tests, 797 body lines retired (856 lines of the file, including the family's own section banner): `run_l1_member_opaque` (3), `run_l2_layout_matrix` (4), `run_l2_libc_layouts` (2), `run_l3_decay` (2), `run_l4_returns_twice` (2) and `run_l5_typedef_names` (8), into a sixth suite module `tests/suite-cimport.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 8,492 → 7,637 lines and 136 → 130 functions; `make test` is 387 shell + 579 native = 966. **The whole L1-L5 family in one batch**, because it is contiguous in the file, shares a single claim — importing a C header must either produce the layout C produces or fail SAFELY — and held the three largest bodies left. **A dead verdict, found by counting:** `run_l2_libc_layouts` names three verdicts but emits two — `l2-libc-opaque` appears only in the two early-return skip paths, so on any machine with a working `cc` it is never reported and there was nothing to port. The static count (32 `echo "PASS` lines across the family) and the live count disagree, and only the live one reconciles against 966. **Seven assertions got stronger, none weaker:** every located diagnostic the shell matched with a path wildcard is now exact text including the resolved header path (`[^ ]*l1-members\.h:27` became `declared at ./tests/fixtures/l1-members.h:27; only pointers to it are valid`, likewise the `l2-arrays.h` rows and `l5-unrepresentable-message`'s own generated header); two L5 refusals moved from a stderr grep to `check-error-at`, pinning file and line as *fields*; `l5-repl-rollback` asserted the session survived with `qgrep -F '7'`, which any line containing a 7 satisfies, and is now the exact line `nuc>   7`, its control — previously only checking that no "unknown type:" appeared — gaining the matching `nuc>   9`;
