and three units ran their `cc` cross-check inside `if command -v cc`, silently passing with no oracle when it was absent, where they now open with `(try (require-cc))` so an absent `cc` is a visible skip rather than a green tick over an assertion that never ran. **Two capabilities:** `check-ir-parses` (`llvm-as`, because `--emit-llvm` never reads back what it writes and exit 0 says only that the compiler produced text) and `opt-o2` (`opt -O2 -S`, the only way to ask whether `returns_twice` changes what the optimizer does) — the L4 unit keeps the shell's negative control, which is what makes it an assertion, since "`-O2` did not tail-call `_setjmp`" is also true of an `-O2` that tail-calls nothing. **One limit raised, not absorbed:** the reader caps a string literal at 4095 decoded bytes (a fixed buffer in `src/reader.nuc`) and the libc survey's Nucleus program is 4490, so it is split into two literals at the declarations/`main` boundary with a comment saying why — moving it to `tests/fixtures/*.nuc` would have been worse, since a new `.nuc` there is an IR-snapshot input and would force a re-take, and raising the cap is a reader change that belongs to review rather than to a test port; recorded in `context/build.md`. **Verified by breaking it**, three ways, each reverted by editing the source back: changing one opaque row's declared line from 37 to 38 printed the whole diagnostic record against the expectation; adding 1 to one field offset in the libc program reported ``line 2 differs / want "timespec … tv_nsec=8" / got "… tv_nsec=9"``, which is also the proof the 13-line oracle compares live glibc data rather than two empty outputs; and feeding `check-ir-parses` a module with `ret i32 zzz` reported `llvm-as: …: error: expected value token`. The family's section banner went with it and its one durable point moved into the module header — the IR sweep over the tree's own modules is structurally blind here, since the whole tree imports six C headers exposing 14 of the 65 struct types the survey measured. `make test` 387 shell + 579 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a `.h` carries 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what a `.h` carries"). 7 shell functions → 32 tests, 662 body lines retired (754 lines of the file): `run_w9_cheader_globals` (5), `run_w9_cheader_identifiers` (4), `run_w9_cheader_imported_types` (4), `run_w9_cheader_niche_types` (4), `run_w9_cheader_struct_tag` (5), `run_w9_cheader_overload_symbols` (5) and `run_w9_cheader_reserved_words` (5), into a fifth suite module `tests/suite-cheader.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 9,246 → 8,492 lines and 143 → 136 functions; `make test` is 408 shell + 558 native = 966. **The successor to the previous batch, one step further out:** a `.nuch` is read by `nucleusc`, a `.h` by a C compiler, and the argument is the same — reading the header is never the claim. A header that merely parses can still bind an `asm` label to a symbol no object defines, or agree about names and disagree about layout, so all seven units end in a C consumer that is compiled, linked against the real object, and run. **Seven hand-rolled setups became one** (`CLib` plus `c-lib`/`c-lib-run`, with `c-header` for a unit whose claim is that a declaration was *refused* and there may be no symbol to link). **`cc-link-run` is `link-run` generalised and made strict:** it takes `.c` alongside `.o`/`.ll`, adds `-I` for the scratch directory, and compiles `-Wall -Werror`, which three of the seven shell units did not; `driver-link-run` underneath takes the driver, so `cxx-link-run` is one line — and the C++ consumer is *why* the reserved-word table carries C++'s keywords, since a generated header is routinely read behind `extern "C"` where `class` and `delete` are as fatal as `union` is in C. **`nm-defined` is the witness a consumer cannot be:** grep over a header cannot tell a correct label from one naming a symbol that does not exist, and running a consumer only proves the labels it happens to call, so `check-labels-defined` requires *every* label the header binds to be a symbol the object defines — stated against the object rather than a hardcoded list, the only form that outlasts a change to the mangling. Two units did this by hand with `nm | awk | sort -u` and disagreed on the filter (`T`/`W` in one, `!= U` in the other). **Two assertions got stronger, none weaker:** the operator check was `! qgrep -E 'asm\("[<>=!+*/%-]+"\)'`, a character class that must be kept in step with the operator set, and is now "every label in every committed `lib/*.h` contains at least one ASCII letter" — the actual claim, needing no maintenance; and `w9-cheader-private-global-not-exported` discarded the compiler's stderr and asserted only that the consumer failed, so one failing for an unrelated reason passed it, where it now requires the diagnostic to name `hidden`. **Verified by breaking it**, five ways, each reverted by editing the source back: dropping the label-stripping from `check-no-stray-hyphen` reported ``hyphen survives into C: extern int64_t my_count asm("my-count")``; inverting the operator predicate reported `lib/allocator.h binds the operator label alloc-handle-alloc`, proof the corpus scan finds labels rather than passing on an empty set; inverting `check-labels-defined` reported `the header binds scale.pPt.i32, which the object does not define` with the real symbol list; searching the corpus for `typedef` named `lib/allocator.h`; and a nonexistent stem named `lib/nosuchheader.h`. The skip path was checked with `c++` hidden from `PATH`. **Four helpers in `tests/nuctests.nuc`:** `cc-link-run`/`cxx-link-run` over a shared `driver-link-run`, `cc-syntax-only-in`, `nm-defined`, and `have-program?`, which `have-cc?` and the new `have-cxx?` are now one line each on top of. **The heredoc trap, hit exactly as recorded, and the 966 invariant caught the consequence:** a naive `^}$` scan for a function's end stopped at the C `main`'s closing brace inside a heredoc and reported the seven bodies as 389 lines when they are 662 — `context/build.md` already warned about it — but the *same* truncated ranges were used to enumerate what each unit asserts, so one verdict at the tail of the niche unit (`w9-cheader-committed-headers-no-niche-tag`, a corpus scan for the `struct _BANG…` tag across `lib/*.h`) was never ported and the deletion retired 32 verdicts against 31 new tests; `make test` came back 408 + 557 = 965 and named the gap immediately. Enumerate a unit's verdicts from the same ranges the deletion will use. Four of the seven had their `spawn` line ~3,600 lines from the body, so body and `spawn` went as separate ranges. `make test` 408 shell + 558 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies 2,684 artifacts byte-identical.

  **TF-6 category (c) — what a `.nuch` carries 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "what a `.nuch` carries"). 4 shell functions → 20 tests, 430 body lines retired (484 lines of the file): `run_w9_nuch_ns_union` (5), `run_w9_nuch_import_order` (5), `run_w9_nuch_declare_generic` (4) and `run_w9_nuch_declare_shadowed` (6), into `tests/suite-modules.nuc`, whose subject they already are. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 9,729 → 9,246 lines and 147 → 143 functions; `make test` is 440 shell + 526 native = 966. **The capability the batch needed is `link-run`.** A unit that compiles a library and its consumer *together* proves nothing about the header — the compiler has seen the source either way. Compiling separately and linking makes the **linker** resolve the symbol, so the claim becomes "the header promised the name the library actually exports", and a wrong answer is an undefined reference rather than a silently different program. `link-run` takes a space-separated list of `.o`/`.ll` paths, links with clang, runs the result and returns stdout; verified by breaking it — dropping the library's object reported `undefined reference to 'w9no-add'` and `undefined reference to 'w9no-counter'`, exactly the names the header carries. **Four hand-rolled library setups became one.** Each unit built the same thing — source under `l/`, header under `h/`, object beside them, source deliberately outside the include path because `resolve-import` tries `.nuc` in every search directory before any `.nuch` — and that is now `NuchLib` plus `nuch-lib`/`nuch-link-run`. **One assertion was a misattribution and the record found it:** `w9-nuch-shadowed-declare-reported` grepped stderr for two strings, and the second (`the header declares helper(i32):i32, the unit has helper(i64):i64`) is a **note**, not the error's message — the §T4.2 defect category (a) found three instances of. It is now `check-error-anywhere` plus `check-note-anywhere`. `w9-nuch-shadowed-both-sites-named` was one regex spanning the `file:line: error:` prefix and the message; `check-error-at` pins the header's path and line 2 as fields, and a second check requires the message to name the defining site — breaking the line to 3 prints the whole diagnostic record, notes included. **Four helpers in `tests/nuctests.nuc`:** `link-run`, `compile-object-in`, `write-into` (a file in a *subdirectory* of the scratch tree, which `test-write-file` cannot reach) and `emit-into`. Two of the four units had their `spawn` line ~3,000 lines from the body — the trap `context/build.md` records — so body and `spawn` were deleted as separate ranges, and one surviving unit's comment that named `run_w9_nuch_declare_generic` was reworded. **One thing not to repeat, now in `context/build.md`:** the deletion script also collapsed runs of blank lines, which reached two pre-existing four-blank separators elsewhere in the file and turned a clean deletion diff into unrelated churn; both were put back.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the C layout units 2026-09-09** (§T6.6 "the C layout units"). 4 shell functions → 25 tests, 804 body lines retired (847 lines of the file, with the rationale headers, the `spawn` lines and the blanks between): `run_s16_pk_packed` (7), `run_s16_pk3_aligned` (7), `run_s16_bf_bitfields` (6) and `run_s16_an_anonymous` (5) — the four largest bodies left in the suite — into a fourth suite module `tests/suite-layout.nuc`. `tests/run-tests.sh` is 10,576 → 9,729 lines and 151 → 147 functions; `make test` is 460 shell + 506 native = 966. **They were large because the same oracle was spelled out three times.** Each of the three layout units generated one `_Static_assert` per struct from `@sX = global i64 N` lines with its own `sed -nE`, ran it under five targets with its own loop, and guarded vacuity with its own `grep -c`; that is now one `check-sizes-cross-target`, and the two other recurring shapes are one function each — `check-agrees-with-cc` (one Nucleus program and one C program printing the same list, each built by its own compiler) and `check-cheader-roundtrip` (a generated header compiled by a strict C consumer, then read back). 123 lines of shared machinery stand behind 455 lines of tests, most of which are the embedded programs themselves. **The clang/`cc` split, made properly.** The shell skipped four units on `command -v clang`; clang is a hard dependency, so those are plain failures now. `cc` genuinely is optional, so the ten units whose oracle is the platform compiler open with `(try (require-cc))` — `skip!`'s second real consumer, verified both ways (`(status skip)` at exit 0, `(status fail)` with `skipped: no cc to build the oracle against` under `--no-skip`). **Five assertions got stronger, none weaker.** `s16-pk-access-align` counted `grep -cE '(load|store) i32[, ].*align 1$' >= 3` over the whole module plus a negative regex for what the count could not see, and now asserts each access inside the function it belongs to (`check-in-define`); `s16-pk3-type-line-and-slots` matched pad sizes as `[0-9]+` and now pins `%B = type { i8, [12 x i8], i32, [12 x i8] }`; `s16-bf-cheader-roundtrip` asked for `: 4;` anywhere in the header and now pins all three bit-field lines; `s16-an-refusals`' `--emit-cheader` case was a stderr grep and is now a `Diagnostic` comparison via the new `check-emit-rejects`, which reaches the one refusal an `--emit-llvm` run cannot; and the cross-target oracle now requires `nucleusc` to **succeed** on each target, where the shell wrote `|| true` and leaned entirely on the assert count. **Five helpers in `tests/nuctests.nuc`:** `cc-syntax-only-for` (a complete compile-time `sizeof`/`offsetof` oracle for a machine this one cannot execute — no sysroot, no link, no run), `cc-compile-strict`, `cc-run-file`, `have-cc?`/`require-cc`, and `check-emit-rejects`; plus `view-upto-byte`, promoted out of `tests/suite-float.nuc` because the suite modules are one compilation unit and a helper left in a sibling would have been reachable by accident rather than shared on purpose. **Verified by breaking it**, three ways, each reverted by editing the source back: dropping `__attribute__((packed))` from the C side of the PK-1 table reported ``x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu: … static assertion failed … 'sizeof(struct A) == 7' … expression evaluates to '12 == 7'``; unpacking `P` failed the type line, and unpacking it with the type line adjusted failed ``in @rd: expected a line matching "*load i32, ptr %*, align 1"`` with the whole function body printed. **One trap recorded in `docs/testing.md`:** a test that writes a header and a C consumer side by side must `#include` the header by **base name** — a quoted include resolves from the including file's own directory, and the path `test-write-file` returns is relative to the project root, which is right for `import-use` and wrong for `#include`. `make test` 460 shell + 506 native = 966; two green full native runs and a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass before the deletion; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites. The **IR snapshot gate did not run**: its baseline lives under `build/`, which is not committed, and this container has none. It is vacuous for this batch in any case — its inputs are `lib/*.nuc`, `examples/*.nuc` and `tests/fixtures/*.nuc`, and the change set is seven files under `tests/`, `docs/`, `design/` and `context/` with nothing in `src/` or `lib/` at all. A fresh baseline was taken afterwards for later batches to verify against, which is sound precisely because no emitted output could have moved.

  **TF-6 category (c) — the float-width oracle 2026-09-06** (§T6.6 "the float-width oracle"; [stage18-tooling/float-width-oracle.md](stage18-tooling/float-width-oracle.md)). 1 shell function → 6 tests, 206 shell lines retired: the whole of `run_s16_fl_float_widths`, into a third suite module `tests/suite-float.nuc`. `make test` is 485 shell + 481 native = 966. This was the unit staged separately because its port was a **rewrite of two Python programs** — a fixture generator emitting 104 matched Nucleus/C float constants across f16/f32/f64/f80/f128, and an IR comparator normalising LLVM's two spellings of a float before comparing — rather than a translation of shell text-munging. **The measurement, which is the point of having staged it.** 206 shell lines (45 of them Python) became 548 lines, 402 of them code: generator 23 → 38, comparator 22 → 71, six `sed -E` substitutions → 102. The prediction in the design document was right about the float arithmetic and wrong about where the lines were — `strtod` did collapse Python's two normalisation branches to one (`fl-f64-bits` is 4 lines, `fl-value-bits` 8, against Python's single unreadable nested expression), and the 3× growth is entirely `re.match` becoming a 20-line prefix walk, Python's dict-as-join becoming a linear scan, and one f-string becoming three failure sites. **One work item raised, not absorbed:** `fl-ident-byte?`/`fl-all-digits?` classify ASCII **bytes**, and `lib/char.nuc`'s `char-is-digit`/`char-is-alnum` take a decoded `Char`, so reaching them costs an `unsafe/cast` to save eight lines; `strview-is-ascii-ws (b:ui8)` is the precedent for byte-level classifiers, so `strview-is-ascii-digit` and an ident-byte predicate beside it are **proposed for review, not written**. The other two staged gaps did not arise: padded hex was never needed (the comparator compares `ui64` bit patterns, and a failure message wants the two compilers' raw spellings anyway — no `lib/fmt.nuc` addition proposed), and `replace` would have covered one of four `sed` substitutions since three delete *patterns*, not literals. **Two assertions got stronger.** FL-5's five `[^,]*` wildcards became an exact comparison of the vararg call's type sequence, and the ABI unit's two `sed` pipelines became one normaliser shared with FL-5. **Verified by breaking it:** dropping the C `L` suffix from the f80 row reported `v28_f80: nucleus 0xK4000C90FDAA22168C235, clang 0xK4000C90FDAA22168C000` — exactly the truncation FL-3 exists to catch; retyping `f2` reported the signature diff; misspelling the probe made both units skip and, under `--no-skip`, fail. Two helpers in `tests/nuctests.nuc`: `cc-emit-llvm` (clang's own IR for a C file) and `compile-path-for` (`compile-path` under `--target=`). **One root cause fixed in passing:** `$(NUCTESTS)` did not depend on `tests/suite-*.nuc`, so editing a suite module did not rebuild the runner — a silently stale binary, which is how the first full run of this batch reported two spurious skips. **One trap recorded in `context/conventions.md`:** `(parse f64 "0x3FF0000000000000")` returns 4.6e18, not 1.0, because a hex float's binary exponent is optional to `strtod`, so an LLVM bit pattern parses as a hex float with every byte consumed and no error. `make test` 485 shell + 481 native = 966; a shuffled `--run`-per-name pass green; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; the IR snapshot verifies with **no re-take** — its inputs are `lib/`, `examples/` and `tests/fixtures/`, none of which this batch touches.

  **Skip as a third verdict, and the float-width oracle staged 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "Skip as a third verdict"; [stage18-tooling/float-width-oracle.md](stage18-tooling/float-width-oracle.md)). `lib/test.nuc` now has three verdicts, not two. `(skip! parts…)` ends a test as skipped with a **required** reason, rendered by the same `str-into` machine as `fail!`; the record is `(status skip) (message "…")` — a field rather than a prefix inside a pass message, so `lib/read.nuc` reads it back and a collector counts it. The shell suite's `PASS  name (SKIP: reason)` was the thing being fixed: it reconciles into the PASS count and vanishes from every summary. `--no-skip` turns every skip into a failure with message `skipped: <reason>`, a **policy flag rather than a mode**, so it composes with `--list` and `--run` (`make run-nuctests NUCTESTS_ARGS=--no-skip`); `make test` prints every skip line and ends `N passed, M failed, K skipped`. `test-run-one` returns the status instead of a bool and `test-main` counts only `TEST-FAIL`, so a default run exits 0 with skips in it. `examples/self-test.nuc` gained `this-one-skips-on-purpose` beside the deliberate failure, so the golden output shows both records. One implementation note: the reason lives in the failure buffer and `test-fail-begin` clears it, so strict mode must copy it out **before** writing the replacement — `str-into` re-evaluates its target once per piece, which makes the aliasing easy to write and invisible until the message comes out empty. **A correction to the fifth batch's headline claim.** "Half the remaining work is blocked on one missing capability" was wrong: clang is already a *hard dependency* — `src/nucleusc.nuc:18681` uses it as the default linker driver and the `Makefile` links with it at four sites — so "is clang present" is not a conditional any test needs. The corrected split of the 152 remaining functions is 101 oracle-free / 3,982 lines, 41 clang-bound but **unblocked** / 4,142 lines, and one unit with a real oracle. That unit, `run_s16_fl_float_widths` (206 lines), is staged separately because its port is a rewrite: two embedded Python programs — a generator emitting 120 matched Nucleus/C float literals across f16/f32/f64/f80/f128, and a comparator normalising LLVM's two spellings of a float constant before comparing against clang's. It should come out *smaller* in Nucleus (`parse-float` delegates to a C99 `strtod` that takes both decimal and hex, where Python needed two branches; the f64→bits bitcast idiom is already `f64-bits`), and three gaps are visible before it starts — padded hex formatting is compiler-internal (`hexu` in `src/strfmt.nuc`, not `lib/fmt.nuc`), there is no `replace` for the ABI unit's four `sed` substitutions, and `_Float16`/`__float128` are a genuine per-clang feature probe, making it the first real consumer of `skip!`. **The instruction attached to the item is why it is separate:** difficulties and substantial code-size increases are work items for review, not things to absorb — a wanted library addition gets designed and reviewed rather than inlined into a test file, and an assertion that cannot be expressed stops the work instead of being weakened. `make test` 491 shell + 475 native = 966; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers green after regenerating `lib/test.{h,nuch}`; CStr 20 sites.

  **TF-6 category (c) fifth batch 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). 5 shell functions → 30 tests, 593 shell lines retired: `run_w9_layout_reachability`, `run_w9_defcast_reach`, `run_w9_two_ns_one_name`, `run_b5_private_definers`, `run_w9_fnslot_arg` (6 each). All five are unit-boundary units, so all five went to `tests/suite-modules.nuc`. `make test` is 491 shell + 475 native = the same 966 verdicts. **The measurement that should drive the rest of the plan:** counted with a heredoc-aware scanner — the naive `^}` scan stops early on any unit that embeds C, which is most of the deferred ones — what remains is 152 functions / 8,599 body lines, split **101 functions / 3,982 lines oracle-free** and **51 functions / 4,617 lines needing `clang`/`cc`/`python3`**. Half the remaining work is blocked on one missing *capability*, not on porting effort: running the host C compiler, and recording a SKIP when it is absent. §T9's deferred list should gain it, and §T7 has to say whether `make test` may keep a shell tail for these. Four helpers, all in `tests/nuctests.nuc`: `check-file-rejects` (with `check-source-rejects` a wrapper on it), `source-compiles?` — a table of accept/reject spellings asks only that, and neither `check-source-rejects` nor `check-source-accepts` can carry the two halves of one row — and `check-same-decl-set` with `count-exact-lines`, because the "moving the import did not move emission" claim compares two modules as a SET (position within the type section is order-dependent and inert and the string pool renumbers, so the artifacts cannot be compared). **Two more shell greps became field assertions:** `w9-defcast-note-names-rule` grepped stderr for `note: a defcast rule converts …`, where `check-note-anywhere` makes "it is a note" a field rather than a prefix in the text; and `w9-two-ns-ambiguous-use` grepped for the message and separately for `w35amb.nuc:3: error:`, where `check-error-at` requires both of one diagnostic. **One process note worth keeping:** the retirement script that had worked for four batches deleted from the comment above a function through its `spawn` line, which is only correct when the two are adjacent — for the `run_w9_*` units the spawn is 2,000–4,500 lines further down, and the first run cut 5,142 lines. Caught immediately by `wc -l`; the file was rebuilt from `HEAD` with a heredoc-aware end-of-function scan and the `spawn` line removed separately, and `git diff --stat` confirmed 593 deletions and no other change. The earlier four batches are unaffected — their deletions matched the expected sizes and each reconciled to 966 verdicts. Retired after two full green native runs, one a `--run`-per-name shuffle. `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; IR snapshot PASS at 2,684 with no re-take.

  **TF-6 category (c) fourth batch 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). 4 shell functions → 30 tests, 492 shell lines retired: `run_b4_redefinition` (14), `run_s16_import_ct` (9), `run_s16_keyword_markers` (4), `run_s16_macrolet` (3). `make test` is 521 shell + 445 native = the same 966 verdicts. **A second suite module, `tests/suite-modules.nuc`**, for what a *unit boundary* does to a name — R4 redefinition now, the `run_w9_nuch_*` and namespace family next; `tests/suite-s16.nuc` was getting long and the split that matters is by subject rather than by batch. One helper: `check-file-exit dir path want`, with `check-source-exit` becoming a wrapper on it — that is the shell's `w1_run`, which several surviving units still use. **Ten near-identical units became ten `deftest`s over one helper, not a table**: category (a)'s rule is that a *fixture corpus* is data, but here each program is three lines written in the test and the ten differ in the definer being redefined, which is the thing a reader is looking for. What they share is the assertion — `check-redefines` — and that is where the upgrade lives: the shell grepped stderr for `redefinition of 'X'` and separately for `<file>:2: error:`, where `check-error-at` requires both of the *same* diagnostic. Retired after two full green native runs (one a `--run`-per-name shuffle) plus `make test`. `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers; CStr 20 sites; IR snapshot PASS at 2,684 with **no re-take** — nothing outside `tests/` changed. **Remaining: 157 shell functions, 8,444 body lines**, of which `run_s16_*` is 20 functions / 2,050 lines.

  **TF-6 category (c) third batch 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). 4 shell functions → 21 tests, 679 shell lines retired: `run_s16_parametric_aliases` (7), `run_s16_d9_ct_types` (7), `run_s16_ref_sigil` (6), `run_s16_decl_attrs` (1). `make test` is 551 shell + 415 native = the same 966 verdicts. Three more helpers, again each demanded by a unit: `compile-object` (a real object-file link rather than a reparse — a parametric alias has to survive one), `test-scratch-sub` in `lib/test.nuc` (a `.nuch` unit needs its source *outside* every search directory, since `resolve-import` tries `.nuc` everywhere first), and an `extra` argument on `build-run-file` carrying `--link-arg=`. Plus `count-lines-with-prefix` and `duplicate-type-name`, because D9's claim is that a type line is emitted **once** — a presence test cannot catch the double emit a re-drain produces, and LLVM rejects the module rather than picking one. **`run_s16_decl_attrs` stays one test and gets better for it:** the shell accumulated `bad=1` across five internal checks and reported at the end, which is exactly the hand-rolled `ok=1 … || ok=0` accumulator the `!void` shape exists to replace; as a `deftest` the first failure ends the test and names itself. One porting correction worth keeping: a `define` line carries its `section`, so the shell's `qgrep -F` substring became an anchored `check-match`, not `check-line`. **The oracle cluster is deferred and now named** — `run_s16_fl_float_widths`, `run_s16_pk_packed`, `run_s16_pk3_aligned`, `run_s16_bf_bitfields`, `run_s16_an_anonymous`, `run_s16_c1_bare_unsigned` and `run_l5_typedef_names` assert Nucleus's layout *against clang's* (two of them generating fixtures and comparing results in embedded Python), and need two things the framework lacks: a way to run the host C compiler and a way to record a SKIP. That is a design decision, not a port. Retired after two full green native runs (one a `--run`-per-name shuffle) plus `make test`. `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers green after regenerating `lib/test.{h,nuch}`; CStr allowlist 20 sites. **IR snapshot re-taken:** six artifacts moved — `lib/test.nuc`'s `.ll`, `.win-gnu.ll`, `.win-msvc.ll`, `.h`, `.nuch` and `examples/self-test.nuc.ll`, all of them the string table growing by `test-scratch-sub`'s literals; no fixture `.ll` or `.err` moved, so the compiler did not change. **Remaining: 161 shell functions, 8,936 body lines**, of which `run_s16_*` is 23 functions / 2,410 lines.

  **TF-6 category (c) second batch 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). 5 shell functions → 35 tests, 792 shell lines retired: `run_s16_bool_truthiness` (5), `run_s16_literal_variables` (13), `run_s16_vararg_promotion` (4), `run_s16_type_aliases` (9), `run_s16_chain_nesting` (4). `make test` is 572 shell + 394 native = the same 966 verdicts. **Four helpers, each demanded by a unit rather than anticipated** — the first batch's units were all single-file and these are not: `compile-path-in` (a namespaced library and a consumer *outside* it, for `deftype-` privacy), `emit-for-file` (`--emit-nuch`, whose stdout is the artifact under test), `build-run-file` (build a second unit against the exported header), `repl-session` (the REPL has its own top-level form chain), and `line-with` (a claim about ONE instruction: `i8 %` is in every module, so a module-wide `check-not-contains` asserts nothing). `compile-path` and `build-run-source` are now one-liners over the `dir`-taking versions; an empty `dir` adds no `-I`. **Two shell comparisons became stronger, not weaker.** The shell diffed IR and C-header files after `grep -v`-ing out `; ModuleID`, `source_filename` and the `/* Generated from` banner, because its two programs lived at different paths; `compile-source` writes every fixture to the same `t.nuc`, so those lines are already equal and `check-golden` compares the whole artifact, banner included. And `refuses_chain` grepped stderr for the message and again for `c.nuc:4:`, where `check-error-at` requires both of the *same* diagnostic — category (a)'s upgrade applied to a bespoke unit. Retired after three green runs, one shuffled. `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers green; CStr allowlist 20 sites; IR snapshot PASS at 2,684 with no re-take, since nothing outside `tests/` changed. **Remaining: 165 shell functions, 9,583 body lines** (§T7's trust-anchor units included), of which `run_s16_*` is 27 functions / 3,057 lines. The previous entry's "177 / ~10,200" was measured differently; the pre-batch figure by this count was 170 / 10,339.

  **TF-6 category (c) first batch 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). Category (c) is 182 shell functions and 10,560 body lines, so the support layer came first: `compile-source`/`compile-path` returning a `Compiled` record (`ok?`, `ir`, `raw`, `diags`), plus `check-source-rejects`, `check-source-accepts`, `source-ir`, `source-cheader`, `build-run-source` and `check-source-exit` in `tests/nuctests.nuc`, and per-test scratch files (`test-scratch`, `test-write-file`, `build/out/nt/<test-name>`) in `lib/test.nuc` — so `--run` reproduces exactly the files the full run wrote and no two tests collide. Category (a) was rerouted through `compile-path`, deleting its own `compile-fixture`. **The thing that makes ruling 3's embedded fixtures practical is that a Nucleus string literal may span lines** — an embedded program is a heredoc, not a wall of `\n`, and only `"` and `\` need escaping. First batch: 5 shell functions → 20 tests in `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, a separate module `tests/nuctests.nuc` imports so the suite file does not become one enormous file (`run_s16_bool_type` 6, `run_s16_macrolet_refused` 10, `run_s16_atom_macro` 2, `run_s16_template_repr`, `run_s16_pointer_kind_names`). Every ported test keeps the name its shell unit printed. Retired after three green runs, one shuffled. `make test` 607 shell + 359 native = 966; `make bootstrap` converges; snapshot re-taken (only `lib/test.nuc`'s six artifacts moved). **Remaining: 177 shell functions, ~10,200 lines**; the `run_s16_*` family is the largest cluster at 32 functions / ~3,900 lines.

  **TF-6 category (b) done 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). The `examples/*.nuc` and `tests/repl/*.in` golden-output loops moved into `tests/nuctests.nuc` — 155 + 16 tests, **discovered by walking the directory** rather than listed, because a table would have made "add an example" a two-file edit and the shell glob did not. `make test` is 627 shell + 339 native = the same 966 verdicts. **Three library gaps, all fixed in the library** (§T3 had named the first two as missing surface): `read-dir`/`dir-count`/`dir-name`/`make-dir`/`dir-exists?` in `lib/file.nuc`, where `DirEntries` is one buffer of NUL-terminated names plus offsets (`Command`'s shape, for `Command`'s reason — `Vector`'s drop frees its array without dropping elements) and entries come back **sorted**, since `readdir` order differs between machines; `command-stdin-path`, because a REPL test *is* a file piped into `nucleusc -i`; and **`command-stderr-to-stdout` now applies under capture**, where it had silently done nothing — a golden file records `2>&1`, one stream, and two separately drained pipes cannot reproduce an interleaving. **One compiler crash, fixed at the root:** `(return (err e))` in a `!void` function segfaulted the compiler, because `union-target-rewrite` sent every plain `err` through `__err-handled` and `emit-err-handled` dereferences the `ok` payload type to build the `(Maybe T)` a handler repairs with — which `!void` does not have. The negotiation is meaningless with no value to supply, so `err` in a `!void` is now `err!`; `err!` and `try` were always fine. **One deduplication:** `src/nucleusc.nuc` dropped its own `opendir`/`readdir`/`closedir` declares and `DIRENT-D-NAME-OFFSET` for `lib/file.nuc`'s `read-dir`, which it already reaches transitively; the offset and its platform caveat now exist once, and the offset is **validated** (`.` and `..` exist in every POSIX directory, so their absence means the layout is wrong and `read-dir` fails rather than returning bytes read out of `d_ino`). Sorted order changed exactly one diagnostic in 2,684 artifacts — `w9-unknown-type-ctor-unimported` now names `lib/vector.nuc` rather than `lib/vector.nuch`, which is the better answer since `.nuc` out-ranks `.nuch` in `resolve-import`, and was previously decided by filesystem order. `check-golden` names the first differing line and its number. Retired after three green runs, one a full `--run`-per-name shuffle. **IR snapshot re-taken twice** (only `lib/file.nuc`, `lib/process.nuc`, `lib/test.nuc`, their three importing examples, three `s19-process-*` fixtures, and the one `.err` above moved). `make bootstrap` converges; `make nuctest` 193 units, 0 failed; 87 headers green; CStr allowlist 20 sites. Category (c)'s ~171 bespoke units remain.

  **TF-6 category (a) done 2026-09-05** (§T6.6 "As landed"). The 168 `run_reject_at`/`run_reject`/`run_accepts` units are gone from `tests/run-tests.sh`, replaced by `tests/manifest/diagnostics.sexp` (168 rows) and `tests/nuctests.nuc`, which reads the manifest and registers one test per row over TF-5's `--diagnostics=sexp`. **The table is the point, not the port**: every one of those units said the same thing about a different fixture, so the shell version was 826 lines of repeated control flow and the native version is a `while` loop. `make test` now runs both — 798 shell + 168 native = the same 966 verdicts as before, no unit lost. Retirement followed §T6.6's rule: three consecutive green runs of the native table, one of them a full `--run`-per-name shuffle, then delete. **Three rows were wrong and the record found all three**, which is the §T4.2 hole closing in practice: `w9-unknown-type-ctor-unimported` and `g5-noinit-ref-note` asserted text that lives on a **note**, and `w5a-hex-escape-no-digit-rejected` had the `path:line: error: ` prefix baked into its pattern — none of which two independent greps over one stderr blob can distinguish. All three are now `(line …)` + `(message …)` + `(note …)`, strictly more precise than what they replaced. **One crash worth recording:** `row-text` guarded with `(= (n 'kind) NODE-CELL)` rather than by kind, and `Node.s` is null on an INT, so reading a row's `(line N)` segfaulted; `diag-text` in `lib/test.nuc` had the identical shape and was fixed with it. `check-note-anywhere` was added so an unpinned `(note …)` cannot silently assert nothing. `TestCase` gained a `data:raw` and its `run` takes a `ptr`, which is what lets a table row and a `deftest` be the same kind of test; `deftest` ignores both. The 62 rationale comments that introduced only retired units moved into the manifest verbatim as `;` blocks — the rest introduce surviving units and stayed. **IR snapshot: the six differing artifacts were `lib/test.nuc`'s and `examples/self-test.nuc`'s own**, re-taken at 2,684; no fixture `.ll` or `.err` moved, so the compiler did not change. `make bootstrap` converges; `make nuctest` 364 units, 0 failed; 87 headers green. Categories (b) 177 golden-output loops and (c) ~171 bespoke units remain.

  **TF-5 done 2026-09-05** (§T6.5 "As landed"). `Diagnostic` (`src/reader.nuc`): `severity`, `path`, `line`, `message`, `notes`, built once by `diag-emit` and rendered by whichever back-end `--diagnostics=text|sexp` selected. `die-at` and `report-at` construct one; so do the five **located** warnings, each of which had been spelling `path ":" line ": warning: "` by hand. **The structure cost no call sites**, which is the whole trick: twenty-odd diagnosing sites already build their notes into the message as `(fstr "…" "\n  note: " …)` and two more are ambient (`g-mono-context`, `g-diag-note`), so `diag-build` splits on that exact marker and `notes` becomes a real field with none of the 688 call sites touched. The text back-end rejoins, making it the identity — and the gate for that was not a new test but the **172 existing reject units**, every one of which asserts its exact diagnostic text; all 965 stayed green on the first run. Two decisions the phase forced. **A note printed *after* the call is not a field**: three sites did that (`report-unterminated`, the stray-`)` note, the preprocessor's sysroot advice) because `die-at` is noreturn and there is no "and also"; they now stage first, and `diag-stage-note` **appends** rather than overwrites so two sites can contribute to one diagnostic. **One diagnostic is one line**, because every newline inside a string is escaped — which is what lets `read-diagnostics` skip a line that is not one, and it has to, because the `clang -E` a C-header import shells out to writes its own text to the same stream. A location-less toolchain message spells the absence as `(file "")` / `(line -1)` rather than omitting fields, so a reader never asks whether a key is present. The test side is `read-diagnostics` + `check-error-at`/`check-warning-at`/`check-note-at`/`check-no-errors` in `lib/test.nuc` over `lib/read.nuc`; `examples/self-test.nuc` gained a `diagnostics` test whose blob **satisfies both halves of the §T4.2 hole separately** (an error at `a.nuc:12`, a message containing `second ns`) and which `check-error-at` rejects as a pair. `run_diagnostics_sexp` is the compiler-side gate: exact form for an error, note-as-field, one line per diagnostic, warnings structured too, a `"` in a field surviving a `readdump` round trip, and `--diagnostics=text` being the default. **966 PASS / 0 FAIL**; `make bootstrap` converges; 87 headers green. **IR snapshot: the eight differing artifacts were `lib/test.nuc`'s and `examples/self-test.nuc`'s own, and nothing else in 2,684** — which is the real evidence, because the snapshot captures each input's `.ll.err`/`.h.err`/`.nuch.err` as well as its output, so every fixture that errors had its diagnostic compared byte for byte. Re-taken at 2,684 for the two edited files. Not converted: `cheader-preprocess-failed` re-runs `clang -E` with stderr attached so the user sees clang's own "file not found" — another program's diagnostic, and wrapping it would claim a structure it does not have.

  **TF-4 done 2026-09-04** (§T6.4 "As landed"). `lib/test.nuc` (376 lines), `docs/testing.md`, and `examples/self-test.nuc` + `tests/expected/self-test.out` — a suite whose last test **fails on purpose**, so the recorded output is the failure record's own regression test, and whose existence is the phase's stated completion condition (an `examples/` program running its own tests without touching the compiler's harness). Shipped: `deftest`; eleven `check-*` predicates (§T1.2's six, plus `check-eq-int`, two negations, and `check`); §T4.3's `ir-define`/`check-in-define`/`check-not-in-define`, which split a module into `define` blocks so `ret void` in @noop cannot satisfy an assertion about @add; `fail!` for writing new assertions; `test-main` answering `--list`/`--run <name>`/all; one s-expression record per test, escaped so it reads back through `lib/read.nuc`. **Two compiler changes the phase required, both general.** `(source-file)`/`(source-line)` emit a `StrView` and an integer literal and resolve at the **call** site inside a macro expansion, which is what lets a `deftest` point at itself and what a TF-5 diagnostic macro will want. And **macro calls now work in top-level position**: the dispatch loop had no macro path at all, so `deftest` died with `unknown top-level form`. Expansion is attempted **only in the `case`'s default arm**, so every built-in form still wins its own name and no existing program can change meaning; a `(do …)` expansion splices its children as separate top-level forms, and the rewritten cell is re-dispatched rather than advanced past, so an expansion may itself be a macro call. **Known limit, deferred as T9.7:** expansion happens after the pre-scans, so a macro-produced definition is not forward-referenceable and a macro cannot produce an `extend` together with the methods satisfying it — fixing that means JIT-compiling `defmacro` bodies ahead of every other top-level form, a front-end staging change nothing in the suite needs. **One trap worth recording:** `str-into` re-evaluates its target once per piece and `test-fail-begin` clears the buffer, so `(str-into (test-fail-begin) ~@parts)` kept only the *last* piece — every assertion in the module went through that one macro, and the bug was invisible until a test actually failed, which is the second reason the example has one that always does. **IR snapshot re-taken** (2670 → 2684 artifacts): the only differing artifacts were `lib_read.nuc`'s six, all from TF-3's `sexp-write-string` being added after that phase's baseline; the corpus then grew by `lib/test.nuc` and `examples/self-test.nuc`. Nothing else in 2,670 artifacts moved — which is the evidence that top-level macro expansion in the default arm, and the two C-header refusals, are byte-identical for every file that does not use them.

  **TF-3 done 2026-09-04** (§T6.3 "As built"). `lib/read.nuc` (792 lines): `read-all`, a `Reader`/`read-one` pair, `node-write`/`node-str`, `node-eq`; `docs/reading.md` and `examples/read-sexp.nuc`. The gate needed an instrument, so the compiler gained **`--dump-ast`** (the reader's output before `desugar` and before the prelude) and `tests/readdump.nuc` prints `lib/read.nuc`'s answer in the same format — parity is a `diff`. **429 files, zero disagreements**: 220/226 fixtures (6 are the reader-error fixtures the compiler itself cannot parse), 156 examples, 42 lib, 12 src, pinned as four `run_reader_parity` units. **Scope deviation, stated:** full parity is unachievable because `def-rmacro` extends the *compiler's* reader-macro table, so the line was drawn at what a data reader owes its callers — collection literals (`[…]`/`{…}`/`#{…}`, whose desugaring infers an element type) and user reader macros are excluded and **positively rejected** with a line number, while `&T` → `ref:T` and the `name:(Type)` colon-paren fuse ARE implemented, because without them 44 fixtures would parse into a silently different tree. The rule that decided it: **a construct that would be silently mis-parsed must be implemented; one that can be positively rejected may be excluded** — and the unit fails if a rejection ever has another cause, so the exclusion cannot quietly grow. **Three bugs found in the compiler's own printer**, every one of them the compiler losing information rather than the new reader disagreeing: `println`/`eprintln` were exported to `lib/io.nuch` **without their newline** (a `NODE-CHAR` fell through `fprint-node`'s `case` and printed nothing, so any multi-TU build importing the header got a `println` that behaved as `print`); `c"…"` printed as `"…"`, dropping NS-4's CStr flag; and non-ASCII string literals came back as mojibake (`"hé"` → `"hÃ©"`) because the default case widened each *byte* to a codepoint and re-encoded it. None was reachable from a committed header, which is how 85 generated headers stayed green over three of them. **IR snapshot re-taken** (2624 → 2670 artifacts): the only differing artifact was `lib_io.nuc.nuch` gaining the two missing newlines, and the corpus grew by the two new files.

  **TF-2 done 2026-09-04** (§T6.2 "As built"). `tests/nuctest.nuc` → `build/nuctest` (328 lines, `make nuctest`): a bounded job pool over Stage 19's `spawn`/`wait-any`, per-unit scratch directories, per-unit capture, ordered replay, `--jobs`/`--filter`/`--shuffle`/`--list`. **All three gates pass** — output byte-identical to the shell harness's full run (970 lines); seeds 1, 7 and 12345 all green *and* byte-identical; **+3.0%** wall clock at `--jobs 16` (63.8s vs 62.0s) against a 10% budget. Decisions beyond the sketch: unit results to stdout and the runner's summary to **stderr** (a summary on stdout would make byte-identity unmeetable); shuffling permutes **dispatch** while replay stays in manifest order, so a shuffled run is byte-identical too and an order bug surfaces as a *diff* rather than a bare red exit (`--list --shuffle N` makes the order inspectable, without which a no-op shuffle would pass its own gate); and the unit list is **generated** by running `run-tests.sh --list` rather than stored as a manifest, so a unit has one place to exist. **Two things the phase found.** `lib/process.nuc` needed a new capability: a pool cannot capture through pipes, because a pipe holds 64 KiB and a child that fills it never exits while a parent in `wait-any` drains nobody — added `command-stdout-path`/`command-stderr-to-stdout` (stage19-process §9), the dogfooding the stage was justified on. And the `--unit` shim cost +37%, then +27% after cutting the walk short at the matching unit; profiling found `$(basename …)` in the top-level dispatch loops — ~180 forks, 335ms, on *every* invocation, paid 525 times by the driver. Parameter expansion took the walk to 43ms and the gate to +3.0%, speeding up the shell harness equally. The lesson is not about the runner: **a shell harness pays a fork where a program pays a function call, and at 525 units that difference was the entire budget.**

  **TF-1 done 2026-09-04** (§T6.1 "As built"). `tests/run-tests.sh --list` and `--unit <name>`, implemented entirely inside `spawn` — the file's only dispatch point — so **no unit function changed**. The suite is **525 addressable units**, not the 176 named functions the plan estimated: the per-file loops spawn one unit per `examples/*.nuc`, `tests/fixtures/*.nuc` and reject-table row, and those are what a runner has to address. A unit's name is `function:first-argument`, because the first argument is already its identity while later arguments are *expected diagnostic text* — joining those in produced 100-byte names full of parens and spaces. `spawn` rejects duplicate names outright. `--unit` deliberately does **not** build (525 concurrent `make`s would reintroduce the `Text file busy` race above) and backgrounds the unit rather than calling it, so a unit that aborts mid-way leaves the same partial output it would leave in the pool. *Gate passed:* all 525 units run alone, concatenated in dispatch order, byte-identical to the full parallel run — 959 PASS, 0 FAIL, the only differing line being `make`'s build banner. **The order-independence audit found nothing**, contradicting the plan's expectation: every unit passes in a fresh process, because the harness's "each unit owns its own `mktemp` space" invariant turns out to hold. It now has a test behind it, so a future `--shuffle` failure at TF-2 is a real regression rather than pre-existing debt.

## Stage 17 addendum — the `lib/` C-string sweep (2026-09-03)

Register: [stage17-native-strings/overview.md](stage17-native-strings/overview.md) §2.7a, which this sweep wrote. The §5.5 tripwire counts `src/` only, so `lib/` was never enumerated and kept C-shaped spellings the stage had already retired everywhere it looked.

  **Five conversions.** (1) `lib/combinators.nuc`'s `join` took `sep:CStr` and a `(UnaryFn S CStr)` renderer — so every renderer had to produce a NUL-terminated buffer, which for a computed name means building a `String` and parking it. Both are `StrView` now, and the internal `join-push-cstr` (a `strlen` + raw byte push) is `string-push-str-unchecked`. `emit-joined`'s separator likewise; it still prints through `printf`, now `%.*s`, because the caller's `emitf` writes wherever it likes and stdio is the only stream a separator can join without importing a Writer that would buffer independently of it. (2) `keyword-intern` took a `CStr`; a keyword name is a `StrView` and now says so. (3) `bool`'s `ToStr` built its view with `(strview-from-cstr (if self c"true" c"false"))` — a `strlen` per formatted bool, and a workaround for the pre-Stage-17 join rule under which two `StrView` literals meeting at an `if` collapsed to `CStr`. That rule was fixed in C8; the workaround was not removed with it. (4) `lib/node.nuc`'s `InternEntry.spelling` was a bare `ptr` holding an interned `Symbol`'s bytes, so `intern-node` unwrapped the Symbol to store it and `sym-node-hash` built an `alloca Symbol` to wrap it back just to read the cached hash. The field is a `Symbol`; `sym-node-hash` is gone. (5) `arena-strdup` — a `strlen` over an untyped pointer — had **no callers**, and `arena-strndup`'s NUL byte was read by none of its one caller's consumers (`emit-string-table` writes its own `\00` and reads `len` bytes). Both are one `arena-bytes (src:ptr n:i64)`, which is what the function always was.

  **What stays, and why it is not vigilance.** §2.7a closes the `lib/` list at four kinds: the lattice's own `*-from-cstr`/`*-to-cstr` doors (each named for what it is), `strtod` and `snprintf` (no native implementation, decided permanent in §2.6), the three `CStr` conformances (`ToStr`/`Hash`/`Eq` — for a `char*` that arrived from C), and compiler-emitted ABI. That last one is the interesting category: `intern-symbol` and `err-find-handler`'s repair-type token are called from **hand-written IR** in `src/`, so a `StrView` parameter would mean hand-lowering a by-value struct argument per target. Not done, and now said at both sites rather than left to be rediscovered.

  **The library was also teaching the wrong idiom.** `lib/hashmap.nuc` and `lib/hashset.nuc` open with `(HashMap CStr i32)` / `(HashSet CStr)` as *the* usage example. `StrView` now. Nine `examples/` still key collections by `CStr` and ~55 open a view with `(strview-from-cstr "literal")` — a round trip through `strlen` that a literal has not needed since the NS-3 flip. Left alone (the brief was `lib/`), recorded in §2.7a.

  **Snapshot re-baselined (2026-09-03).** Reason: 142 artifacts, every one accounted for by three deltas — `@arena-strdup` removed and `@arena-strndup` renamed with its NUL store dropped (arena is imported almost everywhere, so this reaches 130+ artifacts as an identical hunk), `%InternEntry = type { ptr, ptr }` → `{ %Symbol, ptr }` (layout-identical: `%Symbol` is a one-field `{ ptr }`), and the four converted signatures. 955 tests, bootstrap fixed point, `check-headers` after regenerating the four `lib/*.h`/`.nuch` the signatures moved.
