## Stage 13 — Functional refactor R2: raw-array registry → `Vector` (2026-06-27)

**R2 done** ([functional-refactor.md](stage13/functional-refactor.md) §R2).
All 18 of the spec-listed compiler registries now ride `lib/vector.nuc`'s
`(Vector ptr)` substrate instead of hand-rolled `g-X:ptr` + `g-num-X`/`g-cap-X`
globals (with `malloc`/`memcpy`/`free` growth thunks) or fixed `MAX-*` arena
pre-allocs. 136 tests pass; **`make bootstrap` is a byte-identical fixed point**
(`stage1.ll == stage2.ll`); `make update-bootstrap` refreshed the committed
artifacts.

Converted, one at a time behind `make test` gates (Vector is append-only and
order-preserving, so iteration order is unchanged):

- **Batch 1 — hand-rolled growables** (each *deleted* its `g-num-*`/`g-cap-*`
  globals and `memcpy` growth thunk): `g-generics`, `g-protocols`,
  `g-conformances`, `g-proto-supers`, `g-tmpl-conformances` (all `src/generics.nuc`);
  `g-strs` (string-literal table, `src/scope.nuc` — id stays the element index);
  the parallel `g-fnty-names`/`g-fnty-types` (`src/type-mangle.nuc`); the parallel
  `g-deferror-name-sids`/`g-deferror-msg-sids` (the old `g-deferrors-len` is now
  `count - 1`; a reserved index-0 placeholder keeps the vector index equal to the
  1-based runtime error id; sids are stored in the `ptr` slot via an i64↔ptr cast).
- **Batch 2 — fixed arena pre-allocs** (each replaced its `MAX-*`-sized arena
  alloc with `make-vec`; the `MAX-*` overflow guards are kept, now testing
  `(cast i32 (count g-X))`, so behaviour is exactly preserved): `g-structs`,
  `g-uniondefs`, `g-union-templates`, `g-struct-templates`, `g-enumdefs`,
  `g-binops`, `g-macros`, `g-rmacros`, `g-blanket`, `g-cast-rules`.

**Representation note.** The old inline-array registries stored their entries
*by value* in one contiguous block (`generic-new`/`register-struct`/… returned a
pointer into the array). The migration keeps the **element type `ptr`** (per the
substrate-only invariant): each entry is now individually `arena-alloc`-ed and the
vector holds its pointer. This is strictly *safer* than the old code, whose
`memcpy`-on-grow left previously-returned element pointers dangling/stale; the
arena allocations are pointer-stable for the whole compilation.

**Byte-identical, not just re-converged.** The risk table predicted pointer-origin
drift requiring a controlled refresh. In practice the committed boot already
supports every `Vector` op used (`make-vec`/`conj`/`count`/`invoke`), and the IR
emitted for a given source is a function of registry *contents in order* (not the
internal substrate), so the old boot and the R2 compiler emit identical IR for the
same source — `stage1.ll == stage2.ll` held at every step.

**Out of spec (flagged, not converted).** Three more hand-rolled parallel-array
growables matching the same pattern were found that the §R2 list does **not**
enumerate (added later by the type-erasure commit `c4d973e`):
`g-boxedfn-keys`/`g-boxedfn-types` (`src/union-registry.nuc`),
`g-dyn-keys`/`g-dyn-types`/`g-dyn-protos` (`src/union-registry.nuc`), and
`g-vtable-keys`/`g-vtable-names` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`). They sit on the boxing /
vtable-emission path; converting them is a natural follow-up but was left to a
coordinator decision to avoid scope creep on the spec'd task.

## Stage 13 — Functional refactor R3: compiler loop refactor (2026-06-28)

**R3 done** ([functional-refactor.md](stage13/functional-refactor.md) §R3).
Converted `while` counted loops to `dotimes` and `Node*` cdr-list walks to
`doseq-iter + list-iter` across four source-imported compiler files:

- **`src/scope.nuc`**: `program-defn-lookup` (counted `(Vector ptr)` scan → `dotimes`)
- **`src/type-mangle.nuc`**: `fnty-intern`, `fnty-resolve`, `tyvar-index-of` (counted → `dotimes`); `subst-tyvars-sym` (cdr-list walk → `doseq-iter + list-iter`). Added `(import-use list)`.
- **`src/type-utils.nuc`**: no while loops present; left untouched.
- **`src/nuch.nuc`**: eight emission helpers (`emit-nuch-list`, `emit-nuch-defstruct`, `emit-nuch-declare`, `emit-nuch-defenum`, `emit-nuch-defmethod`, `emit-nuch-extend`, `emit-nuch-declare-import`, `emit-nuch-defmethod-import`) → `dotimes`; three cdr-list walks (`emit-nuch-header`, `emit-defunion-import`, `emit-nuch-import-forms`) → `doseq-iter + list-iter`. Added `(import-use list)`.
- **`src/union-registry.nuc`**: seven counted loops → `dotimes` (companion batch by sub-agent).

Non-unit-stride loops and the `scope-lookup` reverse scan were left
as-is per the leave-alone list. 136 tests pass; **`make bootstrap` is a
byte-identical fixed point** (`stage1.ll == stage2.ll`).

### R3 Batch 2 — `src/generics.nuc` counted-loop cluster (2026-06-29)

**Batch 2 complete.** Converted 12 functions' counted `while` scans to
`dotimes` (same-shape swaps; the only IR delta is the `dotimes` init
`(* n 0)` adopting the count's type — clang folds it, and both bootstrap
stages share the change so the fixed point holds):

- `generic-remove-matching-user-method` (first/scan loop only — the
  `(< (+ j 1) …)` in-place compaction loop is LEAVE-ALONE)
- `init-generics` (registry scan over `(count g-binops)`)
- `params-type-eq`, `mangle-fn-name`, `generic-find-method-exact`,
  `generic-binds-for` (method scan + tyvar/spellings scan),
  `generic-resolve` (tier-0/tier-1/tier-2 + nested arg-adapt scans),
  `operator-user-resolve` (exact + widen scans), `unify-tpat`,
  `valid-resolve-type` (widen + generic-template scans),
  `proto-sigs-resolve`, `tmpl-conformance-check-one` (4 scans: null-init,
  arg-seed, bound-recovery with early return, binding substitution)

Leave-alone list respected: `finalize-generics`, `register-generic-defn`,
`recover-assoc-into`, `drain-mono-worklist`, `derive-closure-conformance`,
`emit-extend`, and the `node-type` family are untouched. 136 tests pass;
byte-identical bootstrap.

**Gotcha hit:** a `(return X)` accidentally left inside the `dotimes`
body (one close-paren short on the last in-loop statement) silently
breaks lookups — the function returns on the first non-matching
iteration instead of after the loop. The IR signature is a missing
`inc!`/`br-loop-back` just before a spurious `ret`. Documented in
[functional-refactor.md](stage13/functional-refactor.md) §R3.

### R3 Batch 3 — `src/abi.nuc` + `src/union-emit.nuc` counted loops (2026-06-29)

**Batch 3 complete.** Four same-shape `dotimes` swaps, byte-identical bootstrap:

- `src/abi.nuc`: `abi-union-size`, `abi-struct-align`, `abi-struct-size`
  (counted scans over the `field-types` raw array; the running-offset and
  max-fold accumulators preserved; order-significant `abi-struct-size` stays
  in order since `dotimes` preserves it). `abi-class-eightbyte` is LEAVE-ALONE
  (SysV ABI codegen).
- `src/union-emit.nuc`: `emit-union-construct` (counted scan over `ftypes`;
  sequential GEP offsets preserved). The `emit-match*` family is LEAVE-ALONE.

`src/repl.nuc` `repl-eval-form` was a target but LEFT ALONE: both its remaining
loops start at non-zero (`si:i32 pre-len`, `dj:i32 (+ si 1)`) and don't fit the
counted-from-zero `dotimes` shape without a force-fit. 136 tests pass; byte-identical
bootstrap.

### R3 Batch 4 — `src/nucleusc.nuc` counted-cluster sweep (2026-06-29)

**Batch 4 complete.** 18 loops across 16 functions converted to `dotimes`,
byte-identical bootstrap. Find/lookup shape (`ns-ir-prefix-{set,get}`,
`struct-field-index`, `struct-field-idx`, `generic-resolve-nullable`,
`generic-has-receiver-method`, `vtable-memo-lookup`, `dyn-method-slot`,
`lbl-find`, `enumdef-lookup`, `try-import-path`) plus non-find scans
(`narrow-names`, `ns-prefix-sanitize` byte walk, `admit-erased-conformance`,
`union-drop-arm`, `expand-macro-call` scan, `emit-string-table`, `compile-and-link`).
The paren-placement gotcha from Batch 2 fired twice (`lbl-find`,
`enumdef-lookup`), caught pre-build. LEFT ALONE: `emit-export` (starts at
`i:i32 1`, skips head), `inject-import-aliases` (`[start,end)` slice,
non-zero start), `expand-macro-call` 2nd loop (reverse stride), and the entire
call-emit / body-emit / literal-lowering / closure-synthesis / fixpoint /
state-machine leave-alone list. 136 tests pass; byte-identical bootstrap.

**Strategic finding (Batches 3–4):** the original R3 cluster plan assumed
registry lookups → `find` and registry scans → `any?`/`every?`. That is
**not viable**: every compiler registry is `(Vector ptr)`, and `(Maybe ptr)`
niche-encoding crashes every combinator that internally does
`(match (next it) …)` over a `(VecIter ptr)`. So clusters 1, 2, 6 (the
registry find/any?/for-each sites) stay `dotimes`; only the **AST `Node*`
cdr-list walks** (via `ListIter`, which yields matchable `i64`) can move to
`doseq-iter`+`list-iter`. The remaining R3 work is the `doseq-iter` cluster
over AST cdr-lists (expected to drift; controlled refresh per the bootstrap
policy). See [functional-refactor.md](stage13/functional-refactor.md) §R3.

## Stage 13 — Functional refactor R4: library & examples (2026-06-30)

**R4 done** ([functional-refactor.md](stage13/functional-refactor.md) §R4).
Library and examples refactored to use the R1 combinators, `dotimes`, `doseq`,
`doseq-iter`, `doseq-split`, and `memset` where they fit. 138 tests pass;
bootstrap byte-identical (no refresh — examples don't affect the boot, and the
lib/ changes are inert).

**lib/ changes:**
- `lib/strview.nuc`: `strview-char-count` counted loop → `dotimes`; `strview-hash`
  FNV fold → `reduce` over `ByteIter` (requires `ByteIter` definition to precede
  `strview-hash` in the file — moved the struct + conformance above the hash function)
- `lib/string.nuc`: `string-push-bytes-raw` counted loop → `dotimes`
- `lib/hashmap.nuc`: three state-zeroing loops (`hashmap-init`,
  `hashmap-init-alloc`, `hashmap-resize`) → `memset` (matching `hashset.nuc` pattern)

**lib/ evaluated, left alone:**
- hashset set-algebra (`union`/`difference`/`intersection`): walk raw internal
  `states[]` arrays — probe-table internals, leave-alone per design doc.
- UTF-8 validators (`string-push-str`, `string-from-view`, `string-from-cstr`):
  stateful decode (variable `nbytes` advance per step), not a simple counted loop.
- hash-table probe internals, iterator `next` bodies, parser/lexer state machines
  in `reader.nuc`, `memmove`/capacity-doubling in `vector.nuc`: all leave-alone.

**examples/ changes:**
- Counted loops → `dotimes`: `hello.nuc`, `ifwhile.nuc`, `mutual.nuc`
- Hand-rolled → combinators: `assoc-types.nuc` (`collect-all` → `into-iter`,
  `sum-i32-iter` → `sum`, MapIter drive → `for-each`, Vector print → `for-each`);
  `assoc-iter-return.nuc` (`count-coll` → `reduce`); `rest-defn.nuc` (`sum` →
  `reduce` over `ListIter`, `print-each` → `for-each` over `ListIter`);
  `entry-test.nuc` (HashMapEntryIter → `doseq-iter`)
- Vector iteration → `doseq`: `boxedfn.nuc`, `dyn-protocol.nuc`,
  `comb-transform.nuc`, `comb-order.nuc`
- Cons-list walks → `doseq-iter` over `ListIter`: `list.nuc`, `quasiquote.nuc`
- String-split done-flag loops → `doseq-split`: `string-split-test.nuc` (6 sites),
  `split-iter-test.nuc`

**New dogfood examples:**
- `examples/comb-storage.nuc`: wires R1 closure-returning combinators
  (`compose`/`partial`/`complement`/`constantly`) to a `(Vector (BoxedFn (i32) i32))`,
  walks with `doseq`, invokes each box. Proves combinators compose with storable
  closures end-to-end.
- `examples/dyn-comb.nuc`: heterogeneous values behind `(dyn Render)` in a
  `(Vector (dyn Render))`, walked with `doseq` dispatching the protocol method
  through each box's vtable. Proves combinators compose with `(dyn P)` storage.

**Surprises:**
- `sum` combinator name conflicts with user `sum` defn in `rest-defn.nuc` —
  renamed to `sum-args`.

## Stage 13 — Niche-encoded Maybe iterator integration (2026-06-29)

**Iterator conversion complete.** The pointer-yielding iterators (`ListIter`,
`SplitIter`, `LineIter`) now yield `ptr` directly instead of `i64` (with explicit
casts). This closes the last major gap in niche-encoded `(Maybe ptr)` support:
iterators over pointer collections can now use the standard `(Iterator E)`
protocol with `match`-able `(Maybe ptr)` results.

**Root fix:** `niche-layout-of` in `src/union-emit.nuc:495` rejected niche-encoded
`(Maybe ptr)` because it checked `elem=null` before checking `pkind`. Since bare
`ptr` has `elem=null` (but `pkind=PTR-REF` after the Phase F flip), niche-encoding
`(Maybe ptr)` produced a type with `pkind=PTR-MAYBE` and `elem=null`, which the
function rejected. Removed the `elem=null` check, allowing it to recognize
`PTR-MAYBE` and `PTR-ERRPTR` regardless of whether `elem` is null.

**Changes:**
- `lib/list.nuc`: `ListIter` conforms to `(Iterator ptr)` instead of `(Iterator i64)`;
  `next` returns `(Maybe ptr)` instead of `(Maybe i64)`
- `lib/string-split.nuc`: `SplitIter` and `LineIter` similarly converted
- `src/union-emit.nuc`: removed `elem=null` check from `niche-layout-of`
- `src/generics.nuc`, `src/nuch.nuc`, `src/type-mangle.nuc`: updated cast patterns
  from `(cast ptr:Node (cast ptr cur))` to `(cast ptr:Node cur)` since elements are
  now `ptr` directly
- `examples/comb-shapes.nuc`, `examples/listiter-test.nuc`, `examples/split-iter-test.nuc`:
  updated consumers to use `ptr` instead of `i64`
- `context/conventions.md`: updated documentation to reflect that `ListIter` yields
  `ptr` directly

**Bootstrap:** `make update-bootstrap` required (the `niche-layout-of` fix changes
the compiler's emit behavior). 136 tests pass; `make bootstrap` is a byte-identical
fixed point.

## Stage 12 N8 — split `src/nucleusc.nuc` into focused files (2026-06-22)

**N8 complete — six extractions landed (all from `src/nucleusc.nuc`).** Iterative, one-file-at-a-time code motion; `make` + `make test` (110) + `make bootstrap` green after each, boot refreshed and reconverged when relocation perturbed the IR ordering. `src/nucleusc.nuc`: 12,428 → **7,193** lines.

- **`src/type-utils.nuc` (286 lines):** the `; Types` + `; Stage 10: pointer kinds` sections (`make-type`/`types-init`/`type-to-ir`/`type-to-c`/`ptr-int-ir`/`type-size`/`is-int-type`/…/`ptr-pkind`/`type-as-pkind`/`pkind-meet`/`pkind-flow-check`/`require-derefable`). Imported at the same position (before `abi`); depends only on `compiler-types`, the type singletons, `alloc-type`, `fmt-s`, `die-at`.
- **`src/scope.nuc` (177 lines):** symbol table (`scope-new`/`scope-define`/`scope-lookup`/`terminate-after-noreturn`/`scope-push-cleanup`), string-literal table (`intern-string`), codegen helpers (`new-tmp`/`new-label-id`/`reset-function-state`/`in-jit-module`/`program-defn-lookup`/`program-defn-record`). Imported **before** `abi` because `abi.nuc` calls `scope-define`/`new-tmp`; the lone abi-dependent helper `macro-jit-ensure-decl` stays in `nucleusc.nuc` (after the abi import) to break the otherwise-mutual cycle.
- **`src/generics.nuc` (1,962 lines):** the polymorphism registry + bounded-generic def-time checking + Stage 11 T2 tyvar inference/structural unification + Valid inferred-bound checker. Imported **before** `node-type`/emit-* dispatch (just after `union-registry`), since those resolve `generic-lookup`/`generic-find-method-exact`/`generic-has-receiver-method` at emit time.
  - **`src/type-mangle.nuc` (125 lines) — cycle break:** `generics`↔`union-registry` are mutually dependent (`generics` calls `lookup-struct`/`parse-type-from-node`/…; `union-registry` calls `type-spelling`/`type-mangle-token`/`subst-tyvars-node`). The five cross-edge mangling/substitution helpers (`type-spelling`/`type-mangle-token`/`tyvar-index-of`/`subst-tyvars-sym`/`subst-tyvars-node`, transitive closure, needing only `split-colon-segments`) were pulled out and imported **before** `union-registry`, so each file then sees the other's needed functions.
- **Protocols & node-type → appended to `src/generics.nuc` (D + F):** the `; Protocols & conformances` section (≈920 lines) and the `; node-type` non-emitting typing pass (≈324 lines) are each **mutually recursive with the generics machinery** (generics ↔ protocol-lookup/conformance-lookup/emit-extend/…; generic-body checkers `gcheck`/`valid-walk` ↔ node-type/node-type-call/node-type-sym), so neither can sit in a separately-imported file. Both are co-located in `generics.nuc` (1,962 → 3,232 lines), where a single `(import-use generics)` registers all halves before any body emits — the same cycle-break the plan prescribed for protocols. (A standalone `node-type.nuc` was tried and rejected at build: `unknown: node-type-sym`.)
- **Tagged-sum / Maybe / error-handling codegen → `src/union-emit.nuc` (E, 1,554 lines):** the adjacent, mutually-recursive `; Maybe transition forms` + `; tagged-sum construction and elimination` sections (≈1,535 lines, 33 defns: `emit-make`/`emit-union-construct`/`emit-niche-*`/`emit-match*`/`union-target-rewrite`/`stamp-maybe-type`/`emit-not`/`emit-short-circuit`/`emit-ptr-add`/`emit-signal`/`emit-handler-call`/`niche-layout-of`/…). Imported at the section's original position; nothing before it calls in, the forward callers (emit-* dispatch) follow.
- **Late-binding function-pointer hooks (two new back-edges):** moving the protocol/node-type code into `generics.nuc` and the tagged-sum code into the later `union-emit.nuc` created two single back-edges from earlier-imported files: `union-registry.nuc`'s `struct-template-stamp-types` → `tmpl-conformance-check-instance` (now in generics), and `node-type-call` (generics) → `stamp-maybe-type` (now in union-emit). Each is bridged by a `ptr`-typed global hook (`g-tmpl-conf-check-hook`, `g-stamp-maybe-type-hook`), installed in `init-blanket` at compiler-init time and `funcall`ed at the call site after a `cast` to the precise `(fn …)` type. **No bootstrap-host change was needed** — the hooks are plain `ptr` (the committed host cannot null-initialise an `(fn …)`-typed global), so the new source compiles under the old boot.
- **Mechanism:** `import-use` processes a file inline (prescan-then-emit), and the whole-unit defn-signature prescan registers only `nucleusc.nuc`'s own defns (it does not recurse into imports). So an imported file's functions are visible only at/after its import point; a cross-file cycle is resolved either by co-locating the mutually-recursive halves in one file (D/F), by extracting the smaller cross-edge earlier (the `type-mangle.nuc` precedent), or — for a single irreducible back-edge — by a late-binding function-pointer hook (the two above). All N8 moves are pure code motion but not raw-byte-identical to the prior boot (relocation renumbers `@.str.N`/`%N` and reorders `define`s — identical sorted `define` set + string-constant set); each is a self-reproducing fixed point, so the boot was refreshed (`boot/nucleusc.ll` + both Windows boot IRs + `bin/nucleusc`) and reconverged. New/grown files: `src/generics.nuc` 3,232; `src/union-emit.nuc` 1,554.

## Stage 12 N7 — source migration + `import` flip (2026-06-21)

**N7 landed (the Phase-F-style breaking flip).** Two green sub-steps:

- **Sub-step 1 (mechanical rewrite):** all `(include X)` → `(import-use "X.h")`, all legacy `(import X)`/`(import "x.h")` → `(import-use …)` across `src/`/`lib/`/`examples/`/`tests/`; the auto-prelude (`prepend-prelude-import`) and the REPL macro-preload string switched to `import-use`. Pure source change — the only IR delta from N6 was the two intended embedded string constants.
- **Sub-step 2 (flip + delete):** `emit-import` now delegates to `emit-import-prefixed`, so bare `import` is prefix-qualified (default prefix = lib's last dotted component, or `c` for a C-header string path). The `include` keyword is fully removed (both dispatch cases, `g-special-form-set` membership, the dead `emit-include` in `src/cheader.nuc`, the REPL `include` branch). C headers now flow only through `import`/`import-use`/`import-only` with a string path.
- **Bootstrap:** N1–N6 had left the committed boot at stage 11 (it only knew the old `include`/`import`, which N1–N6 source still used), so N7 needed the documented two-stage refresh — build the N6 compiler with the stage-11 boot, `make update-bootstrap`, then apply N7 and re-converge. Each sub-step reaches a self-reproducing fixed point (`build/nucleusc.ll == boot/nucleusc.ll`).
- **Verified:** `include` is an unknown form; `(import lib prefix)` resolves `prefix/name`; `(import nsgeom)` dispatches `nsgeom/area` to `@geom__area`. No `(import …)`/`(include …)` call sites remain — only `import-use`/`import-only`/`import-prefixed`/`unsafe-import-private`. Docs swept (`docs/toplevel.md` + ~13 other doc files; the `include` row removed, `import` documented as prefix-qualified). 110 tests pass, bootstrap byte-identical.

## Stage 12 N6 — `.nuch` round-trip + tooling (2026-06-21)

**N6 landed (`.nuch` + `--emit-cheader`).** Namespace awareness for the header-emit and import paths so a namespaced library's symbols round-trip with the correct mangled link name.

- **`.nuch` producer (`emit-nuch-header`)**: applies a leading `(ns NAME)` / `set-ir-prefix` before the prescans so `finalize-generics` bakes namespace-mangled overload symbols (`@geom__area.tok`); emits `(ns NAME)` (+ `(set-ir-prefix "...")` when overridden) into the header for non-`user` namespaces.
- **`.nuch` importer (`emit-nuch-import-forms`)**: new `ns` / `set-ir-prefix` dispatch cases set `g-current-ns` (scoped by `do-import`'s save/restore); `emit-nuch-declare-import` (solitary functions) and `emit-extern` (globals) now compute the link name via `ns-ir-base` instead of a hardcoded `@%s`, so `(declare area:i32 …)` rebinds to `@geom__area`.
- **`--emit-cheader` (`emit-cheader-header`/`emit-cheader-declare`)**: applies the leading `ns` / `set-ir-prefix` and emits each C function name via `ns-ir-base` — the C-legal `geom__area`, never `geom/area`.
- All three are identity under `user` → existing headers and the bootstrap stay byte-identical (verified: pre-N6 `bin/nucleusc` and N6 `build/nucleusc` emit identical IR for the compiler source; `make bootstrap` is a fixed point).
- **Deferred:** REPL `apropos`/`locate`/`doc` (and the rest of the documented meta-forms) no longer exist in `repl-eval-form` — dropped in the Stage 11 collections/protocols rewrite of `src/repl.nuc` (last present in commit `f9a4a83`). Restoring the meta-form facility is a separate task; making it namespace-aware presupposes it exists.

**New test:** `tests/fixtures/nsgeomlib.nuc` + a `run-tests.sh` N6 section (`.nuch` carries `(ns geom)`; cheader emits `geom__area` and never `geom/area`; importing the `.nuch` by path resolves `g/area` → `@geom__area`; lib + prelude-excluded consumer link and run `area=42 perimeter=26`). 110 checks pass.

## Stage 12 N5 — `export` re-export (2026-06-21)

**N5 landed.** The `export` facade form (`emit-export`): re-exports an explicit list of qualified symbols under the current namespace's name, reusing the original `Sym`/`ir-name` (a pure resolution alias, no code emitted). `lib/nsgfacade.nuc` (`gfacade` re-exporting `geom/area`/`geom/perimeter`) + `examples/export-test.nuc`. Bootstrap byte-identical.

## Stage 12 N4 — IR mangling + `set-ir-prefix` + cross-namespace conformance (2026-06-21)

**N4 landed.** First stage where IR names diverge for non-`user` namespaces.

- **IR mangling**: symbols in namespace `foo` emit `@foo__bar`; `user` stays `@bar` (bare). `Generic.ir-prefix` snapshots the defining namespace's prefix at `generic-new()` time so monomorphizations in other namespaces still mangle correctly.
- **`set-ir-prefix`**: overrides the IR prefix for the current namespace. An `apply-early-set-ir-prefix` pre-pass applies a leading `set-ir-prefix` before the signature prescan so `finalize-generics` sees the override. Empty string forces bare names (C-ABI escape hatch).
- **String-path `.nuc`/`.nuch` imports**: `do-import` now routes `.nuc`/`.nuch` string paths as Nucleus files, not C headers. Enables `(import-prefixed "/abs/path/lib.nuc" prefix)`.
- **`import-alias-one` fix**: strips namespace qualifier before forming the alias key, so a symbol `geom/area` in the global table aliases as `prefix/area` (not `prefix/geom/area`).
- **Cross-namespace conformance**: `emit-extend` and `verify-conformance-params` apply `strip-ns-qualifier` to type/protocol names so `(extend Circle Area)` in namespace `shapes` resolves against bare-keyed registries.
- **`g-current-ns` initialization** moved before `init-generics` in `compiler-init` to prevent null-pointer crash in `ns-ir-prefix(g-current-ns)`.

**Bootstrap invariant:** Compiler and all libraries are still in `user` namespace — all IR names unchanged. 105 tests pass, `make bootstrap` green.

**New test:** `lib/nsgeom.nuc` (geom namespace library) + `examples/ns-mangle.nuc` + `tests/expected/ns-mangle.out` exercise IR mangling (`@geom__area`) and cross-namespace calls.

## Stage 12 N3 — Public/private + internal linkage (2026-06-21)

**N3 landed.** The trailing-`-` private definer variants are implemented across all
name-introducing forms:

- `defn-` / `defvar-` emit LLVM `internal` linkage (equivalent to C `static`) and
  mark the `Sym` with `sym-private=1`. These symbols cannot link from outside the
  compilation unit.
- `defconst-` / `defenum-` mark their `Sym`(s) `sym-private=1` (no linkage
  dimension; compile-time only).
- `defstruct-` / `defunion-` / `defmacro-` / `defprotocol-` route through the same
  handlers with the `g-defining-private` flag set (no `Sym` in `g-globals`; privacy
  is purely about visibility).

**Import filtering:** `inject-import-aliases` (called by prefixed import forms —
`import-prefixed`, `unsafe-import-private`) skips `sym-private=1` symbols unless
`g-import-include-private=1` is set (set by `unsafe-import-private`). `import-use`
imports everything as before (flat-everything behavior).

**Prescans:** `prescan-struct-names` now also handles `"defstruct-"` / `"defunion-"`;
`prescan-defn-signatures` handles `"defn-"` / `"defunion-"`;
`prescan-protocols` handles `"defprotocol-"` — so private type/protocol/fn definitions
work correctly within the same file.

**Bootstrap invariant:** No compiler symbol is private, so all boot artifacts are
byte-identical. 103 tests pass, `make bootstrap` green.

**New test:** `examples/private-defn.nuc` + `tests/expected/private-defn.out` exercise
`defn-` / `defvar-` / `defconst-` within one file; IR shows `define internal` /
`internal global` linkage.

## Compiler self-adoption of collections (2026-06-20)

Refactor of `src/nucleusc.nuc` to use the Stage 11 collections where they add
safety or clarity, per [stage11/compiler-collections-refactor.md](stage11/compiler-collections-refactor.md).
Two milestones landed, each byte-identical-bootstrap-preserving (102/102 tests):

- **Hand-rolled `Vec` → `(Vector ptr)`** (was behind a `make-vec`/`vec-*`
  wrapper); the `Vec` struct is removed. Every indexed read of the compiler's
  dynamic pointer tables is now bounds-checked.
- **`special-form-named` / `primitive-type-named`** (`(or (= name "lit") …)` walls)
  → `(HashSet CStr)` membership (`init-name-sets` at startup).
- **cleanup3 Steps A+B** (`[stage11/cleanup3.md](stage11/cleanup3.md)`): deleted
  the `vec-push`/`vec-len`/`vec-get`/`vec-pop` wrappers — globals/locals holding a
  table are typed `(ref (Vector ptr))` / `(ref (HashSet CStr))` and use sites call
  `conj`/`count`/`invoke`/`contains?` directly (no per-use cast); predicate params
  typed `CStr`. Collections are built against a shared **arena** `AllocHandle`
  (`g-arena-alloc`, init first in `compiler-init`; new `hashset-init-alloc` /
  `hashmap-init-alloc` mirror `vector-init-alloc`) — no malloc/leak. Residual casts
  confined to construction sites + four Vec-param helpers. **Bootstrap constraint
  found:** a `(ref (Vector ptr))` in any `defn` *signature* stamps `%Vector.ptr`
  at the whole-unit signature prescan, ahead of the allocator import, breaking the
  prelude's macro-JIT module (`%AllocHandle` undefined) under the unmodified boot —
  so `make-vec` returns `:ptr` and the helpers take `:ptr` (one internal cast).
  Full root-cause + the deferred proper fix in `stage11/cleanup3.md`.
- **cleanup3 Stages 1+2 — drain deferral + `make-vec` retype** (two-stage boot
  refresh, `stage11/cleanup3.md`): **Stage 1** taught `drain-pending-union-irs` to
  skip a queued type whose `TY-STRUCT`/`TY-UNION` field deps aren't yet emitted
  (`pending-union-deps-ready`), leaving it queued for a later drain. Emitted-flag
  tracking is global but `g-type-bufp` is one shared buffer every module
  concatenates, so the flag already means "present in the current module." Dormant
  + byte-identical, then baked into boot (`make update-bootstrap`, incl. Windows
  IRs). **Stage 2** retyped `make-vec` → `(ref (Vector ptr))` and the four helpers'
  Vec params to `(ref (Vector ptr))`, removing all workaround casts; the prescan
  now stamps `%Vector.ptr` early and the deferral defers it past `%AllocHandle`.
  Byte-identical against the refreshed boot, 102/102, abi-test green. Parametric
  defn signatures must use the **list form** (`(make-vec (ref (Vector ptr)))`),
  not colon-sugar (`:ref:(Vector ptr)`), which mis-parses the name. Final
  `update-bootstrap` deferred to Step C.
- **cleanup3 Step C — `(v i)` routes `invoke → get → _get`** (`stage11/cleanup3.md`):
  `emit-callable-value` and its type-pass mirror `callable-value-type` now decide by
  the **callee type** via the new side-effect-free predicate
  `generic-has-receiver-method` (first-param `type-eq` for METHOD-USER, `unify-tpat`
  over the receiver for METHOD-GENERIC). A type with an `invoke` method indexes its
  argument as a **value**, so `(v idx)` evaluates a local `idx` and indexes instead
  of reading a field named `idx`; a plain struct (no `invoke`, no custom `get`) still
  lands on the raw `_get` field intrinsic with byte-identical IR. Byte-identical
  bootstrap required purging the callable field-read form from the `(ref (Vector …))`
  method bodies in `lib/vector.nuc` and `lib/string.nuc` (rewritten to `_get`; only
  `Vector` has `invoke`). `examples/callable.nuc` updated to the new contract and
  demonstrates the local-index case. Final `make update-bootstrap` (incl. Windows
  IRs) run; reconverges byte-identically, 102/102, abi-test green.

- **cleanup3 `into` + `#{…}` for name-sets**: the 72-`insert` run for
  `g-special-form-set` and the 19-`insert` run for `g-primitive-type-set` in
  `init-name-sets` replaced by single `(into g-set #{ … } (HashSetIter CStr))`
  forms. Arena allocation + `hashset-init-alloc` lines unchanged. This is the
  endorsed in-function alternative to global collection literals (which remain out —
  no pre-main static-init). Byte-identical bootstrap, 102/102, abi-test green.

By-value fixed-cap tables (`g-structs`, `g-uniondefs`, `g-*-templates`,
`g-enumdefs`, `g-cast-rules`, `g-strs`), symbol-identity dispatch chains, and
map/reduce/filter were evaluated and left as-is (poor fits — interior-pointer
stability, marginal gain, or closure-less boilerplate; reasons in the design doc
§7). No language surface changed. `make update-bootstrap`/commit pending user
go-ahead.

## Known constraints / gotchas

- **`Node.car`/`Node.cdr` and macro parameters are `(raw Node)`** (were untyped `ptr`). This lets macros and AST-walking code chain member access cast-free — `(p car)`, `((p cdr) car)` — instead of `((cast ptr:Node p) car)`. Existing `(cast ptr:Node …)` on node values stays valid (a no-op `ptr`↔`(raw Node)` reinterpret) but is no longer needed. **Resolved** ([stage14/macro-conditional-casts.md](stage14/macro-conditional-casts.md), MC-1…MC-4 all done): a shared `type-join` now absorbs a bare, elem-less `ptr` branch (quasiquote/`gensym`/`null` results) into the typed side's element type at pkind `raw` — so a `cond`/`if` mixing `(raw Node)` with a bare `ptr` branch joins to `(raw Node)` with **no cast**, and quasiquote/`gensym` results are themselves now typed `(raw Node)` rather than bare `ptr`. Pointer *kind* was never actually a collapse source — kinds meet via `pkind-meet`. Only branches of genuinely different *element* types (`(raw Node)` vs `i32`, or two different struct types) still collapse the join to `void`, which remains a real type error (`let`/`set!` `init type mismatch`, or a macro whole-body `cond` silently returning `null`). All vestigial `(cast ptr:Node …)`/`(cast ptr …)` casts have been deleted from `lib/macros.nuc`. See [docs/macros.md](../docs/macros.md) and [docs/types.md](../docs/types.md).
- Variadic arithmetic macros now live in `lib/macros.nuc` and are auto-prepended via `lib/prelude.nuc`; binary primitives are `_+ _- _* _/`.
- Macro JIT names collide after sanitization if two macros share the same non-alphanumeric character; fixed by appending macro index (`__macro_<sanitized>_<index>`).
- REPL `defn` redefinition uses thunk indirection (`@foo` → load `@foo.tgt` → call `@foo.impl.<N>`) because pure resource-tracker swap leaves cross-module call sites with stale baked-in addresses. See `design/stage6-redefinition.md` "Result".
- **REPL `defmacro` segfaults or hangs. RESOLVED (Stage 16, 2026-08-15).** Root cause: the REPL's `defmacro` arm (`src/repl.nuc`) did not call `(open-module-streams)`, which its `compile-time` neighbour does for exactly this reason — the macro body compiler opens by flushing `g-type-stream`/`g-decl-stream`, and between REPL forms those `FILE*`s are closed, so the abort was glibc's `invalid stdio handle` on a closed stream. One line. The prelude's own macros never hit it because they load through `repl-preload-macros`, not that arm, which is why a REPL that could expand `when`/`dotimes` still died on the first macro a user typed. Found while writing the `macrolet` REPL transcript; pinned by `tests/repl/s16-macrolet.in`. Original report follows. Typing a `defmacro` form (e.g. `(defmacro triple (x) (quasiquote ...))`) at `build/nucleusc -i` segfaults or hangs the REPL. Found while smoke-testing the RV-0 repl.nuc triple fix; reproduced identically on the pre-existing baseline compiler via `git stash`, so it is a latent, previously-undocumented bug, not a regression from that change.
- **Top-level dispatch does not expand user macros.** The compiler's top-level form dispatch (the `case hp` block in `src/nucleusc.nuc`) matches literal head symbols (`defn`, `defvar`, `fn-attr`, …) *before* any macro expansion runs — macro expansion only happens for forms nested inside a function/macro body. A top-level invocation of a user `defmacro` (even one expanding to a single `defn`) therefore dies `error: unknown top-level form`, not a successful expansion. This rules out macro-based top-level definition sugar (e.g. a `defisr` macro wrapping several sibling top-level forms) without a dedicated top-level macro-expansion pass. Confirmed twice independently: once during Stage 14 AVR-5 (design/stage14/avr-targets.md §5), where the design's sketched `(defisr <vector> …)` macro was found not implementable for exactly this reason, and again by a separate verification pass reproducing the same minimal-macro failure. Not a bug — an architectural property of the current dispatch loop — but worth knowing before designing any future top-level sugar as a macro rather than a compiler-recognized directive (the `fn-attr` directive is the precedent for the latter path).
- **Binop `node-type` mistypes a literal-left, typed-non-literal-right operand pair. RESOLVED (Stage 15 W2a, [stage15-stress-test/literal-typing.md](stage15-stress-test/literal-typing.md)).** The fix: the type rule now lives in one shared function, `binop-result-type` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`), called by both `binop-coerce` (emit) and `node-type-call` (the type pass), so a binop's inferred type is the *unified* operand type and is symmetric in operand order. Verified against this bullet's own repro: `(defn f (x:ui8):ui8 (return (_+ 1 x)))` no longer emits the malformed `%t3 = trunc i32 %t2 to i8` (the committed boot compiler still does). `lib/avr.nuc`'s local `(unsafe/cast ui8 1)` workaround is now redundant but was left in place (harmless, and untouched by W2a's scope). Original report follows. `(defn f (x:ui8):ui8 (return (_+ 1 x)))`-shaped code (a bare int literal as the binop's *left* operand against a typed, non-literal *right* operand) mistypes in `node-type-call`'s binop typing (`src/generics.nuc`), which reports the literal's default `i32` instead of adapting to the right operand's type — while `emit` correctly adapts the literal. When the result flows into a coercion (return/let-init/typed arg) this manufactures a malformed `trunc i32 <i8-value> to i8`. General, not AVR-specific — hidden in ordinary code because the literal is usually on the right (`(_+ x 1)`); surfaced during AVR-4 (`lib/avr.nuc`'s `bit-mask`, `1 << bit`, where `shl` forces the literal left), worked around locally there with an explicit `(unsafe/cast ui8 1)` rather than fixed at the root. Root fix: adapt to the non-literal operand rather than always operand 0 in the binop type-check, then re-converge the bootstrap. Independently reproduced and confirmed by a separate verification pass: compiling `(defn f (x:ui8):ui8 (return (_+ 1 x)))` and inspecting the emitted IR directly confirms the exact malformed instruction (`%t3 = trunc i32 %t2 to i8` where `%t2` is already `i8`); candidate for AVR-6/AVR-7 or a standalone fix.
- **`lib/hash.nuc` has a lossy `i64→usize` cast that dies on AVR's 16-bit `usize`.** `lib/hash.nuc:82` casts an `i64` hash value down to `usize` unconditionally; on hosted targets `usize` is 64-bit so the cast is a no-op, but on AVR `usize` is 16-bit (per AVR-2's `ptr-int-type`), so the same cast is a genuine narrowing the compiler correctly refuses. This blocks any AVR program that imports `lib/hash.nuc` (directly or transitively via `HashMap`/`HashSet`) before it ever reaches AVR-6's function-value/`dyn` diagnostics — found while building an AVR-6 `dyn` test fixture (`examples/dyn-protocol.nuc` couldn't be used for this reason; a minimal hash-free fixture was substituted). Not yet fixed — a future AVR-2/AVR-7-flavored follow-up should narrow the hash computation itself (e.g. truncate/fold to `usize`'s actual width explicitly) rather than relying on the illegal implicit cast.
- **A type annotation in VALUE position was parsed and then silently discarded. FIXED 2026-08-23** ([stage16-ergonomics/as-sugar.md](stage16-ergonomics/as-sugar.md)) — it is now an `as` cast, so a wrong or nonexistent type there is a located diagnostic instead of a no-op. Found while evaluating that item and worth keeping as a shape: `emit-symbol-ref` called `split-typed` and threw the type half away, so `(printf "%ld\n" x:i32)` on an `i64` local compiled and meant `x`, and — the sharper half — **`x:NoSuchType` compiled too**, since a discarded annotation is never even looked up. Same class as the `defconst` bug W4b swept out of definition *names* with `reject-colon-in-def-name`, one position further along; both are colon-bearing spellings surviving into a position nothing validated. The discarding was deliberate, not accidental — the `for`/`dotimes` idiom `lib/macros.nuc` documents splices the annotated binding name into value position (`(< i:i32 n)`, `(inc! i:i32)`) — which is why the fix had to keep those working, and does: all 16 occurrences tree-wide are identity casts. **Six other passes still strip the same way and must keep doing so** (`emit-set` target, `fn-capture-walk`, `fn-rewrite-captures`, `node-binding-name`, `node-is-const-int-literal`/`const-fold-int`); that is exactly why the cast is emitted at the value read rather than rewritten into the tree. See context/conventions.md §"`defn` bodies are not desugared".
- **Variadic calls did not apply C's default argument promotions. FIXED 2026-08-18** ([stage16-ergonomics/varargs-promotion.md](stage16-ergonomics/varargs-promotion.md)). Found while evaluating [stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md](stage16-ergonomics/bool-truthiness.md)'s C-interop implications and fixed on its own, because it had nothing to do with `bool`. C promotes `_Bool`/`char`/`short` → `int` and `float` → `double` past a variadic callee's fixed prefix (C17 §6.5.2.2p6); `emit-call-with-args` passed the narrow type through, so one five-argument `printf` printed `-1940914476` for `i16 -300` and `0.000000` for `f32 2.5`. The wrong value is instructive: `0x8C4FFED4`, low half exactly `-300` and high half caller debris — *and not stable*, since the same program built from a different boot compiler printed `0x4EC0FED4`. The other four types (`i8`, `ui8`, `bool`, `Char`) printed correctly **by luck**, LLVM's x86-64 lowering materialising a narrow value into a zeroed register. Fix is `vararg-promote` (`src/nucleusc.nuc`), called per argument at index >= `num-params` of a variadic callee, beside the Stage 14 NS-3 StrView collapse — **not** in `coerce-int-val`, and this is the exception that shows the shape of that rule: the chokepoint converts a value toward a *declared type*, and a vararg has none, so the promotion is keyed on POSITION and only the argument walk knows where the `...` tail begins. It still delegates the widening *emission* to `coerce-int-val`, so `zext`-vs-`sext` (and `i1`'s unsignedness) stay decided in one place. Promotion target is the **target's** C `int` — `i16` where pointers are 2 bytes (AVR), `i32` elsewhere, via the `g-target-ptr-bytes` idiom `ptr-int-type` uses — not Nucleus's `int`, a fixed alias for `i32` that would be wrong on an 8-bit device. **Blast radius: 10 of 149 examples changed IR, 0 changed output** — every one a `bool` printed with `%d`, i.e. the corpus carried only the latent form. 777 tests (was 773), `make bootstrap` converges, abi/layout/avr/riscv/riscv-abi/check-headers green. Testing note worth generalising: three of six promoted types print correctly under *both* behaviours, so the unit asserts on the emitted **instruction** as well as the run, plus two negative cases a position-keyed rule needs (a variadic callee's *fixed* narrow parameter must stay narrow; an already-`int`-wide argument must gain no instruction). **Still open, separately:** no `zeroext`/`signext` anywhere in the tree (zero occurrences in `src/`, `lib/`, `docs/`, `context/`), so every `_Bool` parameter and return is declared bare `i1` where clang declares `zeroext i1` — including the 14 exported `_Bool` comparison overloads in `lib/strview.h`, `lib/string.h`, `lib/keyword.h`. Attempts to make that miscompile failed on both x86-64 (`setg %al`) and RISC-V (`sgtz a0,a0`), so it is a spec divergence and cheap insurance, not a live defect.

- **A bare `unsigned` or `signed` is not a type to the C header parser, and that path records no skip reason.** Found 2026-08-26 while evaluating [stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md). `struct { unsigned a; int b; }` abandons the struct and `unsigned f(void);` is dropped entirely, while `unsigned int`, `unsigned char/short/long` and `long unsigned` all work — so it is a hole in the declaration-specifier table, not in the declarator grammar. Zero occurrences across the glibc headers surveyed, which is why the Stage 16 census never saw it; pervasive in third-party headers, **libclang's own `clang-c/Index.h` among them** (`CXString` is `{ const void *data; unsigned private_flags; }`). The sharper half is the diagnostic: the declaration never reaches `g-cheader-skipped`, so the use site reports the bare `unknown: 'f' — not defined anywhere in this compilation unit` instead of Stage 15 W3c's `… its C header declaration was skipped (<reason>)`, leaving the user no thread to pull. Staged there as C1/C2 (~5 lines and a skip-reason record). Same document re-measures the post-L1–L5 parser at **102 of 111** named struct/union bodies laid out and **101 of 103** emitted C types matching clang's `sizeof`, and finds the inline function-pointer item is half-built rather than absent — the branch at `src/cheader.nuc:1398-1405` already collapses the field to `ptr` and merely loses the field *name*, so four of the nine remaining blocked types (`sigaction`, `sigevent`, both pthread cleanup structs) fall to one ~15-line repair.

- **A function pointer's type is not enforced anywhere: every typed slot takes any signature, and an indirect call checks, coerces and ABI-lowers nothing. RESOLVED (Stage 16, 2026-08-26)** — FP-1/FP-2 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §12; the signature check went into `coerce-int-val` ahead of its `sk == dk` short-circuit (and `fn-sig-eq`/`type-eq` moved to `src/abi.nuc` to be reachable from there), and both indirect-call emitters now route arguments through the shared `coerce-call-argument` plus the Stage 8 ABI. Original report follows. Found 2026-08-26 while re-reading [stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §4's "frictions" as language defects; plan in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md). Two independent halves, both live and both silent. **(1)** `safe-coerce-val` short-circuits on `(when (= sk dk) (return v))` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:3605`) before comparing signatures, so a `(fn i32)()` slot accepts an `():f64` function at a `let` init, a `set!`, a `.set!` field store or a call argument — `fn-sig-eq` (`src/generics.nuc:257`) exists, `type-eq` calls it at the call site and correctly answers 0, and the answer is then discarded. Prints `-1780842467`. **(2)** `emit-funcall-value` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:6145`) — the single indirect-call emitter, reached by both `funcall` and a `TY-FN` value in head position — never consults the function type's parameter list after `check-call-arity`, printing each argument as raw `type-to-ir`: no `pkind-flow-check`, no `safe-coerce-val`, no `vararg-promote`, no `abi-classify`. So an `i32` passed to an `f64` parameter emits `call double %t6(i32 %t7, double 4.0)` (the direct spelling is a compile error) and the Stage 8 struct ABI is bypassed entirely — the same function called directly and through a pointer prints `907` and `7`, and a `>16`-byte struct emits `call i32 %t0(%Big %t2)` against a `ptr byval(%Big)` define. **It was already known at one call site and worked around locally**: `src/union-emit.nuc:689` reads "Must go through `abi-emit-struct-call` (not `emit-funcall-value`)". The `BoxedFn` path (`src/nucleusc.nuc:8429`) coerces arguments but does not ABI-lower them — the same rule at a second site with a different subset applied. Ordering consequence recorded in the plan: these must be fixed **before** the C header importer stops erasing function-pointer types (`src/cheader.nuc:787`/`:1404`/`:1654`, which today make `qsort`/`atexit` need `(unsafe/cast ptr f)`), or that change trades a compile error for silent stack corruption in exactly the callback-with-by-value-structs case that motivates it.
- **A struct *value* is not a legal member-access receiver, and the documented workaround is longer than the language requires. RESOLVED (Stage 16, 2026-08-26)** — SV-1/SV-2; reads materialize the value into a fresh slot (`materialize-struct-value`, with `access-receiver-sdef` unwrapping the same receivers in the type pass), while `.set!`/`.&` take the receiver symbol's own slot so a by-value parameter is mutable in place as in C and a temporary stays an error. Original report follows. `(. v x)` / `(v x)` / `(_get v x)` / `(.set! v x 1)` / `(.& v x)` all gate on a `TY-PTR` to struct, so a by-value parameter, a call result, or a `let`-bound struct local is refused (`_get: operand must be pointer to struct or union`) — not C-specific, but it is what makes a by-value C API (libclang's cursors, and every `struct`-returning libc call) verbose. `(addr-of v)` on a `let`-bound local or a by-value parameter already works, so the `alloca` + `ptr-set!` idiom taught by `docs/structs-unions.md:62` and `docs/collections.md:26`/`:73` is one step longer than necessary today, independent of whether the receiver rule is relaxed. Staged as SV-1/SV-2 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md).
- **The four deferred C-parity items (wide/narrow floats, `packed`, bitfields, C11 anonymous members) are planned, and two of their stated blockers are stale.** Planned 2026-08-26 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §§6–11, on the standard that Nucleus should express anything C can; clang was the oracle for every ABI and layout claim. **Floats (FL-1…FL-7):** LLVM already has `half`/`x86_fp80`/`fp128` and knows their calling conventions for scalars (`define x86_fp80 @f(x86_fp80)`, `define fp128 @f(fp128)`, `define half @f(half)`), so the work is a ~21-site scalar-kind addition, not a new numeric tower. The apparent bootstrap blocker — the compiler being written in a Nucleus without f80, so unable to fold an f80 constant — **dissolves**: LLVM rejects decimal at those widths (`floating point constant does not have type 'x86_fp80'`) but f64→f80 and f64→f128 are *exact* widenings, so the renderer is integer bit-shuffling on the f64 pattern, the `f32-const-ir` idiom verbatim. `long double` is target-dependent (`x86_fp80` on x86-64, `fp128` on aarch64 **and** riscv64, `double` on AVR — all measured), so the Nucleus types are the representations `f16`/`f80`/`f128` and the C name maps per target, the `ptr-int-type` pattern. Two traps: the `unsafe/cast` float→float `cond` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:4052`) has two arms and a bare `true (do)` fall-through, so an unnamed width pair emits **no instruction** and passes the value unconverted; and `vararg-promote`'s `TY-F32`-only rule is already correct and must **not** be generalized — `x86_fp80` and `half` both pass through `...` unpromoted (measured). Aggregate ABI needs an X87 class (a struct holding a `long double` is MEMORY: `ptr byval align 16`) and an SSEUP rule (a struct holding a `__float128` is **not** MEMORY — it is one `fp128` in xmm, so the conservative answer is the wrong one). **Anonymous members (AN-1/AN-2): the sibling document's "Nucleus has nothing to lower that onto" is stale.** clang's model is an ordinary nested member with its own type reached by a two-level GEP, and `lookup-or-make-anon-struct`/`-union` (`src/union-registry.nuc:63`/`:107`) already provide exactly that — the C parser already calls the first (`src/cheader.nuc:1445`). Only name lookup *through* the member is missing. **Bitfields (BF-1…BF-4)** are the real work, and they share their prerequisite with anonymous members: both break the invariant "one Nucleus field = one LLVM member at the same index" that `emit-field-load` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:5293`) hard-codes, so both are unblocked by one change — a resolved field reference carrying a GEP *path* plus an optional bit range (FR-1, 17 `struct-field-index` callers, byte-identical on its own). **`packed` (PK-1…PK-3)** is the cheapest and its non-obvious half is that `emit-load`/`emit-store` derive `align` from `type-size` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:9851`/`:9860`), so a packed field must emit `align 1` or the IR carries a false alignment promise; the `<{ … }>` type line is written at **eight** sites. Named as not reachable: **decimal literals at f80/f128 stay f64-rounded** (hex-float literals, FL-3, are exact and mitigate it; a correctly-rounded arbitrary-precision decimal converter is its own project), and **`_Complex` is out of scope** — a distinct type constructor with its own ABI class and Annex G arithmetic, much easier after FL-1 than with it. Gate for all of it is the existing differential layout test (`tests/run-layout-test.sh` diffs `sizeof` and every field offset against the platform `cc`), extended with bitfield *value* round-trips (a bitfield has no `offsetof`, so offsets alone cannot catch a wrong shift or mask); acceptance is the 32-header census going 102/111 → 111/111 and the `sizeof` census 101/103 → 103/103.
- **The C-boundary defect set D1–D7 is closed; D8–D11 (wide floats, packed, bitfields, anonymous members) remain.** 2026-08-26, [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §12 records what each item turned out to be and where it diverged from the plan. Beyond the two resolved defects above: the C importer builds real `TY-FN` types in all four declarator positions including the `signal` shape (which cost a refactor of `c-parse-func-decl`'s tail into `c-finish-fn-decl`, not a new parser), `--emit-cheader` writes real C declarators through `type-node-to-c-decl` (and `extract-type-node` had to stop dropping a function pointer's parameter list), diagnostics render a signature instead of `__fnty_N`, and bare `unsigned`/`signed` parse — as do `long unsigned` and `short unsigned`, which the sibling document claimed worked and did not. Measured consequences: `qsort`/`atexit` take a Nucleus function with no cast; `struct sigaction` and `sigevent_t` moved from the opaque roster to the clang-differential layout oracle, leaving only `FILE` (bitfields); and a C consumer built `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` against a generated header passes a function in, calls one back out, and is called through a struct member.
- **The three float widths C has and Nucleus did not — `f16`/`f80`/`f128` — are in, and D8 is closed.** 2026-08-26, FL-1…FL-7 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §13. clang is the oracle throughout: 104 constants across the five widths, the three FL-4 aggregate ABI shapes, and the varargs line all match it exactly. Where it diverged from the plan: **FL-3's hex-float literals needed a 128-bit significand, not just a lexer arm** — rendering `0x1.8p+3` through `strtod`'s f64 would have made the hex spelling no more precise than decimal at f80/f128, which is the whole point of having it, so `hexfloat-parse` accumulates the digits into two i64 limbs with a sticky bit and `hexfloat-fit` rounds that to any of the five formats (RNE, subnormals, overflow-to-inf) in pure integer arithmetic; `Val` gained `lit-lex` because `coerce-int-val` re-renders a literal at the destination width from `lit-f64`, which is only as precise as f64. **Hex integer literals came with it** (`0xFF` was an undefined symbol before); octal deliberately did not, since `0644` currently means six hundred and forty-four and changing that silently is the worse trade. **§10's "naming `f80` pins you to x86" became a diagnostic** rather than a caveat — it was emitting `x86_fp80` on aarch64, which no backend can select — so `reject-unavailable-float` now covers both that and AVR, while C's `long double` stays portable (`f80` on x86, `f128` on aarch64/riscv64, `f64` on AVR **and under MSVC**, which the plan did not list and which has to be checked before the architecture). **FL-7 does not unblock `max_align_t`**, contrary to the plan: clang's definition is blocked by a member `__attribute__((__aligned__(…)))`, which is PK-2/PK-3 work; §11's census figure moved with it. Two costs worth recording: making `long double` representable **changed the compiler's own emitted IR** (it imports `<stdlib.h>`, so `strtold`/`qecvt`/`qfcvt`/`qgcvt` + the `_r` pair now emit `declare` lines the stale boot compiler had skipped) — stage2 == stage3 confirmed the new fixed point before `make update-bootstrap`, and the general rule is that any change to what the C importer can represent moves the compiler's declare set; and `tests/fixtures/l1-members.h` plus the L5 message test changed subject to `__int128`, since `long double` stopped being "a builtin with no Nucleus width". 857 tests, bootstrap converges, abi-test/layout-test/check-headers green.
- **The libclang question was re-opened on its own stated trigger and re-closed the same way; a cross-target layout oracle came out of it.** 2026-08-26, [stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §8. That document's §7 named "bitfields or anonymous members get a Nucleus surface" as the moment to re-price, and [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §§6–9 decides to implement both. **Recommendation unchanged — finish the parser — but §5.2's argument for it had to be withdrawn**: "bitfields and anonymous members are hard because Nucleus has no way to express them" is stale for anonymous members (§9 above), so the trigger's own logic did not apply to the item it was written for. What decides it instead is a count: **four of roughly fifteen items in §§6–9 are parser work** (`: width`, one attribute read, one specifier-table entry, one call to an existing memoizer) and libclang replaces those four and none of the other eleven. Structural reason: `defstruct` must be able to *declare* a bitfield and a packed struct and `--emit-cheader` must *write* both back out as C, so the layout algorithms must live in `abi.nuc` whichever front end reads the headers — which takes `packed`, the one row §2 conceded to libclang outright, down with it. libclang's real surviving advantage is correct bitfield offsets on every target, and **that is better bought as a test oracle than as a linked dependency**: `clang --target=<triple> -ffreestanding -fsyntax-only` over a generated C file of `_Static_assert`s is a complete compile-time `sizeof`/`offsetof` oracle for every target clang supports, with no execution, sysroot, linking or libclang. Verified on x86_64/aarch64/riscv64/avr and verified to fail on a wrong assertion. It matters because `tests/run-layout-test.sh` *runs* both binaries and so validates the **host only**, while bitfield and packing rules are target-parameterised: `struct BF { int a:3; unsigned b:5; short c:9; int d; }` is 8 bytes on x86-64 and is not on AVR (16-bit `int`), where `int c:24` is a hard error rather than a different layout. A host-only test would have shipped a wrong AVR bitfield layout in silence. §7's second trigger is retired and replaced with a narrower one: re-open if the hand-written allocator disagrees with clang on a target we ship, which the cross-target oracle makes a measurable event rather than a judgement call.
- **Correctly-rounded decimal float literals staged separately** — [future/decimal-float-literals.md](future/decimal-float-literals.md) (DL-1…DL-4). `float-literal-value` evaluates every decimal float literal at host `f64`, so `(defvar x:f128 1.1)` gets the f64 value of 1.1 widened exactly rather than the f128-correct 1.1 (53 significant bits where f128 has 113); C converts the decimal string directly to the destination format. FL-3's hex-float literals are exact and mitigate it, but nothing in the source distinguishes an intentionally short decimal from a truncated one, so the natural spelling stays the wrong one. The work is one function plus fixed-width big-integer support, routed through `float-literal-ir-at` — which also retires `f32-const-ir`'s existing decimal→f64→f32 double-rounding (the W2d note). Deliberately not scheduled: nothing depends on it, it adds no type, changes no ABI and moves no layout, and a *nearly* correctly-rounded converter is indistinguishable from a correct one until it is not — a poor passenger on a larger change. A diagnostic is not the answer either, since the compiler cannot tell a short decimal from a truncated one and would fire on almost all correct code; document at the point of use in `docs/types.md` instead.
- **`__attribute__((packed))` is in, on both sides of the C boundary, and D10 is closed.** 2026-08-26, PK-1/PK-2 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §14. `(defstruct :packed Name …)` is the surface, `struct epoll_event` now imports at 12 bytes matching `cc` — the single `sizeof` mismatch in [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §1's 103-type census, and the one row §2 conceded to libclang outright — and `--emit-cheader` writes the attribute back out so a C consumer compiled `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` `_Static_assert`s the same size. The layout half is one substitution (`abi-field-align` returns 1 when packed), reused by the size walk, the eightbyte classifier and the riscv flattener so none of them grew a branch; the type line is one helper replacing six open-coded `%NAME = type {` sites. **The access half was the non-obvious one, as the plan said**: a packed field's alignment is a property of where it sits, not of its type, so `emit-load`/`emit-store` gained `-at` forms taking it explicitly. Three divergences: `packed` had to enter the anonymous-struct/union memoizer's **key**, since two structurally identical bodies with different packing are different types and the second would silently have taken the first's layout; `cheader-adopt-shape` had to copy it, because sharing a field table is not sharing a layout; and **one of C's three attribute positions must be ignored** — `typedef struct { … } S __attribute__((packed));` is warned-and-discarded by both clang (`-Wignored-attributes`) and gcc (`-Wattributes`), measured rather than assumed. A pre-existing bug fell out of the same shape: `typedef struct { … } __attribute__((packed)) Name;` had been reading `__attribute__` itself as the typedef name. **The cross-target oracle earned its place on its first run and not on the predicted defect**: `clang --target=<t> -ffreestanding -fsyntax-only` over generated `_Static_assert`s found that AVR's `BIGGEST_ALIGNMENT` is 8 bits, so every type there is byte-aligned and `struct { int8_t; int32_t; int16_t; }` is 7 bytes, not the 12 Nucleus had been emitting since long before packing existed — a host-only layout test structurally cannot see it. Known limit, shared with C (`-Waddress-of-packed-member`): `(.&  p field)` yields a plain `(ref T)` that carries no alignment, so a load through *that* pointer re-claims natural alignment; closing it needs PK-3's mechanism. 864 tests, bootstrap converges with no boot refresh (so PK-1/PK-2 are inert for the compiler's own IR), abi-test/layout-test/check-headers/avr-test green.
- **`__attribute__((aligned(N)))` is in, at the struct and at the member, and §7 PK-3 closes with it.** 2026-08-28, [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §14.7. `(defstruct :align 32 CacheLine v:i64)` and `(defstruct Slot c:i8 (:align 16 v:i32))` are the surface, all three positions C honours are read on import (with `aligned`/`__aligned__` and an argument that may be an integer or `__alignof__(T)`/`sizeof(T)`), and `--emit-cheader` writes both levels back out. **`max_align_t` now lays out at 32 bytes matching `cc`** — the type [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §1 first misattributed to `long double` and whose real blocker was the member `__attribute__((__aligned__(__alignof__(long double))))` in clang's own `__stddef_max_align_t.h`; the nine blocked types are eight. **The plan's central measurement was wrong and it was the one that priced the item**: "`sizeof` 16 while its LLVM type stays `{ i32 }` — the alignment is not in the type" was read off a *declaration*; against a definition clang emits `%struct.A = type { i32, [12 x i8] }`, and must, because LLVM computes array strides and GEP offsets from the element list alone. So PK-3 is a padding machine plus a source-index→element-index remap (`field-pad-before`/`struct-tail-pad`/`field-ir-index`), not a flag — with the off-by-one that the pad before field `i` shifts field `i` itself — plus explicit alignment on every `alloca`/global of an over-aligned struct, which incidentally fixed a latent FL-4 defect (a struct containing an `fp128` needs 16 and was getting the pointer size). Three rules measured rather than assumed: `aligned` only ever RAISES (`aligned(2)` on an `int` struct stays 4, so the rule is a max); it composes with `packed` in both directions rather than overriding (`packed, aligned(4)` is 8 bytes with the `int` still at offset 1; a member `aligned(4)` inside a packed struct puts it back at 4); and the argument form that matters is `__alignof__(T)`, which PINS a member's natural alignment rather than raising it — evaluating it is the whole of what unblocked `max_align_t`. **The cross-target oracle caught the AVR half of PK-1's own mistake**: an `aligned(N)` raises past `BIGGEST_ALIGNMENT` there too, so PK-1's AVR short-circuit at the top of `abi-alignof` had to move below the aggregate arms. One near-miss recorded: folding member alignments into `hash-struct-shape` renamed every anonymous C type the importer memoizes (42 lines of bootstrap diff) until only NON-ZERO alignments entered the key — "no alignment" and "alignment 0" are the same shape. 871 tests, bootstrap converges with no boot refresh, abi-test/layout-test/check-headers/avr-test/riscv-test green.
- **Bitfields are in, on both sides of the C boundary, and D9 closes with them.** 2026-08-28, FR-1 + BF-1…BF-4 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §15. `(defstruct A (:bits 3 a:i32) (:bits 0 z:ui32) d:i32)` is the surface, `: width` is read on import in every position C admits (including an unnamed `unsigned :3;` and the zero-width boundary member), and `--emit-cheader` writes the width back out. **`FILE` now lays out at 216 bytes with `_flags` at 0 and `_lock` at 136, matching `cc`** — the marquee blocked type of the 111-type census and the one [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §8.3 held up as the argument for linking libclang in for offsets. **FR-1 landed byte-identically on its own**, exactly as §11 asked: a one-element GEP path *is* the single hard-coded `getelementptr` the four access sites used to write. The design is that a struct's size, its LLVM element list, each field's GEP index and each bit position all come from **one walk** (`struct-walk`) — the first version answered them in four places and they disagreed within the hour. A run of adjacent bit-fields is ONE opaque `[k x i8]` element written by the field that opens it, which is why `field-ir-index` is not the identity even without `aligned(N)`. Access is an `iM` window over exactly the bytes the bits touch (`M = 8·ceil((bit%8 + w)/8)`) — a power-of-two window reads past the end of a struct whose last field crosses its declared type's boundary, reachable in a packed struct — with the mask built as `lshr iM -1, (M-w)` so no >64-bit constant is ever printed. `.&` refuses a bit-field, which is C's own constraint. **The cost was not the type; it was that C leaves allocation implementation-defined and the targets disagree**, and all three divergences were found by `clang --target=` rather than by reading a spec: **AVR drops the declared-type rule entirely** (GCC's `PCC_BITFIELD_TYPE_MATTERS` is off there — `struct { uint32_t a:5; b:5; c:5; }` is 2 bytes and packs across byte boundaries; only a zero-width member still forces a **byte**); **`packed` drops the crossing rule but NOT the zero-width one** (`packed { uint32_t x:1; uint32_t :0; uint32_t y:1; }` is 5 bytes on x86-64); and **aarch64 gives every bit-field its declared type's alignment, named or unnamed, and keeps a zero-width one's even under `packed`**, where the SysV targets give an unnamed bit-field none. The first implementation was green on eight shapes and wrong on all three rules — shapes I, J and K in `s16-bf-layout-cross-target` exist to tell them apart (11 shapes × 5 targets). Two tests changed subject: `l1-member-opaque`/`l1-member-fails-safe` moved from a bit-field to a multi-declarator member (still refused, same line, so the pinned line-number table did not move), and `l2-libc-opaque` became `l2-libc-file-layout`, a positive check against `cc`. One root fix rode along: `make update-bootstrap` left `build/nucleusc.ll` derived from the *previous* boot compiler, so the next `make bootstrap` diffed a stale stage1 against a fresh stage2 and reported a divergence that was really that staleness — the target now drops both derived artifacts.
- **C11 anonymous members are in, the flexible array member with them, and the 111-type census closes at 111/111.** 2026-08-28, AN-1/AN-2 + C1a in [stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-boundary-defects.md) §16. `sigcontext` (256 bytes), `rusage` (144) and `cmsghdr` (16) all size exactly as `cc` sizes them, and every one of the nine types [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §1 listed as blocked now emits a `%Name = type` line. **The plan's prediction held: it was two functions on FR-1.** An import-side member that reaches its `;` with no declarator and an aggregate type gets a minted `__anon.N` name (the shape BF-4 already used for `__bf.N`; both spellings are unspellable as C or Nucleus identifiers, which makes "the compiler minted this" decidable with no extra state), and `struct-field-ref` falls through a direct miss into a depth-first search over minted members, prepending one `(sd, field-ir-index sd i)` level per step. Two hits at any depth is C's own ambiguity and gets a located diagnostic. Two details the path forced: a **union** level contributes no GEP (its members are at offset 0), and `owner` — read for exactly one question, is this load packed — must be won by a packed level anywhere in the chain. **`(:anon T)` is the `defstruct` surface**, minting `__anon.<field index>` so the layout prescan and the emission agree on the name; it is the one place in §§6–9 where Nucleus admits a declaration C does not, since C writes an anonymous member's body inline, so `--emit-cheader` refuses it with that reason rather than emitting something a C compiler would reject. **C1a rode along because §11's acceptance test needed it**: `cmsghdr`'s blocker was `[]` with no extent, and **`(array T 0)` could not be reused** — zero is already the layout prescan's provisional-length marker, which `type-to-ir` asserts never reaches IR — so a flexible member is `(array T -1)`, printing `[0 x T]` and sizing as 0. `l2-layout-unfoldable` asserted `[]` was refused; that row became `l2-layout-flex-array`, a positive check of the type line and of `sizeof` against `cc`. 883 tests, bootstrap converges byte-identically, abi-test/layout-test/check-headers/avr-test/riscv-test/riscv-abi-test green.
- **The last three C declarator shapes are in: multi-declarator field lines, typedef declarator lists, and the with-body aggregate array typedef.** 2026-08-29, CD-1/CD-2/CD-3 in [stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md) §8, closing the three §6 deferrals that are about the declarator *grammar* rather than the type system (and, with them, the last three open rows of [cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §2's table). `struct cd { int a, b; char *s, *t; int x, y[3]; unsigned p : 3, q : 5; }` lays out element-for-element as `cc` does; `typedef int a, *b;` records both names with their own pointer depths; and `typedef struct Tag { … } Name[N];` — the one *silent* defect of the three, since it registered `Name` as a plain struct alias and passed it `byval` where C decays it to a pointer — is an `(array Tag N)` in the typedef table. **The design's estimate of the value was low, and in a way worth naming: "0 of 9 in glibc" was a census of the types a previous survey had already listed as blocked, which structurally cannot see a type that survey never reached.** CD-1 unblocks `struct tcp_info` (104 bytes, alignment 4, element list identical to clang's — glibc writes `tcpi_snd_wscale : 4, tcpi_rcv_wscale : 4` as one declarator list) and, outside libc, `SDL_Rect` and through it `SDL_Surface` and `SDL_MessageBoxColor` — six new types on `SDL2/SDL.h` alone. **The load-bearing fact is that `c-parse-type` collapses pointer depth into a bare `ptr` with no pointee**, so a starred first declarator genuinely leaves nothing for a later one to start from; the fix asks that question directly (`c-span-has-star` over the span the type parse consumed) and refuses `int *p, q;` rather than guessing — a located opaque error and no `%X = type` line, which is the L1 discipline applied to CD-1's own residue. **CD-3 needed the §3.3 "two sites name-for-name" rule and a third mechanism**: the whole-unit prescan registered every body-bearing typedef's declarator as an opaque `StructDef`, and `parse-type-name` probes the struct registry before the C typedef table, so leaving that in would have shadowed the array typedef in exactly the positions (`defn` signature, `defvar` type) the prescan exists for; and an *untagged* body gives the prescan nothing to anchor the element on, so both passes mint `__carr.<typedef name>` — the `__bf.`/`__anon.` shape, for the same decidability reason. The L1 fixture's `c-parse-type:529` witness moved for the second time (bitfield → multi-declarator at BF-4 → mixed pointer depth now); the row guards "an unreadable by-value aggregate body marks the enclosing declaration", not a spelling. 894 tests (+6), **bootstrap converges with no boot refresh** — the 186-module IR sweep, the 96-output header-mode sweep and `build/nucleusc.ll` are all byte-identical, because the compiler's own six C headers contain none of the three shapes — abi-test/layout-test/check-headers green.
- **C headers are now read for the emission target, and a declarator list after a struct body no longer drops names.** 2026-08-29, C4 in [stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md](stage16-ergonomics/cheader-parser-vs-libclang.md) §9 and CD-4 plus the `--emit-cheader` C-typedef `#include` in [stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md) §9 — the last three open entries across the two documents. **C4:** `clang -E` ran with no `--target` and no sysroot, so every cross-compile read *host* headers; an AVR build of `(import-use "string.h")` emitted `declare i64 @strlen(ptr)` and `declare ptr @memcpy(ptr, ptr, i64)` for a machine whose `size_t` is 16 bits, along with 21 symbols avr-libc does not have (`__memcmpeq`, `strerror_l`, …) and missing four it does (`strlwr`, `strrev`, `strupr`, `memrchr`). It now emits `i16` and avr-libc's own 42 declarations. **The half that no front end supplies** — and the reason this was two items, not one — is that `clang -E` does not fold `sizeof`: the array-extent evaluator computes sizes itself from `abi-sizeof`, so the C type mapping had to become target-correct alongside (`c-int-type` — `int` is 16 bits on AVR and is also the width clang gives an enum there; `target-long-size` tested `= 4` and so gave AVR's 2-byte-pointer target an 8-byte `long`; `size_t`/`ssize_t` stay intercepted *by name*, for the reason the L2 comment records, but at the target's pointer width rather than a hardcoded 64). End to end: `struct { char buf[sizeof(void*) * 4]; int n; }` is `{ [32 x i8], i32 }` on x86-64 and `{ [8 x i8], i16 }` on AVR. **The host path is empty by construction** (no flags unless the target triple differs from the host's) and measured byte-identical over 187 modules. **The interesting design question was failure**, since making the target's headers authoritative makes their absence reachable, and on this container only AVR has them: refusing would have retired every cross lane in `make test` and `make windows-boot`, so a target read that fails retries on the host and warns — the pre-C4 behaviour, announced instead of silent. A header on *no* search path is now a located error with clang's own message beneath it, where it used to be swallowed whole (`clang -E`'s exit status was never examined); that tightening found `(import "prelude")` in two fixtures and one `run-tests.sh` heredoc — a string import not ending in `.nuc` routes to the C-header path, so the form had always been a silent no-op. **CD-4:** `typedef struct { … } A, B;` dropped `B` and `struct S { … } x, y;` left `x, y;` for the function-declaration parser; both are a declarator loop now, and the whole design is that `c-struct-decl-declarator` is what the *first* declarator goes through too — a second copy of the rule is how `B` came to be dropped. Zero occurrences across 39 standard headers (552 declarator runs after a `}`, none with a comma), which is what §8.2 predicted; the payoff is third-party, where `png.h`'s `typedef struct png_image_struct { … } png_image, *png_imagep;` made `png_imagep` an unknown type until now. **The `#include`:** §6 recorded the missing include as out of scope with a counter-argument ("a consumer is expected to include the C header itself"), and the counter-argument does not survive being measured — `off_t` renders *bare*, so the generated header was `error: unknown type name 'off_t'` under `clang -fsyntax-only`, not merely untidy. The decisive point is that the emitter already accepts the rule: it writes `#include <stddef.h>` unconditionally because it spells `size_t`. `CTypedef` gained the *import* spelling (never the linemarker's `/usr/include/…` path, which would be unportable and wrong), and a C struct **tag** still gets no include, deliberately — an incomplete tag is legal behind a pointer, and closing the by-value case needs a second provenance field on `StructDef` with four writers (filed, §9.3). 902 tests (+8), check-headers 69/69 with no regeneration, abi-test/layout-test/avr-test green, **boot refreshed** (`src/cheader.nuc` changed) and the new fixed point confirmed.
- **The last D9 residue is closed: a type defined in a `(compile-time …)` block can now be named in a *signature*.** 2026-08-29, D9a in [stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md](stage16-ergonomics/repl-libraries.md) §3.3. D9 ruled such a `defstruct` a **program** type but left the *name's visibility* open, filed as needing "a much wider blast radius"; it did not. `prescan-struct-names` and `prescan-struct-layouts` were flat walks over the top-level form list, and a `compile-time` form's `cdr` is a cons chain of exactly the shape they already consume — so both become `-mode` variants carrying an `in-ct` flag and **recurse into themselves** one level down, with `emit-compile-time` running both over its own body before its defn-signature loop (the only registration the REPL gets, since a `compile-time` entry reaches that function with no unit prescan). The layout half is load-bearing rather than symmetric: a by-value CT type in a signature needs its size before `abi-classify` reads it, and an unlaid-out struct is *sized 0* rather than diagnosed — `define i32 @f(i0 %p.arg)` without it, `i64` with. The binding constraint is conventions.md's "mirror the emitter's SKIPS": over-registering is worse than the bug, because a name that resolves with no `%T = type` line emitted is silent invalid IR at exit 0. `emit-compile-time` has an arm for `defstruct` and nothing else this pass registers (probed: `defstruct-`, `defunion`, `deftype` and a nested `compile-time` are each already `unknown: <head>` inside a block), and the decision is shared not duplicated — `ct-emits-defstruct` is one predicate both the filter and the emitter call. Of the sibling prescans only the two TYPE passes descend; the value passes deliberately do not, because a CT `defvar`'s `@g = global` and a CT `defn`'s `define` go to the CT module **alone** — measured, a *backward* reference to either already emits invalid IR today, so descending would only spread that. Making CT values program-visible needs the ruling D9 took for types taken again for values, and is deferred with that reason. 908 tests (was 905): `s16-d9-ct-type-in-signature` (asserts the lowered `i64` parameter *and* the runtime value), `s16-d9-ct-type-in-ct-signature`, `s16-d9-ct-descent-mirrors-skips`, plus `tests/repl/prelude.in` §8 for both REPL shapes — the first two FAIL on the pre-change binary.
