IR-neutral: 187 `examples/`+`lib/` modules and 96 header outputs byte-identical on `.ll`, stdout, stderr and exit code. `make bootstrap` converges; the compiler's own IR moved (+136 lines), so the boot was refreshed at the green milestone and the new fixed point re-confirmed (boot == stage1 == stage2 == stage3). `make abi-test`, `make layout-test`, `make check-headers` (69/69) and `make avr-test` all PASS.
- **`src/repl_shim.c` is retired — the compiler is now pure Nucleus, and nothing in the build compiles C.** 2026-08-30, §11 of [stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md](stage16-ergonomics/c-header-layout.md), closing the "The rest of `src/repl_shim.c`" bullet §6 filed and serving the overview's *"any libc detail must be reachable from pure Nucleus"* item, whose motivating instance the shim was. Both halves fell to work already on the ground: the `setjmp` half to L1–L5 (`jmp_buf` is 200 bytes of real storage and `(import-use "setjmp.h")` is enough), and the float-printing half to **FP-2**, which gave an indirect call the direct call's ABI lowering and coercion — so a parameter typed `(fn f64)()` is callable and `repl_print_f64`'s whole reason for existing (calling a JIT'd `double`-returning thunk) evaporated. The one thing that did **not** port literally is the shim's `static jmp_buf repl_jmpbufs[16]`: a `defvar`'s type is resolved by `prescan-defvar-name`, which runs *before* any C header import, so the tag is still `cheader-prescan-opaque`'s layout-less placeholder and `(array __jmp_buf_tag 16)` is refused — correctly, and while `(defvar env:jmp_buf)`, `(defstruct H xs:(array __jmp_buf_tag 4))` and an `(alloca (array __jmp_buf_tag 16))` in a body all work, which pins the boundary exactly (new conventions.md entry). Each protected frame therefore **allocas its own jump buffer** and publishes the pointer into a global `(array raw 16)` stack — strictly better than the C shim, since the buffer now lives in precisely the frame the `longjmp` returns into and the stack needs no target-dependent extent. R1's two load-bearing properties are unchanged: the body is still a callback (so the `_setjmp` frame is live when the throw fires) and the depth counter still makes an unprotected `die-at` exit rather than jump into a returned frame. Two boot refreshes were budgeted and **one** was needed — the natural Nucleus spellings (`@repl-protect`) are already distinct from the C symbols (`@repl_protect`), so there was no duplicate-definition window and no rename to undo; step 2 changed only `Makefile`/`build.ps1`, so `boot/nucleusc.ll` was already the fixed point. 913 tests, 0 FAIL (unchanged), all 14 `tests/repl/` fixtures green including `repl-s16-macrolet`'s `error (recovered)` line; **187-module IR sweep byte-identical** on `.ll`, stderr and exit code, and an 11-form REPL float/recovery probe byte-identical against the pre-change binary; `make bootstrap`, `abi-test`, `layout-test`, `check-headers` (69/69) and `avr-test` all green. The compiler's own IR gains 6 `declare`s and 2 types. One caveat recorded rather than fixed (§11.5): the Windows boot IRs are cross-emitted from the **host's** headers, so their `[1 x %__jmp_buf_tag]` alloca carries glibc's 200 bytes — inert until now because every host-header type in the compiler's IR was used only behind a pointer. It does not reach a real Windows build, since `build.ps1` drives the boot binary in batch mode only and the self-hosted emission reads the real Windows headers; `--sysroot` on `make windows-boot` closes it outright.
- **Quasiquote nests: a macro can write a macro, and the gate was never weakened.** 2026-09-11, Q1–Q5 in [stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md](stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md) — Stage 20's own deferral, brought back and folded into the stage rather than promoted to one, because it is the same subject. `emit-qq-form`/`emit-qq-list` tested `qq-is-tagged form 'unquote` at every depth with no counter, so an inner backtick protected nothing and every `~` inside it fired at the outer level; the visible cost was that **a `macmap` could not be written inside a macro body** (`undefined: p`). The fix is an `i32` threaded through three functions plus a 12-line `emit-qq-tagged` that rebuilds a tagged two-element form as data with its operand walked at an adjusted level — **a parameter and not a `g-qq-level`**, because a global needs save/restore around the level-1 `emit-node` re-entry and `die-at` longjmps past it. **The load-bearing design claim was that the level-1 delta is exactly one table row** (a `` ` `` seen inside a `` ` `` now walks its operand at level 2), which with `design/stage20-macros/nested-qq-scan.py`'s measurement — **0 nested backticks in 471 files** — predicted a gate no earlier Stage 20 phase could hold: `checked 2690 artifact(s) / PASS: emitted output is byte-identical to the snapshot`, **no normalisation, no allowlist**, run twice, once for Q1 alone and once with Q2's new `unquote outside quasiquote` diagnostic on top. §4 answered the `node-type`↔`emit-node` lockstep question for all eight sites that mention `quasiquote` *before* writing code and found nothing to edit — five prescans skip the subtree whole, `node-type`'s arm is already `null`, the reader already reads nested backticks, and `src/nucleusc.nuc` has no `.nuch` — which is why the change was 25 lines and not a sweep. Four deltas (§10), three correcting a claim made higher up in the same document. **`~~x` is not the idiom; `~'~x` is:** Nucleus's unquote requires its operand to evaluate to a `Node*` where Common Lisp's comma inserts any datum, so CL's `,',k` is `~'~k` here (the outer `~k` yields the node, `'` makes the inner template hold it as a literal, the inner `~` reads it back) and `~@'~xs` is its splicing counterpart, while a bare `~~k` means *evaluate `k` at the inner expansion*, where the outer macro's parameters no longer exist — well-defined, almost never wanted, and pinned by the equivalence `~~'v` ≡ `~v`. **The `print` family was never a fold** — `print`/`println`/`eprint`/`eprintln` are four-line wrappers over `str-into`, so Q5 was one macro and not five, and `str-into` is two lines where it was an eight-line three-arm `cond` recursion. Two pre-existing defects were surfaced and deliberately not fixed, both byte-identical on the pre-change binary: **an unquote whose operand is not `Node`-typed is undiagnosed** (`~5` emits `call ptr @__cons(ptr 5, …)` and returns from LLVM as `integer constant must have integer type`, naming generated code the author never wrote — a typing question about unquote operands, not about levels), and **a macro cannot produce a `defmacro` at the REPL** (`unknown: defmacro`; the top-level dispatcher does not re-dispatch a definer out of an expansion, with or without nesting), which is why `tests/repl/s20-qq-levels.in` drives a nested template from inside a function body plus a recovered error and the same call again — `g-qq-used`'s save/restore across the REPL's longjmp is the one piece of state this path shares with another subsystem, and `emit-qq-tagged` is now its second setter (conventions.md updated). **Q5's cost is ordering, not bytes.** Its delta over 2,696 artifacts is 2,651 identical, 34 differing only in `@.str` constants, **4 a verified pure permutation** (`%TestCase`, its `Maybe` payload union and `%Maybe.TestCase` emit after `%Diagnostic`/`%SexpStr` instead of before, because `macmap` leaves a `macrolet` in the tree until emit where the old recursion was fully macro-expanded before the body was walked, so the parametric stamp is reached later — identical sorted line multiset, no instruction moved anywhere), 1 real (`lib_fmt.nuc.nuch`, the changed source's own generated interface) and 6 new (the example). But `lib/fmt.nuc` now *uses* a nested template and `src/` calls `str-into` at 47 sites across seven modules, so the committed boot must understand levels **before** Q5 can compile at all — the pre-change compiler reports `lib/fmt.nuc:200: error: undefined: p` on any program that prints. That makes the boot refresh a hard ordering constraint rather than milestone bookkeeping, and `make` cannot bridge it (`$(BIN)` depends on `lib/*.nuc` and builds with `$(BOOT)`): it is [context/build.md](../context/build.md)'s fourth root cause, handled by its documented shortcut — refresh from the already-feature-carrying `build/nucleusc`, then change `lib/`. 996 tests (+9: seven `s20-qq-*` units, `examples/qq-levels.nuc` and its golden, and the REPL fixture), 0 failed; `make bootstrap` converges at a new fixed point; `make check-headers` 87/87 with only `lib/fmt.nuch`'s one line moved; abi-test, layout-test, avr-test, riscv-test and riscv-abi-test all PASS. **IR snapshot re-baselined** (`snapshot --force`, 435 inputs → 2,696 artifacts): the reason is the six artifacts `examples/qq-levels.nuc` adds plus Q5's 39 classified differences above — Q1 and Q2 themselves needed no re-take, having verified byte-identical against the old baseline.

- **A macro body calling the program's own `defn` is not just unsupported — one of its three failure modes is silent, and another segfaults the compiler.** Probed 2026-09-11 while pulling stage888's "a macro body may call only what the compiler binary exports" into Stage 20 as [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md) (part three, phases L1–L8). Callee above the macro: `Symbols not found: [ f ]`. Callee below it: LLVM's own `use of undefined value '@f'`, with LLVM's line numbers, against text the author never wrote. Callee whose name is one of the **1,658** ordinary-Nucleus-looking names among `build/nucleusc`'s **2,285** `-rdynamic` exports: the compiler's function runs instead, mid-compilation, and chooses the expansion — `(defn in-jit-module ():i32 (return 7))` plus a macro that branches on it compiles clean and exits **99**; give the collision a mismatched signature (a program `desugar-form` taking an `i32`) and `build/nucleusc` takes SIGSEGV, no `unsafe/` and no C anywhere in the program. The same restriction binds `(compile-time …)` blocks. **The REPL has none of it** — the identical file, recursive helper and all, evaluates at the prompt, because Stage 16 R3/R4 JIT-compiles every top-level form as it arrives; so this is one of the compiler's two modes missing what the other already does, not a feature the compiler lacks. The design's rule is provenance, not link order: host iff the callee is under the compiler's own `lib/` root **and** exported, otherwise the program's own definition, mirrored into the JIT — which closes the silent bind as a side effect and costs the bootstrap nothing, since `lib/` imports nothing from `src/` and every program-defn call a macro body makes in this tree (`node-at` in `with-handler`) satisfies both conditions.

- **Stage 20 L1 — spans and provenance, inert.** 2026-09-11, the first phase of
  [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md).
  `ProgDefn` grows `ir-start`/`ir-end` (the `define`'s byte span in `g-def-buf`)
  and `from-lib`; `g-ct-lib-root` records the directory this invocation resolved
  `lib/prelude.nuc` through, captured in `do-import` from the **resolved** path
  because which of `try-import-path`'s five steps found it is the whole answer
  (§3.4). Nothing reads any of it yet, so the gate is byte-identity: `make
  bootstrap` converges (stage1.ll == stage2.ll), `make test` 996/0/0, unchanged.
  **Two corrections to §5.1.** `program-defn-record` was called one line *after*
  `emit g-out "define "`, not before it, so every recorded `ir-start` would have
  pointed past the define's own header; it moved above that `emit`, which is
  emission-neutral because neither it nor `defn-ir-name` touches a stream. And
  `ir-start == 0` is a legitimate offset — the first `define` in the buffer — so
  only `ir-start == ir-end == 0` may mean "no span", which is why
  `program-defn-note-end` re-asks the `g-out` guard rather than reading
  `ir-start` as a flag, and why a record that declines (the idempotent
  already-recorded path) returns null instead of the existing record. The guard
  itself is `ct-span-recordable` — `g-out` must be `g-def-stream-program` *and*
  that must be `&g-def-buf`, which excludes the macro/CT module streams, the
  REPL's per-entry buffers and `import-ct`'s sink. **The verification was free
  because `assemble-module-ir` appends `g-def-buf` last**, so the definition
  buffer is the emitted module's own tail and a throwaway span dump could be
  checked against the `.ll` with no new plumbing: over the compiler's own 12.5 MB
  of definitions, **1,946 spans, ascending, disjoint, each exactly one
  `define …}\n\n` block whose `@name` equals its recorded `ir-name`**, with
  `@__nucleus_init` and the `@g-*` globals the only text outside a span, and
  `g-ct-lib-root` = `lib` from the repo root (§3.5 edge 2). `from-lib` splits
  654/1,946 exactly along `lib/` vs `src/`. The root is stored as a bare
  directory and the prefix test requires a following `/`, so `lib` cannot claim
  `libfoo/x.nuc` — a false positive there is §1.3's silent bind surviving, the
  one direction §3.5 edge 5's spelling tolerance may not take.

- **Stage 20 L2 — `host-exports?`, inert, and the precondition proved rather
  than assumed.** 2026-09-11, §5.2 of
  [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md).
  One memoised predicate in `src/nucleusc.nuc` answering "does the running
  compiler binary export this exact name?" — `-rdynamic` keeps hyphens, so
  `node-at` is looked up as `node-at` and there is no mangling step. Nothing
  calls it yet, so the gate is byte-identity: `make bootstrap` converges
  (stage1.ll == stage2.ll), `make test` 996/0/0, unchanged.
  **`LLVMLoadLibraryPermanently` + `LLVMSearchForAddressOfSymbol`, not `dlsym`**
  — the §7 phase list still said `(import-use "dlfcn.h")` and §5.2 had already
  overruled it, because `make windows-boot` cross-emits the same source to
  `x86_64-pc-windows-{gnu,msvc}` and a POSIX-only extern lands in both. Verified,
  not argued: both Windows IRs emit clean and contain **0** occurrences of
  `dlsym`, carrying `declare i32 @LLVMLoadLibraryPermanently(ptr)` /
  `declare ptr @LLVMSearchForAddressOfSymbol(ptr)` instead.
  **The `LLVMLoadLibraryPermanently(null)` call is load-bearing, and that was
  measured.** `SearchForAddressOfSymbol` iterates only the handles
  `LoadLibraryPermanently` has opened, so with the call removed a throwaway probe
  answered **0 for every one** of `node-at`, `alloc-node`, `intern-symbol`,
  `desugar-form` and `printf` — and with it, **1** for all five, **0** for
  `__gs_no_such_symbol_42`. That failure direction is why a positive had to be
  shown: a false negative mirrors instead of binding to the host, which is the
  safe answer and therefore the silent one, and "it returned 0 and nothing
  crashed" is indistinguishable from a working lookup of a name that is absent.
  The memo is two index-parallel `Vector`s scanned linearly, the shape
  `g-macro-decls` and `repl-preamble-has-decl` already use: interning makes
  `Symbol` identity and spelling the same question, and the closure walk L3 will
  build asks once per callee.

- **Stage 20 L3 — the compile-time mirror, built and proven, still called from nowhere.** 2026-09-11, the third phase of [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md). `ct-mirror-flush` walks the closure of a set of root `ProgDefn`s over the **IR text** of their `g-def-buf` spans — `@`-tokens are the ground truth of what a function references, and inline `c"…"` never appears inside a `define` — classifying each callee by §3.1's two conditions: a `lib/` defn the host exports becomes a `declare`, anything else with a span is copied in and recursed through. The copy rewrites `define internal ` to `define weak_odr ` **in the mirror only**, on `def-linkage`'s own REPL reasoning (a separate module is a separate object), so no byte of the emitted `.ll` moves. `progdefn-declare-to` unifies the ABI-lowered declare across all three sites that had grown their own copy (`repl-preamble-declare-progdefn`, `macro-jit-ensure-decl`, the mirror), emission-neutrally; `emit-qq-helpers` gained a target and a visibility prefix so a mirror's helpers are `private` and host-sized. Proven end to end rather than by inspection: a macro's helper chain three deep, including a `defn-`, mirrored and **called through the JIT** for the right answers (17 and 41), with the walk stopping at `alloc-node` as a `declare` and a second overlapping flush declaring `@ctm-mid` rather than redefining it — `g-ct-mirror-defined` doing its job, since without it the second flush is `Duplicate definition of symbol`. **`make bootstrap` converges byte-identical and the mirror counter reads 0 as a measurement**: the gate harness emitted an unconditional marker and a full bootstrap plus test run produced none. Two lessons. **§5.6's `weak_odr` collision worry was already answered by Stage 12 N4** — a private defn's IR name is namespace-mangled (`@ctm_p1__ctm-priv`), so two same-named privates never meet in the mirror's symbol table. And **`cstr-residue` in `tests/suite-audits.nuc` is a live tripwire for phases like this**: the throwaway harness's single `(as CStr (LLVMGetErrorMessage err))` took `src/nucleusc.nuc` from 15 C-string sites to 16 and failed the suite, which is how the leftover instrumentation was caught instead of shipped — Stage 18's whole-tree audit paying for itself on a phase written six weeks later.

- **Stage 20 L4 — the phase that turns it on: a macro body may call the program's own code.** 2026-09-11, the fourth phase of [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md), and the one that makes L1–L3 do something. `macro-jit-ensure-decl` now takes the call's line and splits on §3.1: it emits the `declare` either way, and when the callee is **not** both under the library root and exported by the running binary it records the name as a mirror root. Roots accumulate on `MacroDef.ct-roots` (a new `CtBlock` record carries a `compile-time` block's, keyed by the `__compile_time_main_N` symbol), and `expand-macro-call` / `jit-call-ct-main-sym` call `ct-mirror-flush` immediately before their `LLVMOrcLLJITLookup`. All four of §1's probes flipped in one change: mode 1 (`my-ident`) went from `Symbols not found` to **exit 7**; §1.5's `depth`/`mk-int` compiles in batch and answers **4**, retiring the deferral's headline claim that a macro body cannot recurse; mode 3's `in-jit-module` went from a silent **99** to **7**; and the `desugar-form` probe — a program defn colliding with a compiler symbol at a *mismatched* signature — runs instead of dumping core (**139 → 7**). `--warn-ct-shadow` / `--no-warn-ct-shadow` names that last shape, defaulting on, on §6's measurement rather than on taste: a 435-file sweep of `examples/`, `lib/`, `tests/fixtures/` and `src/nucleusc.nuc` fires it **zero** times. The mirror counter was likewise **measured, not inferred** — a throwaway unconditional marker (since removed, and grepped for) over `make bootstrap` plus that sweep produced 273 markers, every one `0`. `make bootstrap` byte-identical; `make test` 1005/0/0. L7 closed here too: a `(compile-time …)` block needed nothing beyond the second flush site.

  Two corrections to the design, both about *where the roots live*. **§5.3's "alongside the existing per-module `g-macro-decls` latch" is a trap**: that latch is reset per body and **never restored**, which is survivable for a dedup set and fatal for a result the caller reads afterwards. The shape that proves it is the one §5.3 never mentions — a `macrolet` compiled **inside** a `defmacro` body, which opens a second body midway through the first, so the reset points the global at the inner binding's vector and the calls the inner template splices (which belong to the *outer* macro) land nowhere. Measured by removing the restore and rebuilding: `Symbols not found: [ mk, base ]` — mode 1 again, in the one shape a tree of top-level macros never reaches. And **§5.5's stated reason for flushing at expansion rather than at `defmacro` time is wrong**: a root is recorded by `macro-jit-ensure-decl`, which needs a `ProgDefn`, and `program-defn-record` runs at `emit-defn` — so "a callee defined between the `defmacro` and its first call site" has no `ProgDefn` when the body compiles, gets no `declare`, and never becomes a root. It is mode 2 either way (verified: still `use of undefined value '@helper'`). The site is right for two reasons the document does not give — a macro defined and never called emits no module, and a `macrolet` compiled mid-`emit-defn` would otherwise meet the enclosing function's span with `ir-end` still 0.

- **Stage 20 L5 — mode 2 becomes a located message, and L7 confirmed with two probes it had not taken.** 2026-09-11, §5.8 and §7 of [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md). A macro or CT body calling a `defn` written **below** it used to leak LLVM's own diagnostic — `probe.nuc:1: compile-time: IR parse error: <compile-time>:189:18: error: use of undefined value '@helper'`, LLVM's line numbers against text no author wrote — and now says `macro 'probe' calls 'helper', which is defined later in this unit` with the note that names the fix. L3's three silent refusals (`g-ct-mirror-refuse`, which L4 left falling through to `macro 'x': JIT lookup failed`) became located messages at the two flush sites, where the body's name is still known. `make bootstrap` byte-identical, mirror counter **measured** 0; `make test` 1014/0/0 (+9); a 478-file sweep produced 282 markers all 0, **zero** `--warn-ct-shadow` fires, **zero** `use of undefined value` / `IR parse error`, and **zero** of L5's own messages.

  **§5.8's stated test is one condition short, and the missing one is a case that must not error.** "Resolves in `g-globals` to a `TY-FN` `Sym` with no `ProgDefn`" is exactly true of a `defn` defined **inside** the `(compile-time …)` block being compiled: `program-defn-record` declines under `in-jit-module` by design — that defn already lives in the CT module and re-declaring it there is a redefinition error — while `emit-compile-time`'s own signature prescan registers it as a global `TY-FN` `Sym` with `@fname`. Measured by building the check without the guard: `(compile-time (defn ct-outer …) (defn ct-inner …) (printf … (ct-outer 21)))`, which compiles and prints 42, fails `compile-time block calls 'ct-inner', which is defined later in this unit`. The check is therefore **four** conditions — not a local, `TY-FN`, not in `g-decl-stream`, and not in a new per-body `g-ct-module-defns` — fed from the CT prescan (so one CT defn may call another defined below it) *and* from `emit-defn` under `in-jit-module` (so a mangled ir-name is covered too). Each of the other three legitimate reasons got a probe of its own and a test: a C-header extern, a top-level `declare`, and a call through a function-pointer local.

  **A second raw-LLVM leak, on a path `macro-jit-ensure-decl` never saw.** It had one call site, `emit-call-with-args`, so a macro body that took a program defn's *address* — `(let (f:(fn i32)(i32) twice) (f 21))` — emitted `store ptr @twice` into a module that declared nothing and leaked `use of undefined value '@twice'` for a callee defined **above** the macro: mode 2's symptom without mode 2's cause, and "no raw LLVM text on any macro path" does not hold while it stands. One added call at `emit-symbol-ref-bound`'s function-materialization branch — the chokepoint AVR-6's addrspace check already names as the only way a function becomes a value — supplies the declare and the mirror root together, emission-neutral outside a JIT module.

  **L7 needed no code, and said so only after being probed properly.** L4 had run `(compile-time (printf … (twice 21)))`; L5 ran the two shapes past it. A CT block calling a *recursive* helper prints 55 for `fib(10)` with nothing added, and a CT block whose callee is defined below it now takes L5's message naming the block rather than LLVM's. The only CT-only code in L5 is the prescan half of `g-ct-module-defns`, which exists because a CT block can define `defn`s and a macro body cannot — the one place the two consumers of this mechanism are not symmetric. Two smaller findings recorded in §7 L5: L3's `"reads a program global"` reason records the **global's** name rather than the reading function's, so it needs its own sentence instead of the shared template; and a **bounded generic** called from a macro body now takes the forward-reference message, accurately (its stamp is emitted when the mono worklist drains, at the end of the unit) but with a note whose advice does not apply — before L5 the same shape leaked `use of undefined value '@ident.i32'`, so it is an improvement either way, and calling a generic from a macro body stays unsupported.
- **Stage 20 L6 — a macro body may read the program's globals, initializers and all.** 2026-09-12, §5.7 and §11 Q3 of [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md). `ProgGlobal` joins `ProgDefn` in the registry (`program-global-lookup`/`-record`/`-note-end`, `src/scope.nuc`), `emit-defvar` opens the span **above** the array backing global so `@g.data` is inside the text a mirror copies, and `ct-mirror-classify` gives a global the same three-way split a function gets: a host-exported `lib/` global stays a `declare` — which is what keeps §3.2's one arena and one intern table — one an earlier flush defined is declared, and anything else is copied in and scanned. `make bootstrap` byte-identical with the mirror counter **measured** 0 (a throwaway marker, since removed and grepped for): a 478-file sweep produced 282 markers, every one `0 0`, with zero `--warn-ct-shadow` fires and zero `use of undefined value` / `IR parse error`. `make test` 1022/0/0; `abi-test`, `layout-test`, `avr-test` green. `s20-macro-helper-reads-program-global` moved out of `suite-refusals.nuc` and into `suite-s16.nuc` at exit 7, which is the item L5 worded as a limitation.

  **§11 Q3 answered by probe, and the probe's own prediction was wrong.** The question was `internal` or `weak_odr` for a mirrored global, with the design expecting `internal` to give "two copies, silently diverging". It gives neither copy: two macros with two private helpers over one `defvar- g-s20p` make the second flush a real second module that *declares* what the first defined, and `internal` keeps that definition out of the dylib's symbol table — `JIT session error: Symbols not found: [ q3_p1__g-s20p ]`, after which `nucleusc` **dumped core** rather than reporting, a property of a poisoned materialisation that §1.1's clean `JIT lookup failed` does not predict. `weak_odr` gives one copy, and the probe's two expansions read 1 and 2. Narrower than it reads, too: only a `defvar-` reaches the rewrite, since an imported file's global already says `weak_odr` and an entry-file `defvar` carries no linkage word at all.

  **§5.7's snapshot-and-restore of `g-init-drained` is wrong in both directions, and the cursor is not needed at all.** The section names the real trap — a compile-time drain that consumes the cursor silently deletes those initializers from the program's own `@__nucleus_init` — and then prescribes a fix that emits `set!`s for globals the mirror does not define (an undefined `@g` in the module) and, because it restores the cursor, makes the *next* flush re-emit the same jobs and run a shared global's initializer twice, which is the "one value, not two" gate failing. The filter that is correct is the one `InitJob.ir-name` was added for: the jobs of the globals **this** mirror defines, which `g-ct-mirror-defined` already makes a define-exactly-once set. `g-init-drained` is never touched, so the program keeps every initializer by construction. Proven both ways in one fixture: `probe` expands to 1 because the compile-time copy ran, `main` exits 43 because the program's copy ran too, and the emitted `.ll` is asserted to still contain `store i32 %t1, ptr @g-s20r` inside `@__nucleus_init` — a consumed cursor would compile, link, expand to 1, and exit **1**.

  **Two holes this document did not have, each the previous phase's lesson one level out.** A macro module needs the `declare` for a global it **names**, and unlike a call there is no chokepoint that produces one — a read, a `set!`, an `addr-of` and a member GEP each spell `@g` by their own route — so a macro body reading a global *directly* had no root at all and leaked `use of undefined value '@g-s20k'`, L5's "no raw LLVM text on any macro path" failing in a shape L5 could not reach. Rather than instrument four sites and leave a fifth, `ct-scan-body-globals` asks the module's finished IR, which is the ground truth §5.4 already trusts, writing its declares to a side buffer so `ct-decl` is never grown under a live `StrView`. And **a flush nests**: emitting a `defvar`'s queued initializer runs `emit-node`, which expands any macro in it, and that expansion flushes a mirror of its own — with L3's per-flush vectors reset-but-not-restored (the `g-macro-decls` idiom L4 had already found fatal once) the outer walk resumes on the inner's state and the file fails `Symbols not found: [ s20-readn ]`, measured. `ct-mirror-flush` is now a save/restore wrapper, and `g-ct-init-id` is claimed at mint time so two live flushes cannot both be `@__ct_init_0`. Smaller: the copied-global set and the initializer IR grow each other, so the walk iterates to a fixpoint; and a global span is the one span whose own definitions the scan must skip, because G-2 shape 4 puts two `@name = ` lines in it.


- **Stage 20 L8 — the cross-target refusal, the global shadow, and the stage closed.** 2026-09-12, §5.9 and §7 L8 of [stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md](stage20-macros/macro-call-linking.md). A mirror is a **host-triple** module assembled out of `g-def-buf` spans that were ABI-lowered for `--target=`, so under a cross target the compiler now refuses rather than running wrong-ABI text: `macro 'xt' needs the program's own 'xt-helper' at compile time, and the program is lowered for 'avr' while a macro body runs on the host, 'x86_64-pc-linux-gnu'`. Before it, that file compiled clean and the macro's answer came out of AVR-lowered i32 code executed on x86_64. **§5.9's own test is wrong**: `g-target != g-host-target` compares two `Target` objects and `target-init` builds a second one for *any* `--target=`, the host's own triple included, so the object test refuses an identical ABI — the comparison is on the two interned triples, which is what `src/cheader.nuc` already does for the same question, and `--target=x86_64-pc-linux-gnu` on an x86_64 host exits 0 under it. Only a **flush** is refused, so a body naming nothing but the compile-time runtime is untouched: `make avr-test` 8/8, both riscv units unchanged, a 392-file sweep of `examples/` and `tests/fixtures/` under `--target=avr` and `--target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu` with **zero** refusals, and both Windows boot IRs cross-emitting clean (into a scratch directory — `make windows-boot` overwrites the committed artifacts). That last property is the phase's unplanned dividend: **a flush is refused iff a flush is required, so §6's mirror counter is now readable from the outside.** `nucleusc --target=riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu --emit-llvm src/nucleusc.nuc` exiting 0 *is* "the compiler's own compilation mirrors nothing", which through L7 could only be had from a throwaway marker. §8 asked for that assertion in `tests/suite-audits.nuc` and no phase shipped it; it is still unshipped (30 s for `src/nucleusc.nuc` alone), but `s20-macro-cross-target-host-resolved` now pins the shape.

  **`--warn-ct-shadow` widened to a program GLOBAL, and only because the measurement allowed it.** The zero-fire property across `lib/`, `src/` and `examples/` is the entire licence for the warning to default on (§3.5, §11 Q2), so the extension was gated on re-measuring it rather than on the hazard being symmetric: a 478-file sweep fired **0** of either spelling, and `make test` is clean of both. The hazard is *not* quite symmetric and the note says so — before L6 a mirrored global was refused outright, never silently bound — but the collision and the rule are identical, and the probe is equally discriminating, since the compiler's own `g-block-term` is never 7 mid-compilation. A global reference has no line of its own (L6 reads them out of the body's finished IR), so the warning is located on the `defmacro`/`compile-time` form.

  **What six phases of incremental editing left stale.** `docs/macros.md` said "Three consequences" over four bullets — L6 added the fourth and did not count. `design/overview.md`'s Stage 20 entry still described L2 as `dlsym`, which is the spelling §5.2 exists to reject, and still read as a plan. `design/stage20-macros/overview.md` §1.3 still stated part one's premise ("a macro body cannot call a recursive helper it defines itself … any tree walk must be written iteratively, inline") as current, and §8 still deferred the whole feature. And **deferred/overview.md's "Name pasting" entry named a blocker that no longer exists**: it rested on decision 8, "call nothing outside `lib/node.nuc`", which §3.1 retired — a macro body may call its own helper, so `(str "g-" …)` → `intern-node` → a spliced symbol works, measured at exit 42. The entry stays deferred on the argument that survives (a pasted name is ungreppable; a two-column table is not), which is the half that was always load-bearing. Two claims held up under re-probing: `die-at` in a macro body is still `unknown: die-at` — the blocker is *scope*, not linkage — and macro expansion still has no depth limit, which L8 checked costs a **SIGSEGV** rather than a hang, on `bin/nucleusc` (pre-L1) exactly as on the current build.

  `make bootstrap` byte-identical with the mirror counter **measured** 0 (a throwaway unconditional marker, since removed and grepped for): 0 over `make bootstrap`, 0 over a 478-file sweep of `examples/`, `lib/`, `tests/` and `src/`, and 5 under `make test` — every one of them inside a unit that exists to exercise the mirror, which is what makes the zeroes a measurement rather than a broken marker. `make test` 1025/0/0 (1022 + `s20-macro-cross-target`, `s20-macro-cross-target-host-resolved`, `s20-macro-shadows-compiler-global`); `avr-test`, `abi-test`, `layout-test`, `check-headers` all green. Smaller edges: `(invoke v 0:usize)` does not parse — the `name:type` sugar needs a name, so an index literal is `(as usize 0)` — and `&rest` in test code is read as the variadic marker rather than as `addr-of rest`.
- **Stage 20 C1/C2 — a macro argument may be computed.** 2026-09-14, phases C1 and C2 of [stage20-macros/computed-macro-arguments.md](stage20-macros/computed-macro-arguments.md) at §2.1 width (a). `~e` in **macro-argument position** — and nowhere else — now means "evaluate `e` at compile time and substitute the `(raw Node)` it returns", so `(macmap ((name arity) `(defn ~name () ~arity)) ~(build-rows))` works and the `:rest` family, `case` and the variadic operators inherit the same capability with no change of their own. The spelling was free: it was `unquote outside quasiquote` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:12246`), and that arm is untouched — a `~` nested one level inside an argument, in a function call's arguments, in a `defn` body or at top level still takes it, which `s20-computed-arg-only-in-macro-argument` pins in all four positions. **The evaluator is the macro-body path with a different caller**, which is the whole reason width (a) is cheap: `ct-eval-node` synthesizes a `MacroDef` with `pcount` 0 over a two-cell `(<nothing> e)` and hands it to `compile-macro-body` at `body-start` 1, so the mirror, the closure walk, `--warn-ct-shadow`, L5's forward-reference refusal, L6's nested flush and L8's cross-target refusal are all inherited rather than rebuilt — each verified by probe, not assumed. `ct-subst-args` sits at the top of `expand-macro-call`, **before** the arity checks so "expects N args, got M" counts what the macro receives, and rebuilds the call only when a `~` is actually present, so every other program reaches the body on the reader's own node.

  **The one genuinely new piece is the type check, and §4.4 was right to make it a question.** A body that does not yield a node is refused at the argument's own line — `the computed argument '~5' must evaluate to (raw Node), not i32` — rather than reaching LLVM as `integer constant must have integer type` about generated code. It lives in `compile-macro-body`'s body loop, gated on a new `is-arg` parameter, because that is where the `Val` is and the last body form is the return value; the accepted set is a `TY-PTR` whose element is `Node` or absent, since the compiler's own node producers (`macroexpand-form`, `expand-macro-call`) are declared bare `ptr` and `?&Node` is a pointer with the null niche. **The quasiquote hole itself stays open** — `~5` *inside* a quasiquote is still LLVM's message, which [quasiquote-levels.md](stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md) §10.3 filed and still owns; C2's obligation was not to inherit it, and it does not.

  **The diagnostic subject had to move.** L5's `ct-body-who` says `macro 'x'`, and for a computed argument the fault is in the **caller's** text, so a `g-ct-body-arg` latch — saved and restored beside `g-ct-body-name`, since a `macrolet` compiles a body inside another one — switches all three messages (`ct-forward-ref-check`, `warn-ct-shadow`, `warn-ct-shadow-global`) to `the computed argument '~(rows)'`, with the note's "above the macro" becoming "above the call".

  **§4.4's four questions, answered by probe.** *The REPL needs no gate*: `~e` evaluates at the prompt (42 / 7 / 42 over three shapes, one of them a `macrolet` binding and one calling a REPL-defined `defn`), because `ct-mirror-flush`'s `g-interactive` early return is enough — the REPL JIT-compiles every top-level form anyway. The one REPL limit found is **pre-existing and unrelated**, identical on `bin/nucleusc`: a prompt-typed `defn` whose body builds nodes dies `use of undefined value '@alloc-node'`, so a REPL producer has to be a quasiquote or come from an import. *`macrolet` gets it for free* — confirmed rather than asserted, and the unit is mid-function, which is also what says `ct-eval-node` must bracket `push-function-state` the way `macrolet-bind` does. *Cross-target does what L8 says*: a computed argument needing a mirror is refused naming the argument, one whose body touches only the compile-time runtime cross-compiles untouched, and `make avr-test` is 8/8. *The substitution does not recurse*, by decision and by test.

  `make bootstrap` byte-identical — nothing in the tree spells `~` at source level — with the mirror counter **0**, read the way L8 made readable rather than from a throwaway: `--emit-llvm` of `src/nucleusc.nuc` exits 0 under `riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu` and both Windows triples, with zero refusals. `make test` 1034/0/0 (1025 + 9: four feature units in `suite-s16.nuc`, five diagnostic units in `suite-refusals.nuc`); `avr-test`, `abi-test`, `layout-test`, `check-headers` all green. C4 still owns the [deferred/overview.md](deferred/overview.md) rewrite and the overview record.
- **Stage 20 C3 — and the computed argument may be N of them.** 2026-09-14, phase C3 of [stage20-macros/computed-macro-arguments.md](stage20-macros/computed-macro-arguments.md). `~@e` evaluates `e` and splices the list it returns in as **N arguments**, which is what serves the `:rest` family: `(macfoldr _+ 0 ~@(nums))` folds over a computed list, and so do `macfoldl`, `+`, `case` and any user `:rest` macro, none of them changed. It is **the same evaluation as `~e`** — one `ct-eval-node` call, one `__ct_eval_N` module — differing only in what the caller does with the result, so L5's forward reference, L6's nested flush, L8's cross-target refusal, `--warn-ct-shadow` and `macrolet` support all arrived already working (each re-probed on the `~@` spelling, not assumed). The whole change is 40 lines: `ct-subst-args` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:11380`) now normalizes each argument to *the list of elements it contributes* — one cell normally, the evaluated list for `~@e` — and appends copies, which is why an empty splice contributes **zero** arguments rather than one null one, and why the arity check at `expand-macro-call` still counts what the macro receives (`macro 'one2': expects 1 args, got 0`).

  **Two smaller decisions.** The shape check is its own function (`ct-eval-require-list`, `:1563`) rather than a clause of C1's type check, because they answer different questions at different times: `ct-eval-require-node` is a **compile-time type** test inside `compile-macro-body` and still fires first (`~@5` is `must evaluate to (raw Node), not i32`), while the shape is a property of the **value** and is only knowable after the JIT call. It walks the whole list rather than testing the head, so a dotted tail is refused at the cell that has one instead of being silently dropped — the difference between a diagnostic and a lost argument. And `ct-eval-node`/`ct-eval-arg-spelling` grew a `splice` flag whose only job is the marker the diagnostics quote back: a computed argument's fault is in the caller's text, so the text has to be echoed as the author wrote it, `~@(nums)` and not `~(nums)`.

  `make bootstrap` byte-identical, mirror counter **0** by L8's reading (`--emit-llvm` of `src/nucleusc.nuc` exits 0 under `riscv64-unknown-linux-gnu` and both Windows triples, zero refusals). Nothing in the tree spells `~@` at source level — every `~@` in `lib/macros.nuc` is *inside* a quasiquote, where `emit-qq-list` consumes it and `ct-subst-args` never sees it. `make test` 1040/0/0 (1034 + 6: five feature units in `suite-s16.nuc`, one diagnostic unit in `suite-refusals.nuc`, plus `~@` cases folded into C2's two existing boundary/arity units); `avr-test`, `abi-test`, `layout-test`, `check-headers` all green. **One thing C3 makes more likely to be hit and does not fix:** the natural way to write a producer for a spliced list is to interpolate a computed integer, `` `~n `` with `n:i32`, which is [quasiquote-levels.md](stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md) §10.3's still-open hole — the unit that counts spliced arguments had to fold quasiquoted literals instead. Probing it while writing that unit found the hole has **two** manifestations, both reproduced byte-for-byte on the committed `bin/nucleusc` and so both pre-existing: nested in a list (`` `(_+ 0 ~n) ``) it is the recorded `compile-time: IR parse error: '%t14' defined with type 'i32' but expected 'ptr'` about generated text, but **bare** (`` `~n `` as the body's value) it is instead `macro 'x': returned null` — `compile-macro-body`'s body loop keeps the last value only when its type is `TY-PTR`, so a non-node value is dropped and the macro silently yields null, with no line and no mention of `~`. The second is the worse message of the two and §10.3 does not record it. Owned there, not here.

- **Stage 20 U1 — the unquote operand is typed, warn-only, and the sweep found nothing to warn about.** 2026-09-15, phase U1 of [stage20-macros/unquote-operand-typing.md](stage20-macros/unquote-operand-typing.md), which closes [quasiquote-levels.md](stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md) §10.3. `emit-qq-form`'s level-1 unquote arm used the emitted `Val` without looking at its type, and §10.3's two faces are that one omission seen from two distances: nested (`` `(_+ 0 ~n) ``) hands a non-pointer to `@__cons` and LLVM objects first, bare (`` `~n ``) *is* the body's value and `compile-macro-body`'s `TY-PTR` `last-val` filter drops it into `macro 'm': returned null`. **One check retires both** — 21 lines, `qq-require-node-operand` (`src/nucleusc.nuc:1559`) reusing C2's `ct-node-typed?` and `ct-eval-arg-spelling` verbatim, called from the two level-1 arms and nowhere deeper (deeper is data, and `emit-qq-tagged` is untouched). It is a **sibling** of `ct-eval-require-node` rather than a reuse of it, and that is the one thing the design did not anticipate: `ct-eval-require-node`'s subject comes from `ct-body-who`, which assumes a compile-time body, but quasiquote is legal in an ordinary `defn`, where it would announce a plain function as "compile-time block".

  **A third manifestation, not in §10.3:** `emit-qq-list`'s splice arm has the same hole against `@__append`, so `` `(_+ 0 ~@n) `` with `n:i32` was a fourth undiagnosed shape. The `splice` flag C3 added for exactly this purpose made it free — the diagnostic quotes back `~@n`, and the note switches to `` `~@` splices its value in as source ``, matching `ct-eval-require-list`'s existing wording.

  **The sweep is the phase.** `ct-node-typed?` is stricter than LLVM (LLVM takes any `ptr` into `@__cons`; the predicate takes bare `ptr` and `ptr:Node` only), so U2's promotion to `die-at` is conditional on the tree containing no counter-example. **478 `.nuc` files in `lib/`, `src/`, `examples/` and `tests/` compiled individually with the new binary, stderr captured: zero fires.** The 20 warnings the sweep did surface are all pre-existing C-header/cheader-export ones. **Coverage was proven, not assumed** — 196 of the 478 exit non-zero (163 refusal fixtures, plus the 13 `src/` modules and 20 `tests/suite-*.nuc` that do not stand alone), so a second compiler was built whose check warns on *success* instead of failure: it reports the level-1 arm reached **50,207 times across 64 files**, and separately no failing fixture contains an unquote token at all. `make bootstrap` byte-identical (`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`), `make test` **1040/0/0** with no expectation edited. Both §10.3 rows now warn at the author's own line naming the author's own text (`u1-nested.nuc:3: warning: the unquote operand '~n' must evaluate to (raw Node), not i32`), with the old symptom still following behind it until U2 replaces it.

- **Stage 20 U2/U3 — the unquote operand is an error, the macro body is held to the same rule, and `node-int` exists.** 2026-09-15, phases U2 and U3 of [stage20-macros/unquote-operand-typing.md](stage20-macros/unquote-operand-typing.md) (§7, §8), closing [quasiquote-levels.md](stage20-macros/quasiquote-levels.md) §10.3 and its §8 bullet, and resolving [computed-macro-arguments.md](stage20-macros/computed-macro-arguments.md) §4.6's usability note. U1's warning became a located `die-at` with nothing else moved — the half that needed no new measurement, because U1 had already made it. The half that did is the second: `compile-macro-body`'s body loop already ran C2's check on the last body form *for the computed-argument caller only*, and dropping that `is-arg` conjunct extends it to every `defmacro` and `macrolet` body, which is where `(defmacro m () 5)` lives. `(compile-time …)` is not on this path — it assembles its own module — which is exactly what makes `ct-body-who` safe to reuse: at this site it can only ever say `macro '<name>'`, never "compile-time block", the misnaming that forced U1 to build a sibling rather than reuse it.

  **The new site got U1's gate, not U1's result.** Landed warn-only, swept the same 478 `.nuc` files in `lib/`, `src/`, `examples/` and `tests/`: **zero fires**, the only 20 warnings being the identical pre-existing C-header and cheader-export set U1 saw. Reach was proven by the success-warning probe [context/conventions.md](../context/conventions.md) now prescribes — **12,111 reaches across 403 of the 478 files**, an order of magnitude broader than U1's arm because every compilation unit compiles `lib/macros.nuc`'s macro bodies (23 reaches is the floor for any file that gets past its imports). The 75 zero-reach files were read individually: twelve mention `defmacro`/`macrolet`, and all twelve are accounted for — six `src/` modules and five `tests/suite-*.nuc` that do not stand alone (covered by `src/nucleusc.nuc`'s 1,822 and `tests/nuctests.nuc`'s 477), plus `examples/avr-isr.nuc`, where the only occurrence is a comment explaining that a top-level macro would not work. The four failing fixtures that do contain a macro reach exactly 23, the prelude baseline measured against a one-line program. Zero, so it was promoted; the one class worth worrying about, `macro-error` as a terminal form (it is `:void`), does not occur.

  **Three things the plan did not settle.** The line is the **body form's**, not `ff`'s — `ff` is the whole `defmacro`, so `(ff 'line)` would report line 1 for a body ending ten lines down; `node-line` gets the form's own line and falls back for an interned symbol, and the C1 caller keeps `(ff 'line)` because its `ff` *is* the caller's text, so C2's tested line numbers do not move. The note branches on `g-ct-body-arg` while the message does not, which is the right split: `die-at`'s line is already generic because `ct-body-who` names either subject. And `node-int` is named only in `qq-require-node-operand`'s note, not in the shared tail, because the shared tail is C1's shipped text. **A bonus the sharp-edge docs record:** a macro body that is a `cond` collapsing to void — `docs/macros.md`'s long-standing edge, whose second symptom was `returned null` — is now `macro 'm' must evaluate to (raw Node), not void` at the body form's line. `returned null` survives only for a node-*typed* value that is null at run time, which is not a type mistake.

  **U3 answered §3's open question with a yes.** `src/nucleusc.nuc:980` is `(import-use node)`, so adding a six-line `node-int` to `lib/node.nuc` put an 18-line `weak_odr define ptr @node-int` into the compiler's own IR. The bootstrap is unaffected regardless (stage1 and stage2 compile the same source), byte-identical with no boot refresh — but the **committed generated headers** are not: `lib/node.h` and `lib/node.nuch` failed `tests/suite-audits.nuc` as `STALE` until `scripts/check-headers.sh --fix` ran. That is the real cost of a `defn` in `lib/`, and §2's "no compiler change" framing hid it. `node-int` takes an `i64` with no `line` (a synthesized node has no source position, and `node-line`'s fallback is the mechanism for that — `intern-node` is the precedent), an `i32` widens at the call, and it is compile-time-runtime resolved like `node-at`, so a macro body calling it records no mirror root and cross-compiles unaffected.

  `make bootstrap` byte-identical after each of the three phases; `make test` **1043/0/0** with no expectation edited, `abi-test`/`layout-test`/`avr-test`/`check-headers` green. Five new units: `s20-unquote-operand-not-a-node` (nested, bare, and the splice shape §10.3 never recorded) and `s20-macro-body-not-a-node` in `tests/suite-refusals.nuc` — through `check-ct-refused`, which pins that LLVM's own text no longer leaks behind the diagnostic — and `s20-node-int` in `tests/suite-s16.nuc`, whose second case is the `~@e` row producer built out of computed integers that §4.6 said had no clean spelling.

- **Stage 20 S1/S3 — the string-literal table is per module, and a literal-free program carries none.** 2026-09-15, phases S1 and S3 of [stage20-macros/string-table-per-module.md](stage20-macros/string-table-per-module.md) (§6, §7), promoted out of [deferred/overview.md](deferred/overview.md) and closing the defect [stage20-macros/overview.md](stage20-macros/overview.md) §9.2 had to replace its byte-identical gate over. `g-strs` is one vector per *compilation* and `emit-string-table` wrote **all** of it into whichever module was being assembled, so every macro body's quasiquote interned its symbol spellings into the table the **program** module emits — constants nothing in the program references — and adding one macro to `lib/prelude.nuc` renumbered every string after it in every program. **Measured: a literal-free program 106 dead `@.str` constants → 0; a hello-world over `lib/io.nuc` 186 dead → 1; `build/nucleusc.ll` 10,147 constants → 6,237, 3,911 dead → 0, 13.94 MB → 13.53 MB (−405 KB, −2.9%).** The two `macrolet` roster tables in `src/repl.nuc` accounted for 145 of those dead constants, not the 125 §1 estimated, because several of their spellings are minted by other macro bodies too. Live constants are unchanged at 6,236 → 6,237, the one addition being `vector-bounds`' `"remove-at"` literal from the `Vector` stamp the new truncate instantiates — which is the check that says no live constant was lost.

  **The plan's swap does not survive its own exception, and that is the delta.** §3 ruled that `ct-mirror-flush` must keep emitting the *program's* table, because it copies program `define` spans into the mirror and those spans name program `@.str` ids. But `expand-macro-call` flushes a mirror before **every** macro expansion and a macro body is itself compiled with `emit-node`, so a macro whose body calls another macro that calls a program defn builds a mirror **while the outer body's table is live**; `ct-mirror-build-init` reaches the same place from the other side, running `emit-node` over a program global's initializer. Under the swap, "leave that call alone" therefore means "emit the CT body's table" — exactly what §3 forbids. Measured, not argued: with the mirror given a compile-time body's watermark on a throwaway build, an eight-line program (a macro calling a macro calling a `defn` with a string literal) fails `compile-time: IR parse error: use of undefined value '@.str.29'`. Nor can the swap be patched, because the mirror would then need *both* tables and both start at `@.str.0`.

  So `g-strs` is **never redirected**. Each compile-time module path records `(count g-strs)` as a **watermark** in the same "redirect global state" `let` the plan named; `emit-string-table` takes it as a `from` parameter and emits only `[base, end)`; `strs-truncate-to` (beside `intern-string` in `src/scope.nuc`, the `repl-vec-truncate` idiom) drops the tail once the module is assembled. Every reader keeps seeing the program's ids at all times, so the nested flush is correct **by construction** rather than by a restore someone has to remember, and a `die-at` that unwinds past a truncate leaves a few dead constants instead of leaving the program without its table. §3's table also has **four rows where the tree has five call sites** — the fifth is `src/repl.nuc`'s, which assembles a REPL entry's own *program* module and so takes the whole table — and there are **two** swap sites, not three: `assemble-module-ir` is the one that stops carrying the others' strings and needs no watermark of its own.

  **The gate is the direct method, because `make bootstrap` diverges by construction until S2** and its diff is *only* string-table renumbering (`@.str.10147` against `@.str.6236`, no instruction moved): `src/nucleusc.nuc` compiled with `build/nucleusc`, that IR linked into a stage-2 binary, `src/nucleusc.nuc` recompiled with it — **byte-identical**. `make test` **1044/0/0** (1043 + S3's unit). **S2 done 2026-09-15 under explicit authorisation:** `make clean && make && make update-bootstrap && make clean && make && make bootstrap` passed first time — `PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll` at the new fixed point, `make test` 1044/0/0, and `boot/nucleusc.ll` plus both Windows boot IRs rewritten in lock-step. The divergence was staleness and nothing else, as the direct method had already established.

  **S3 is two synthesized programs rather than a corpus walk**, `string-table-per-module` in `tests/suite-audits.nuc`: a program with no literal of its own — its own `defmacro`, a `dotimes`, a `when`, and the expansion of all three — must emit no `@.str` at all, and a one-literal control must emit exactly `@.str.0`, because without the control the first claim is equally satisfied by a compiler that emits no string table ever. It fails against the old compiler, which puts **107** constants into that literal-free program. **`S4` is open and was found by S1's own sweep:** the last three dead constants in the tree (`"arena malloc"`, `"arena grow"`, `"intern: out of memory"`, over 229 programs in `tests/fixtures/` and `examples/`) are not a macro-body leak but `emit-import-forms`' throwaway `sink` for a compile-time-only import — the emitter runs unchanged into it, so the `@.str` *references* are discarded while `intern-string` still appends. It is **not** the same one-line watermark, because `emit-toplevel-forms` runs `drain-mono-worklist` at every depth and a drain writes to `g-def-stream-program`, the real program buffer, so a stamp emitted inside the sink window may legitimately reference a string interned inside it. That residue is also why S3 could not make the honest tree-wide claim ("no program module defines an `@.str` nothing references") without an allowlist; it becomes available once S4 lands.

- **Stage 20 S4 — the compile-time-only import's sink, and the tree-wide claim with no allowlist.** 2026-09-15, phase S4 of [stage20-macros/string-table-per-module.md](stage20-macros/string-table-per-module.md) (§8 the plan, §9 as built), closing the residue S1's own sweep found and the [deferred/overview.md](deferred/overview.md) entry with it. After S1 the tree still carried dead `@.str` constants in 41 of 229 program modules, all three texts from `lib/arena.nuc` and `lib/intern.nuc`: `emit-import-forms` points `g-def-stream`/`g-out` at a throwaway `sink` for a compile-time-only import and runs the emitter unchanged into it, so the bodies' `@.str` *references* are discarded while `intern-string` still appends. **Neither of S1's two tools transfers**, and §8.1 says why: `emit-toplevel-forms` runs `drain-mono-worklist` at every depth and a drain writes to `g-def-stream-program`, the real program buffer, so one sink window receives discardable and live strings interleaved and a positional id cannot tell them apart — truncating dangles a live stamp reference, keeping is the leak, and promoting a survivor out of the tail renumbers everything after it.

  **So the fix is at the consumer, not the producer: emit what the module references rather than what was interned.** `emit-string-table` already writes `@.str.<sl 'id>` from the stored id rather than the loop counter, so the table may be **sparse** — dropping an entry renumbers nothing and LLVM has no opinion about a gap. It gained a fourth parameter, a nullable `(Vector i32)` of per-id marks; `assemble-module-ir` scans `g-type-stream`, `g-decl-stream` and `g-def-buf` for `@.str.<digits>` (the whole digit run, so `@.str.1` does not match `@.str.10`) and the other four call sites pass `null`, meaning the whole `[from, end)` window. This closes the class **by construction rather than by enumeration** — any future path that interns into a discarded stream is covered without being found first, which matters because S1's sweep is how this one surfaced.

  **The plan was one row short, and it is the row that would have produced a broken module.** §8.2 names `g-deferror-name-sids` as the set that is not in the buffers; there are **two** such vectors. `emit-deferror-table` emits `@nuc_err_names` from that one and `@nuc_err_messages` from `g-deferror-msg-sids`, both *after* the string table, so a filter built to §8.2 as written emits a module that references its error messages and defines none of them. `tests/fixtures/s1-sugar-rets.nuc` was already the witness in the tree — its `@.str.4` and `@.str.6` are the two messages, referenced by nothing but that array. The generalisation is the census rule S1 met once already: a derived referenced-set must enumerate every **emitter** of a reference, and a table built from parallel id vectors has one emitter per vector. Two smaller things the plan does not mention and the code needs: index 0 of the deferror vectors is the reserved "no error" placeholder emitted as `ptr null`, so the marking loop starts at 1; and the table's trailing blank line stopped being a function of the count — `(when (> (count g-strs) from) …)` meant "this module emitted something" only while every id in the window *was* emitted, and it is now a latch set by the emit itself. `(import-ct intern)` over a bare `main` is the witness: its whole table is the sink's residue, and without the latch the module keeps a separator with nothing above it.

  **Only `assemble-module-ir` filters**, and the two other program-shaped modules keep the whole table for the same reason: the scan does not cover the text they emit. `ct-mirror-emit-module` assembles from four further buffers (`mdecl`, `mdef`, `initir`, `emit-qq-helpers`' output) and `repl-jit-module-rt-rewrite` puts the REPL preamble — a second module buffer — above the table. Both are JIT-only and never reach a file, so the residue costs bytes in a module parsed once and never written.

  **§8.4's prediction held exactly, and it was the sharpest correctness test available.** S1 left `build/nucleusc.ll` with zero dead constants, so the filter is a no-op on the compiler's own module: **`make bootstrap` byte-identical, `PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`, no boot convergence, no committed artifact touched.** The compiler's table went 6,237 → 6,238 constants, the one addition being `vector-bounds`' `"set"` literal from the `(Vector i32)` stamp the marks vector instantiates — the same shape of live addition S1's truncate made, and the check that says no live constant was lost. **Tree-wide: 392 files swept, 229 emitting a module and 163 refusing** (this tree's deliberate refusal-fixture count), **3,494 `@.str` defined / 72 dead before → 3,422 / 0 after**. §6.5's "three dead constants" is three distinct *texts*; the population was 72 instances over 41 modules. The live set is provably untouched — per-file `defined − dead` is identical for all 229, and a line-level old-vs-new IR diff over the same 229 finds **every** differing line is a removed `@.str.N = private unnamed_addr constant …`, with nothing renumbered and no instruction moved. **Compile-time cost is not measurable:** `src/nucleusc.nuc --emit-llvm`, three runs each, 7.75/7.83/8.08 s before against 7.92/7.92/8.05 s after — a ~0.07 s mean delta on a 7.9 s compile of a 13 MB module, inside the spread of the runs themselves.

  **S4's test is S3's claim made tree-wide with no allowlist.** `string-table-live-tests-fixtures` and `string-table-live-examples` in `tests/suite-audits.nuc`, beside S3's two synthesized programs, which stay: those pin the claim exactly and cheaply, these make it corpus-wide. The walk is `w4a-no-line-zero`'s, and it counts the modules **emitted** rather than the files seen, because a refusal fixture produces no module and says nothing about this. Per directory rather than one unit over both, following the file's own `reader-parity-*` convention — the suite shards by unit, and one combined unit made its shard the critical path at 101 s against a next-longest 55 s. Each module's IR is walked once for `@.str.` tokens, bit 1 for an id defined at the head of a line and bit 2 for one named anywhere else; line-initial is what makes "definition" decidable, since a newline cannot occur inside a constant's own `c"…"` bytes. **It fails against the pre-S4 compiler** (run over a symlinked tree, so nothing in the repository moved): `tests/fixtures/s1-sugar-rets.nuc: 3 @.str constant(s) defined and referenced nowhere`. `make test` **1046/0/0**; the two units cost 37 s and 46 s serial, taking the sharded suite from **83.8 s to 98.9 s** wall (+18%), measured A/B against a baseline binary built from a copy of `tests/` with them removed.

- **Stage 21 item 2 — one reader, R-1…R-4 built 2026-09-16 → 2026-09-18.** [stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md](stage21-cleanup/one-reader.md) §10 "As built"; closes the "there are two readers" item at [stage21-cleanup/overview.md](stage21-cleanup/overview.md) #2. `lib/read.nuc` is now the compiler's sole reader: `src/reader.nuc` (1,260 lines) is deleted outright, its diagnostics layer (`Diagnostic`, `diag-*`, `die-at`, `report-at`, `cstr-byte-at`/`text-byte-at`, the `parse-error` deferror) moved to a new `src/diagnostics.nuc` (181 lines), and `src/nucleusc.nuc` adopts the library reader through one shim, `read-source-or-report (text (table (ref (Vector RMacro)))):!raw:Node`, that renders a `ReadError` via `report-at`/`diag-stage-note` and runs a temporary post-order `mint-collection-gensyms` walk so every emitted `.ll` stays byte-identical with no boot refresh. `def-rmacro` now registers **at read time**, inside `read-one` itself, rather than at emit time after the whole file had already been read — so it is file-scoped and forward-only and, for the first time, actually affects the file that wrote it; the reader macro table (`RMacro` = `prefix:StrView wrap:Symbol`) is a field on the `Reader` struct, seeded by `read-macro-table-new`, and a `def-rmacro` prefix must start with one of eight bytes (`` $ ' , @ ^ ` | ~ ``). The 4095-byte string-literal cap (`src/reader.nuc`'s `lex-string`, a fixed 4096-byte alloca) is gone — `lib/read.nuc`'s `rd-string` reads into an unbounded `String` — closing the deferred item of the same name in one stroke ([deferred/done.md](deferred/done.md)).

  **One correction from the plan.** The typed error did not ship as a `(Result raw:Node ReadError)` template instance as designed; it is a hand-written `(defunion ReadResult (ok v:raw:Node) (err e:ReadError))`. Template stamping (`type-spelling`) loses pointer *kind* — a stamped `raw` comes back `ref` — which would make the `ok` arm wrongly reject a null `raw:Node`. `ReadError` itself (`code:i32 line:i32 msg:StrView note:StrView`, arena-owned) is unchanged from the design. This is a new rough edge, not a defect in the shipped code, and is recorded at [deferred/overview.md](deferred/overview.md).

  **Two pre-existing compiler bugs surfaced and were fixed along the way, neither designed for.** (1) `coerce-via-cast-rule` passed a struct **first-class** instead of by value when `try` converts a typed error through a `defcast E Err` — reached the moment R-2 gave the reader a real struct-shaped `E`, fixed at the call site. (2) the REPL used to **die entirely** on a syntax error raised while reading an *imported* file — `read-source-or-die`'s batch path now calls `repl-throw` when `g-interactive`, so the session recovers, a retry re-reports the same error, and an unrelated import still works; documented at `docs/compiler.md`'s REPL error-recovery paragraph.

  **Gates.** `dump-ast-corpus` (the `--dump-ast` read → print → read round-trip, R-4's replacement for the four old parity units) re-taken at **457 inputs, 6 rejected, 1,371 artifacts**, byte-identical against the pre-R-1 baseline. `ir-snapshot` re-taken at **439 inputs, 2,720 artifacts** — ten expected per input for `lib/read.nuc` itself (`.ll`, `.nuch`, `.h`, plus the `.win-*.ll` variants) and one `.ll` each for `lib/test.nuc`, `examples/read-sexp.nuc` and `examples/self-test.nuc` — both `verify` **PASS**, no instruction moved. `make bootstrap` reaches the same fixed point with **no boot refresh** (`PASS: stage1.ll == stage2.ll`), which is R-2's central claim borne out: the shim's post-order gensym walk keeps every `__gs_N` numbered exactly as the old compiler numbered it. `make test` **1048/0/0** (was 1046) — `tests/suite-s21.nuc` (245 lines) plus four new fixtures (`s21-def-rmacro.nuc`, `s21-long-string-literal.nuc`, `s21-read-error-value.nuc`, `s21-typed-error-propagation.nuc`) and a REPL transcript pair for `def-rmacro`.

  **Net line change** (`git diff --stat`, the eleven reader-related tracked files): `examples/read-sexp.nuc` +2/−1, `lib/read.h` +/−74, `lib/read.nuc` +/−514, `lib/read.nuch` +/−53, `src/nucleusc.nuc` +/−412, **`src/reader.nuc` −1,260 (deleted)**, `src/repl.nuc` +/−86, `tests/nuctests.nuc` +/−18, `tests/readdump.nuc` −43 (deleted), `tests/suite-audits.nuc` +/−163, `tests/suite-refusals.nuc` +/−12 — **777 insertions, 1,860 deletions** across those eleven files. Plus the new, previously-untracked `src/diagnostics.nuc` (181 lines) and 394 lines of new test surface (`tests/suite-s21.nuc` and the four `s21-*` fixtures/transcripts).

  **The one pending step — delete `mint-collection-gensyms` so `emit-collection-lit` mints the gensym itself — landed 2026-09-18 with item 1's boot refresh**, folding the `__gs_N` renumbering into that one refresh; see "Boot refresh for Stage 21" above. PK-1 (the sibling `pointer-kind-spellings.md` item) landed the same day, after this entry — see the Stage 21 item 1 PK-1 section above.
